Shipping it exposed a second half the section had not seen: root-level `se`/`llm`/`store`/`embeddingModels` were imported only from inside `importSNSite`, which `processTurExchange` called only for a non-empty `snSites` — so a bundle carrying defaults and no site, exactly the seed's shape, was parsed and silently dropped. New `TurSNSiteImport.importInfraOnly` plus a routing branch guarded on the four root lists. The payload ships as reviewable JSON, not a committed binary: `seed/defaults/export.json` holds a GLOBAL Lucene SE (`./store/lucene-se`) and Lucene vector store (`./store/lucene-vector`), both secret-free and under the `/app/store` volume, zipped by `jar --no-manifest` in both Dockerfiles into `/app/export/defaults.zip` ahead of the `chown` so uid 1001 can read it; no LLM, whose exported key is encrypted with the source install's `TurSecretCryptoService` key and would decrypt nowhere else. Verified by injecting each failure before trusting the green: neutering the mixin, deleting the routing branch and adding a `tenantId` to the seed each turned exactly the intended test red, and `TurExportMappersTest` keeps a standing negative control proving a plain mapper still emits the field. 7117 unit tests green.
- **T949** — A failed seed no longer claims to have run, and the successes of a partial run are not repeated. The section left the partial open — mark the directory or mark each file — and one measurement settled it: `TurSNSiteImport` deletes and recreates a site that already exists, so re-importing a ZIP that already landed destroys it and any change made to it since. All-or-nothing would have turned one corrupt archive into a wipe of the ones beside it. So there are two markers because a failed file and a finished directory are different facts. `EXPORT_AUTO_IMPORT` still short-circuits every later boot exactly as before — an existing installation and a ZIP dropped in after completion both behave unchanged — but it is written only when every archive succeeded. A per-file `EXPORT_AUTO_IMPORT_<digest>` records each one that did, so the retry re-runs the failures alone. The id column is fifty characters and a ZIP name is not bounded, hence the digest, with the name in `value` where whoever is diagnosing a stuck seed can read it. The other half of the defect was that failure did not only mean an exception: `importFromMultipartFile` reports a corrupt archive by *returning* `ImportResult.error(...)`, which this runner ignored while logging success — so the extraction failure most likely to hit a truncated Docker layer was the one it could not see. There was no test for this runner at all, which is how both halves survived. Eight now, and four injected faults each turn the intended ones red: writing the marker regardless (the pre-fix state), ignoring the returned error, not recording the per-file successes, and dropping the digest so a long name overflows the column. 7176 tests green.
- **T950** — An import that created no site reports none. The two questions the nullable site name was answering at once — did this bundle fail, and did it contain a site — are now a small `StructuralImport` record with a field each, so a site-less envelope comes back as `siteImported = false` with `siteName = null` instead of announcing a site called "unknown". The sentinel could not simply be dropped: null was the caller's signal for failure, so a bundle with no site had to return something non-null or the whole import aborted, and "unknown" was that something. Separating the fields is what makes both answerable, which is why the record rather than a one-word change. Nothing downstream needed touching: the admin panel already gates its site row on `siteImported` and already types `siteName` as nullable, so the honest values are the ones it was written for. The log line now says "none" where it used to print the sentinel. Three faults were injected and each turns a test red: restoring `"unknown"`, and reading any name as proof a site landed, both trip the infra-only case, and removing the abort on a failed read trips the new unreadable-envelope test — the arm the sentinel existed to protect, and the reason a naive fix would have swapped one silent wrong answer for another. 7178 tests green.
- **T963** — The gate now runs: agent and site creates admit at the HTTP boundary, and the token budget admits in TurLlmModelFactory, the one seam every LLM call passes.
- **T981** — Import and marketplace install now ask what the archive would add and refuse the whole batch before writing; the first-boot seed stays exempt and a test fails if that changes.
- **T982** — TurTenantQuotaIT runs the gate against a real Hibernate session with two FREE tenants and proves the count is scoped: the second is admitted its first agent while the first is full.
- **T983** — The end-user quota page now covers the import refusal — what counts as an addition, that it is all-or-nothing, what the message tells you to trim, and that the boot seed is exempt.

## Block K — Agent CI (flow/prompt eval gate, §XV)

- **T285** — **`TurAgentEvalSet` + `TurAgentEvalCase` golden-set model + CRUD** — per-agent regression cases `(seedTurnsJson, expectedSlotsJson, expectedOutcome, expectedNodeId, rubric)`; `TurAgentEvalSetAPI` under `/api/ai-agent/{id}/eval-set`; Liquibase `v2026.3.1.17.yaml`; exported/imported with the agent (cases inline) via `TurAIAgentExchange.evalSets`. (§XV.1)
- **T286** — **Eval runner + judge scoring** — `TurAgentEvalRunnerService` replays each case through the real `TurAgentChatExecutor` + `TurChatFlowEngineService`, captures slots/cursor/outcome, scores via deterministic diff + the bilingual LLM judge, persists a `TurAgentEvalReport` (green run → new baseline; baseline-break → `regressed`). (§XV.2)
- **T287** — **Pre-publish gate in the flow editor** — `TurAgentEvalGateService` (status GREEN/RED/REGRESSED/NEVER_RUN/NOT_CONFIGURED) surfaced in the editor Lint sidebar via `ChatFlowEvalGatePanel` (run button + findings); a blocking set hard-blocks the chat-flow `PUT` (422) when red/regressed. (§XV.3)
- **T288** — **Agent-eval CI** — `POST /api/ai-agent/{id}/eval/run` + `-Pagent-eval` Maven profile (mirrors `-Pllm-it`; runs `*AgentEvalIT.java`, env-gated by `OPENAI_API_KEY`). (§XV.4)

## Block L — Cost & token governance (provider-agnostic, §XVI)

- **T289** — **`TurLLMPrice` per-model price table + per-turn cost capture** — admin-editable `tur_llm_price` (`vendorId, modelName, input/outputPricePerMillion`, USD; defaults seeded for OpenAI/Anthropic/Gemini via Liquibase `v2026.3.1.40`) resolved through `TurLLMPriceService.computeCost` (cached `turLlmPrice`, self-proxy + evict-on-write). Extended the always-on relational `llm_token_usage` with `agent_id` / `stage` / `cost_usd`: `TurLLMTokenUsageService.recordUsage` now computes + freezes the per-turn USD cost and tags the originating agent and cost stage (`chat.live` from the dispatcher, `chat.background`/`chat.skill` from summaries/compression/weekly-report/skill runner). Provider-agnostic — local/embedded/unpriced models record `cost_usd = 0`, so it works on the Spring AI path without the native-SDK seam. (§XVI.1)
- **T290** — **Live AI-spend dashboard** — `TurLLMCostAPI` (`/api/v2/llm/cost/summary` + `/timeseries`) rolls the frozen `cost_usd` up per agent / model / stage / tenant plus a daily series (new `TurLLMTokenUsageRepository` cost aggregations); `TurLLMPriceAPI` (`/api/v2/llm/price`) is the price-table CRUD (upsert on the natural key). New admin **Cost Governance** page (`/console/cost-governance`, own sidebar item + i18n): date-range, summary cards, daily-spend recharts line, by-agent/model/stage breakdown tables, and an inline price editor. Always-on (relational, no Mongo/Redis dependency); complements — does not duplicate — the F.13 vendor-Usage import. (§XVI.2)
- **T291** — **Turn-time soft budget gate** — per-agent `monthlyBudgetUsd` / `perTurnSoftCapUsd` / `budgetDowngradeLlmId` (Liquibase `v2026.3.1.41`, surfaced in the agent settings form). `TurChatCostBudgetGate.resolveInstance` (called at the top of `TurAgentChatExecutor.execute`, before model build) sums month-to-date spend and on breach swaps to the configured cheaper LLM or warns and proceeds; `warnIfTurnOverCap` (post-turn, in the dispatcher) flags unusually expensive turns. Soft by construction — never aborts a turn; generalizes F.13 T185 to the Spring AI path. (§XVI.3)

## Block F — Native LLM SDK exploitation (§X)

- **T130** — **`TurNativeProviderClient` seam** — interface + `TurNativeProviderClientImpl` exposing per-instance native OpenAI (`com.openai:openai-java`) / Anthropic (`com.anthropic:anthropic-java`) SDK clients; decryption + base-URL resolution reuse the existing provider/`TurSecretCryptoService` plumbing (no parallel security path). (§X.2)
- **T131** — **`TurNativeChatExecutor` prompt-assembly fusion** — the native (OpenAI Responses) path now resolves the per-turn persona (`TurAgentPersonaResolver`) and composes its `instructions` + input list through the same `TurChatPromptAssembler` the Spring AI executor uses, then splits the assembled `List<Message>` back into instructions + flat input. RAG override base, persona voice + brand-context, MCP server instructions, rich-content guidance and chat-memory (T30 relevance + T115 compression) now apply identically on both paths; flow harness stays Spring-AI-only (native handles plain turns). `TurAgentChatFlowContext` made public for the cross-package assemble call. (§X.2)
- **T132** — **Per-LLM-instance capability matrix** — `llm_instance_capability` table (Liquibase `v2026.3.1.34.yaml`) + `TurLLMInstanceCapability` entity + `TurNativeCapability` enum + `TurNativeCapabilityService` (`turNativeCapability` cache) + `TurLLMInstanceCapabilityAPI` (`/api/llm/{instanceId}/capability`); empty matrix = unchanged Spring AI path. Frontend matrix UI (T186 heatmap) still pending. (§X.2)
- **T133** — **OpenAI `web_search` built-in tool** — wired into the Responses path via `TurOpenAiResponsesService` (capability `openai-web-search`). (§X.3.a)
- **T134** — **OpenAI `file_search` built-in tool** — Responses `file_search` over hosted vector stores (`vectorStoreIds` from capability config; `openai-file-search`). (§X.3.b)
- **T135** — **OpenAI `code_interpreter` built-in tool** — Responses managed auto-container path (`openai-code-interpreter`), alongside T80's in-process sandbox. (§X.3.c)
- **T137** — **OpenAI `image_generation` inline tool** — Responses `image_generation` (`openai-image-generation`). (§X.3.e)
- **T138** — **OpenAI remote `mcp` tool** — Responses `mcp` connector to a remote Streamable-HTTP/SSE server (`serverLabel`/`serverUrl` from config, `requireApproval=never`; `openai-mcp`). (§X.3.f)
- **T136** — **OpenAI `computer_use` built-in tool** — the multi-turn screenshot/action loop (`TurOpenAiComputerUseService`) driving the Responses `computer_use_preview` tool: model emits `computer_call` → execute action → feed screenshot back as `computer_call_output` via `previous_response_id`, until a final answer (capped, `truncation=auto`, safety-check acknowledgement opt-in). Action execution + screenshots go through the pluggable `TurComputerUseDriver` seam; Turing ships `TurNoOpComputerUseDriver` (replies with guidance until a real driver bean — e.g. a browser backend — is configured), so the `openai-computer-use` capability is selectable without bundling a browser dep. Config via capability JSON (`model`/`environment`/`displayWidth`/`displayHeight`/`startUrl`/`maxIterations`/`autoAcknowledgeSafetyChecks`). (§X.3.d)

### Block F.3 — Anthropic server-side tools (§X.4)

- **T139** — **Anthropic `web_search_20250305` server tool** — new `TurAnthropicMessagesService` (Messages API analog of `TurOpenAiResponsesService`) maps enabled `anthropic`-vendor capabilities to Anthropic server-side tools that execute in Anthropic's infrastructure (zero Java callbacks, single-turn CALL semantics). `TurNativeChatExecutor` now branches by vendor — OpenAI → Responses, Anthropic → Messages — sharing the T131 prompt-assembly fusion; the `anthropic-web-search` capability (`ANTHROPIC_WEB_SEARCH`) wires `web_search_20250305` (GA, no beta header). `buildTools` collects `anthropic-beta` tokens for the beta-gated tools that follow (T140/T141). No frontend change — selectable via the existing `/api/llm/{id}/capability` matrix API (matrix UI is T132). (§X.4.a)
- **T140** — **Anthropic `web_fetch_20250910` server tool** — `ANTHROPIC_WEB_FETCH` (`anthropic-web-fetch`) wires the `web_fetch_20250910` tool so Claude pulls a URL/PDF directly (no Turing crawler). It's beta-gated: `buildTools` pushes the `web-fetch-2025-09-10` token, which `buildParams` attaches as the `anthropic-beta` request header. No frontend change (matrix API path). (§X.4.b)
- **T141** — **Anthropic `code_execution_20250522` server tool** — `ANTHROPIC_CODE_EXECUTION` (`anthropic-code-execution`) wires the `code_execution_20250522` Python-sandbox tool (beta token `code-execution-2025-05-22`); free when paired with web_search/web_fetch in the same turn. Multiple beta-gated tools accumulate their tokens into one comma-joined `anthropic-beta` header. No frontend change (matrix API path). (§X.4.c)
- **T142** — **Anthropic `bash_20250124` + `text_editor_20250728` tools** — new `TurAnthropicEditorService`: a multi-turn client-tool authoring loop on the standard Messages API. `ANTHROPIC_TEXT_EDITOR` (`anthropic-text-editor`) executes the editor commands (`view`/`create`/`str_replace`/`insert`/`undo_edit`) against the per-conversation `TurAgentWorkspace` (T111) via the pure, unit-tested `TurWorkspaceTextEditor`, so Claude iteratively edits a markdown playbook that persists in (and is served from) the workspace. `ANTHROPIC_BASH` (`anthropic-bash`) is delegated to the pluggable `TurAgentBashExecutor` seam (`TurNoOpAgentBashExecutor` default → errors-as-text, mirroring the computer-use driver) since real shell needs a sandbox. Claude is stateless so the full conversation is resent each step (`Message.toParam()`); every `tool_use` in a turn gets a `tool_result`. `TurNativeChatExecutor`'s Anthropic branch routes these (owning the turn) ahead of the one-shot Messages path. No frontend change (matrix API path). (§X.4.d)
- **T143** — **Anthropic `computer_use_20251124` tool** — new `TurAnthropicComputerUseService` (beta Messages API analog of `TurOpenAiComputerUseService`): the multi-turn screenshot/action loop reusing the same pluggable `TurComputerUseDriver` seam (its DTOs are vendor-neutral). Claude emits a `tool_use` action → driver executes → screenshot returned as a `tool_result` image block, repeated until a final answer; Anthropic is stateless so the full `BetaMessageParam` conversation is resent each step (`BetaMessage.toParam()` appends the assistant turn). `TurNativeChatExecutor`'s Anthropic branch routes `ANTHROPIC_COMPUTER_USE` here (owning the turn) and everything else to the one-shot Messages path. Ships the no-op driver (`TurNoOpComputerUseDriver` from T136 — guidance reply until a real driver bean), so `anthropic-computer-use` is matrix-selectable without a browser dep; Anthropic additionally runs a server-side prompt-injection classifier on each screenshot. Beta flag `computer-use-2025-01-24` (the SDK's paired constant). No frontend change (matrix API path). (§X.4.e)

### Block F.4 — MCP Connector (both vendors) (§X.5)

- **T144** — **Anthropic MCP Connector** — new `ANTHROPIC_MCP` (`anthropic-mcp`) capability wires the Messages API `mcp_servers` request parameter (beta `mcp-client-2025-11-20`), the Anthropic analog of the OpenAI remote `mcp` tool (T138) — Claude calls any remote MCP server with no Turing-side tool code, in a single CALL turn. Because the connector is a top-level request parameter (not a `tools` entry) and the non-beta Messages builder has no typed setter for it, `TurAnthropicMessagesService.buildMcpServers` builds each server as a raw JSON object injected via `putAdditionalBodyProperty("mcp_servers", …)` — leaving the shipped server-tool path (web_search/web_fetch/code_execution) and its tests untouched; the `mcp-client` beta token is added only when ≥1 server is produced. Per-instance `configJson` carries `serverUrl` + `serverLabel`/`serverName` (mirroring OpenAI MCP) plus optional `authToken` (→ `authorization_token`) and `allowedTools` (CSV → `tool_configuration.allowed_tools`); a server missing url/name is skipped with a warning. Frontend: the agent capability picker auto-surfaces "Anthropic MCP" (registry-driven, mutually exclusive with "OpenAI MCP" under the `mcp` function); per-instance config is API-only, exactly as for OpenAI MCP. (§X.5.a)
- **T145** — **Cross-vendor MCP federation layer** — a thin `TurMcpFederationDirectory` keeps a single per-agent allow-list (the existing `ai_agent_mcp_server` relation) and, when the agent opts in via the new `mcp_native_federation` flag (Liquibase `v2026.3.1.49`, default false), hands the matching subset to whichever native vendor runs the turn. It returns one synthetic `OPENAI_MCP`/`ANTHROPIC_MCP` `EnabledCapability` per enabled **HTTP** server (slug + url in the config), which `TurNativeChatExecutor` appends to the turn's capability list — so each server is wired through the **already-shipped** remote-MCP path (T138 OpenAI tool / T144 Anthropic connector) with **no change to either vendor service's signature**. To avoid double-wiring, the executor excludes exactly those federated server ids from the Turing MCP **client** path (new `TurChatToolResolver.resolve(…, excludeMcpServerIds)` overload); `COMMAND`/stdio servers are never federated and stay on the client. Default-off ⇒ byte-for-byte legacy behaviour (servers reached only via Turing's MCP client; non-native turns unaffected). Frontend: a "Native MCP federation" toggle on the agent MCP page (`mcpNativeFederation` on the DTO, copied in the update path). (§X.5.b)
- **T146** — **Turing-as-MCP-Server public consumption surface** — promotes the inbound `/mcp` server (T245/T246) from an OAuth-only internal feature to an **API-key-gated public surface** any MCP client (Claude Desktop, Cursor, an OpenAI Responses agent) can consume. New opt-in `turing.mcp-server.api-key-enabled` (default false): when set together with `require-auth=true`, the new `TurMcpApiKeyAuthenticationFilter` (wired into the `/mcp` `TurMcpSecurityConfig` chain) accepts a Turing API key (`TurDevToken`) in the `Key` header (or `apiKey` query param) **alongside** OAuth — each scheme on its own header, so they coexist without colliding on `Authorization: Bearer`. An API-key principal is granted **read-only** MCP access (`SCOPE_mcp:read`); the gated write/ingestion tools (T253) still require an OAuth token holding `write-scope`, so a public key can never mutate the index. The fail-closed posture is preserved — `require-auth=true` with no `JwtDecoder` **and** `api-key-enabled=false` still denies every request; the loopback gate (T245) and scope policy are untouched. Reuses the existing Dev Token admin UI for key issuance (no new frontend). Verified by `TurMcpApiKeyAuthenticationFilterTest` + the unchanged `TurMcpAuthIT`/`TurMcpServerIT`. (§X.5.c)

### Block F.5 — Voice agents (Realtime API) (§X.6) — block complete

- **T151** — **Voice authoring of chat-flows** — the shared `AiAuthoringPanel` (chat-flow/intent/persona AI authoring) gained an opt-in mic (`enableVoice`) that dictates the instruction via Web Speech STT (`useTuringVoice`): the live transcript streams into the composer (appending to anything typed), the author reviews and sends, so the spoken instruction routes through the **existing** chat-flow authoring skill (`POST …/chat-flow/chat` → `ChatFlowGeneration`) and the live flow preview updates — speak "add an aiQuestion for cargo, then route by role" → the graph changes. Enabled on the chat-flow AI-authoring page; intent/persona panels are unchanged (default off). Recognizer locale follows the app language (pt-BR/es-ES/en-US); the mic only renders when the browser supports STT. en/pt i18n. Completes the F.5 voice block. (§X.6.e)
- **T150** — **Simultaneous interpretation (spectator translation)** — while a voice session converses in the visitor's language, an operator opens the kiosk with `?spectator=<lang>` to watch a live translation of every utterance. `useTuringRealtimeVoice` gained completed-utterance callbacks (`onUserUtterance`/`onAssistantUtterance`, fired on the transcription/transcript `.done` boundaries — not per delta); the kiosk feeds each segment to the new `postVoiceTranslation` SDK call → `POST …/voice/translate`, backed by `TurVoiceTranslationService` (one-shot, provider-agnostic translation via the **default LLM** — works with any configured vendor today; a dedicated `gpt-realtime-translate` model can replace the per-utterance call later). Originals show instantly and the translation fills in when it lands; a failed segment is non-fatal. Unit-tested (6 tests). (§X.6.d)
- **T149** — **Voice-first kiosk mode** — full-screen, chrome-free voice UI at `/console/voice-kiosk?agent=<id>` (also `&llm=`/`&locale=`), mounted as a standalone route **outside** the console shell (no sidebar) for trade-show booths, lobby kiosks and accessibility deployments. One big mic orb (listening halo + speaking pulse), live user/assistant transcripts, mute + end controls, all driven by the T148 `useTuringRealtimeVoice` hook; feature-detects browser support and surfaces a missing-agent hint. New `voiceKiosk` i18n namespace (en/pt). (§X.6.c)
- **T148** — **`/voice/session` endpoint + `useTuringRealtimeVoice` full-duplex hook** — backend `TurAIAgentVoiceAPI` (`GET …/voice/available` to gate the UI, `POST …/voice/session` to mint) over `TurRealtimeVoiceSessionService`: resolves the agent's LLM instance (same logic as the chat API), verifies the `OPENAI_REALTIME_VOICE` capability is enabled, primes instructions from the agent system prompt, reads optional model/voice from the capability `configJson`, and mints via T147. Caller-fixable preconditions → 400; provider/transport failures → 502 (`TurRealtimeVoiceException` mapped in `TurGlobalExceptionHandler`). SDK: `fetchVoiceAvailability` / `postVoiceSession` (CSRF-primed) + the new **`useTuringRealtimeVoice`** React hook — a true bidirectional session (mic capture → 24 kHz PCM16 over a browser-subprotocol-auth WebSocket straight to the vendor; audio deltas scheduled back through Web Audio; server-VAD turn-taking, live user/assistant transcripts, tool-call passthrough, mute). Additive: the existing Web Speech `useTuringVoice` is untouched. (§X.6.b)
- **T147** — **`TurRealtimeVoiceProvider` seam** — pluggable real-time voice transport (the voice analog of `TurNativeProviderClient`): `TurOpenAiRealtimeVoiceProvider` mints a short-lived **ephemeral** session against the OpenAI Realtime `/realtime/client_secrets` endpoint so the browser holds the WebSocket transport to `gpt-realtime` / `gpt-realtime-mini` / `gpt-realtime-1.5` directly with only the `ek_…` token — the account API key never leaves the server (credentials reuse the `TurNativeProviderClient` seam). `TurRealtimeVoiceProviderFactory` resolves by instance plugin type (OpenAI only today; other vendors → "voice unsupported"). New `OPENAI_REALTIME_VOICE` (`openai-realtime-voice`, function `voice`, `ownsTurn`) capability auto-surfaces in the registry/picker; the response carries the resolved model/voice + derived `wss://…/realtime?model=` URL. Response parsing tolerates both the GA top-level and the nested `client_secret` envelope. Not wired into the text-chat `TurNativeChatExecutor` — voice is minted out-of-band by the T148 endpoint. (§X.6.a)

### Block F.6 — Citations & verifiable RAG (§X.7)

- **T152** — **Anthropic Citations (`citations: enabled`)** — wires the long-registered (T435) `citations` request option to real behaviour: when an agent enables it (`requestOptionsJson` `{"citations":"true"}`) on a Claude instance, the turn takes the native Anthropic Messages path **even with no native tool capability enabled**, the executor retrieves the top-K knowledge-base passages (`TurRagSearchToolService.retrievePassagesForCitations`, reusing the post-T24 hybrid retrieval), and `TurAnthropicMessagesService` attaches them to the question as `document` content blocks with `citations: {enabled:true}` (`TurAnthropicCitationSupport`). Claude's per-sentence citations come back as char/page locations, are decoded into `TurChatCitation` (resolved onto the source passage's `sourceId`/`url`) and streamed as a new `type:"citations"` SSE event after the answer token. Both SDKs (`@viglet/turing-sdk` + `@viglet/turing-react-sdk`) parse the event (`onCitations` + `citations` on the response/message) so the answer text is never corrupted; the inline citation-underline UI is T154. Strictly opt-in (null/false → byte-for-byte unchanged path). Covered by `TurAnthropicCitationSupportTest` + `TurNativeChatExecutorTest`. (§X.7.a)
- **T153** — **Anthropic Search Results content type** — Turing's retrieval hits now reach Claude as `search_result` content blocks (the GA encyclopedia-quality citation format — no beta header) instead of T152's `document` blocks. `TurAnthropicCitationSupport.searchResultBlocks` wraps each retrieved passage as `{source: url|sourceId, title, content:[text], citations:{enabled:true}}`, and `TurAnthropicMessagesService.seedConversation` attaches those to the question turn. Response citations now also decode the `search_result` location (`searchResultIndex` → documentIndex, `start/endBlockIndex` → indices, `locationType:"search_result"`), resolved onto the source passage exactly like the char/page paths. The `document`-block builder (T152) stays for future inline-PDF use (T176); the SSE/`TurChatCitation` contract and both SDKs are unchanged (`locationType` is a free string). Covered by new `TurAnthropicCitationSupportTest` cases. (§X.7.b)
- **T154** — **Citation-aware chat UI** — the answer now underlines the exact span Claude grounded each claim on, with a hover popover showing the source quote + a deep link. Backend: `TurAnthropicCitationSupport.appendTextAndCitations` decodes text + citations in one pass so each `TurChatCitation` carries `answerStart`/`answerEnd` (char offsets into the assistant answer — Anthropic attaches each citation to a response text block, so the span is that block's range). SDK: `answerStart`/`answerEnd` added to `TurChatCitation`; the React SDK's `useTuringChat` now carries `citations` on the assistant message (mirroring `sources`). UI: new headless `@viglet/turing-react-ui` `TuringCitedAnswer` (+ pure `segmentCitedAnswer`) renders the answer text with cited spans wrapped via an injectable `renderMark`; the admin skins it with a shadcn `HoverCard` (source title, `(p. N)` for PDF, the cited quote, deep link). When citations are present the admin renders the cited-answer view; otherwise the normal markdown path (`TuringRichContent`). en/pt i18n (`chat.citations.*`). Covered by `cited-answer.test.tsx` (segmentation + render) and a new backend offset test. (§X.7.c)
- **T155** — **Citation drift detection** — turns provable provenance into a time-aware, auditable record. When citations are on and `turing.genai.citation-drift.enabled=true`, `TurCitationDriftService.tapForPersistence` taps the native Anthropic SSE stream and persists every per-sentence citation (`source_id`, cited span, the question, the answer instant) as a `chat_citation_record` row (Liquibase `v2026.3.1.50`). A daily ShedLock-guarded `TurCitationDriftDetectionJob` re-resolves recent records against the live index (`TurRagSearchToolService.retrieveRawForCitations`, the same retrieval that produced them) and flips `citationStale=true` when the source was **re-indexed since the answer** (a current chunk's `modification_date` is newer than `answerProducedAt`) or the cited span is **no longer present** in the source's current chunks; sources that no longer retrieve are left `UNVERIFIABLE` (not flagged). `GET /api/chat/sessions/{conversationId}/citation-drift` (`?recheck=true` for an on-demand "prove it now" pass) returns the per-citation verdicts + a `staleCount`, answering the compliance question "what document did this answer cite, and has it changed since?". Strictly opt-in — default off writes no rows and the scan is a no-op, so T152-T154 citation streaming is byte-for-byte unchanged. Covered by `TurCitationDriftServiceTest` (verdict policy, parse/dedup, scan flag-and-save, date parsing). (§X.7.d)

### Block F.12 — Reasoning summary + observability hardening (§X.13)

- **T180** — **Logprobs reranker (confidence signal)** — a new opt-in `LLM_LOGPROBS` SN RAG rerank strategy (Global Settings, alongside `LLM`/`CROSS_ENCODER`/`COHERE`) that folds the model's token-level certainty into the retrieval order. For each candidate `TurLlmLogprobsRerankStrategy` runs a one-shot yes/no relevance classification through the native OpenAI Responses path with `top_logprobs` on (`TurOpenAiResponsesService.classifyRelevanceProbability`, `maxOutputTokens=1` + `include=message.output_text.logprobs`); the pure `TurLogprobConfidence` converts the first token's `yes`/`no` log-probs into P(relevant) and blends it (50/50) with the reciprocal-rank retrieval signal, so a hedged top hit is demoted and a confident lower hit promoted (the "the LLM hedged but we still ranked it #1" failure mode the T337 LLM-list reranker can't see). Resolves its client from the default LLM; fail-open per the `TurRagReranker` contract (default LLM not OpenAI / no key / no logprobs → empty → retrieval order kept). en/pt i18n for the strategy option. Covered by `TurLogprobConfidenceTest` + `TurLlmLogprobsRerankStrategyTest` + `TurOpenAiResponsesServiceTest`. (§X.13.c)
- **T179** — **Reasoning effort budget (per-stage)** — wires the long-registered (T435) `reasoning-effort` request option (`minimal`/`low`/`medium`/`high`) to real behaviour and adds the per-stage policy `TurReasoningEffortResolver` (`Stage.LIVE`=low, `BACKGROUND`=high, `JUDGE`=medium — the documented seam future background/judge Responses calls consult). On an OpenAI **reasoning model** the option forces the native Responses path (like T178) and `TurNativeChatExecutor` resolves the LIVE-stage effort — the agent's explicit SELECT, or the live default (low) when only the T178 summary was enabled — into `TurOpenAiResponsesService.ReasoningOptions.effort`, which the request builder maps to `reasoning: {effort}` (guarded by `isReasoningModel`, so a chat model never receives it). Reuses the `ReasoningOptions` seam shipped with T178; strictly opt-in + fail-safe (no opt-in / chat model → unchanged request). Covered by `TurReasoningEffortResolverTest` + `TurOpenAiResponsesServiceTest` + `TurNativeChatExecutorTest`. (§X.13.b)
- **T178** — **Reasoning summary surfacing ("Why this answer" panel)** — wires the long-registered (T435) `reasoning-summary` request option (`auto`/`concise`/`detailed`) to real behaviour: when an agent enables it on an OpenAI **reasoning model** (o1/o3/o4-mini/GPT-5, detected by `TurOpenAiResponsesService.isReasoningModel`), the turn takes the native Responses path **even with no native capability enabled** (forced like T152 citations; on a chat model the option is a no-op and the turn stays on Spring AI), the request carries `reasoning: {summary}`, and the model's reasoning summary (`ResponseReasoningItem.summary()`, never raw chain-of-thought) is captured in `runLoop` and emitted as a new `type:"reasoning"` SSE event after the answer token. Both SDKs (`@viglet/turing-sdk` + `@viglet/turing-react-sdk`) parse it (`onReasoning` + `reasoning` on the response/message); the admin chat renders a collapsed **"Why this answer"** panel below the bubble (markdown on expand). en/pt i18n (`chat.reasoning.*`). Strictly opt-in + fail-safe (NONE / non-reasoning model → byte-for-byte unchanged). Covered by `TurOpenAiResponsesServiceTest` + `TurNativeChatExecutorTest` + `agent-chat-tab.reasoning.test.tsx`. (§X.13.a)

### Block F.13 — Safety, compliance, multi-tenant cost (§X.14)

- **T181** — **`safety_identifier` propagation** — the native OpenAI Responses path now sends a stable, privacy-preserving per-user `safety_identifier` on every call so OpenAI can detect and rate-limit per-user abuse on its edge. New `TurSafetyIdentifierService` derives `SHA-256(Keycloak sub | tenantId | salt)` (lowercase hex — the subject never leaves the box; the tenant id keeps users distinct across tenants and stable across sessions; the optional `turing.safety.identifier.salt` makes the hash opaque). `TurNativeChatExecutor` resolves it on the **request thread** (the security context isn't bound on the reactive worker that issues the provider call) and threads it through `TurOpenAiResponsesService` → `ResponseCreateParams.safetyIdentifier(...)`. Fully fail-open + opt-out: `turing.safety.identifier.enabled=false`, an anonymous turn (no stable `sub`), or any hashing error leaves the request byte-for-byte unchanged. Covered by `TurSafetyIdentifierServiceTest` + `TurOpenAiResponsesServiceTest` + `TurNativeChatExecutorTest`. (§X.14.a)
- **T182** — **OpenAI `omni-moderation-latest` pre-filter** — a new `TurModerationService` runs text through OpenAI's multimodal moderation endpoint (free at the API tier) at two gates: **(1) the user query** — `TurAIAgentChatAPI.doChat` moderates the latest user turn and, when flagged, short-circuits with a localized refusal SSE turn (pt/es/en) instead of calling the LLM; **(2) crawler-discovered document chunks** over `turing.safety.moderation.chunk-min-chars` (default 512) — `TurSNGenAi.addDocument` drops flagged chunks before they reach the index via `filterModeratedChunks`. The endpoint needs an OpenAI key, so the service resolves a client from the default LLM (when OpenAI) else the first enabled OpenAI instance — independent of which vendor answers the turn. Strictly opt-in (`turing.safety.moderation.enabled=false` default) and fully fail-open: disabled / no OpenAI instance / API error → clean verdict, so legitimate traffic and ingestion are never blocked on an outage. Covered by `TurModerationServiceTest` + `TurAIAgentChatAPITest` + `TurSNGenAiTest`. (§X.14.b)
- **T186** — **Capability matrix UI heatmap** — a new admin "Capability Matrix" page (`/console/ai-analytics/capability-matrix`, admin-gated) renders an (LLM instance × native capability) heatmap: rows are LLM instances, columns are provider-native `TOOL` capabilities from the T432 registry grouped by `provider` × `category`, and each cell is coloured **wired** (enabled on the instance) / **available** (vendor matches but not enabled) / **not-applicable** (provider mismatch). Backed by a new aggregate endpoint `GET /api/capability/matrix` (`TurCapabilityRegistryAPI.matrix`) returning each instance's resolved plugin type + enabled capability keys in one shot, cross-referenced client-side against `/api/capability/registry`. en/pt i18n. Covered by `TurCapabilityRegistryAPITest`. (§X.14.f)
- **T185** — **Soft budget caps per agent** — *delivered earlier by Block L's T291* (`TurChatCostBudgetGate`): the per-agent `monthlyBudgetUsd` + `budgetDowngradeLlmId` turn-time gate that downgrades to a cheaper LLM once month-to-date spend breaches the cap. T184's `budget-status` endpoint surfaces the same numbers in the AI-Spend card. No additional work. (§X.14.e)
- **T184** — **Per-tenant AI Spend widget** — the System Info Overview gains an "AI Spend (30 days)" card (`system-info.page.tsx`) showing total cost / tokens / requests, a per-stage breakdown, and per-agent **budget alerts**, with a link to the full Cost Governance dashboard (Block L / T290). The card reuses the live cost summary (`/v2/llm/cost/summary`) and a new backend endpoint `GET /v2/llm/cost/budget-status` (`TurLLMCostAPI.budgetStatus`) that returns, for every agent with a `monthlyBudgetUsd`, its month-to-date spend, a linear month-end projection, and over/projected-over flags (the read the T291 turn-time gate does, surfaced for the UI). Fail-soft: a cost API error just hides the card, never blocking System Info. en/pt i18n. Covered by `TurLLMCostAPITest`. (§X.14.d)
- **T183** — **Anthropic Admin + Usage API integration** — a nightly `TurAiUsageImporterJob` (ShedLock, `turing.usage.import-cron`, default 03:40) pulls the last `lookback-days` (default 2 — vendor figures settle over a day or two) from the Anthropic Admin API's usage report (`/v1/organizations/usage_report/messages`) and cost report (`/v1/organizations/cost_report`) and persists them to the new `tur_ai_usage_daily` table (Liquibase `v2026.3.1.70`) — the authoritative vendor billing number, distinct from Block L's per-turn estimate. `TurAnthropicUsageClient` does the HTTP (Admin key via `turing.usage.anthropic.admin-api-key`, `x-api-key` + `anthropic-version` headers, fail-open to empty); the pure `TurAnthropicUsageParser` decodes the bucketed `data[].results[]` shape tolerantly (sums `uncached_/cache_creation_/cache_read_input_tokens`, accepts `amount` as string or number). `TurAiUsageImportService` merges usage (tokens) + cost (USD) on the shared `(day, workspace, model, service tier)` key — each report contributes its metric once per its own key so sums are **never double-counted** — and re-imports idempotently (window delete before insert). Strictly opt-in (`turing.usage.anthropic.enabled=false` + no Admin key → no-op). Covered by `TurAnthropicUsageParserTest` + `TurAiUsageDailyRepositoryIT` (boots context → applies the migration). (§X.14.c)

### Block F.14 — Bold creative bets (§X.15)

- **T191** — **Agent that improves itself overnight** — the production self-improvement flywheel, in propose-not-apply mode. New `TurOvernightImprovementJob` (nightly `@Scheduled` + ShedLock `@SchedulerLock`, cron `turing.distillation.overnight-cron`, default 03:20) sweeps agents that opted in via `overnightImprovementEnabled` (Liquibase `v2026.3.1.74`) and kicks off a **propose-only** distillation. The existing T169 pipeline distills the agent's recent production traffic (T167 stored completions) into a fine-tuned candidate; the poller, on a propose-only job (`proposeOnly` flag), scores baseline vs candidate against the agent's eval golden sets (T126/T286), **always reverts the model**, and — only when the candidate beats the baseline — opens a `TurAgentSuggestion(DISTILLATION_CANDIDATE)` for human approval (new `TurDistillationProposalService`; reuses the T447 suggestion surface + admin approve/reject API). Approving swaps the agent's eval LLM instance model to the candidate (resolved the same way distillation does); rejecting keeps the incumbent — never auto-applied. New `TurDistillationState.PROPOSED`. Agent-settings "Self-Improvement Overnight" toggle (en/pt). Covered by `TurDistillationProposalServiceTest` + `TurOvernightImprovementJobTest`. (§X.15.e)
- **T190** — **Self-installing enterprise connectors** — a per-agent opt-in (`connectorSetupEnabled`, Liquibase `v2026.3.1.73`) that lets an agent walk a customer through "connect my Notion": surface the vendor's token-generation steps, then TEST the supplied credential before it's saved/enabled via the existing integration / MCP-server CRUD. New `TurConnectorSelfInstallService` ships a built-in connector catalogue (Notion, Slack, GitHub, Google Drive, Confluence, generic MCP — each with auth shape, probe path, required extra headers and human setup steps) + a fail-soft, time-bounded `testConnection` (lightweight authenticated GET; classifies success / rejected-credentials / unreachable, never throws). The **computer_use leg (T136)** rides the existing `TurComputerUseDriver` seam: `computerUseAvailable()` reports whether a real driver is deployed (so the steps could be driven in a browser); with the shipped no-op driver it degrades to guided instructions + the probe — the same "ship the scaffold against the no-op driver" pattern T136/T494 use. New `TurAIAgentConnectorAPI` exposes `GET …/connector/catalog`, `GET …/connector/{key}/guide`, `POST …/connector/test` (all 403 unless the flag is on). Agent-settings "Self-Installing Connectors" toggle (en/pt). Covered by `TurConnectorSelfInstallServiceTest` (catalogue/guide/probe via `MockRestServiceServer`). (§X.15.d)
- **T189** — **Real-time multilingual call-center augmentation** — a per-agent opt-in (`callCenterEnabled`, Liquibase `v2026.3.1.72`) that turns a live call into operator agent-assist: each transcribed customer utterance is (1) translated into the operator's working language (T150 `TurVoiceTranslationService`), (2) answered with citations from the customer's index (T248 `TurMcpRagToolService.ragAnswer` — multi-engine retrieval + grounded LLM + source list, degrading to ranked sources when no default LLM), and (3) translated back into the customer's language so the operator can read it aloud verbatim — all in one call. New `TurCallCenterAssistService` orchestrates the translate→answer→translate-back loop (fail-soft: a failed translation falls back to the untranslated text rather than aborting); new `TurAIAgentCallCenterAPI` exposes `GET /api/v2/ai-agent/{id}/call-center/available` + `POST …/assist` (both 403 unless the flag is on). Pure composition of shipped services — reuses the realtime voice (T147–T151), cited RAG (T248), and handoff (T55) primitives. Agent-settings "Call-Center Augmentation" toggle (en/pt). Covered by `TurCallCenterAssistServiceTest`. (§X.15.c)
- **T188** — **Headless enterprise-search MCP backbone — `describe_backbone` discovery tool** — the T245–T253 MCP surface already publishes a complete read/answer/agent/analytics/write tool set; what was missing to consume Turing as a *backbone inside another AI product* (not just its chat UI) was zero-config discovery. New `TurMcpBackboneToolService` adds the `describe_backbone` MCP tool: one call returns a JSON manifest of this instance — product/version, live capability flags (search, cited `rag_answer`, headless agents count, analytics, write/ingestion — each read from the feature's own source: `TurLlmSummaryService.isAvailable()`, the enabled-agent count, the write toggle), the full tool catalogue grouped by purpose with a one-line description each, and a getting-started sequence — so a consuming orchestrator self-onboards in a single round-trip. Registered first in the catalog (`TurMcpServerConfig`), read-scoped by default (`TurMcpToolScopePolicy` unchanged), curated description in `prompts/tools/mcp/describe_backbone.md`. Site enumeration is not duplicated — the manifest points at `list_sites` / `get_site_fields`. Covered by `TurMcpBackboneToolServiceTest` + a `describe_backbone` assertion in `TurMcpServerIT`. (§X.15.b)
- **T187** — **"Live answers" hybrid mode** — a per-agent opt-in (`liveAnswersEnabled`, Liquibase `v2026.3.1.71`) that makes one native OpenAI/Anthropic turn fuse four information sources into a single cited answer: the indexed knowledge base, `web_search`, `web_fetch`, and `code_execution`. New `TurLiveAnswersService` is a thin composition layer over the two-level capability gate — it retrieves the top-K SN/RAG passages for the question and injects them as a `[KB-n]`-tagged grounding block (the indexed-hits leg the OpenAI Responses path otherwise lacks; skipped when the Anthropic citations / native-PDF document path is already attaching them, to avoid double-grounding) plus the `prompts/live-answers.md` fusion-guidance prompt that teaches the model to weave whichever of its admin-selected native tools are relevant into one well-attributed reply. It never force-enables a capability the instance doesn't support; the admin selects `web_search` / `web_fetch` / `code_execution` under Tools & Capabilities as usual. `TurNativeChatExecutor` applies the augmentation once after prompt assembly so both vendor paths pick it up. Surfaced as a "Live Answers (hybrid)" switch in the agent settings form (en/pt). Default off → existing agents byte-for-byte unchanged. Covered by `TurLiveAnswersServiceTest` + `TurNativeChatExecutorTest`. (§X.15.a)

### Block F.11 — Files API + native PDF (§X.12)

- **T175** — **`TurFilesApiBridge` (vendor Files-API upload cache)** — mirrors an indexed binary into the OpenAI (`files.create`, `purpose=user_data`) or Anthropic (`beta.files.upload`, beta `files-api-2025-04-14`) Files API and caches the returned `file_id` in a new content-addressed `llm_vendor_file` table (Liquibase `v2026.3.1.68`), keyed by `(pluginType, instanceId, SHA-256(bytes))` so the same binary uploads exactly once per vendor account. Reuses the T130 `TurNativeProviderClient` credential plumbing; fail-open (non-native vendor / missing key / vendor error → `Optional.empty`, caller falls back to text). The roadmap framed the cache as `TurStorageObjectStat` metadata, but that record has no metadata map and the filesystem backend has no sidecar — a dedicated content-hash-keyed table is the portable home and the basis for T177's cross-agent dedup. Covered by `TurFilesApiBridgeTest`. (§X.12.a)
- **T176** — **Native PDF grounding for Claude agents** — a new `native-pdf` Request Option (registry-driven, auto-rendered in the agent Request Options UI under Anthropic / Provenance) makes retrieved PDFs reach Claude as native `document` blocks instead of Tika-extracted text only, so tables/layout/figures are preserved and per-sentence citations come back as **page** locations (decoded by the existing T152/T154 path). `TurNativeDocumentGroundingService` takes the citation-path retrieval hits and, for each PDF-backed passage still present in `TurStorageService`, downloads the binary once per source (25 MB cap), mirrors it into the vendor Files API via T175 (caching the `file_id` for dedup/T177), base64-encodes it and attaches it as `TurCitationDocument.NativePdf`. `TurAnthropicCitationSupport.documentBlocks` emits a citations-enabled base64 PDF block, and `TurAnthropicMessagesService.seedConversation` sends the whole batch as uniform `document` blocks (citation indices stay aligned) when any passage is a native PDF. Pairs with T152 citations; rides the existing non-beta Messages path. Strictly opt-in + fail-open (storage off / non-PDF / oversize / download or upload error → unchanged text grounding). Covered by `TurNativeDocumentGroundingServiceTest` + `TurAnthropicCitationSupportTest` + `TurNativeChatExecutorTest`. (§X.12.b)
- **T177** — **Vendor-side file reuse across agents in one tenant** — broadens the T175 dedup key from per-instance to per-**account**, so N agents on different instances that share one vendor account (a tenant's own key, or a platform GLOBAL instance) reference a single uploaded `file_id` instead of re-uploading the same PDF once per instance. `TurVendorFile` gains an `accountKey` column — a SHA-256 fingerprint of the decrypted credentials `(pluginType, baseUrl, apiKey)` from the T130 `TurNativeProviderClient.credentials` — and the cache lookup becomes `findByPluginTypeAndAccountKeyAndContentHash`, backed by a new `uq_llm_vendor_file_acct` unique constraint (Liquibase `v2026.3.1.69`, additive — the legacy `(pluginType, instanceId, contentHash)` constraint stays; `instanceId` is kept as upload provenance). The account is the true validity boundary of a `file_id` (it lives under the API key), so instances with distinct credentials stay isolated and dedup only ever fires on byte-identical content. Covered by `TurFilesApiBridgeTest` (cross-instance reuse + credential isolation). (§X.12.c)

### Block F.7 — Batch + token economics (§X.8)

- **T162** — **Pre-flight cost quote in chat UI** — before sending, the agent chat now quotes the projected turn cost and gates on the agent's `perTurnSoftCapUsd`. `POST /api/ai-agent/{agentId}/preflight-cost` (`TurAgentPreflightCostAPI`) counts the draft prompt's input tokens (exact via T161 where supported), prices it against the model's rate (T290 `TurLLMPriceService.computeCost`, using the instance's `numPredict` or a 500-token output estimate), and returns `{enabled, inputTokens, estimatedCostUsd, softCapUsd, overSoftCap, exact}` — `enabled:false` when no cap is set. The React chat tab (`agent-chat-tab.tsx` + `agent-preflight-cost.service.ts`) quotes on send and, only when over the cap, opens a confirm dialog ("this turn will cost ~$X.XX — send anyway?"); errors fail open (send without prompt). en/pt i18n (`chat.costGate.*`). Covered by `TurAgentPreflightCostAPITest` + the existing chat-tab vitest suite. (§X.8.g)
- **T161** — **Anthropic token counting (`/v1/messages/count_tokens`)** — exact pre-flight prompt-token count replacing the `chars/4` `cl100k_base` heuristic where the vendor supports it. New `TurTokenCountingService.countExact` calls Anthropic's native count endpoint (collapsing the prompt to one system + one user message to dodge role-alternation constraints; empty for non-Anthropic / on error → heuristic fallback). Plumbed into the T123 budget gate: `TurTokenBudgetService.check(agent, instance, messages)` uses the exact count when the agent enables the `token-counting` Request Option and the instance supports it, else the heuristic. Exposed at `POST /api/v2/llm/{id}/token-count` (`{systemPrompt?, text}` → `{inputTokens, exact}`) for the T162 cost quote. The Request Option already exists in the T435 registry UI, so no new frontend. (§X.8.f)
- **T160** — **Service tier routing** — the `service-tier` Request Option already surfaced by the T435 registry UI (auto / default / flex / priority) now actually shapes the live request. New vendor-neutral `TurServiceTier` (maps onto OpenAI Responses `service_tier` verbatim and onto Anthropic's `auto` / `standard_only`) + `TurServiceTierResolver` with agent-option → per-instance `providerOptionsJson.serviceTier` → global `turing.genai.service-tier` precedence (empty → provider default, byte-for-byte unchanged). `TurNativeChatExecutor` resolves it per turn and threads it into both native paths (`TurOpenAiResponsesService` / `TurAnthropicMessagesService` request builders). Background workloads no longer take this path at all — they run on the Batch tier (T157/T159), the cost counterpart to choosing `priority` for live chat. No new frontend (the registry SELECT already exists). Covered by `TurServiceTierResolverTest`. (§X.8.e)
- **T159** — **LLM Judge eval at batch tier** — an agent's rubric judgements can run as a single LLM-Judge **Batch** (50% off, ~24h) for overnight CI instead of synchronous calls. The bilingual judge system prompt + verdict parser were factored into the pure, unit-tested `TurEvalJudgePrompt` (shared by the sync and batch paths, so they build/parse identically). Since the rubric judge only needs the rubric + scripted user turns (both on the eval case), no conversation replay is required: `TurAgentEvalRunnerService.submitJudgeBatch` builds one `TurBatchChatRequest` per enabled non-blank-rubric case (customId = case id) and submits a chat batch via `TurBatchInferenceService`; `TurEvalJudgeBatchHandler` parses the verdicts on completion and logs the pass/fail tally + each failure's rationale. Triggered by `POST /api/ai-agent/{agentId}/eval/run-batch` (returns `scheduled:0` when the tier is off or no batch-capable LLM, so callers fall back to the synchronous `/run`). Opt-in via `turing.batch.enabled` + `turing.batch.eval.enabled`. Covered by `TurEvalJudgePromptTest`. (§X.8.d)
- **T158** — **Bulk re-embedding via Batch** — switching a collection's embedding model now schedules an OpenAI embeddings **Batch** re-embed (50% off, 24h SLA) instead of a synchronous re-embed. Because only the vector changes on a model switch (text + metadata are unchanged), `TurBatchReEmbeddingService` reads the collection's chunks (`streamExport`), submits their text as an embeddings Batch keyed by chunk id (new `TurBatchEmbeddingProvider` seam + pure, unit-tested `TurOpenAiEmbeddingBatchJsonl`; `batch_job.jobKind` distinguishes EMBEDDING from CHAT, Liquibase `v2026.3.1.52`), and `TurReEmbeddingBatchHandler` writes the precomputed vectors straight back via the store's `importChunks` — no source re-fetch, no re-chunking. Filled the Lucene gap: `TurLuceneStoreProvider.importChunks` + `TurLuceneVectorStore.addPrecomputed` now persist precomputed vectors verbatim (re-embedding only the records that lack one). Triggered by `POST /api/store/{id}/collections/{collectionName}/reembed-batch?embeddingModelId=…` (409 when the tier is off or the vendor has no embeddings Batch API, so the caller falls back to synchronous reindex). Opt-in via `turing.batch.enabled` + `turing.batch.embedding.enabled`. Covered by `TurOpenAiEmbeddingBatchJsonlTest` + new `TurLuceneVectorStoreTest` precomputed-write cases. (§X.8.c)
- **T157** — **Nightly batch summarization** — the per-site AI Insights workload now pre-warms through the Batch tier (50% off) instead of synchronous full-price LLM calls. The insight prompt is unified in a new `TurSNSiteInsightsPromptBuilder` (shared by the on-demand `TurSNSiteSummaryAPI` and the batch job, so a batch-warmed entry is a byte-for-byte cache hit), and `TurNightlyBatchSummarizationJob` (ShedLock, `turing.batch.summarization.cron`, default 03:30) submits one request per SN site — chunked at `maxRequestsPerBatch` — with `customId == siteId`. On completion (hours later) `TurSiteInsightsBatchHandler` writes each result into `TurLlmCacheService` under the site id — the exact key `TurLlmSummaryService.generate` reads — so the operator's next visit is a warm hit. Triple-gated (`turing.batch.enabled` + `turing.batch.summarization.enabled` + a default LLM whose vendor has a Batch API); any gate unmet is a no-op and the on-demand synchronous path is untouched. Cache is in-process so the warm entry lands on the poller node (cost optimization, not a correctness contract). Covered by `TurSNSiteInsightsPromptBuilderTest`. (§X.8.b)
- **T156** — **`TurBatchInferenceService`** — unified, vendor-neutral abstraction over the OpenAI Batch API (Files-then-Batch JSONL flow, `client.batches()`) and Anthropic Message Batches (inline `POST /v1/messages/batches`), both at the **50% Batch discount**. A `TurBatchProvider` seam (resolved by plugin type via `TurBatchProviderFactory`, mirroring `TurGenAiLlmProvider`) exposes `submitChat` / `status` / `results` / `cancel`; OpenAI requests are serialized by the pure, unit-tested `TurOpenAiBatchJsonl` (input/output JSONL both directions), Anthropic uses the SDK builders + result stream. Vendor states normalize to a 6-value `TurBatchState`. Fire-and-forget: `submit(...)` persists a `batch_job` row (Liquibase `v2026.3.1.51`) and returns immediately; the cluster-wide-once `TurBatchJobPoller` (ShedLock, `turing.batch.poll-cron`) refreshes each job and, on completion, routes results to the `TurBatchCompletionHandler` registered for the job's `purpose` (the seam T157/T159 plug into). Strictly opt-in (`turing.batch.enabled`, default false) — when off, `submit` returns empty and every consumer runs its synchronous path unchanged; vendors without a Batch API (Ollama/Gemini/Azure) resolve to no provider and fall back too. Read-only operator surface at `GET /api/system/batch/{status,jobs}`. Covered by `TurOpenAiBatchJsonlTest`. (§X.8.a)

### Block F.8 — Memory tool + context editing (§X.9)

- **T166** — **Per-user persistent persona memory (`memoryScope=user`)** — a new per-agent `memoryScope` (`CONVERSATION` default | `USER`, `TurMemoryScope` enum + `memory_scope` column, Liquibase `v2026.3.1.65`) repoints the T163 memory tool at a per-user store. New `TurAgentMemoryScopeResolver` derives a stable scope id (`usermem-<sanitized Keycloak sub>`) from the security context, which `TurNativeChatExecutor` passes into the memory loop as the workspace conversation-scope slot — so the same `memory/` files persist across every conversation an authenticated user has with the agent (the agent remembers "Alex prefers terse answers"). Falls back to per-conversation memory when no stable identity is resolvable (anonymous visitor turn), so user memory is never written under an unattributable scope. Persists across import/export (`TurAIAgentExchange`) and the agent update API. Frontend: a "Memory scope" select on the agent settings form (en/pt i18n). Strictly opt-in (default `CONVERSATION` = unchanged T163 behaviour). Distinct from the T446 structured fact store — this is the file-based memory tool scoped to the user. Covered by `TurAgentMemoryScopeResolverTest`. (§X.9.d)
- **T165** — **Anthropic Compaction (`compact_20260112`)** — a new `compaction` Request Option lets Claude summarize older turns server-side when the window fills. Builds on the T164 `TurAnthropicContextManagement` helper: the `compaction` lever adds the `compact_20260112` edit to `context_management.edits` plus the extra `compact-2026-01-12` beta (alongside `context-management-2025-06-27`), wired into the same two Anthropic tool-loop paths (standard Messages raw body property + beta memory loop typed config). **Coordinates with T115** (Turing-side workspace compression, the audit-trail summarizer): when the agent has `chatMemoryCompressionEnabled`, `resolve(...)` forces Anthropic compaction **off** so the two never double-summarize — Turing wins. Context editing (T164) is independent and unaffected by that precedence. Strictly opt-in. Covered by new `TurAnthropicContextManagementTest` compaction + precedence cases. (§X.9.c)
- **T164** — **Context editing (`clear_tool_uses_20250919`)** — the long-registered (T435) `context-editing` Request Option now shapes the live Anthropic request: new shared `TurAnthropicContextManagement` resolves the per-agent option (`requestOptionsJson` `{"context-editing":"true"}`) and attaches `context_management.edits=[{clear_tool_uses_20250919}]` + the `context-management-2025-06-27` beta so Claude server-side auto-evicts stale tool results (→ placeholder) when the window saturates. Wired into both Anthropic tool-loop paths: the standard Messages path (web_search/web_fetch/code_execution + coexisting Turing tools — injected as a raw `context_management` body property since the non-beta builder has no typed setter) and the beta memory loop (T163 — typed `.contextManagement(...)`). The helper already carries a `compaction` lever (T165) so that task only flips a switch. Strictly opt-in (`Options.NONE` → byte-for-byte unchanged); the owns-turn editor/computer-use loops are intentionally not wired yet. Covered by `TurAnthropicContextManagementTest` + memory/messages service + `TurNativeChatExecutorTest`. (§X.9.b)
- **T163** — **Anthropic Memory tool (`memory_20250818`)** — a new `anthropic-memory` native capability gives Claude the six file commands (`view`/`create`/`str_replace`/`insert`/`delete`/`rename`) backed by `TurAgentWorkspace`. Like the editor/computer-use tools it's a client-executed multi-turn loop that owns the turn: `TurAnthropicMemoryService` runs the **beta** Messages API with the `BetaMemoryTool20250818` tool + the `context-management-2025-06-27` beta flag, and each `memory` `tool_use` is executed by the pure, SDK-free `TurWorkspaceMemoryHandler` against the per-conversation workspace under the `memory/` subpath (the model's `/memories` root maps there; `..`/`|` traversal rejected, directory `view`/`delete` supported). Routed from `TurNativeChatExecutor` ahead of the editor branch. Registry-driven, so the capability auto-appears in the agent picker (label "Anthropic Memory") and the per-instance matrix — no frontend change. Strictly opt-in (two-level gate: instance matrix + agent selection); empty matrix = unchanged Spring AI path. Covered by `TurWorkspaceMemoryHandlerTest` (all six commands + path mapping/rejection) + `TurAnthropicMemoryServiceTest` + `TurNativeChatExecutorTest` routing. (§X.9.a)

### Block F.10 — Predicted Outputs + strict structured outputs (§X.11)

- **T171** — **OpenAI Predicted Outputs (`prediction`)** — new `TurOpenAiPredictedOutputsService` runs an edit-style turn through the **Chat Completions** API with the `prediction` parameter set to the base text, so OpenAI fast-paths the unchanged span (3–5× latency drop when the output mostly overlaps the input). Chat Completions, not the Responses API, because `prediction` is a Chat-Completions-only parameter (`ResponseCreateParams` has no equivalent) — the native agent chat turn (`TurOpenAiResponsesService`) is untouched; this is a dedicated service for discrete edit operations that already hold a base text. Gated by `TurPredictedOutputsResolver` (agent `predicted-outputs` Request Option → instance `predictedOutputs` → off), since rejected prediction tokens are billed; a non-OpenAI instance yields empty so the caller falls back. The T435 toggle was already registered but inert — this is its first consumer. Call-site wiring (rephrase-shorter / editor loops) is T172. Covered by `TurOpenAiPredictedOutputsServiceTest` + `TurPredictedOutputsResolverTest`. (§X.11.a)
- **T426** — **AI Authoring on provider-bound structured output (retire `BeanOutputConverter` free-text)** — `TurAiAuthoringService` no longer relies on the model voluntarily emitting bare JSON. For a **no-tool** authoring turn (Persona, ChatFlow authoring) on an OpenAI/Anthropic default LLM, the `AiAuthoringResponse<T>` shape — taken from `BeanOutputConverter.getJsonSchema()` — is bound to the provider via the T173 (OpenAI `json_schema`, `strict:false` since the converter schema isn't strict-mode-shaped) / T174 (Anthropic forced tool) services, so the reply is a JSON object **by construction** — eliminating the prose-around-JSON / empty-turn failures the free-text path fell over on (gpt-4o-mini). The conversation is flattened into the one-shot structured call (the form state — the real working memory — already rides in the system prompt). Strictly additive + fail-safe: tooled turns (Intent's icon search), non-OpenAI/Anthropic providers, an empty provider reply, or JSON that doesn't parse all fall through to the **unchanged** legacy `BeanOutputConverter` + brace-scan-recovery + graceful-fallback path (so the band-aid still backstops the cases structured output can't cover). A new `strict` overload on `TurOpenAiStructuredOutputsService` lets T173 keep `strict:true` while authoring uses `strict:false`. No frontend change (same request/response contract). Covered by `TurAiAuthoringServiceTest`. (§X.11.c)
- **T174** — **Anthropic structured outputs (forced tool + ZDR-cached schema)** — the Claude analog of T173. Anthropic has no `response_format`, so `TurAnthropicStructuredOutputsService` forces a **single tool** whose `input_schema` is the desired shape and pins `tool_choice` to it (`toolToolChoice(name)`): the model's only move is a `tool_use` block whose `input` **is** the validated object (extracted + re-serialized to a JSON string). The tool carries `cache_control: ephemeral`, so a stable schema reused across turns is served from Anthropic's prompt cache (the "ZDR-cached schemas" angle). Shares the cross-provider `TurStructuredOutputsResolver` with T173; a non-Anthropic instance yields empty so the caller falls back. No frontend change (the `structured-outputs` toggle ships in the T435 Request Options UI). Covered by `TurAnthropicStructuredOutputsServiceTest`. (§X.11.d)
- **T173** — **OpenAI strict structured outputs (`text.format = json_schema, strict:true`)** — new `TurOpenAiStructuredOutputsService` runs a turn through the Responses API with the `text` config set to a strict `json_schema`, so the reply is a JSON document validated against the supplied schema **by construction** — retiring the `json_object` JSON-mode workarounds (the gpt-4o-mini truncation / prose-around-JSON / dropped-comma failures recorded in agent memory). The schema is a raw JSON-Schema `Map` (not a Java class) since Turing's schemas are dynamic; a `strictObjectSchema(properties)` helper enforces OpenAI's strict-mode rules (`additionalProperties:false` + all properties `required`). Gated by the new **cross-provider** `TurStructuredOutputsResolver` (agent `structured-outputs` Request Option → instance `structuredOutputs` → off) which also feeds the Anthropic leg (T174); a non-OpenAI instance yields empty so the caller falls back. No frontend change — the `structured-outputs` toggle already ships in the T435 Request Options UI. Covered by `TurOpenAiStructuredOutputsServiceTest` + `TurStructuredOutputsResolverTest`. (§X.11.c)
- **T172** — **"Rephrase shorter" on an answer (Predicted Outputs consumer)** — assistant bubbles in the agent chat gain a **Shorter** action (next to the T170 thumbs) that rewrites the answer more concisely. New `TurChatRephraseService` + `POST /api/ai-agent/{agentId}/chat-rephrase` (`TurChatRephraseAPI`): it resolves the agent's LLM (explicit `llmInstanceId` or the deterministic default, via the JOIN-FETCH `findById`) and, when the agent opted into `predicted-outputs` (T171) on an OpenAI instance, sends the previous answer as the `prediction` so the rewrite is near-instant — exactly the "output mostly overlaps the input" case. Universal by design: a non-OpenAI provider (or opt-out) still rewrites via a provider-agnostic Spring AI completion, just without the acceleration; `usedPrediction` reports which path ran. Supports `shorter`/`simpler`/`longer`/`formal`/`friendly` styles (free text passes through). Frontend: `onRephrase` on `ChatMessageList` + a `RephraseButton`, wired in `AgentChatTab` as a local in-place override (`rephrasedById`, keyed by message id) so the SDK transcript — and the next turn's context — is never mutated; `chat-rephrase.service.ts` + en/pt i18n. Covered by `TurChatRephraseServiceTest` + `agent-chat-tab.rephrase.test.tsx`. (§X.11.b)

### Block F.9 — Stored Completions + Evals + Distillation flywheel (§X.10)

- **T167** — **`TurStoredCompletionsService`** — the long-registered (T435) `stored-completions` Request Option now shapes the live OpenAI request: when an agent opts in (`requestOptionsJson` `{"stored-completions":"true"}`), `TurNativeChatExecutor` resolves a `StoredCompletionsDirective` (store flag + structured metadata map) and threads it into `TurOpenAiResponsesService`, which sets `store: true` + `metadata` on the `ResponseCreateParams` so every Responses turn is persisted on OpenAI's side tagged with `agentId`/`conversationId` (and `flowId`/`nodeId`/`experimentKey`/`variantLabel` when a future caller has them — the native chat path has no chat-flow context). Blank metadata values are omitted and each value is truncated to OpenAI's 512-char limit. This is the eval/distillation flywheel's foundation: T168 Evals and T169 distillation slice the stored traffic by this metadata. Strictly opt-in (null/false → the request leaves `store`/`metadata` unset, byte-for-byte unchanged); no frontend change (the toggle already ships in the T435 Request Options UI). Covered by `TurStoredCompletionsServiceTest` + `TurOpenAiResponsesServiceTest` (store/metadata applied) + `TurNativeChatExecutorTest`. (§X.10.a)
- **T170** — **DPO loop from operator feedback** — closes the flywheel's preference half. Operators thumb-up/down assistant turns in the agent chat (new optional `onFeedback` on the shared `ChatMessageList` → thumbs on assistant bubbles; other chat surfaces unchanged), each recorded as a `chat_turn_feedback` row (Liquibase `v2026.3.1.67`) via `POST /api/ai-agent/{agentId}/chat-feedback` (`TurChatFeedbackService`, the rated answer's prompt resolved as the nearest preceding user turn). The pure, SDK-free `TurDpoDatasetBuilder` turns the binary signal into an OpenAI **DPO** preference dataset: it groups feedback by normalized prompt and emits one `{input, preferred_output, non_preferred_output}` example per prompt that has **both** a thumb-up (chosen) and a thumb-down (rejected) answer — so the dataset is built **incrementally** as contradictory feedback accumulates (a prompt with only ups, only downs, or identical answers contributes nothing). `GET /api/ai-agent/{agentId}/dpo-dataset` exports the JSONL + pair/feedback counts, ready to feed a `method:dpo` fine-tune through T169's plumbing. Covered by `TurDpoDatasetBuilderTest`; the agent-chat-tab vitest suite stays green (feedback uses a direct service call, matching that component's non-React-Query pattern). (§X.10.d)
- **T169** — **Distillation pipeline** — closes the self-improvement flywheel: a "Distill" action on the agent eval-gate panel exports the agent's Stored Completions (T167, filtered by the `agentId` metadata) via `client.chat().completions().list`, reconstructs each into a fine-tuning training example (input turns from the completion's `messages` sub-resource + the assistant answer from its choice), and — when at least `min-examples` are usable — uploads the JSONL training file and submits an OpenAI **fine-tuning job** against `gpt-4o-mini`. The pure, SDK-free `TurOpenAiDistillationJsonl` builds/validates the chat-format training file (one `{"messages":[…]}` per line, examples needing a real input turn + a trailing assistant turn). A `distillation_job` row (Liquibase `v2026.3.1.66`) is the durable hand-off: the cluster-wide-once `TurDistillationJobPoller` (ShedLock, `turing.distillation.poll-cron`) drives each RUNNING job — on fine-tune success it **swaps the candidate model into the LLM instance, runs the eval gate (T286) against it, and keeps the swap only if the gate passes** (else reverts to the recorded original model and marks `EVAL_FAILED`). `POST /api/ai-agent/{agentId}/eval/distill` returns `{started,…}` or `{started:false,reason}`. Frontend: a "Distill" button + result line on the eval-gate panel (`useDistillAgent`). Strictly opt-in + fail-open: gated on `turing.distillation.enabled` (default false) AND an OpenAI-backed eval LLM; off / non-OpenAI / too-few-completions / API error → no-op with a reason and the model is never touched. Covered by `TurOpenAiDistillationJsonlTest`. (§X.10.c)
- **T168** — **OpenAI Evals API integration** — the agent's golden-set rubric fixtures (T286) can be pushed to the **OpenAI Evals API** so a server-side `label_model` grader judges them on OpenAI's infrastructure instead of Turing's local LLM judge. New pure, SDK-free `TurOpenAiEvalsPayload` builds the two request bodies — the eval definition (a `custom` item schema with `rubric`/`turns` string fields + one `label_model` testing criterion that labels each item `pass`/`fail`) and the run (the fixtures as inline `jsonl`/`file_content`) — handed to the SDK via `putAllAdditionalBodyProperties` (the typed Evals builders are deeply nested; raw-JSON-body is the same pattern used for Anthropic `mcp_servers`). `TurOpenAiEvalsService` registers the eval (`client.evals().create`) + starts the run (`client.evals().runs().create`) and returns the eval/run ids + dashboard `report_url`. The grader judges purely from the provided rubric + scripted turns (the same inputs `TurEvalJudgePrompt` feeds the local judge), so no server-side completion generation is needed — Turing pushes the fixtures and OpenAI grades them. `TurAgentEvalRunnerService.submitToOpenAiEvals` gathers the rubric fixtures (mirroring `submitJudgeBatch`) and `POST /api/ai-agent/{agentId}/eval/run-openai-evals` triggers it. Strictly opt-in + fail-open: gated on `turing.evals.enabled` (default false) AND an OpenAI-backed eval LLM; off / non-OpenAI / no rubric cases / API error → `{submitted:false}` and the synchronous local judge is unchanged. API/CI-only, like the T159 Batch judge (no frontend button). Covered by `TurOpenAiEvalsPayloadTest`. (§X.10.b)

### Block F.15 — Capability surfacing & UX (§X.18)

- **T435** — **REQUEST_OPTION knobs as registry-driven settings** — the registry now carries a curated `kind=REQUEST_OPTION` catalogue (reasoning effort/summary/logprobs, structured/predicted outputs, service tier, token counting, stored completions, citations, context editing, moderation, safety_identifier) tagged with a render `valueType` (`BOOLEAN`/`SELECT` + `options`); `TurCapabilityDescriptor` gained `valueType`/`options`. New per-agent `requestOptionsJson` column (Liquibase `v2026.3.1.48`, JSON map keyed by capability key) + the **Request Options** agent settings page (`/request-options`, `AIAgentRequestOptionsForm`) rendering them grouped by category as toggles/selects — **never** the tool picker — with provider-mismatch locking. This gives the F.13 request-shape tasks (T152/T160/T164/T167/T171/T173/T178–T182) one home; each reads its own key when building the provider request. en/pt i18n. (§X.18.d)
- **T434** — **Agent "Tools & Capabilities" picker** — new agent sidebar item (`/capabilities`) + `AIAgentCapabilitiesForm`, rendering `kind=TOOL` from `GET /api/capability/registry` grouped by abstract `function`: a function offered by ≥2 providers is a **mutex radio** (Turing ↔ OpenAI ↔ Anthropic), a single-provider function a toggle. Provider-native options are **locked with a tooltip** when no matching-vendor LLM instance is bound to the agent, and `ownsTurn` capabilities (computer_use) carry a "runs the turn alone" warning. Saving writes Turing-tool picks to the agent `nativeTools` and provider-native picks to `nativeCapabilities` (T433) — which opts the agent into the per-capability execution model. New `TurCapabilityRegistryService` + `capability.model.ts`; en/pt i18n. (§X.18.c)
- **T433** — **Per-agent capability selection + union execution ("capacidade é mutex, resto coexiste")** — new per-agent `nativeCapabilities` CSV column (mirrors `nativeTools`; Liquibase `v2026.3.1.47`) + `TurNativeCapabilityResolver`. `TurNativeChatExecutor` now resolves `agentSelected ∩ instanceCapable`; on a non-owns-turn native turn it attaches the agent's Turing/MCP/custom tools whose abstract `function` isn't claimed by a selected provider-native pick and runs them through a real client-side tool loop in both `TurOpenAiResponsesService` (Responses `function` tools) and `TurAnthropicMessagesService` (custom `tool_use`/`tool_result`), backed by the shared `TurNativeFunctionToolSupport`. **Opt-in/legacy**: `nativeCapabilities == null` keeps the pre-T433 winner-takes-all path (all instance caps, zero Turing tools), so every existing agent is unchanged until the T434 picker writes a selection. `ownsTurn` capabilities (computer_use) and the text_editor/bash authoring loop still own the whole turn. No frontend (picker = T434). (§X.18.b)
- **T432** — **Capability registry / taxonomy** — new `genai/nativeapi/capability` package turning the flat `TurNativeCapability` enum into a tagged taxonomy: each constant now carries `kind` (`TurCapabilityKind` TOOL/REQUEST_OPTION/PLATFORM — all current natives are TOOL), an abstract `function` (`TurCapabilityFunctions`, e.g. `web-search`/`code-exec` so cross-vendor and cross-source alternatives group into one mutex row), a `provider` (`TurCapabilityProvider`, derived from plugin type), a `category`, and `ownsTurn` (computer_use). `TurCapabilityRegistry` merges the enum with the in-JVM Turing native-tool groups (`TurNativeToolService`) into one `TurCapabilityDescriptor` list — the only real cross-source overlap today is `code-exec` (Turing code-interpreter ↔ OpenAI code_interpreter ↔ Anthropic code_execution). Exposed read-only at `GET /api/capability/registry` (`TurCapabilityRegistryAPI`). Foundation both the agent picker (T434) and the heatmap (T186) render from; no behaviour change to the chat path. (§X.18.a)

## Block F.16 — Gemini native API exploitation + cross-vendor parity (§X.19/§X.20)

- **T504** — **Anthropic extended / interleaved thinking** — closes the cross-vendor "reasoning budget + show-the-thinking" contract (OpenAI T178/T179, Gemini T498, now Claude). `TurAnthropicMessagesService` opt-in via the per-instance `thinkingBudget` provider option: a positive budget enables Claude's extended thinking (`ThinkingConfigEnabled.budgetTokens`); because Anthropic requires `temperature = 1` with thinking, the per-instance temperature is **not** applied when it's on (it would 400). `interleavedThinking:true` adds the `interleaved-thinking-2025-05-14` beta so Claude reasons *between* tool calls in the F.3 loop. The thought summary is harvested from the response's `thinking` blocks each round (`extractThinking`, latest non-blank wins) and surfaced as the shared **`reasoning` SSE event** — landing in the same T178 "Why this answer" panel as the OpenAI/Gemini legs, zero frontend change (`TurnResult` gained a `reasoningSummary`, `emitTurn` emits it after the answer token). Absent/non-positive budget → byte-for-byte unchanged request. Surfaced as an `anthropic-extended-thinking` REQUEST_OPTION in the T435 picker. Covered by `TurAnthropicMessagesServiceTest` (budget/interleaved resolution, thinking-config + temperature-drop, default-off); the live thinking-block round-trip is real-LLM-gated. (§X.13)
- **T503** — **OpenAI Responses `background` mode** — long reasoning/agentic turns (deep `code_interpreter`, computer-use loops) no longer hold the connection: `TurOpenAiResponsesService` submits the Responses call with `background:true` (forcing `store:true`, which OpenAI requires for a background response) and `runLoop` resumes by polling `responses().retrieve(id)` until a terminal status (`completed`/`failed`/`incomplete`/`cancelled`) — `awaitBackground` + the pure `isTerminal` guard. Opt-in via the per-instance `backgroundEnabled` provider option (+ optional `backgroundPollIntervalMs` default 1 s / `backgroundMaxWaitMs` default 120 s); absent/false → unchanged synchronous create. Applies per tool-loop round, so it covers one-shot server-tool turns and the coexisting-tool loop alike. **Fail-open**: a retrieve error or the wait ceiling returns the latest response so text extraction still runs. Surfaced as an `openai-background` REQUEST_OPTION in the T435 picker. The polling runs on the existing `boundedElastic` turn thread (the chat `Flux`), so it frees the HTTP connection, not the worker. Covered by `TurOpenAiResponsesServiceTest` (option resolution, `background`+`store` request flags, `isTerminal`); the live retrieve-poll round-trip is real-LLM-gated. (§X.13)
- **T502** — **Anthropic prompt caching (`cache_control`) over the T499 seam** — plugs Claude into the cross-vendor context-caching seam. New `TurAnthropicContextCacheProvider` (`anthropic` leg of `TurContextCacheProvider`): unlike Gemini's explicit remote `cachedContents` object, Anthropic caching is **inline** — you mark message blocks with an ephemeral `cache_control` breakpoint in the same request — so the provider creates nothing remote; `ensureCache` just answers "opted in? at what TTL?" by returning a ref whose `handle` is the TTL token (`5m`/`1h`). `TurAnthropicMessagesService` resolves that ref through the seam and, when present, (a) carries the **system prompt** as a `systemOfTextBlockParams` text block with `cache_control`, and (b) stamps the **last retrieved `search_result` block** (T153) with `cache_control` so the whole grounding-passage prefix is cached — the F.6 citation path and the standard Messages path both get the discount. Opt-in via the per-instance `cacheControlEnabled` provider option (+ optional `cacheControlTtl`, default `5m`); absent/false → byte-for-byte unchanged requests (plain `.system(String)`, unstamped blocks). The native-PDF `document` block path is left uncached (base64 PDFs aren't a stable cross-turn prefix). Together with T499 this gives every vendor a caching story behind one provider-agnostic seam. Covered by `TurAnthropicMessagesServiceTest` (opt-in gate + 5m/1h TTL resolution), `TurContextCacheSeamTest` (provider gating + factory routing) and `TurAnthropicCitationSupportTest`; bean wiring validated by the `TestSupportSmokeIT` context boot. (§X.20)
- **T501** — **Native video/audio understanding via Gemini (chat slots + index-time)** — a media-catalog differentiator the text-only stack can't touch. `TurGeminiVideoUnderstandingService` hands a clip to Gemini natively (frames + audio) and gets back a **timestamped transcript** + **scene/visual description**.
