The section's own framing needed correcting twice along the way, both times by measurement rather than by preference: its premise that only the repository layer was guarded, and then its assumption that six components would each project the same way. The five that did were the five whose consumers read them; the one that did not is the one whose consumers pass it on. Every lazy collection anyone actually reads is out of the cache. 7196 tests green.
- **T898b** — The cache holds four entity kinds instead of six. `customFacetsByFieldId` and `customSorts` are projected into nested `CustomFacetView` / `CustomSortView` records inside the snapshot, built while the session is open, so the cached value carries neither the lazy `items` collection nor the back-reference the entities brought with them. The custom-sort query still fetch-joins items that are now dropped on projection — a narrowing this record makes safe to do later. The shrinkage is not self-reported: the T898 lint prints the entity kinds it finds behind a `@Cacheable`, and bypassing its allowlist prints four where it printed six. `facetLabelsByFieldId` looked equally easy and was left alone: its type is shared with the non-snapshot path through `TurSNFacetDefinitionFactory`, so converting it changes a signature the entity path also calls. Verification is where this earned its keep. The six suites that construct the snapshot do so positionally with empty collections, so mapping every projected name to `null` left 166 tests green and proved nothing about either projection — and there was no test for `build(...)` at all. The new `TurSNSiteSearchSnapshotServiceTest` covers it: the same null-mapping fault now turns three of its cases red, and a projection that invents a missing description, stealing the fallback its consumer owns, turns a fourth. T898 stays open for the four kinds that remain, each held by a named coupling rather than by inertia, and the lint's entry now says which. 7162 tests green.
- **T898c** — The cache holds three entity kinds instead of four. `facetLabelsByFieldId` is now a map of `FacetLabelView` records, projected in-session, and `TurSNSearchProcess#facetLocalesFor` builds the transient `TurSNSiteFieldExtFacet` the `TurSNFacetDefinition` contract wants at that boundary — with the same builder its own no-label fallback already used. The contract itself is untouched and stays entity-bearing, correctly: the template and provisioning paths share it and the provisioner persists what it is given. The record carries locale and label and not the entity's `id`, because the only reader on the far side, `TurSNFacetRenderer#groupLabel`, matches on the locale and reads the label. I had first carried the id on the belief that `TurSNSiteFieldExtFacetDto` copies it into the search response; it does copy it, but that DTO is an internal `facetMap` value and never reaches the response, so the claim was wrong and the component came back out. The lint reports the result independently: bypassing its allowlist prints three entity kinds where it printed four, and six before T898b. Verification took three passes to become worth anything. A projection losing its label or its locale, and the per-locale query being skipped, each turn the new snapshot-service case red. The record-to-entity hand-off in the consumer did not: dropping the label there left the suite green, because the one facet test asserted only that the widget had facets. Asserting the rendered group header instead still passed — the fixture's locale label and its `facetName` fallback were both "Category", so the assertion could not tell which one produced it. With the label made distinct the same fault finally turns it red. 7164 tests green; T898 stays open for the three that remain.
- **T898d** — The cache holds two entity kinds instead of three. `allLocales` is now a `LocaleView` list carrying language, core and position; six consumers read `.language()` and the two that hand a locale to the search engine rebuild a transient `TurSNSiteLocale` — the idiom `TurSearchEnginePlugin#createStandaloneIndex` already uses on itself. The rebuild sets the site deliberately: the Elasticsearch plugin passes `getTurSNSite()` to `findByTurSNSiteAndEnabled` and Lucene resolves its MLT fields from it, and because both guard on null rather than throwing, omitting it would have degraded similar-documents and duplicate-clustering on those two engines without a single failure to show for it. That is the fault the new boundary test pins, by capturing the locale the plugin actually receives. Two of the four injected faults were invisible at first and are the reason the round has three test additions rather than one. Dropping the site or the core from the rebuilt entity turned the boundary test red immediately. Dropping the core from the projection, and reversing the order the query returned, did not: every service suite constructs its snapshot directly, so nothing exercised the projection itself, and the order is load-bearing because `TurSNSiteSearchService#resolveDefaultLocale` takes `findFirst()`. With the two projection cases added, all four turn red. The lint reports the arithmetic independently — bypassing its allowlist prints two entity kinds where it printed three, four before T898c and six before T898b. 7167 tests green. T898 stays open for `TurSNSite`, held by T952 and by the metric-access write, and `TurSNSiteFieldExt`, held by three finders and by the facet contract.
- **T952** — `getSNSite` loads the site instead of serving the snapshot's cached one, and the twenty-two call sites did not have to change at all. Measuring first reversed the section's own premise. It said the walks work because one finder pre-fetches the two associations the callers happen to use; they do not. `agent.getLlmInstances()` and `agent.getPersonas()` are dereferenced past that fetch join at eight places — `TurGenAiContextFactory:125`, `TurSNSiteGenAiAPI:428`, `TurSNAnswerService:123`, `TurAgentPersonaResolver:167`, `TurSNSitePersonaContentFitAPI:129`, `TurChatSlotExtractionService:557`, `TurChatMultiModalSlotService:416` — and both are LAZY. `TurDefaultAgentResolver.resolveEffectiveAgent` returns the binding's own agent untouched and only re-loads on the default-agent fallback, so a site with its own agent was reading proxies off an instance detached in an earlier request; the fallback's agent is fine precisely because `findById` fetch-joins seven associations. The next lap of that treadmill was already run, not pending. The measurement also killed the shape the section proposed. A GenAI read-model cannot serve these callers: two of them hand the agent to `TurAgentChatExecutor`, which reads about fifty-five accessors and four lazy collections across eight collaborators, and seven others pass the site entity itself to `findByTurSNSiteAndLanguage`, which wants a managed one. Nor is a site read-model wanted — not one of the twenty-two reads `name`, `id` or `description`; `getTurSNSiteGenAi()` is the only property any of them touches. So the seam moved rather than the callers: one indexed query per genai/ann request buys a managed entity inside the caller's own session. Nothing in the search path calls this method, so the search hot path is untouched. Reverting to the cached instance turns two tests red. 7168 green.
- **T898e** — The cache holds one entity kind instead of two. `enabledFields` is a `FieldExtView` list carrying all twenty-one scalars — a copy on purpose, because its consumers hand the value on rather than read it, and the transient entity they still take is rebuilt at two boundaries: `TurSolrQueryBuilder#getEnabledFields`, where the cached and freshly-loaded halves meet, and the facet-definition call in `TurSNSearchProcess`. No downstream caller changed. The objection that parked this was the shadow copy, and it is answered rather than argued away: a reflective test populates every declared scalar, projects, rebuilds and compares each getter, so a field dropped between the three turns red naming itself. Three self-checks failed before they worked, each found by injecting the fault first. Dropping `showAllFacetItems` was caught immediately. Swapping `facetType` with `facetItemType` was **not** — they are the same enum and my hash-derived samples gave them the same constant, so the test claimed to catch a swap and did not; samples are now counted per type, and the swap turns it red. Then the T898 lint itself: blinding its `@Entity` check left it green, because the single allowlisted entry masked the fact that nothing was being detected at all. Every acknowledged entry must now still be detected, which also fails a stale one left behind after its cache was converted. The lint had a second flaw this exposed — it treated everything under `persistence.model` as an entity, so `FieldExtView`'s enum components were reported as cached entities. `@Entity` is the precise definition and is now the whole rule. 7196 tests green; only `TurSNSite` remains.
- **T953** — No `@Cacheable` in the codebase holds a JPA entity any more, and not one of the sixty-five signatures changed. The estimate this task was filed with — ten — counted the direct calls and not the cascade; propagating a record through them reaches sixty-five parameters across nine files, most of which also serve indexing and console callers holding a managed instance, so they would have become overloads rather than replacements. The correction is what chose the shape. The cache now holds a `TurSNSiteSearchSiteView` of all thirty-two scalars, and `snapshot.site()` rebuilds a transient entity from it, so every collaborator keeps the type it declares. Two associations ride along denormalised to exactly what is read: three GenAI fields for `TurSNHybridRankingService`, and the already-resolved `pluginType` on a rebuilt vendor, which is all `getPluginForSite` looks at — nothing else on this path touches the SE instance, `TurSolrInstanceProcess` loading its own site by name. The rebuild is strictly safer than what it replaces: the entity declares nine lazy collections the snapshot query fetch-joins none of, so on the cached instance they were proxies that threw, and on the rebuilt one they are empty. `populateMetrics` takes a managed reference by id rather than the rebuilt instance, so the foreign key is never in doubt. Four faults each turn a test red — a lost scalar, a lost ranking field, a lost vendor plugin, and blinding the lint's entity walk, which the emptied allowlist no longer masks because a positive control replaced it. The suite also caught what the rebuild really changes: `TurSNSite` overrides no `equals`, so a stub matching the site by identity no longer matches; production compares sites nowhere, and that stub now matches on the id. 7200 tests green.

## Block AD — Provider coverage + cross-vendor capability exploitation (§XXVIII)

- **T522** — **Multi-provider "second opinion" answer cross-check** — for high-stakes answers, a cheap model from a **different vendor** critiques the primary RAG answer (a cross-vendor LlmJudge) and the agreement is surfaced beside it as a confidence signal — it never alters the answer. New `genai/secondopinion/`: `TurSecondOpinion` (verdict: agree + optional confidence + rationale + critic model/vendor) and `TurSecondOpinionService`, which resolves the configured critic instance, **enforces the cross-vendor constraint** (skips when the critic's plugin type equals the answerer's — a same-family second view shares blind spots), builds its resilience-wrapped `ChatModel`, runs a strict fact-checker judge prompt over the question + retrieved context + answer, and parses an `AGREE`/`DISAGREE` verdict (+ optional `Confidence: N%`, reusing the T180 yes/no normalization idea; OpenAI critics can later supply calibrated `top_logprobs` confidence). Wired into the CALL branch of `TurChatStreamingDispatcher` beside the T516 guardrail (uses the final text + drained RAG context, passes the answering `llmInstance` for the vendor check) and emitted as a new `secondOpinion` SSE event. Opt-in via `GLOBAL_SECOND_OPINION_ENABLED` + `GLOBAL_SECOND_OPINION_LLM` (admin GET/PUT); both SDKs parse the event onto `TurChatSecondOpinion` (`onSecondOpinion` + message field), and the admin chat renders an agrees/disagrees confidence badge (en/pt). Fail-open everywhere; disabled = byte-for-byte legacy path. **Completes Block AD's cross-vendor bridges.** (§XXVIII.18)
- **T521** — **Managed reranker strategies (AWS Bedrock Rerank + Google Vertex AI Ranking)** — two additional Block N `TurRagRerankStrategy` backends for cloud-native deployments that don't want to self-host a cross-encoder, both fail-open like the rest of Block N (any error → empty → `TurRagReranker` keeps retrieval order). `TurBedrockRerankStrategy` (`BEDROCK`) calls the AWS Bedrock `Rerank` API (reusing the T520 `bedrockagentruntime` client; IAM creds; region `GLOBAL_RAG_SN_RERANK_REGION`; model is the `GLOBAL_RAG_SN_RERANK_MODEL` ARN or a bare id like `amazon.rerank-v1:0` expanded to the region's foundation-model ARN). `TurVertexAiRerankStrategy` (`VERTEX_AI`) calls the Vertex AI Ranking (Discovery Engine `rankingConfigs:rank`) REST endpoint with a `GoogleCredentials` bearer (service-account JSON in the encrypted rerank-key field, else Application Default Credentials), project/location from `GLOBAL_RAG_SN_RERANK_VERTEX_PROJECT`/`_LOCATION`, model default `semantic-ranker-default-004`. Both reorder via the shared `TurRerankIndexMapper` (the pure response→index mapping is unit-tested). Added to `TurRagRerankStrategyType` + the strategy enum/selector; admin Global Settings gains the two options + region / project / location / service-account fields (en/pt). Selecting either is the only change — every existing strategy path is untouched. (§XXVIII.17)
- **T520** — **Managed-RAG retrieval backend seam (`TurRetrievalBackend`) + Bedrock Knowledge Bases** — an opt-in <em>alternative</em> to the built-in vector/hybrid index for tenants standardized on a cloud RAG stack, without rewriting the retrieval core. New `genai/rag/backend/`: `TurRetrievalBackend` (returns Spring AI `Document`s exactly like the built-in path, so retrieved passages flow through reranking / `sources[]` / native-citations verbatim), `TurRetrievalBackendType` (`BUILT_IN` default | `BEDROCK_KB`), `TurRetrievalRequest`, and `TurRetrievalBackendResolver` — the single seam the core consults: it returns an override <em>only</em> for an explicitly configured **and** available managed backend, else `Optional.empty()` so `TurRagSearchToolService.retrieveDocuments` runs the built-in path byte-for-byte unchanged (one early, fail-safe override at the top; a backend error degrades to built-in). `TurBedrockKnowledgeBaseBackend` implements the AWS Bedrock KB `Retrieve` API (new `software.amazon.awssdk:bedrockagentruntime` dep, pinned to the spring-ai-bom AWS SDK version; IAM creds mirror T506), mapping each `KnowledgeBaseRetrievalResult` to a `Document` carrying `source_id`/`url`/`title` — so a Bedrock-KB passage becomes a **citation source through the existing `TurCitationDocument` path** (this delivers the Bedrock half of T519's native-citation bridge — no separate decoder). Config is deploy-time infra (`turing.retrieval.*`, `TurRetrievalProperty`, like `turing.storage.*`): default `BUILT_IN` = unchanged. Vertex AI Search / RAG Engine are future adapters on the same seam. (§XXVIII.16)
- **T519** — **Native-citation RAG across vendors (Cohere `Command`)** — decodes Cohere's native citation-mode citations onto the existing T152–T155 `TurChatCitation` / `citations` SSE contract, so a Cohere-grounded answer renders through the exact same chip/popover UI as the already-wired Anthropic/Gemini native citations — no new UI. New `genai/nativeapi/cohere/`: `TurCohereCitationSupport` (pure, unit-tested — `documentsArray` encodes the retrieved `TurCitationDocument`s as the Cohere v2 `documents` array with stable `doc-N` ids; `decode` maps the response `message.citations[]` `start`/`end`/`text` + `sources[].id` back onto `TurChatCitation`, resolving `doc-N` → sourceId/title/url, `answerStart`/`answerEnd` from the span, `locationType="search_result"`) and `TurCohereMessagesService` (calls Cohere's native `POST /v2/chat` over `java.net.http` with the passages as documents, decodes, and emits the answer token + `citations` event in the same `Mono.fromCallable(runTurn)…flatMapMany(emitTurn)` shape as `TurAnthropicMessagesService`). Wired into `TurNativeChatExecutor`: a `cohere` branch taken **only** when the agent's `citations` Request Option is on (Cohere otherwise stays on the OpenAI-compatible Spring AI path, unchanged), reusing `retrievePassagesForCitations` + `TurCitationDriftService.tapForPersistence` like the Anthropic path. Opt-in + dependency-light (HTTP, no new SDK). **Bedrock Knowledge Base citations** (the other half of this bridge) ship with **T520**, where the `bedrockagentruntime` `RetrieveAndGenerate` dependency naturally lands — its KB response decodes onto this same contract. (§XXVIII.15)
- **T518** — **Cost-aware cross-provider fallback / meta-provider** — an OpenRouter-style layer that fails over to another vendor when a provider errors/rate-limits and (optionally) routes the cheapest-capable instance first. `TurFallbackChatModel` is a delegating `ChatModel` over an ordered candidate list (each already a `TurResilientChatModel`, so the meta layer only fails over once a candidate has exhausted *its own* retry/circuit-breaker pipeline); CALL fails over on a candidate exception, STREAM fails over **only before the first token** (mid-stream replay would duplicate SSE text), and it tracks `lastServedInstanceId()` so cost attributes to the vendor that actually served. `TurMetaChatModelResolver` builds the chain from a Global-Settings instance-id list (`GLOBAL_LLM_FALLBACK_CHAIN`) ordered by `TurLlmFallbackMode` — `PRIORITY` (configured order) or `CHEAPEST` (ascending `TurLLMPriceService` rates) — each candidate decrypted + resilience-wrapped via `TurLlmModelFactory`. Wired at the two single model-resolution points: the agent chat turn (`TurAgentChatExecutor`, after the T291 budget downgrade) and the resolver-based stages (`TurDefaultChatModelResolver` — rerank/judge/background/summary/catalog). Per-tenant cost attribution rides the existing `tenantContext` token-usage recording. Admin GET/PUT on `/api/system/global-settings` + a routing-mode select and per-instance chain toggles on the Global Settings LLM card (en/pt). Empty chain = byte-for-byte legacy single-model path; fail-safe everywhere (a broken chain reverts to the primary). Composes with — does not replace — the T291 monthly-budget downgrade. (§XXVIII.14)
- **T517** — **Cross-provider per-stage model "lanes"** — declaratively bind whole classes of LLM work to different vendors, monetizing the Block AD multi-provider breadth. New `system/lane/` seam: `TurModelLane` (`FAST` / `REASONING` / `CHEAP`) + `TurModelStage` (each pipeline call site — `AUTOCOMPLETE`/`SPELL`/`QUERY_REWRITE` → FAST, `ROUTER`/`RERANK`/`JUDGE` → REASONING, `BACKGROUND_SUMMARIZATION` → CHEAP — fixed-mapped to a lane in code, so the admin only configures three lanes). `TurModelLaneService.resolveInstanceId(stage)` applies the single rule **stage → lane → bound instance (present + enabled) → else default LLM**, fail-safe: a blank/missing/disabled binding silently reverts that stage to the legacy single-model path. `TurDefaultChatModelResolver` gains a lane-aware `resolve(TurModelStage)` overload (shared lookup-decrypt-build). Wired into two representative stages: the SN hybrid-ranking LLM reranker (`RERANK` → reasoning lane) and the nightly batch summarization job (`BACKGROUND_SUMMARIZATION` → cheap lane). Bindings live in Global Settings (`GLOBAL_MODEL_LANE_FAST`/`_REASONING`/`_CHEAP`, seeded blank) with admin GET/PUT on `/api/system/global-settings` + three lane selectors on the Global Settings LLM card (en/pt). Every lane blank = byte-for-byte legacy behaviour (all stages on the default LLM). (§XXVIII.13)
- **T516** — **Pluggable answer-grounding guardrail (`TurAnswerGuardrail` seam)** — validates that a streamed RAG answer is actually grounded in the retrieved context (and screens it for unsafe content / PII) at answer time — the managed complement to the next-day T155 citation-drift scan. New `genai/safety/guardrail/` seam mirroring the Block N reranker: `TurAnswerGuardrailStrategy` (type enum `NONE`/`BEDROCK`/`OPENAI_MODERATION`/`MISTRAL_MODERATION` + lenient `fromValue`), a by-type `TurAnswerGuardrailStrategyFactory` (falls back to `NONE`), and a `TurAnswerGuardrailService` facade that is the single fail-open + opt-in boundary. **Adapters:** `TurBedrockAnswerGuardrail` (the flagship — AWS Bedrock Guardrails `ApplyGuardrail` over the T506 dependency: contextual-grounding score + relevance + PII redaction in one managed call, decoded into a provider-agnostic `TurAnswerGuardrailVerdict`), `TurOpenAiModerationGuardrail` (reuses T182 `omni-moderation` via a new `moderateAlways`), `TurMistralModerationGuardrail` (GDPR-native `mistral-moderation-latest` over HTTP), and the no-op `NONE`. Wired into the CALL branch of `TurChatStreamingDispatcher` after the answer is assembled: the retrieved chunk **texts** are teed into the existing `TurRagSourceCollector` (server-side only — never on the `sources[]` event) and fed to the guardrail, which emits a new `grounding` SSE event with the verdict (and, when `block-on-violation` is set, replaces the answer with the redacted/blocked text). Both SDKs (`@viglet/turing-sdk` + `@viglet/turing-react-sdk`) parse the `grounding` event onto `TurChatGrounding` (`onGrounding` callback + message field); the admin chat renders a confidence badge beside the bubble (en/pt). Opt-in via `turing.safety.guardrail.*` (`TurGuardrailProperty`, default off → byte-for-byte legacy path); fail-open everywhere — a guardrail outage never blocks an answer. (§XXVIII.12)
- **T515** — **Mistral OCR / Document-AI extraction bridge** — augments Apache Tika on the indexer write path for scanned PDFs and image-only documents, which Tika reads poorly. New `TurMistralOcrService` over the Mistral `/v1/ocr` API (`mistral-ocr-latest`): PDFs via `document_url`, images via `image_url`, both as base64 data URIs (no upload step); the per-page `pages[].markdown` is concatenated into layout-aware text. Wired as an **augmentation** in `TurAssetTrainingService` — `augment()` only calls OCR when the file is OCR-eligible (image/PDF) AND Tika produced no usable text, so the common text-document path is untouched and spends no OCR credits. Opt-in via `turing.ocr.*` (`TurOcrProperty`: `enabled` + `mistral.apiKey`/`model`/`endpoint`/`timeoutSeconds`); disabled or keyless → inert, returns the Tika text. Fail-soft: any OCR error keeps the Tika text and never blocks indexing. The structured Markdown also feeds T387 manifest derivation downstream. (§XXVIII.11)
- **T514** — **Quantized / Matryoshka embeddings for the Lucene store** — opt-in scalar quantization on the embedded Lucene KNN store to shrink the index and speed search at a measured recall cost. New `TurLuceneQuantization` enum (`NONE`/`INT8`/`INT4`/`SEVEN_BIT`/`BINARY`) maps to a Lucene 10.4 `Lucene104HnswScalarQuantizedVectorsFormat` `ScalarEncoding`, built into a `FilterCodec` (per-field `PerFieldKnnVectorsFormat` over the `embedding` field) the writer applies on first open; the wrapper raises the format's advertised 1024-dim cap to 4096 so 1536/3072-dim models (OpenAI/Gemini) still index quantized. Selected per store via the `quantization` provider option on the store instance — `TurLuceneStoreProvider` resolves it once and threads it through every `openIndex` so the codec is consistent (one `IndexWriter` per directory). `NONE` (default) keeps `float32` — byte-for-byte legacy. Pairs with Matryoshka `outputDimensionality` truncation (already on the Voyage/Gemini embedding models) to compound the shrink. Changing the mode only affects new segments; Lucene records the codec per segment, so a re-index converts everything. (§XXVIII.10)
- **T513** — **Domain-specialized embedding model per SN site** — a per-site `embeddingModelId` override on `TurSNSiteGenAi` (Liquibase `v2026.3.1.83`, nullable camelCase column) so a legal-docs site can pick `voyage-law`, a code-search site `voyage-code`, a finance site `voyage-finance` (or a Cohere domain model), while every other site stays on the platform default. `TurSNHybridRankingService` resolves the site override first (falling back to the Global Settings default) for **both** the index and query halves of the public hybrid-ranking path — they always use the same resolved model, so the per-site `sn_<siteId>` collection never mismatches within one config. Admin selector added to the SN GenAI "Hybrid Ranking" card (en/pt), populated from the embedding-model list; flows through the entity (no DTO). Null/blank = platform default → existing sites unchanged. Operational note: switching a site's model changes the vector space, so its collection must be cleared + re-indexed. (§XXVIII.9)
- **T512** — **Contextualized chunk embeddings (`voyage-context-3`)** — attacks the classic RAG failure (a chunk that's meaningless without its section) by embedding each chunk *with* its sibling chunks. New `TurContextualEmbeddingModel` capability mix-in + `TurVoyageContextualEmbeddingModel` over Voyage's `contextualizedembeddings` REST API (nested `inputs[doc][chunk]` request → `data[].data[].embedding` response; query/single-chunk calls degrade to one-doc-one-chunk with the asymmetric `query`/`document` input type; `output_dimension` Matryoshka). The `voyage` provider returns it for a `*context*` model variant or the `contextual` option (text-only stays default). `TurResilientEmbeddingModel` passes the contextual call through the resilience pipeline. `TurRagContextBuilder.reindexByMetadataContextual` embeds a document's chunks together and upserts the precomputed vectors via the store's `importChunks` seam, **fail-soft** back to per-chunk `reindexByMetadata` on a non-contextual model, a vector-count mismatch, or any error; the asset-training indexer calls it so it's wired end-to-end. Query time is unchanged. Opt-in = the embedding-model choice. (§XXVIII.8)
- **T511** — **Multimodal embeddings retrieval mode (search text → match an image)** — embeds images and text into *one* vector space so a plain-text query matches PDF page-images, diagrams and screenshots the text-only path can't. New `TurMultimodalEmbeddingModel` capability mix-in (alongside Spring AI `EmbeddingModel`) + `TurVoyageMultimodalEmbeddingModel` over Voyage's `multimodalembeddings` REST API (`voyage-multimodal-3`, base64 image parts, asymmetric `document`/`query` input types, `output_dimension` Matryoshka). The `voyage` provider returns it when the model is a `*multimodal*` variant or the `multimodal` provider option is set (text-only stays default — legacy configs byte-for-byte unchanged); `TurResilientEmbeddingModel` transparently passes the image side through the retry/CB/time-limiter pipeline so the capability survives the resilience wrap. `TurMultimodalRetrievalService` bridges the model + the embedded Lucene vector store + the indexer write path: `indexImage` embeds + upserts a precomputed vector tagged `_modality=image`; `searchImagesByText` embeds the query in the shared space and KNN-matches image vectors — both fail-soft (text-only model → no-op). REST surface `/api/v2/multimodal` (`available` / `image` upload / `search`). (§XXVIII.7)
- **T505** — **Generic OpenAI-compatible provider (`openai-compatible`)** — generalized the fixed-endpoint `gemini-openai` seam into a first-class, vendor-agnostic `TurOpenAiCompatibleLlmProvider`. The `baseUrl` is required per-instance config (from the `baseUrl` provider option or the instance URL — no default, since the URL *is* the vendor); the API key is sent as a Bearer token and is **optional** so a local vLLM/LM-Studio endpoint works with a blank key. Chat (buffered `OpenAiChatModel`), token-by-token streaming (`TurOpenAiStreamingChatModel`), and embeddings (`OpenAiEmbeddingModel`, where the endpoint exposes `/embeddings`) — one seam reaching **DeepSeek, xAI Grok, Groq, Cerebras, OpenRouter, Together, Fireworks, and local vLLM/LM-Studio**. Seeded `OPENAI_COMPAT` vendor (`TurLLMVendorOnStartup` + Liquibase `v2026.3.1.77` for existing installs), added to `LlmProviderType.KNOWN`, and a no-default-URL branch in the admin `applyVendorDefaults`. Capability matrix stays empty (maps to `ANY`) — no provider-native server tools assumed. (§XXVIII.1)
- **T510** — **Google Vertex AI provider (`vertex-ai`)** — the enterprise path for Gemini on GCP (`TurVertexAiLlmProvider`). The existing `gemini` provider hits the *public* `generativelanguage.googleapis.com`, which can't serve a GCP-regulated tenant; Vertex AI adds IAM, VPC-SC, CMEK, regional endpoints and provisioned throughput. It **reuses the native Gemini stack** — same `GoogleGenAiChatModel` and `TurGeminiEmbeddingModel` (so T489+ prompt-assembly and T495 asymmetric-task-type embeddings come for free) — and **differs only in transport + auth**: the `com.google.genai.Client` is built in Vertex mode (`vertexAI(true)`, `project` + `location` provider options) instead of with an API key. Auth mirrors the Bedrock pattern: a service-account JSON in the encrypted key field (or a `credentialsJson` option) → explicit `GoogleCredentials`, else Application Default Credentials (workload identity / gcloud / GCE metadata), scoped to cloud-platform. Seeded `VERTEX_AI` vendor (`TurLLMVendorOnStartup` + Liquibase `v2026.3.1.82`), added to `LlmProviderType.KNOWN` + IAM-oriented `applyVendorDefaults`. (§XXVIII.6)
- **T509** — **Mistral AI provider (`mistral`)** — `TurMistralLlmProvider`, prioritized for the non-technical reason that a **European, GDPR-native** option matters to EU customers and Viglet's positioning. Mistral's API (`https://api.mistral.ai/v1`) is OpenAI-compatible, so chat (Mistral Large/Small, Codestral; default `mistral-large-latest`), token-by-token streaming, and embeddings (`mistral-embed`) reuse the proven OpenAI seam. Seeded `MISTRAL` vendor (`TurLLMVendorOnStartup` + Liquibase `v2026.3.1.81`), added to `LlmProviderType.KNOWN` + `applyVendorDefaults`. **Reranker scope note:** Mistral exposes no managed rerank endpoint, so the planned `MISTRAL` reranker strategy was **not** added (it would have been a dead 404) — Mistral reranking is already available via the existing `LLM` rerank strategy pointed at a Mistral instance. (§XXVIII.5)
- **T508** — **Cohere full provider (`cohere`)** — promoted Cohere beyond the existing `COHERE` reranker strategy to a first-class chat + embeddings provider (`TurCohereLlmProvider`). Rides Cohere's **OpenAI-compatible** endpoint (`https://api.cohere.ai/compatibility/v1`), so `Command` chat (default `command-r-plus`), token-by-token streaming (`TurOpenAiStreamingChatModel`), and `Embed v4` embeddings (default `embed-v4.0`, multilingual/multimodal/Matryoshka) all reuse the proven OpenAI seam. Cohere's *native* built-in citation mode is deferred to the §XXVIII.15 cross-vendor bridge (T519). Seeded `COHERE` vendor (`TurLLMVendorOnStartup` + Liquibase `v2026.3.1.80`), added to `LlmProviderType.KNOWN` + `applyVendorDefaults`. (§XXVIII.4)
- **T507** — **Voyage AI embeddings + `VOYAGE` reranker** — the retrieval specialist Anthropic recommends, in two halves. (1) A `voyage` embedding provider (`TurVoyageLlmProvider`, embedding-only — chat throws, mirroring the inverse Anthropic case) backed by `TurVoyageEmbeddingModel`, a custom Spring AI `EmbeddingModel` over the Voyage embeddings REST API (`voyage-3`/`voyage-3-large`) that applies the asymmetric `input_type=document|query` via the standard VectorStore calling convention, plus optional `output_dimension` Matryoshka truncation. (2) A `VOYAGE` Block N `TurRagReranker` strategy (`TurVoyageRerankStrategy`, `rerank-2.5`) reusing the shared `TurHttpRerankClient` — now generalized to take the result-count param name (Voyage uses `top_k`, not `top_n`) and to parse the `data` envelope (not just `results`). Seeded `VOYAGE` vendor (`TurLLMVendorOnStartup` + Liquibase `v2026.3.1.79`), added to `LlmProviderType.KNOWN` + the reranker enum/selector (global settings model + page + en/pt i18n) + `applyVendorDefaults`. (§XXVIII.3)
- **T506** — **AWS Bedrock provider (`bedrock`)** — `TurBedrockLlmProvider` over Spring AI's Bedrock Converse API (`spring-ai-bedrock-converse`), the one provider authenticated by **IAM** (region + credentials chain) rather than an API key, so it acts as a gateway to Claude/Llama/Mistral/Nova/Titan on a single endpoint. Credentials resolve via `TurBedrockCredentials`: explicit static keys (`accessKeyId` provider option + the encrypted API-key field as the secret, optional `sessionToken`) or, when none are set, the AWS `DefaultCredentialsProvider` chain (env / instance profile / IAM role) — the data-residency path AWS-standardized enterprises require. Chat via `BedrockProxyChatModel` (native Converse streaming inherited); embeddings via a custom `TurBedrockEmbeddingModel` over `InvokeModel` (Titan `amazon.titan-embed-*` and Cohere `cohere.embed-*`, with asymmetric `search_document`/`search_query` input types) since the Converse module is chat-only. The model id is the Bedrock id or inference-profile ARN. Seeded `BEDROCK` vendor (`TurLLMVendorOnStartup` + Liquibase `v2026.3.1.78`), added to `LlmProviderType.KNOWN`, IAM-oriented branch in `applyVendorDefaults`. (§XXVIII.2)

## Block AE — Documentation parity & didactic refresh, post-T410 (§XXIX)

> **Block complete (2026-06-30).** All fifteen tasks shipped (T524–T538) — the
> second `docs-turing` round: parity for everything shipped T424→T523 (12 pages
> rewritten/created across the provider, RAG, reranking, manifest, capabilities,
> client-tools, personas, showcase surfaces) + a zero→first-agent didactic layer
> (learning-path spine + per-pillar checklist passes) + the `COVERAGE.md` gate.
> Docs land in the `vigletdocs.github.io/docs-turing` repo; this ledger tracks task status only.
>
> **Follow-up (T539):** the JavaScript/Node developer surface — the vanilla SDK,
> the CLI and the flow DSL — had no pages (the T538 sweep folded the CLI into
> `developer-guide`); T539 gave each its own didactic page.

- **T539** — **JS/Node developer-surface pages (new)** — three new pages under **Developers**, written from the verified package source (`frontend/packages/{sdk,cli,flow-dsl}`): **`javascript-sdk.md`** for the zero-dep vanilla `@viglet/turing-sdk` (the client/API-fns/controllers mental model, `createTuringClient` + CSRF priming, the search/chat/autocomplete/slots/similar controllers, streaming SSE event shapes, frontend client tools, the analytics/GA4 bridge, and the Adobe EDS pattern); **`cli.md`** for `@viglet/turing-cli` (the `turing` command — init/dev/deploy/eval/eval-record/logs, `turing.config.json` + token/basic auth resolution, project layout, and the eval YAML assertion table); **`flow-dsl.md`** for `@viglet/turing-flow-dsl` (`defineFlow`/`defineBundle`/`transpileFlow`/`transpileBundle`, the full node-type catalog, slot/persona declarations, sub-flow bundles, and the build→deploy paths). Wired into `sidebars-turing.ts` after `react-sdk`; the CLI row in `COVERAGE.md` graduated from 🧩 folded → its own page, and rows added for the vanilla SDK + flow-dsl. (§XXIX.5)
- **T538** — **Coverage audit + sidebar wiring (closes Block AE)** — authored `docs-turing/COVERAGE.md`, the living gate mapping every shipped `CHANGELOG` block/feature → its doc page + anchor (or an explicit "🔒 deliberately internal" row for engineering/repo/marketing blocks: A–C, D/E/H internals, P open-core, Q `viglet-core`, AC caching, O GEO, AB public site), with a final **Orphans: none** assertion. Wired the four new pages into `sidebars-turing.ts` — `manifest` under Search it, `reranking` under Ask it, `capabilities` + `client-tools` under Automate it. Retired `_documentation-update-plan.md` (Block U scope) to a pointer at `COVERAGE.md`. Swept the older blocks called out in §XXIX.5 (M → folded into `rag.md`; Q → internal; R → `manifest.md`; S → `react-sdk.md`) and confirmed each is documented or deliberately internal. (§XXIX.5)
- **T537** — **Didactic pass — "Automate it" + "Run it" + Developers + Security pillars** — audited all pages in the remaining sidebar categories against the §XXIX.2 checklist. Openers were already concept-first (Block U + this block's rewrites); the systematic gap was missing "Related Pages" footers, added to **installation-guide, configuration-reference, genai-llm, developer-guide, react-sdk, conversion-analytics** and standardized `multi-tenancy`'s "See also" → "Related Pages" — each with annotated cross-links to siblings (incl. the new capabilities/reranking/client-tools pages). Every pillar page now opens with what-it-is/when + closes with Related Pages. (§XXIX.4)
- **T536** — **Didactic pass — "Search it" + "Ask it" pillars** — audited every page in both sidebar categories (Enterprise Search: search-engine, semantic-navigation, dsl-query, dsl-compatibility, spa-pages, integration, integration-aem, import-export; RAG & Chat: rag, embedding-stores, embedding-models, chat, chat-memory, intent, personas) against the §XXIX.2 checklist. Most already complied (Block U house style + this block's rag/embeddings/personas rewrites); brought the two outliers up to standard — `dsl-query` gained a concept-first "what it is + when you'd reach for it" opener (vs `/search`, + the agent DSL tools), `dsl-compatibility` gained a concept opener explaining what the matrix is and when to read it, and both "See Also" footers were standardized to "Related Pages" with annotated cross-links. (§XXIX.4)
- **T535** — **Learning-path spine** — rewrote `getting-started/intro.md` into a guided **5-step learning path** (Understand the model → first search → first RAG answer → first agent → first automation), each step linking its deep page, with a "see it all wired together" pointer to the showcase. Anchored `getting-started/core-concepts.md` as Step 1 (callout back to the path + forward to Step 2), and reordered the `index.mdx` Quick Start so "Start here → the learning path" is step 01. This is the spine every page's "when you'd reach for it" points back to (feeds the T536/T537 didactic passes). (§XXIX.4)
- **T534** — **Expanded `showcase.md` into the canonical end-to-end walkthrough** — restructured the Atlas Store page (Block Y, T451–T457) into a didactic "see it all wired together" narrative: an ordered walkthrough (typed search → RAG chat → agent power → creative compositions → voice/memory/ops) where each surface links its deep page, making it a map for the learning path. **Correction:** Block X creative bets (T442–T450) have **shipped** (each with a Liquibase changeset + service + tests), so they're documented as real **opt-in, default-off** features — not "previews" as the stale §XXIX.2 note said; fixed that note. Verified the wired surfaces + per-agent flags against the §XXIII/§XXIV CHANGELOG. (§XXIX.3)
- **T533** — **Expanded `personas.md` for audience-fit (Block AA)** — added an **Audience Personas & Content-Fit** section to the speaker-only page (verified against the §XXVI CHANGELOG): the `personaKind` discriminator (SPEAKER/AUDIENCE/BOTH, default SPEAKER unchanged; AUDIENCE never resolves as voice, T464), the audience reader profile + evaluation notebook (SN_DOC/ASSET/URL sources, fail-soft extraction, T465), the deterministic multilingual readability scorer (EN/PT/ES Flesch, T466), the grounded LLM content-fit evaluator fused 50/50 with readability (T467), the fit-report API + red/amber/green report (≥70/≥40/<40, `POST /api/persona/{id}/content-fit`, T468), and persona-from-audio drafting (`/derive-from-audio`, never auto-saved, T470) over the pluggable `TurTranscriptionProvider` seam (T469). Added `personaKind` to the anatomy table + the new endpoints to the REST reference. (§XXIX.3)
- **T527** — **Expanded `tool-calling.md` + `ai-agents.md` for cross-vendor native parity** — added a **Reasoning, caching & background execution** section to `ai-agents.md` covering the three cross-vendor request options behind shared seams (verified against the §X.13/§X.20 CHANGELOG): extended thinking / reasoning budget across OpenAI·Anthropic·Gemini onto the shared `reasoning` event incl. Anthropic interleaved thinking + temperature=1 (T504), explicit context caching via the vendor-neutral `TurContextCacheProvider` seam (Gemini remote `cachedContents` / Anthropic inline `cache_control` / OpenAI automatic, T499/T502), and OpenAI Responses `background` mode (T503). Refreshed the stale OpenAI-only "Provider-native tools" note (Anthropic+Gemini have shipped → cross-link Capabilities), added a tool-loop note to `tool-calling.md` (interleaved thinking + background mode), and dropped the stale repository-cache section (uncached since T486). (§XXIX.3)
- **T526** — **Expanded `genai-llm.md` + `tool-calling.md` for Gemini native primitives (F.16)** — new **Gemini native primitives** section in `genai-llm.md` distinguishing native `gemini` (engages only on a non-empty capability matrix; empty = unchanged Spring AI path) from `gemini-openai`, covering the server-side built-in tools (Google Search grounding + Search-Suggestion chips, URL Context, code execution, native image gen, Computer Use, T490–T494), reasoning/batch/files/embeddings (thinking budget + thought summaries T498, Batch chat+embeddings T496, Files bridge ~48 h T497, asymmetric embeddings T495), and the innovation bets (explicit context caching T499, full-context retrieval-free answering T500, native video/audio understanding T501). Added a **Provider-native server tools** cross-vendor matrix to `tool-calling.md` distinguishing them from Turing's own tools. Verified against `TurNativeCapability` + the §X.19 CHANGELOG. (§XXIX.3)
- **T532** — **New `client-tools.md` (AG-UI)** — documents the agent↔UI surface (§XXII), verified against the CHANGELOG entries: live read-only tool activity (`toolCallEventsEnabled` → `tool_call` SSE start/end with redacted arg digest + `durationMs`, `TuringToolActivity` atom, T436/T437) vs the interactive client-tool round-trip (`clientToolsEnabled` + `clientToolsJson`, park keyed by `(conversationId, callId)` → `client_tool_call` SSE → `POST /api/v2/chat/client-tool-result` → re-dispatch, only-declared/404, in-memory park, T438) with a Mermaid sequence, the SDK `registerClientTool`/`clientTools` API + hop-cap-10 + CopilotKit `useCopilotAction` parity (T439), and generative UI (`useGenerativeUI` + `TuringGenerativeContent`, name→component 1:1, T440). Cross-linked react-sdk / ai-agents / developer-guide / capabilities. (§XXIX.3)

Fixed the stale provider table (dropped Azure → 11 vendors), embedding-provider list, "three backends" → four (added embedded Lucene), the MinIO-only Knowledge-Base line (now pluggable storage), and cross-linked the new `reranking.md`. All bridges flagged opt-in + fail-safe. (§XXIX.3)

(§XXIX.3)
- **T525** — **New `capabilities.md`** — documents the F.15 capability registry/taxonomy (T432–T435, T132): the three `kind`s (TOOL / REQUEST_OPTION / PLATFORM), the descriptor fields (`key`/`function`/`provider`/`category`/`ownsTurn`/`valueType`/`options`), and the **two-level gate** — instance matrix (`llm_instance_capability`, `GET/PUT/DELETE /api/llm/{id}/capability`) ∧ per-agent `nativeCapabilities` picker, intersected at runtime — with a Mermaid flow, the "silent Spring AI fallback if either gate is missed" gotcha, and the null-`nativeCapabilities` legacy rule. Dropped the stale repository-cache section (repos uncached since T486). (§XXIX.3)

## Block AF — Domain layer: bounded completion, stop-criteria & enforcement (§XXX)

- **T540** — **Benchmarked the double-mapping cost on the hot path** — a gated (`-Dturing.bench=true`), warmed-up nanoTime harness (`TurDomainMappingBenchmark`, no JMH dep) measuring `entity → MapStruct → record` (`TurSNSiteDomainMapper.toDomain`) against direct-getter access on the exact `TurSNSite` aggregate behind the EAGER 200ms→5s incident. Result (JDK 21, warm, 5M ops): MapStruct hop ≈**48–53 ns/op** (≈40 ns/op over the getter baseline), ≈0.00005 ms — 4–5 orders of magnitude below a millisecond-scale search request, i.e. immeasurable on the hot path. The incident cost was the EAGER graph walk a by-ID record structurally cannot trigger, never the copy. **Decision fed to T541: keep ports on the hot read paths; a projection query is only warranted where the source entity is heavy to _load_ (many columns / large LOBs), not to _map_.** Numbers recorded in the benchmark Javadoc; not run in CI. (§XXX.1)
- **T541** — **ADR + stop-criteria for the domain layer** — wrote `docs/adr/0001-domain-layer-bounded-completion.md` (a new `docs/adr/` home), the first written rationale for the hexagonal pilot that previously lived only in class Javadoc + "scaffolding only" commit messages. Codifies the boundary: a `*Domain` record + port **only** for reads on a hot path (search/chat/RAG/autocomplete), a cached value, a cross-aggregate read (EAGER-cascade hazard), or a LazyInit-prone read (escapes its session) — records reference neighbours **by ID**; the JPA repository stays for admin/CRUD reads and **all** writes (`@Transactional` + `LEFT JOIN FETCH`, per T488). States the explicit **"not 100%"** decision, that **ports are read-only forever** (no `save`/`delete`), and the single criterion a *new* aggregate must meet to earn a record — partial coverage is the intended end state, not a debt. Mirrored the rule into `agents.md` ("Domain layer (ports & adapters)") and agent memory. (§XXX.2)
- **T542** — **Finished the valuable read-path slice** — audited the hot read paths (search / chat / RAG) against the T541 boundary and found the "keep" set already largely conformant: the LLM chat APIs (`TurLLMChatAPI`/`TurLLMSemanticChatAPI`) correctly split port (non-secret scalar read) vs JPA entity (decrypted `apiKey` + provider factory), `TurSpotlightService` reads the site via the port and stubs an entity only for the port-less spotlight query, `TurSNSearchProcess` operates on a snapshot-provided entity (not a per-request lazy proxy), and the genai secret/provider/collection reads legitimately need the managed entity. The one genuine gap — `TurDslToolService.loadFieldConfig`, which builds the **cached** `TurSNSiteFieldConfigModel` read-model but still sourced enabled fields as JPA `TurSNSiteFieldExt` entities — was migrated to `TurSNSiteFieldExtRepositoryPort.findBySnSiteIdAndEnabled(...)` (records), removing that JPA repo dependency from a chat-path service so a cached read-model no longer touches entities. No hot path is left half on entities where a record was warranted; remaining entity reads on those paths are intentional per the boundary, not gaps. (§XXX.3)
- **T543** — **Pruned the speculative scaffolding** — systematic pass against the T541 boundary. Deleted the orphan `TurPersonaDomain` record (a "scaffolding only" projection with **zero** consumers repo-wide — no port, no adapter, no mapper, never read) and its now-empty `domain/persona` package. The pass confirmed nothing else prunes: all **8** surviving `*RepositoryPort`s have a genuine hot/search consumer (agent/LLM/MCP/custom-tool ports feed chat; SN-site/field-ext/locale/metric ports feed search + the GraphQL/DSL search surface), so none is "admin-only ceremony", and `TurSNSiteCoreUsageDomain` — which looked port-adjacent — is live via `TurSEInstanceAPI.findCoreUsage`. The two-sources-of-truth surface is now exactly the keep set. (§XXX.4)
- **T544** — **Closed the write-path question** — rewrote the Javadoc on all **8** surviving `*RepositoryPort`s (agent / custom-tool / LLM / MCP / SN-site / field-ext / locale / metric-access) from the "read-only for the pilot / future slices will introduce `save`/`delete`" promise to the final decision: **read-only by design, permanently**. Writes stay on the JPA repository + `@Transactional` + `LEFT JOIN FETCH` (per T488) — a write gains nothing from an immutable read record and everything from a managed, dirty-checked entity. Each port now points to `docs/adr/0001-domain-layer-bounded-completion.md`. The layer no longer advertises itself as perpetually in-progress. (§XXX.5)
- **T545** — **`TurDomainLayerConventionsTest` enforcement lint** — build-failing convention test (mirrors `TurCacheAnnotationConventionsTest` / `TurLiquibaseChangelogAgnosticTest`) that keeps the domain layer inside the ADR-0001 boundary so it can't silently drift back toward 100%. Three checks: (1) every `record` under `com.viglet.turing.domain` carries **no** JPA / Hibernate / Jackson annotation (reflection over type + record components + fields + accessors); (2) the domain layer depends inward only — no `domain/` source imports a JPA repository or the `persistence.adapter` package — plus a curated set of hot-path consumers migrated to a port (`TurDslToolService`, `TurSpotlightService`, `TurSNSiteNamesAPI`) must not regress to the JPA repo they left; (3) every surviving `*RepositoryPort` has exactly one `*RepositoryAdapter` and one `*DomainMapper`. Green against the current 8 ports / 8 adapters / 8 mappers. (§XXX.6)
- **T546** — **Collapsed the one pure-ceremony port to a mapper-backed reader** — the ADR criterion is that a `*RepositoryPort` earns its interface only with a second implementation or a **mocking need**. Audit of all 8 ports found **7 are mocked in unit tests** (`TurAIAgent`/`TurLLMInstance`/`TurMcpServer`/`TurSNSite`/`TurSNSiteFieldExt`/`TurSNSiteLocale`/`TurSNSiteMetricAccess` — `@Mock` in their API/service unit tests) and stay as ports; only **`TurCustomToolRepositoryPort`** had no mock (its adapter had its own direct test), making the interface pure ceremony over a lone permanent JPA adapter. Collapsed it: deleted the port + `TurCustomToolRepositoryAdapter`, replaced by the concrete `TurCustomToolReader` (`@Component`, same mapper-backed `findById`/`findAll`/`findAllEnabled` returning immutable `TurCustomToolDomain` records — ~90% of the benefit without the interface), rewired the two consumers (`TurCustomToolAPI`, `TurCustomToolCallbackService`), renamed the adapter test → `TurCustomToolReaderTest`. `TurCustomToolDomain` record is unchanged. The layer is now **7 ports + 8 records + 1 reader**; `TurDomainLayerConventionsTest` stays green (it walks `*RepositoryPort`s, so the collapsed aggregate simply leaves the port set). No further collapse warranted — the mapping cost was already immeasurable (T540) and the remaining 7 have a live mocking need. (§XXX.7)

## Block AG — Bento UI: complete the interface migration (§XXXI)

- **T619** — **Re-add persona from-audio authoring (T470) + persona-suggest (T471) to bento (closes the Block AG persona-parity gap)** — the T571 audit flagged both tools as console-only and dropped in the T570 console retirement; this re-adds them to the bento persona surface. Their **backends were untouched** (`/api/persona/derive-from-audio`, `/api/persona/suggest` + the transcription seam all survive) — only the frontend entries were deleted, so the four deleted TS units (`persona-audio` + `persona-suggestion` model/service) were resurrected from git history verbatim, and all i18n keys (`persona.fromAudio.*`, `forms.persona.suggest.*`) already survived. **From-audio** returns as a `BentoPersonaFromAudioButton` header action on the persona list (matching the "Persona dialogue" affordance, not the old floating FAB): uploads a recording → backend transcribes + drafts a persona → opens the bento new-persona form pre-filled via router state (the loader already read `location.state.draft`). **Persona-suggest** returns as a first-class bento launch page (`/bento/persona/suggest`, `BentoHero` + frosted `BentoFormSection` cards replacing the console `SectionCard`/`SubPage` chrome) reached from a persona-list header action, reusing the shared `FitBar`/`ContentFitDetails` view and ranking content best-fit-first over the T467 evaluator. `tsc -b --noEmit` green. (§XXXI.10)
- **T572** — **Onboarding & delight polish (Phase 5 — closes Block AG)** — the four-part delight round over the migrated bento surface. **Consistent empty-states:** one canonical `BentoEmptyState` (frosted `bento-glass` panel + tonal icon chip + title + hint + optional CTA, center/start align); the list mosaic's `EmptyHintTile` now delegates to it, so all ~29 entity lists and any standalone "no data" panel share one visual language. **Keyboard-shortcut guide:** new `BentoShortcutsDialog` opened by `?` from anywhere (or the user menu), listing only the shortcuts that really work — ⌘K/Ctrl+K (palette) + `?` globally, ↑/↓/↵/Esc in the palette — platform-honest (⌘ on macOS, Ctrl elsewhere). **Reduced-motion audit:** the one animation still outside the guard — the tile→detail View-Transitions `bento-hero` cross-fade — is now cut to an instant swap under `prefers-reduced-motion`; the tour/shortcut dialogs reuse the already-guarded `.bento-shell-header` entrance so they add no unguarded motion. **First-run tour:** `BentoFirstRunTour`, a three-card welcome (hub-and-spoke rail T573 · ⌘K palette T549 · customizable layout T575) shown once on the first landing at `/bento/home`, skippable/keyboard-navigable, remembered in `localStorage` (defensive — storage-unavailable never loops), and replayable from the user menu. New shell state + `?`/first-run wiring in `bento.root.page.tsx`; `bento.tour.*`/`bento.shortcuts.*` i18n in en+pt; 3 new component test suites (+12 tests) + `tsc -b --noEmit` + full bento/i18n suite green. No new task number (next stays T619). (§XXXI.10)
- **T571** — **Full a11y + responsive + performance + e2e pass (Phase 5 — completes Block AG's committed scope)** — the audit-and-fix pass over the migrated surface. **a11y:** added a keyboard **skip-to-content** link (first focusable element) + a focusable, `aria-label`led `<main id="bento-main-content">` to the bento shell (`bento.skipToContent`/`bento.mainContent` in en+pt); verified the `prefers-reduced-motion` guard already covers every bento animation (tiles, shell-in, pulse, ambient blobs, dropdowns, view-transitions). **e2e/smoke:** new deterministic `console-redirect.test.tsx` locks the T569 `consoleSuffixToBento` cutover contract (root/home → bento home, 1:1 mirror passthrough, `embedding-model`→`embedding`, the `admin-settings/*` flattening) so old `/admin/...` deep-links can't silently break; suite now **98 tests / 23 files**. **performance:** a production `vite build` confirms **every bento route is its own lazy code-split chunk** (`React.lazy` per route) and the T570 deletion left no broken bundle (the only >1 MB chunks are the pre-existing design-system/federation vendor bundles, unrelated to Block AG). **responsive:** confirmed the shell's mobile posture is intact (nav rail `md`-only, header `⌘K` as the mobile entry point). Migration-completeness finding: persona **from-audio authoring** (T470) + **persona-suggest** (T471) were console-only and not carried into bento — tracked as **T619** (💭) to re-add or confirm the drop. `tsc -b --noEmit` + 98 vitest + production build green. T572 (delight polish) remains 💭. (§XXXI.10)
- **T570** — **Retire the legacy console (Phase 5)** — deleted **181** now-dead files: all 26 console `*.routes.tsx` route wrappers, 116 console page files (the `console.root`/`console.page` shell, every `*.root.page`/list/detail entry page, the `home`/`dashboard` pages), the console chrome components (`app-sidebar`, `app-footer`, `nav-main`, `nav-secondary`, `page.tsx`, `page-content.tsx`, `grid.list.tsx`, `use-grid-adapter`, `use-mobile`), the superseded console entity `*.form.tsx` files, and the console-only feature services/models/queries the bento migration replaced. The routes barrel (`app/routes/index.ts`) now exports only `BentoRoutes` + `VoiceKioskRoutes`. The dead set was computed by a static import-graph reachability sweep from `src/main.tsx` (production entry), so nothing bento or the surviving surfaces reach was removed — **~113 `console/**` files survive as the shared implementation bento reuses verbatim** (chat/chat-flow stack, dashboard widgets, LLM/persona/SN/system form sections, ai-analytics content pages, etc.); relocating them out of the `console/` folder for cosmetic purity was rejected as an over-refactor. Test files, shadcn `components/ui/*` primitives, shims, and ambient `.d.ts` were kept (unreachable-from-`main` but not dead). Migration-completeness note for the T571 audit: persona **from-audio authoring** (T470) and **persona-suggest** (T471) were console-only and are **not** present in the bento persona surface — their orphaned code was removed here; re-add to bento or confirm intentional drop during the audit. `tsc -b --noEmit` + 93 vitest green. (§XXXI.10)
- **T569** — **Cutover: Bento is the default interface (Phase 5 opens)** — flipped the app entry from the legacy console to bento per ADR 0002. The root `/` now redirects to `/bento/home` (was `/admin`), and the whole console context-path tree (`/admin/*`) is replaced by a single splat route mounting a new `ConsoleToBentoRedirect` (`app/routes/console-redirect.tsx`) that rewrites any retired console deep-link to its bento equivalent — a prefix swap for the surfaces that mirror 1:1, plus a small longest-prefix remap table for the ones that differ (`admin-settings/{users,groups,roles}` → `admin/*`, `admin-settings/tokens` → `token/instance`, `admin-settings/{settings,genai}` → `global-settings`, `admin-settings/{system-info,import,logging}` → their bento surfaces, `embedding-model` → `embedding`). `App.tsx` dropped the `ConsoleRootPage` shell + all ~28 `{XxxRoutes}` children (only `BentoRoutes` + the standalone chrome-free `VoiceKioskRoutes` survive outside bento); the "Preview" badge is gone from the bento header; the login default `returnUrl` points at `/bento/home` so there's no post-login redirect hop. The console page/route files stay on disk (dead) until T570 deletes them. `tsc -b --noEmit` + 93 vitest green. (§XXXI.10)
- **T576** — **SN instance sub-sections → bento (completes the SN migration)** — `app/bento/sn/`. Each reuses the existing console form/list components verbatim through a new **`baseRoute` prop** (default `ROUTES.SN_INSTANCE`; bento passes `ROUTES.BENTO_SN_INSTANCE`) so save/cancel/delete/row-edit navigation stays inside `/bento` — added to the custom-sort/search-rule/result-ranking/merge/spotlight/behavior/locale/custom-facet/faceted-field forms + the locale & facet draggable lists. The five GridList-style lists share a new `BentoSnSectionList` scaffold (BentoHero with a back-link eyebrow + "New" action over the reused `GridList`/`BlankSlate`); `top-terms` and the `custom-facet-item` editor reuse the console page via new `baseRoute`/`header` props (the `header` drops the sidebar-coupled `SubPageHeader`, mirroring T566). The SN instance config-sections panel now routes **every** section (incl. insights + top-terms) to its bento sub-route — no section links out to the console anymore. `tsc -b --noEmit` + 31 bento tests green. (§XXXI.6)
- **T568** — **Tenant settings + Tenant admin + User account → bento (Phase 4 complete)** — `app/bento/{user,tenant}/`. **User account** is a `BentoHero` + pill tab-bar (Profile · Preferences) over an `<Outlet />` mounting the reused console `UserProfilePage`/`UserPreferencesPage`; both gained an optional `header` prop (default = console `SubPageHeader`) so bento passes `header={null}` to drop the sidebar-coupled header (the tab bar already labels the section) — the `useSubPageBreadcrumb` call is harmless since `BreadcrumbProvider` wraps the whole app. **Tenant settings** (org switcher + self-service signup) and **Tenant admin** (all-tenants suspend/reactivate/impersonate) have no console chrome, so each is a `BentoHero` over the reused `TenantSettingsPage`/`PlatformTenantAdminPage` verbatim. With this, **Phase 4 is complete** — every console CRUD/functional/admin surface now exists in bento (Phase 5 = cutover/retirement). `tsc -b --noEmit` + 31 bento tests green. (§XXXI.9)
- **T567** — **Admin settings → bento (Phase 4 opens)** — `app/bento/admin/`. - **T563** — **Chat workspace + Conversation detail → bento (Phase 3 opens)** — `app/bento/{chat,conversation}/`. Both are app-like surfaces with their own internal chrome, so they "sit inside the shell" per §XXXI.8 rather than adopting the hero/save-bar CRUD. Chat reuses `ChatPage` verbatim (tab bar + model picker + session sidebar); it gained an optional `emptyStateHeader` prop so the "no LLM / no agent" blank slates render a `BentoHero` instead of the console `PageHeader` — keeping the reused surface clear of `SidebarTrigger` in the SidebarProvider-free bento shell. Conversation (T120 spectator/co-pilot three-pane live monitor) reuses `ConversationPage` verbatim inside a sized full-height wrapper. Route (`/bento/chat`, `/bento/conversation/:conversationId`) + `bentoRoute` on the `chat` nav item. `tsc -b --noEmit` clean. (§XXXI.8)
- **T562** — **Page CRUD → bento (Phase 2 complete)** — `app/bento/page/`. A "page" is a ZIP-deployed SPA (upload / open / delete, no edit form), so instead of the hero/save-bar entity scaffold this re-skins the console site grid onto a `BentoHero` (with an Upload action) + frosted tiles carrying the manifest meta (version/author/framework + SN-site badge), the `/pages/{name}` open link, a per-tile delete (controlled `DialogDelete`), and the upload dialog — reusing `TurPageService` verbatim (route-less, folder-less). Route (`/bento/page`) + `bentoRoute` on the `pages` nav item. With this, **all Phase-2 surfaces are migrated** (SN instance/fields/coverage/onboarding, skill editor, asset browser, Page). `tsc -b --noEmit` + 31 bento tests green. (§XXXI.5)
- **T561** — **Asset browser/manager → bento shell** — `app/bento/asset/`. The asset/file manager (MinIO storage) is an app-like surface (toolbar + breadcrumb + file table + resizable preview panel + RAG "train AI" flow), not a hero/save-bar CRUD, so it "sits inside the shell" per §XXXI.7. Extracted the ~290-line browser body from the console page into a shared `AssetBrowser` component (folder navigation is component state — a `prefix` — not URL routes, so it's route-shell agnostic); both the console page and the new bento page render it under their own header (`PageHeader` vs `BentoHero`). Route (`/bento/asset`) + `bentoRoute` on the `assets` nav item. `tsc -b --noEmit` + 31 bento tests green. (§XXXI.7)
- **T560** — **Skill folder editor (mini VS Code) → bento shell** — `app/bento/skill/`. - **T559** — **SN onboarding wizard → bento** — the guided multi-step setup (sample docs → LLM-derived schema review → NL→facet eval → go live, incl. the T386/T387 manifest plan/diff) is a self-contained stepper, so it is reused verbatim under `/bento/sn/onboarding` via a new optional `baseRoute` prop on `SNOnboardingWizardPage` (default `ROUTES.SN_INSTANCE`): the completion actions ("Configure site" / "Add fields") now land the user on the **bento** SN instance detail + field list instead of the console. - **T558** — **SN fields sub-CRUD + field-coverage → bento** — `app/bento/sn/` gains the nested field editor + coverage as **bento sub-routes** under `sn/instance/:id/` (`/field`, `/field/:fieldId`, `/field-coverage`), each framed by a `BentoHero`. The field list reuses the console `SNSiteFieldGridList` verbatim (its row edit link is relative, so it resolves inside the shell) + `SNFieldWizardDialog`; the field editor reuses `SNSiteFieldForm`, which gained an optional `baseRoute` prop (default `ROUTES.SN_INSTANCE`) so save/cancel navigate back to the bento field list instead of the console. - **T557** — **Semantic Navigation instance → bento (Phase 2 opens)** — `app/bento/sn/` list + instance detail + form, the heaviest console area. The bento form body holds the **config sections** — search-engine binding (`turSEInstance.id` required `Select`) + the storage-gated search-template `Select` — while identity (name/description/icon) lifts to the hero; the console's client-side name pattern/uniqueness check is enforced server-side instead (create/update surfaces a 409 as a toast). Routes (`/bento/sn/instance`) + `bentoRoute` on the `semanticNavigation` nav item so SN graduates onto the rail/⌘K. `tsc -b --noEmit` clean. (§XXXI.6)
- **T575** — **Bento list drag-reorder + emphasis UX (Enhancement)** — the customization UI on the T574 seam. `BentoListPage` gained an optional `listId` that turns any entity list customizable: a "Customize layout" affordance opens an edit mode where `@dnd-kit` drag reorders tiles (`PointerSensor` + `KeyboardSensor` for the T550 a11y baseline — focus a tile, Space to grab, arrows to move), a per-tile **S/M/L segmented picker** sets emphasis directly (one click to any size — decoupled from position), and `grid-flow-dense` packs the mosaic so SMALL tiles leave no holes. Emphasis replaces the binary `featured` flag on `BentoEntityTile` (three sizes + content density; the home/area mosaics keep the legacy `featured` boolean as a fallback). Save writes the user's protective override; admins also get **"Set as default for everyone"** (global template); customizers get **"Reset to default"**. The editable grid is **WYSIWYG** — the "New" tile stays in place (non-draggable) so edit and saved views share the exact same slots/packing. Pure `resolveBentoLayout` resolver (user entries → default), React-Query hooks (`useBentoLayout` + save/global/reset mutations) over `TurBentoLayoutService`, all 12 bento lists wired (`languageModel`/`aiAgent`/`searchEngine`/…), en+pt `bento.layout.*` i18n. 31 bento vitest green + `tsc -b --noEmit` clean. (§XXXI.11)
- **T574** — **Bento list layout persistence + cascade resolution (Enhancement)** — the persistence + resolver seam under the customizable mosaic. Today `BentoListPage` renders item `idx===0` as the 2×2 featured tile purely from array order; T574 makes **emphasis** (`SMALL|MEDIUM|LARGE`) and an explicit **displayOrder** persisted per list surface, decoupled from position. New `bento_layout` entity/repository (whole-layout JSON blob per `(tenantId, scope, ownerId, listId)`, `@TenantId`-isolated, unique index) + `TurBentoLayoutService` resolver implementing the **user override → admin global template → featured=idx0 default** cascade: a user override is protective (pins their layout) and clears via reset to re-inherit the template; an admin `GLOBAL` save (owner = `""` sentinel) is the template non-customizers follow. REST at `/api/v2/bento-layout/{listId}` — GET resolve (returns source USER/GLOBAL/DEFAULT + `canEditGlobal`), PUT user override, PUT `/global` (admin-`@Secured`), DELETE reset. Jackson 3 (`tools.jackson.*`) serialization; Liquibase `v2026.3.1.84`. 8 resolver-cascade unit tests green. T575 is the `@dnd-kit` reorder + emphasis UI on top. (§XXXI.11)
- **T556** — **Persona + Git → bento (Phase 1 complete)** — `app/bento/persona/` + `app/bento/git/`. - **T555** — **Routine + Chat Webhook → bento** — `app/bento/routine/` + `app/bento/webhook/`. Both entities use `name` (not `title`) and a **boolean** `enabled`, and have no icon — so each page aliases identity at the boundary (`toView`/`fromView`: title↔name, 0/1↔bool) and runs the shell with `hideIcon`. Routine keeps kind (Native/Groovy) + native-tool-name / Groovy `Textarea` + timeout; Chat Webhook keeps the method+URL, payload template + headers JSON, slot trigger + include-slots, and the write-only auth-header / signing-secret security section (blank-preserves-stored). - **T554** — **MCP Server + Custom Tool → bento** — `app/bento/mcp/` + `app/bento/custom-tool/`. Custom Tool keeps its full authoring surface **inside the bento form body**, reused verbatim: the LLM-description `PromptEditor`, the `useFieldArray` parameters + return-type editor, and the CodeMirror Groovy editor with validate / Vibe-Coding / T41 live-draft push-clear controls — only identity (title/description/icon/status) lifts to the hero. Relaxed the shared shell's entity constraint to tolerate `null` title/description (Custom Tool's nullable columns). `tsc -b --noEmit` + 25 bento tests green. (§XXXI.5)
- **T553** — **API Token + Integration → bento** — `app/bento/token/` + `app/bento/integration/`. Integration is a well-behaved BYO-infra shell (title+icon+numeric-`enabled`; endpoint + API-token `Select`; T372 GLOBAL read-only badge/notice). API Token has **no icon / status / tenant**, so it drove a new `hideIcon` option on the shared shell (static default-icon chip, never emits an `icon` patch); its hero owns title+description while the read-only generated secret + copy button stays in a form section (existing tokens only). Added `useTokenInstance` + `useIntegrationInstance` detail hooks, both routes, `bentoRoute` on the existing `integration` nav item, and a new `apiToken` management-section nav item (reusing the `apiToken.*` i18n). `tsc -b --noEmit` + 25 bento tests green. (§XXXI.5)
- **T552** — **Embedding Store + Embedding Model → bento** — two adjacent BYO-infra CRUDs on the T548 scaffold (`app/bento/store/` + `app/bento/embedding/`), both violet. Store is a straightforward `title`+numeric-`enabled` shell (vendor `Select`, endpoint/collection/credential, and the full per-vendor `providerOptionsJson` draft — Chroma/Milvus/PGVector fields + `initializeSchema` — ported verbatim with unknown-key preservation). Embedding Model uses **`modelName` as its identity** (no `title`), so the page aliases `title`↔`modelName` at the boundary (`toView`/`fromView`) and the shared shell is otherwise unchanged — the form keeps provider (LLM instance | `TRANSFORMERS_LOCAL`), model reference, local ONNX/tokenizer paths, and batch size. Added the missing `useStoreInstance` detail hook; both keep the T372 GLOBAL-is-read-only path (badge + notice + read-only fields). Routes + nav-config `bentoRoute` wired. `tsc -b --noEmit` green. (§XXXI.5)
- **T551** — **Search Engine (SE) → bento (Phase 1 opens)** — first BYO-infra surface on the T548 scaffold: `app/bento/se/` list + instance detail + form. List is a `BentoListPage` (emerald tone, vendor meta chip, GLOBAL badge for shared-pool rows); detail is a thin `BentoEntityShell` wrapper wiring the existing `useSeInstance`/update/delete React-Query hooks (added the missing `useSeInstance` detail hook), with the vendor `Select` + endpoint `Input` in a single `BentoFormSection` (title/description/icon lifted to the hero, per the pattern). Kept the **T372 GLOBAL-is-read-only** behavior and the Solr-property catalog lock (`seInstanceReadOnly`): both drive a new `readOnly` mode threaded through the shared shell — `BentoEntityShell` (suppresses inline-edit/icon-picker/status-toggle/save-bar/delete, renders a `badge` + `notice` slot), `BentoInlineEdit` (plain text), `BentoHeroIconPicker` (static chip), and `BentoListPage` (`hideNew`). Wired the route (`/bento/se/instance`) + filled the nav-config `bentoRoute` so SE graduates onto the rail/⌘K. Added read-only shell coverage; `tsc -b --noEmit` + bento tests green. (§XXXI.5)
- **T573** — **Nav rail → hub-and-spoke (fixes sidebar overflow before fan-out)** — T549's rail listed migrated leaf surfaces grouped by section; with only 3 migrated it fit, but as all ~22 surfaces graduate it would overflow the fixed viewport height with **no scroll fallback** — the same "sidebar estoura a tela, não aparece tudo" problem the flat console `AppSidebar` has in icon-collapsed mode. Restructured the rail to list **Home + the three section hubs** (Generative AI · Enterprise Search · Management) and nothing else, so it stays a fixed handful of icons no matter how many surfaces migrate. Each hub is a new shared `BentoAreaPage` (`app/bento/area/`) that renders every surface of its section as a bento mosaic (migrated → in-shell, unmigrated → console via `bentoNavTarget`); leaves are reached from the hub or jumped to directly via `⌘K`. Collapsed the two divergent taxonomies into one source of truth: `bento-nav.config.ts` now carries per-item `descriptionKey`/`tone`/`span` + per-section `icon`/`descriptionKey`/`areaRoute` + a `useVisibleBentoSections` grouping helper, and the home page reads it (retiring its own drifted `SECTIONS` array — home mosaics now match the hubs exactly, each section heading links to its hub via a new `BentoSection.titleHref`). `resolveBentoParent` is now config-driven (home → hub → list → detail). Rail also got a defensive `overflow-y-auto`. New/updated vitest: `useVisibleBentoSections` + section helpers, `BentoNavRail` (4 links only, hub routes, active-state). `tsc -b --noEmit` + 24 bento tests green. (§XXXI)
- **T550** — **Bento conventions before fan-out (Phase 0 complete)** — locked the rules so ~20 Phase 1–4 screens don't drift. Wrote `components/bento/CONVENTIONS.md` (thin-config structure, i18n key reuse + **object-form `t(key, {defaultValue})`** — the string form renders the raw key under the test mock, a11y baseline, responsive tile-span rules, test patterns). a11y fixes: extended the `prefers-reduced-motion` guard in `bento.styles.css` to the ambient blobs + save-bar entrance (previously unguarded), moved the palette's console-link indicator from a bare `<svg aria-label>` to an `aria-hidden` glyph + `sr-only` text (screen-reader-announced + queryable), and made ARIA values string-literals (`aria-expanded="true"`, `aria-selected` string) per jsx-a11y. Added vitest + RTL coverage for every shared component under `components/bento/__tests__/` (16 tests): `useVisibleBentoNav` privilege gating + `bentoNavTarget`, `BentoListPage`/`BentoEntityTile` (New tile, featured-first, empty hint, status pill), `BentoCommandPalette` (filter, no-match, Enter/click navigation, console-fallback indicator), and `BentoEntityShell` (new-mode Save, dirty-reveals-Save, status pill). `tsc` + all 16 tests green. (§XXXI.4)
- **T549** — **Global Bento navigation + `⌘K` command palette** — the console has a sidebar; bento home was launcher-only, so deep areas cost too many clicks. Added a single source of truth, `components/bento/bento-nav.config.ts` (mirrors the console `AppSidebar` taxonomy — primary / generative-AI / enterprise-search / management sections, with a `bentoRoute` filled in only for migrated surfaces + a `consoleRoute` fallback + privilege gating via `useVisibleBentoNav`), consumed by two new components: **`BentoNavRail`** (a slim fixed frosted icon rail on the left, desktop-only, listing the migrated surfaces grouped by section with tooltip labels + `aria-current`; `md:pl-16` on the shell reserves its gutter) and **`BentoCommandPalette`** (a keyboard-first `⌘K` launcher built on the Radix `Dialog` primitive — **zero new deps, no `cmdk`** — with a filter box + arrow-key/Enter-navigable result list; migrated areas navigate inside the bento shell, unmigrated ones link out to the console marked with an external-link glyph so `⌘K` reaches everything during the migration). Wired into `BentoRootPage`: a global `⌘K`/`Ctrl+K` handler (ignored while typing in a field), a header search/`⌘K` trigger button (the mobile entry point where the rail is hidden), plus a new `bento.*` i18n namespace (en/pt). As each phase migrates a surface, setting its `bentoRoute` graduates it onto the rail automatically. `tsc -b --noEmit` green. (§XXXI.3)
- **T548** — **Shared Bento CRUD scaffold (the lever)** — extracted the hand-rolled ~360-line LLM detail `Shell` + duplicated list pages into three reusable components under `components/bento/`: **`BentoEntityShell`** (generic over the entity — owns the identity hero with inline-edit title/description + icon picker + optional Active/Idle pill, the immediate-save-vs-staged-create modes, the scroll-linked `--bento-fade` hero→save-bar morph rAF loop, and the delete flow; the form body is a render-prop child that keeps its own fixed `BentoSaveBar` + `BentoFormSection` fields unchanged), **`BentoListPage`** (the `bento-grid` mosaic + dashed New tile + featured-first tiling + empty-state hint, parameterized by a `renderTile`), and **`BentoEntityTile`** (the common icon-chip + status-pill + title + meta content tile). Proved by re-skinning the three existing surfaces onto it: `bento.llm.instance.page.tsx` dropped from ~360 lines to a thin config, and both `bento.llm.instance.list.page.tsx` / `bento.ai-agent.list.page.tsx` collapsed to a single `BentoListPage` call each. Each remaining Phase 1–4 screen is now a thin config + its field groups, not a bespoke page. `tsc -b --noEmit` green; behavior preserved line-for-line. (§XXXI.2)
- **T547** — **Migration architecture & cutover strategy (ADR)** — wrote `docs/adr/0002-bento-ui-migration-cutover.md`, the gate that fixes scope for the whole block. Decides the one fork that blocks everything: **Bento becomes _the_ interface** (console retired), not a permanent parallel alternative. Picks the cutover mechanism — **route swap + declarative `<Navigate>` redirects, not a runtime feature flag** (a flag would force dual-tree maintenance for the whole migration; the additive `/bento` tree already gives a safe half-migrated state since nothing is removed until Phase 5). Defines the safe rollout (Phases 1–4 add bento routes, console stays live/reachable → T569 cutover + console→bento redirects + drop Preview badge → T570 delete migrated console pages). Fixes scope as a closed **surface inventory** (~20 remaining surfaces mapped to their console `*.routes.tsx` files and phases) so Block AG has a definition of done and doesn't balloon; new features land in bento directly, never on this backlog. Mirrored into IMPROVEMENTS §XXXI + agent memory. (§XXXI.1)

## Block AH — LLM instance model picker

- **T577** — **Model field is now a vendor-aware editable combobox** (was free text). New provider seam `TurGenAiLlmProvider.listModels(...)` (empty default) with **live** implementations for OpenAI / OpenAI-compatible / Gemini-OpenAI / Anthropic / Gemini / Ollama via a shared, resilient `TurLlmModelListingSupport` (plain `RestClient` per vendor — `/v1/models`, `x-api-key`+`anthropic-version`, `models.list`, `/api/tags` — never SDK-coupled, never throws). `TurLlmModelDiscoveryService` resolves the vendor, runs the live query with the typed-but-unsaved key (or the saved instance's decrypted key) and **falls back to the bundled `resources/llm/model-catalog.json`** (curated per plugin, loaded once by `TurLlmModelCatalog`) when live returns nothing — surfacing the source (`LIVE`/`CATALOG`/`NONE`) so the UI can badge it. Exposed at `POST /api/llm/models`. Frontend adds a dependency-free `ModelCombobox` (design-system Popover + filterable list, **stays editable** so any unlisted id can still be typed) fed by a React Query hook (`useLlmModels`, key never stores the secret; refetches with the latest key on open), wired into both the bento and console LLM instance forms. en/pt i18n added. Also fixes the runtime fallout of surfacing current models: Anthropic Opus 4.7/4.8, Sonnet 5, Fable 5 and the OpenAI o-series **reject `temperature`/`top_p`/`top_k` with a 400** ("`temperature` is deprecated for this model"), so a stored instance's sampling params would fail every call — `TurSamplingParamFallbackChatModel` now wraps the Anthropic + OpenAI chat/stream models and transparently retries once without sampling params on that rejection (runtime detection, no per-model capability table to rot). Backend `mvn compile` + frontend `tsc -b --noEmit` green.

## Block AK — Live Preview as a per-turn prompt-diagnostics surface (§XXXIV)
