- **T618** — **Capture the assembled prompt per turn for true (verbatim) replay.** T612 replays a conversation's *current* persisted state (leaf cursor + slots + resolved persona) through the shared assembler = what the *next* turn would send; T618 persists the exact assembled system message + the T617 whole-message list **at send time**, keyed by conversation id + turn index, so the Live Preview can load any *past* turn byte-for-byte (even after the flow advanced, slots were overwritten, or memory was compacted). New `com.viglet.turing.genai.capture` package: `TurPromptCaptureService` writes each turn to the pluggable `TurStorageService` (not the primary DB) under `tenants/{agentId}/{conversationId}/prompt-captures/turn-{n}.json` — captured at the single choke point of each executor (`TurChatStreamingDispatcher.dispatch` for the Spring-AI CALL/STREAM paths; `TurNativeChatExecutor` after the shared assembler + live-answers grounding, before the vendor branches). Opt-in per-agent (`TurAIAgent.promptCaptureEnabled`, Liquibase v2026.3.1.86, default off) + fail-open (a capture error never breaks the turn) + no-op when storage is disabled. Bounded like the T427 trace but on the storage seam (`TurPromptCaptureProperties`): a per-conversation turn ring (`maxTurnsPerConversation`, pruned on every write), a per-entry byte cap (`maxBytesPerEntry`, oversized captures skipped), and a scheduled retention sweep (`TurPromptCaptureCleanupJob`, `retentionDays`, ShedLock-guarded, tolerant `lastModified` parsing that never deletes an undated entry). The preview's replay endpoint gained an optional `turnIndex`: `TurSystemPromptPreviewService.buildReplayPreview(agent, conversationId, turnIndex)` loads the capture verbatim (single opaque `CAPTURED` segment + the whole message list) instead of reconstructing, and every replay advertises `availableTurns`; `TurSystemPromptReplayDto` grew `turnIndex`/`verbatim`/`capturedAt`/`availableTurns`. The Live Preview's replay bar grows a captured-turn picker ("Current state (reconstructed)" vs "Turn N · timestamp (verbatim)") and the banner labels a verbatim load. Per-agent toggle on the agent settings form; en/pt i18n; `TurPromptCaptureServiceTest` (8) + `tsc` green.
- **T612** — **Replay the assembled prompt from a real conversation.** T611 let the operator *simulate* a mid-conversation turn (pick a node, type collected values); T612 replays the **real** one. New `GET .../system-prompt/preview/replay?conversationId=…` → `TurSystemPromptPreviewService.buildReplayPreview` reads the conversation's persisted leaf state (its governing flow, cursor node and merged collected slots via `TurChatFlowEngineService`) and the persona the runtime actually resolved (`TurAgentPersonaResolver`, honouring a `__activePersonaId` flow override — authoritative over the static first-edge walk), then feeds them through the **same** shared `TurPromptAssemblyPipeline` the runtime uses — so `assembledText` is the ground-truth prompt for that conversation's next turn, not a synthesized entry turn. The conversation's T427 tool-call trace (`TurToolCallTraceService`) rides along in a new `replay` envelope (`TurSystemPromptReplayDto` + `TurSystemPromptToolCallDto`) so the operator reads "what the prompt said" against "what the model actually called". A blank/unknown id, or one whose flow isn't this agent's, degrades gracefully to a plain preview with `replay.resolved=false` (never silently renders a different agent's flow). The Live Preview grows a "Replay a real conversation" input, a provenance banner (conversation · flow → node · persona), and a tool-call trace section; the synthetic flow/node/vars pickers hide while replaying. Read-only/opt-in; assembled prompt unchanged. `TurSystemPromptPreviewServiceFlowPersonaTest` extended (13 green); `tsc` green.
- **T611** — **Node picker + simulated variables in the preview.** The preview only ever showed a flow's *entry* node with empty state — a per-*flow* view. Now `GET .../system-prompt/preview` takes optional `nodeId` + `vars` (a JSON object of collected slot values): `TurSystemPromptPreviewService` seeds the synthesized `TurChatFlowState` with the picked node id + those variables (invalid JSON → empty state), so the flow addendum's "Already collected: {…}" line and next-step hint reflect a real mid-conversation turn. Persona overrides upstream of the picked node are still captured (best-effort first-edge walk). The preview DTO exposes the flow's pickable nodes (`flowNodes`) + the rendered `selectedNodeId`; the Live Preview adds a node selector and an add/remove key-value "Simulated collected values" editor (remounted per flow). Makes it a per-*turn* inspector. en/pt i18n; preview-service tests + `tsc` green.
- **T610** — **Cross-segment redundancy & contradiction lint.** The existing conflict-check only looked <em>within</em> one fragment; this extends `TurSystemPromptValidatorService` to compare the assembled segments (persona / agent / MCP / active-flow addendum — read from the segments the preview already assembled, no re-parsing) against each other. **Redundancy** (`cross_segment_redundancy`, WARNING): a normalized rule-line repeated across ≥2 segments is flagged with the segments it duplicates across and the wasted token count (the "forbidden vocab in Persona + agent REGRAS DURAS" / "never ask CPF twice" waste, now a number). **Contradiction** (`cross_segment_contradiction`, ERROR): a directive reduced to its core (polarity markers + fillers stripped, EN/PT) that appears affirmatively in one segment and negated in another (e.g. agent "never answer general knowledge" vs a flow "always answer …") — opposite instructions on the same point. Deterministic + fast (runs on every page load); semantic contradictions beyond polarity stay the deep-LLM check's job. en/pt i18n; validator tests extended (18 green).
- **T609** — **"Inert this turn" detection.** When a chat flow governs a turn, its addendum orders the model to ignore the agent prompt's welcome / first-interaction / routing scaffolding — ~40–50% dead weight on an active-flow turn that the model still attends to. New `TurInertRegionDetector` scans the agent prompt by markdown heading section and flags the inert ones two ways: a **heuristic** (bilingual EN/PT welcome/greeting/first-interaction/menu/routing heading vocabulary, active only when a flow governs) and an **author marker** (`<!-- turing:concierge-only -->` = inert when a flow is active; `<!-- turing:flow-only -->` = inert on a no-flow turn) that takes precedence for precise attribution. Regions (label + char offsets + token cost + reason) ride on the AGENT `TurSystemPromptSegmentDto`; the Live Preview slices the segment at those offsets and renders the inert spans **dimmed** with an "inert this turn · ~N tok" tag so the dead weight is visible and quantified. Advisory/read-only — never rewrites the prompt. `TurInertRegionDetectorTest` (6) + `tsc` green.
- **T608** — **Per-segment token accounting + metrics in the preview.** The T613/T614 pipeline already emits a `chars/4` token estimate + STABLE/PER_TURN class per segment and the grand `totalTokens`/`stableTokens`/`cacheBreakpointIndex` on the preview DTO, but nothing surfaced them. The Live Preview now shows a **metrics strip** — grand total tokens, the **cacheable-prefix vs per-turn split** (what T614's STABLE-first ordering can reuse across turns), and per-segment **token count + share-of-total** in each passage's provenance label — reading the pipeline's numbers directly (no re-parsing of the assembled string). Added an **optional per-turn cost estimate**: `TurSystemPromptPreviewService` resolves the agent's first enabled LLM instance and prices the assembled system prompt's input tokens via `TurLLMPriceService` (0.0 for unpriced/local models, so the "$0 local" story stays honest); new `estimatedCostUsd` + `costModelName` on the DTO, rendered as `≈ $x/turn (model)` when priced. TS model + component + en/pt i18n updated; `tsc -b --noEmit` + backend tests green.
- **T607** — **Flow-aware persona in the preview + validator.** The preview and `TurSystemPromptValidatorService` both hardcoded `agent.getDefaultPersona()`, so a `persona`-type flow node that switches the active voice at runtime (via `__activePersonaId` → `TurAgentPersonaResolver`) was invisible — the preview showed the wrong persona and the validator missed the misconfiguration. `TurSystemPromptPreviewService` now walks the selected flow from its start node through the transparent nodes to the entry anchor (bounded, loop-guarded), records any `persona`-node override on that path, and reconciles it against the agent's catalog exactly as the resolver does (honour only an in-catalog, speaker-usable persona; otherwise silently fall back to the default). The resolved persona feeds the assembly context, and the PERSONA segment is labelled *"switched by the selected flow's persona node"* (or warns when the node points at a persona missing from the catalog). New `diagnoseFlowPersonas(agent)` walks every flow and the validator turns each unhonourable override into a `flow_persona_not_in_catalog` / `flow_persona_audience_only` WARNING (source `FLOW`, en/pt i18n). Read-only/opt-in; assembled prompt unchanged. Covered by `TurSystemPromptPreviewServiceFlowPersonaTest` + validator tests (19 green).

## Block AL — Prompt assembly as a composable, cache-friendly pipeline (§XXXV)

- **T613** — **`TurPromptContributor` SPI + single-pass assembler (single source of truth).** The turn's system message was built by string concatenation spread across `TurChatPromptAssembler` + `TurPersonaPromptComposer`, while the Live Preview *rebuilt a lookalike* separately — the root cause behind every Block AK symptom (the persona lie, the stray `===`, drift as the assembler grows). New `com.viglet.turing.genai.prompt` package: `TurPromptContributor` interface emitting `TurPromptSegment {origin, title, text, stability, tokens}`, a `TurPromptAssemblyContext`, and `TurPromptAssemblyPipeline` that runs the ordered contributor list **once** and joins the segments. Six built-in contributors (persona-head, agent-base, MCP, capability, skill, flow) each own their block at its source (the `render.md`/`answer-as-app.md`/… resources moved into `TurCapabilityPromptContributor`; the persona few-shot text is shared via a new `TurPersonaPromptComposer.fewShotBlock`). `TurChatPromptAssembler` now builds the system prompt through the pipeline by default (`turing.prompt.assembly.pipeline-enabled=true`), and `TurSystemPromptPreviewService` renders the **same** segments the runtime assembles — killing the reconstruction path. The legacy layout is reproduced **byte-for-byte** (a persona head that wraps the body with the `\n\n----\n` separator), proven by `TurPromptAssemblyPipelineTest`; `pipeline-enabled=false` restores the pre-pipeline path as an escape hatch. Per-segment token estimates + STABLE/PER_TURN class now flow to the preview DTO for free (`tokens`, `stability`, `totalTokens`, `stableTokens`). Backend `mvn test` green.
- **T614** — **Stability-classed ordering + provider cache breakpoint** ⭐. Confirmed cache-hostile ordering: the per-turn few-shot block sat *between* the stable persona and the stable agent-base/MCP/tool guidance, so a provider prefix cache could only cover up to the persona — the heavy stable prefix was re-billed every turn. Each `TurPromptSegment` now carries a `TurPromptStability`; opt-in `turing.prompt.assembly.stable-first-ordering=true` reorders every STABLE segment (persona-static, agent-base, MCP, capability, skill) contiguously before the PER_TURN tail (few-shot, flow addendum) and exposes `cacheBreakpointIndex` — the segment index at which the uncacheable tail begins — so the native cache providers (T499/T502) can set a prefix breakpoint there. Off by default (reordering changes what the model sees); the byte-identical legacy order stays the default and reports `cacheBreakpointIndex = -1`. Ordering + breakpoint covered by `TurPromptAssemblyPipelineTest`.
- **T615** — **Retired the pre-pipeline concatenation + its escape hatch.** After T613/T614 soaked, deleted the historical inline system-prompt path: `TurChatPromptAssembler` lost `buildSystemPromptLegacy`, `augmentWithCapabilityPrompts`, the five capability-prompt `@Value` resources + their `@PostConstruct` loader (all now owned by `TurCapabilityPromptContributor`), the `TurChatFlowEngineService`/`TurPersonaPromptComposer`/`TurMcpInstructionsProvider`/`TurSkillRunnerService` collaborators (now the flow/persona/MCP/skill contributors), and the `turing.prompt.assembly.pipeline-enabled` flag itself. `TurPromptAssemblyPipeline` is now the **single** system-prompt assembly path — the preview mirror can no longer drift from a second builder. Assembled output is unchanged (still the byte-identical legacy layout by default), so no runtime re-validation was needed; the pipeline byte-for-byte test is untouched and green. `TurPersonaPromptComposer` stays (still used by `TurMcpPromptConfig` and, via `fewShotBlock`, by the persona contributor). The remaining tail — bringing chat-memory compression (T115) and the RAG/relevance history fan-out behind the SPI — is now tracked as **T616**.
- **T617** — **Retired the message-pipeline escape hatch + mirrored the full message list in the preview.** After T616 soaked, deleted the inline history-prefix path: `TurChatPromptAssembler` lost `buildMemoryPrefixLegacy` and the `turing.prompt.assembly.message-pipeline-enabled` flag (and its now-unused `TurChatMemoryRelevanceRetriever` / `TurChatMemoryCompressionService` collaborators — those live in the two message contributors), so `TurMessageAssemblyPipeline` is the **single** history-prefix assembly path. Assembled message list unchanged (the default-on pipeline was already byte-identical), so no runtime re-validation. Preview fidelity: the Live Preview mirrored only the *system* message; it now also renders the **whole message list** — a new `TurSystemPromptMessageDto` (role/origin/label/content/tokens/runtimeOnly/note) on the preview DTO, built by running the *same* `TurMessageAssemblyPipeline` (no drift). A config-level preview has no live conversation, so the T115 summary + T30 relevance sources surface as informational `runtimeOnly` placeholders (the same treatment few-shot/RAG segments get), themed by origin; the Live Preview grows a "Message list" section showing the system message, the history-prefix turns, then the recent window. `TurSystemPromptPreviewServiceFlowPersonaTest` extended; the assembler test dropped its now-moot pipeline-vs-legacy byte-identity cases; en/pt i18n + `tsc` green.
- **T616** — **Message-level SPI: memory summary + relevance turns behind one pipeline.** T613/T615 unified the *system* message; the turn's *history prefix* (the T115 compression summary + the T30 BM25 relevance-retrieved older turns) was still concatenated inline in `TurChatPromptAssembler.assemble`. New message-level sibling of the prompt SPI in `com.viglet.turing.genai.prompt`: `TurMessageContributor` (emits `TurPromptMessage {role, content, origin, label, tokens}` from a narrow `TurMessageAssemblyContext`), run once by `TurMessageAssemblyPipeline`. Two built-in contributors — `TurMemorySummaryMessageContributor` (order 100, wraps `summaryBlock`) and `TurRelevanceMessageContributor` (order 200, wraps the new `TurChatMemoryRelevanceRetriever.retrievePrefix`) — reproduce the exact `[summary] + [retrieved]` layering; the assembler prepends the pipeline's output to the client history and fans the whole list out through its single `user`/`assistant` conversion, so the entire message list now has one assembly point. `retrievePrefix` was extracted from `enrich` (which now delegates `retrievePrefix + history`, contract + tests unchanged). Default-on behind the `turing.prompt.assembly.message-pipeline-enabled=true` escape hatch (inline `buildMemoryPrefixLegacy` restores the pre-T616 path); the two paths are proven **byte-identical** by `TurChatPromptAssemblerMemoryPipelineTest`, ordering by `TurMessageAssemblyPipelineTest`. Preview-mirroring of the prefix + retiring the flag after soak tracked as **T617**.

## Block AJ — Evaluation platform: pluggable graders + datasets (§XXXIII)

- **T603** — **Continuous / online eval (eval in prod, not just pre-publish) — Block AJ COMPLETE.** New `TurOnlineEvalService` (`com.viglet.turing.genai.eval.online`) samples an agent's recent **live** sessions from the T87 chat-analytics store (the same index the T447 miner + T596 importer read) and grades them in the background with the **same pluggable CODE+MODEL grader stack** the pre-publish runner uses — but with **no replay**: it rebuilds a `TurEvalGradingContext` from each conversation's already-produced turns (T61 chat memory), captured slots + final node (T66 submission), session outcome, and T427 tool trace, then runs `TurEvalGraderRegistry.resolveStack` + `TurEvalStackAggregator` exactly as `scoreCase` does. Expectation-based graders (slot/outcome/node) naturally skip un-annotated live traffic, so the useful online stack is content/quality graders (rubric, model-judge, contains/regex, tool-called). Each sweep records a `TurOnlineEvalSnapshot` (sampled/graded counts, grader mean-score + pass-rate, and three **composed** signals — negative-sentiment rate (T87), stale-citation rate (T155, via `TurCitationDriftService`, only when drift detection is on), failing-session rate (T447, `TurSelfTuningMiner.findFailingSessions` reused verbatim)). The first snapshot per agent is the healthy **baseline**; later windows flag `driftDetected` + a human-readable reason when the mean score / pass-rate drop a configurable margin below baseline **or** any absolute signal crosses its threshold (drift below `minSamplesForDrift` is never flagged; "Set baseline" re-baselines after a fix). Opt-in **twice** (global `turing.genai.online-eval.enabled` + per-agent `onlineEvalEnabled`) and fail-open (disabled store / missing LLM / any error ⇒ a non-persisted "unavailable" snapshot, never an exception on the schedule). One additive public helper `TurAgentEvalRunnerService.resolveJudgeModel(agent)` centralises MODEL-grader model resolution. Nightly cluster-once `TurOnlineEvalJob` (`@SchedulerLock`, gated); `TurAgentOnlineEvalAPI` (`/api/ai-agent/{id}/eval/online` — `GET /history`, `GET /latest`, `POST /run`, `POST /baseline/{snapshotId}`, role-gated). Liquibase v2026.3.1.94 (`ai_agent.online_eval_enabled` + `online_eval_grader_stack_id`, `tur_online_eval_snapshot`). UI: Eval Studio (`/bento/eval`) gains an **Online** tab (`BentoEvalOnlinePanel`) — agent picker, quality/drift timeline (recharts), snapshot cards with drift/baseline badges + Set-baseline + the six metric tiles — and the agent settings form gains a Continuous-eval toggle + grader-stack field. `TurOnlineEvalServiceTest` (10: fail-open/opt-in, signal rates, no-replay grading, drift vs baseline, min-samples guard, re-baseline) + `bento.eval.page.test.tsx` (→14, adds the Online tab empty-state + drift-snapshot cases); backend compile + Liquibase-agnostic suite green, frontend `tsc -b --noEmit` + vitest green. Detection only — complementary to (not a duplicate of) the T447 self-tuning loop.
- **T594** — **Calibration & inter-annotator agreement (the model grader made trustworthy).** Two additions to the T592/T593 human-review layer, both opt-in and LLM-free. **(1) N reviewers + agreement:** a review task now collects individual `TurEvalReviewVerdict` rows; `TurEvalReviewTaskService.submitReview` appends a verdict (a reviewer may vote once per task — a repeat 409s) and only computes the **consensus** (strict-majority pass, mean score) + merges it back once `requiredReviewers` verdicts are in (default 1 = the legacy single-reviewer path, byte-identical). `POST .../review/{taskId}/reviewers?count=` bumps the required count on an open task; `GET .../review/agreement` returns **Fleiss' kappa** + observed agreement + the Landis-&-Koch band over every multi-reviewer task. **(2) MODEL-grade calibration audit:** `POST .../review/audit?sampleSize=&requiredReviewers=` deterministically stride-samples MODEL-graded cases from the agent's latest report into `AUDIT` review tasks (the model's original pass/score stashed on the task, an evidence snapshot as the transcript); once humans re-judge them, `GET .../review/calibration` returns **Cohen's kappa** between the LLM judge and the human consensus, the 2×2 confusion (over-pass / over-fail counts), and a plain-language judge-prompt tuning hint (never auto-applied). New pure `TurEvalAgreement` helper (Fleiss / Cohen / interpret), `TurEvalReviewVerdict` entity + repo, `TurEvalAgreementDto` / `TurEvalCalibrationDto` / `TurEvalReviewVerdictDto`, and four task columns (`taskType`, `requiredReviewers`, `modelPass`, `modelScore`) via Liquibase v2026.3.1.92. UI: the Eval Studio review inbox gains a **Calibration & agreement** header panel (both kappas + the tuning hint + an "Audit MODEL-graded run" control), N-reviewer progress badges, an `audit` tag, and the model's verdict on audit tasks. New `useEvalAgreement` / `useEvalCalibration` / `useSampleAudit` / `useSetRequiredReviewers` hooks. `TurEvalAgreementTest` (7) + `TurEvalReviewTaskServiceTest` (→12) + `bento.eval.page.test.tsx` (calibration panel) green; backend compile, frontend `tsc -b --noEmit`, and vitest green.
- **T599** — **Eval Studio bento page.** New `/bento/eval` (`BentoEvalPage`) is the bento home for the evaluation platform: a `BentoHero` + frosted pill tab-bar over five tabs — **Datasets** (name / row count / version, from `GET /api/eval/dataset`), **Grader stacks** (name / grader count, from `GET /api/eval/grader-stack`), the LLM-assisted **Generate** tab (T597), the per-agent **Runs** tab, and the human-review **inbox** (T593) — each a frosted `bento-tile`/`bento-glass` surface with empty states. The **Runs** tab (`BentoEvalRunHistory`) picks an agent and surfaces its **run history**: a new backend `GET /api/ai-agent/{agentId}/eval/history` (`TurAgentEvalGateService.history` maps every persisted `TurAgentEvalReport` newest-first, parsing the per-case breakdown) feeds a **score/regression timeline** (recharts `LineChart`, oldest→newest), an append-only **run list** (score, cases passed, `baseline`/`regressed`/`pending review` badges), a one-click **Run gate now** (`POST /eval/run`, refreshes the timeline), and a **per-case drill-down** (pass/fail, actual-vs-expected outcome, failing slot diffs, rubric rationale). New `TurEvalStudioService.listRunHistory`/`runEval` + `useEvalRunHistory`/`useRunEval` hooks + `queryKeys.evalStudio.runHistory`; `pendingReview` added to the `TurAgentEvalReport` TS model. Registered in `bento.routes.tsx` (`ROUTES.BENTO_EVAL`) + the bento nav (`bento-nav.config.ts`, `generativeAi`, `home.features.evalStudio` en/pt). The flow-editor `ChatFlowEvalGatePanel` links here. `bento.eval.page.test.tsx` extended to 10 (adds the Runs tab empty-state + drill-down/slot-diff cases); `TurAgentEvalGateServiceTest` to 11 (adds `history` newest-first + empty); backend compile, frontend `tsc -b --noEmit`, and vitest green.
- **T601** — **NL→facet eval unified onto the grader + dataset model, folded into the one Studio.** Two halves. **(1) Scorer-as-grader:** `TurNLFacetGrader` (CODE, `nl-facet`) brings Block R's `sn.dsl.eval` onto the shared grader model — it deserializes the Block R `expectation` + `schema` shapes from its `configJson`, parses the natural-language `query` over the schema via the `TurNLFacetParser` SPI, scores the structured result with the pure `TurNLFacetEvalScorer`, and maps the `CaseResult` (score / passed / findings + ungrounded) to a `TurEvalGraderResult`; applies only when a parser is available, fails closed. **(2) Pack-as-dataset + one Studio:** new `TurNLFacetDatasetService` migrates a `TurNLFacetEvalPack` into a reusable, versioned `TurEvalDataset` — the pack-level `index`/`locale`/`schema` land once on the new `TurEvalDataset.metadataJson` (kind `nl-facet`, Liquibase v2026.3.1.93), one case per `TurEvalDatasetRow` (prose query → `seedTurnsJson`, query + golden expectation → row `metadataJson`); `toPack` round-trips losslessly back into a pack, which the intact Block R `TurNLFacetEvalService` runs. `TurSNNLFacetEvalAPI` gains `POST/GET /dataset`, `GET /dataset/{id}`, `POST /dataset/{id}/run`, `DELETE /dataset/{id}` (role-gated). The Eval Studio (`/bento/eval`, T599) gains an **NL→Facet** tab (`BentoEvalNLFacetPanel`): import a pack as a dataset, list the saved NL→facet datasets alongside the chat-flow ones, and run one with a per-case pass/fail + ungrounded-field report. The Block R scorer / pack `POST /api/sn/nl-facet-eval` path are untouched (additive bridge). `TurNLFacetGraderTest` (6) + new `TurNLFacetDatasetServiceTest` (6, round-trip / list filter / run delegation / non-NL→facet rejection); `bento.eval.page.test.tsx` (→14, adds the NL→Facet tab empty-state + run-report cases); backend + Liquibase-agnostic suites green, frontend `tsc -b --noEmit` + vitest green.
- **T602** — **Public eval API + SDK/CLI (a reusable CI gate over any dataset).** New `POST /api/eval/run` (`TurEvalRunAPI`, role-gated `AI_AGENT_EDIT`) runs a named `dataset × grader stack` against an agent runtime and returns a `TurEvalRunResultDto` with a single CI verdict — `gatePassed = every-case-passes AND (minScore == null || score ≥ minScore)`. Both `dataset` and `graderStack` accept an **id or a human name** (resolved id-first via new `findFirstByNameOrderByCreatedAtDesc` / `findFirstByName` repo methods, then by name); a blank stack runs the legacy default stack. Backend: `TurAgentEvalRunnerService.runDataset(agentId, datasetId, graderStackId)` builds a transient, non-persisted `TurAgentEvalSet` carrying only the `datasetId` + `graderStackId` bindings and reuses the exact per-case replay + grader-stack scoring of `runAgent`; it replays with `persistSideEffects=false` (an ad-hoc run never parks human-review tasks — a deferring HUMAN grader just marks its case `pendingReview`, failing the gate) and persists the report via a new `persistDatasetRunReport` that — unlike the golden path — **never** becomes/demotes the agent's green baseline and is never flagged `regressed`, so an arbitrary dataset run can't corrupt the pre-publish gate baseline. The report still lands in the agent's history/Eval Studio timeline, tagged with the dataset id + version. CLI: `turing eval --dataset <id|name> [--stack <id|name>] [--min-score <n>]` (new remote mode in `@viglet/turing-cli`, `eval/remote.ts`) prints a per-case summary and exits 1 when the gate fails; the same logic ships as the programmatic SDK `runEval(client, opts)` + `formatRunResult`, re-exported from the package root. Extends the Agent-CI gate of T428/T430 to any reusable T595 dataset. `TurEvalRunAPITest` (11: id/name resolution, default stack, minScore pass/fail, red run, 400/404/422) + CLI `eval-remote.test.ts` (5: payload shape, defaults, validation, report formatting) green; backend compile + full CLI build/test green.
- **T600** — **Weighted grader stacks + reusable stack templates.** New pure `TurEvalStackAggregator` replaces the hardcoded mean + all-must-pass with a **weighted** aggregate (weight ≤ 0 → 1), a per-grader **pass threshold** (a grader passes only if its result passed AND its score ≥ its threshold), and a **blocking policy** (the case passes when every *blocking* grader passed; non-blocking graders count toward the score but never fail the case) — consuming the `weight`/`threshold`/`blocking` fields persisted since T587. With the default stack (weight 1, threshold 0, all blocking) this is exactly the legacy mean + all-must-pass — **byte-identical**. New named, agent-decoupled `TurEvalGraderStack` (reuses `TurEvalGraderConfig` via a nullable `stack_id`) is shareable across agents; a set binds one via `TurAgentEvalSet.graderStackId` and the registry then resolves the stack's configs instead of the set's own (`TurEvalGraderRegistry` gained the stack repo + `resolveFromConfigs`). `TurEvalGraderStackService` + `TurEvalGraderStackAPI` (`/api/eval/grader-stack`, role-gated) create-with-entries / list / get / delete; `TurEvalGraderStackDto`. Liquibase v2026.3.1.91 (`tur_eval_grader_stack` + `stack_id` on the config + `graderStackId` on the set). `TurEvalStackAggregatorTest` (6) + `TurEvalGraderStackResolutionTest` (1) green.
- **T597** — **LLM-assisted dataset generation & augmentation (draft-only, human-reviewed).** New `TurEvalDatasetGenerationService` (`com.viglet.turing.genai.eval`) reuses the T387 manifest-derivation posture (ground the model on real artifacts, parse conservatively, fail-open, **never auto-trust**). Two operations, neither persists anything: `generateFromAgent(agentId, count)` grounds the model on the agent's title/description, **system prompt**, declared **slots** (`TurAIAgentSlotRepository`), and chat-flow **goals** (node id/type/`aiInstruction`|label, bounded to 40 nodes) and asks it to synthesize scripted user-turn cases + expected outcome/node/slots + a rubric; `augmentDataset(datasetId, variantsPerRow)` paraphrases each existing row's seed turns into fresh variants that carry over the original expectations (outcome/node/slots/reference/rubric). Both return **draft** `TurEvalDatasetRowDto`s (id `null`, tagged `generated`/`augmented`, JSON provenance metadata) — empty-turns rows dropped, unknown outcomes normalized to `ANY`, verbatim-echoed paraphrases skipped. Fail-open: no usable LLM (agent's enabled instance or global default) or a garbled/unparseable reply → empty list. The only write path is `saveReviewed(name, datasetId, rows)`: it maps the reviewed DTOs to canonical nodes and routes through the shared persistence — a new `TurEvalDatasetImportService.appendCanonicalRows(datasetId, …)` (append after existing rows + bump `updatedAt`) when a target is given, else `importCanonicalRows(name, …)` — so a generated row is byte-identical in shape to an uploaded / mined / promoted one. `TurEvalDatasetAPI` gains `POST /generate?agentId=&count=`, `POST /{datasetId}/augment?variants=`, and `POST /save-reviewed?name=&datasetId=` (role-gated `AI_AGENT_EDIT`). UI: the Eval Studio (`/bento/eval`) gains a **Generate** tab (`BentoEvalGeneratePanel`) — a generate-from-agent card + augment-a-dataset card feed an editable **review form** (per-row name / turns / expected outcome / node / rubric, discard + remove), then a save-to picker (new dataset name **or** append to an existing one). New `useGenerateRows`/`useAugmentDataset`/`useSaveReviewedRows` hooks + `TurEvalStudioService` methods + `TurEvalDatasetRowDraft` TS type. `TurEvalDatasetGenerationServiceTest` (7: grounded parse, drop-empty+clamp, outcome fallback, two fail-open paths, augment carry-over, save-new) + `TurEvalDatasetImportServiceTest`/sibling suites green; `bento.eval.page.test.tsx` → 8 (Generate tab + draft-review-and-save); backend compile, frontend `tsc -b --noEmit`, and vitest green. **Phase 4 (datasets) COMPLETE.**
- **T598** — **Dataset versioning + drift.** New immutable `TurEvalDatasetSnapshot` (dataset id + version + `rowsJson` = frozen `List<TurEvalDatasetRowDto>`); `TurEvalDatasetVersionService.snapshot(id)` freezes the current rows at the dataset's version then bumps the live version by one, and `diff(id, from, to)` compares two snapshots' rows by id into added / removed / changed (`TurEvalDatasetDiffDto`). Every eval report now **pins the dataset + version it scored against** (`TurAgentEvalReport.datasetId`/`datasetVersion`, 0 for inline-case runs; surfaced on the report DTO + gate) so a regression comparison knows whether the dataset itself drifted between runs. `TurEvalDatasetAPI` gains `POST /{id}/snapshot`, `GET /{id}/versions`, `GET /{id}/diff?from=&to=`. Liquibase v2026.3.1.90 (`tur_eval_dataset_snapshot` + `datasetId`/`datasetVersion` on the report). Report DTO extended via back-compat secondary constructors. `TurEvalDatasetVersionServiceTest` (2) covers snapshot+bump and the three-way diff.
- **T596** — **Dataset import/export (file + history sources).** *File import/export/CRUD:* `TurEvalDatasetImportService` imports a dataset from an uploaded **JSON array**, **JSONL**, **CSV**, or **OpenAI-Evals JSONL** file: JSON/CSV rows are remapped through an optional **column mapping** (source key → canonical `turns`/`expectedSlots`/`expectedOutcome`/`expectedNodeId`/`rubric`/`referenceAnswer`/`tags`/`metadata`); a scalar `turns` becomes a single-turn array; OpenAI-Evals `input` user messages become turns and `ideal` becomes the golden reference. CSV is parsed by a small in-house RFC-4180-ish parser (quoted fields, embedded commas, escaped quotes — no new dependency). Exports a dataset as JSONL (canonical row per line). New `TurEvalDatasetAPI` under `/api/eval/dataset` (role-gated `AI_AGENT_VIEW`/`AI_AGENT_EDIT`): `GET` list (summaries), `GET /{id}` (with rows), `DELETE /{id}`, `POST /import` (multipart), `GET /{id}/export`. *History sources (this round):* new `TurEvalDatasetSourceImportService` builds a dataset from the platform's own history — `POST /import/transcripts?agentId=&limit=` turns an agent's recent conversations (T61 chat-memory turns + the T66 terminal submission) into candidate golden rows (user turns → `turns`, last assistant reply → golden `referenceAnswer`, submission `endNodeId`/`__abandoned__` → `expectedOutcome`+`expectedNodeId`, captured slots → `expectedSlots`, tagged `transcript`); `POST /import/failures?agentId=&limit=` reuses the **T447** self-tuning miner's failing-session set (new `TurSelfTuningMiner.findFailingSessions` refactored out of `mine`, same 7-day/200-session window + failure heuristic) as negative-example rows tagged `mined-failure` (no golden answer — the observed bad outcome/sentiment stashed in metadata, `expectedOutcome` stays `ANY` until a human curates). Both compose onto the file importer's `importCanonicalRows` so a mined dataset is byte-identical in shape to an uploaded one; fail-open to a `400 "No rows"` when the chat-memory/analytics stores are disabled (the NoOp default). `TurEvalDatasetImportServiceTest` (6) + new `TurEvalDatasetSourceImportServiceTest` (6, golden/abandoned/failure/skip-empty/empty-source/blank-agent).
- **T595** — **Reusable, agent-decoupled eval datasets.** New `TurEvalDataset` (versioned, agent-decoupled) + `TurEvalDatasetRow` (scripted turns + expected slots/outcome/node + golden `referenceAnswer` + `tags`/`metadataJson`) with repositories and Liquibase v2026.3.1.89. `TurAgentEvalSet` gained an optional `datasetId`: when bound, the runner's new `resolveCases` sources the set's cases from the dataset's rows (fetched via `TurEvalDatasetRowRepository`, never a lazy collection — the replay is non-transactional) mapped to transient `TurAgentEvalCase`s; unbound sets use the inline cases exactly as before (byte-identical). The bound row's `referenceAnswer` now threads through `runCase`/`scoreCase` into `TurEvalGradingContext` (new `referenceAnswer`, back-compat secondary constructor), and both reference graders (`embedding-similarity`, `model-judge`) fall back to it when their config carries no `reference` — completing the T591 dataset bridge. Grader fallback tests added to `TurEmbeddingSimilarityGraderTest`/`TurModelJudgeTest`; eval + Liquibase suites green. Back-compat needs no data migration; dataset CRUD/import + the inline-as-dataset view land with T596/T599.
- **T593** — **Human review inbox (bento UI + merge-back + promote-to-golden-row).** Backend `TurAgentEvalReviewAPI` under `/api/ai-agent/{agentId}/eval/review` (role-gated `AI_AGENT_VIEW`/`AI_AGENT_EDIT`): `GET` the open (PENDING) tasks, `GET /{taskId}` one task (transcript + expected summary + verdict), `POST /{taskId}` submit `{pass, score, notes}` (`TurEvalReviewTaskService.submitReview` stamps `reviewedBy`/`reviewedAt`, flips to `REVIEWED` — a second submit 409s — and **merges the verdict back** into the matching case row of the agent's latest report, recomputing `passedCount`/`pendingReview`/`passed` so a fully-reviewed run turns green or stays red per the human call), and `POST /{taskId}/promote?datasetId=` closes the loop via `promoteToDataset`: it appends a golden `TurEvalDatasetRow` to a chosen T595 dataset (transcript user-turns → `seedTurnsJson`, last assistant reply → golden `referenceAnswer`, expected outcome/node lifted from the parked summary, `promoted` tag + JSON provenance metadata), bumps the dataset `updatedAt`, and returns the refreshed summary. UI: the **Review inbox** tab folds into the T599 Eval Studio (`/bento/eval`) — `BentoEvalReviewInbox` picks an agent (`useAiAgents`), lists its pending tasks, shows the replayed transcript + expected panel, submits the verdict form, then offers a dataset picker + **Promote** button. New `useEvalReviewInbox`/`useSubmitReview`/`usePromoteReview` hooks + service methods + `TurEvalReviewTask`/`TurEvalReviewRequest` TS models; `queryKeys.evalStudio.reviewInbox`. `TurEvalReviewTaskServiceTest` extended to 5 (adds promote-to-dataset); `bento.eval.page.test.tsx` to 5 (adds the review tab + task-list cases); backend compile, frontend `tsc -b --noEmit`, and vitest green.
- **T592** — **Human-in-the-loop review grader (the third grader kind).** New HUMAN `grader "human-review"` (`TurHumanReviewGrader`) can't decide automatically, so it **defers** (`TurEvalGraderResult.deferred`). The runner reacts: the case is neither pass nor fail (`pendingReview`), the report is amber (new `pendingReview` on `TurAgentEvalReport` + both DTOs, Liquibase v2026.3.1.88, never the green baseline), and a `TurEvalReviewTask` is parked (PENDING) via new `TurEvalReviewTaskService` — a transcript + expected-summary snapshot, **idempotent** per (agent, case, grader) while still PENDING, and created **only on a real run** (a `persistSideEffects` flag threaded through `runCase`/`scoreCase` keeps the T447 trial-scoring path side-effect-free). `tur_eval_review_task` (FK ON DELETE CASCADE) carries nullable reviewer-verdict columns (`reviewedPass`/`reviewedScore`/`reviewerNotes`/`reviewedBy`/`reviewedAt`) for the T593 inbox. `TurAgentEvalGateService` gains a `PENDING_REVIEW` status — a WARNING by default, an ERROR that blocks publish when the set is `blocking` (block-until-reviewed) — unless the run also regressed (hard ERROR wins). DTOs extended via back-compat secondary constructors (existing callers/tests untouched). `TurEvalReviewTaskServiceTest` (2) + gate `PENDING_REVIEW` cases (2) green.
- **T591** — **Reference-answer grading (embedding cosine + MODEL faithfulness).** New CODE `grader "embedding-similarity"` (`TurEmbeddingSimilarityGrader`) embeds the final answer and a golden `reference` through the platform's configured embedding provider (via `TurRagContextBuilder.buildFromGlobalSettings()` — key/model resolution handled for it) and scores their cosine similarity, passing at/above `minSimilarity` (default 0.8); no LLM call. Applies only when a `reference` is configured **and** a default embedding model exists (`getDefaultEmbeddingModelId`); fails closed when embeddings can't be built or the answer is empty. The MODEL side of reference grading reuses `model-judge`: PAIRWISE already compares vs a reference, and LABEL/SCORE now include a `reference` block when supplied (faithfulness/correctness). Bridges eval to the Block M grounding contract. Reference comes from `configJson` today; the dataset-row golden answer binds when datasets land (T595). `TurEvalModelJudge.user` refactored into per-strategy heads (complexity). `TurEmbeddingSimilarityGraderTest` (6, mocked `EmbeddingModel`/`RagInfrastructure`) covers gating, identical/orthogonal vectors, configurable threshold, and fail-closed.
- **T590** — **MODEL grader strategies (generalized LLM judge).** New `grader "model-judge"` (`TurModelJudgeGrader`, kind MODEL) generalizes the lone rubric judge into a `TurEvalModelStrategy` family — **LABEL** (pass/fail), **SCORE** (0..1, passes at a config `passThreshold`, default 0.5), **CRITERIA** (a `criteria` checklist verified as a whole) and **PAIRWISE** (the answer judged against a `reference`). Each carries a per-grader `prompt` override (falls back to the case rubric) and an optional per-grader `model` (LLM instance id resolved via `TurLLMInstanceRepository` + `TurLlmModelFactory` + `TurSecretCryptoService`, else the run's judge model). `TurEvalModelJudge` builds strategy-flavored system/user prompts that all emit the **same** compact JSON verdict shape as `TurEvalJudgePrompt`, so its robust bilingual parser is reused verbatim (the LlmJudge path — deliberately not a free-text-JSON structured-output guardrail, per the gpt-4o-mini pitfall). Fails closed on missing model / judge error. `TurEvalGraderConfigView` gained `configStringList`. `TurModelJudgeTest` (9) covers prompt building, SCORE threshold gating, LABEL verdict, applies-gating and fail-closed paths (Mockito `ChatModel`). Sync path only; folding the T159 Batch / T168 OpenAI-Evals offload tiers under the grader is deferred (💭) — the rubric-judge offload is unchanged.
- **T589** — **Custom Groovy CODE grader.** New `grader "groovy"` (`TurGroovyGrader`) runs an admin-authored Groovy `script` (from `configJson`) through `TurGroovyGraderEngine`, which reuses the Custom Tool sandbox model — the same `GroovyShell` parse + `TurCustomToolCallbackService.sanitizeGroovySource` (made public for reuse) + compiled-script cache-by-hash — with a **vetted grading-context binding** (`answer`, `answers`, `turns`, `slots`, `outcome`, `node`, `conversationId`, `caseName`) and no arbitrary platform helpers. The script returns a `Map [score,pass,rationale]` (fields optional; `pass` defaults to `score >= 1`), a `Boolean`, or a `Number` score (clamped to `[0,1]`); a compile/run exception fails closed with the message as rationale so a broken grader never breaks the run. Opt-in (applies only when a `script` is configured), lets a team assert domain rules the built-in library can't express without a backend change. `TurGroovyGraderTest` (9) covers map/boolean/number returns, bindings, clamping, and errors-as-fail; the Custom Tool sanitize regression test stays green.
- **T588** — **Built-in CODE grader library (config-driven, CI-safe).** Eight new `TurEvalGrader`s under `grader/builtin/`, each keyed by `graderId` and driven by its `configJson`: **exact** (`expected` + ignoreCase/trim), **contains** (`substring`), **regex** (`pattern` + ignoreCase/fullMatch, invalid pattern fails not throws), **json-path** (jayway over the final answer — `path` + optional `expected`), **slot-tolerance** (one captured slot vs `expected` with numeric `tolerance` or ignoreCase string equality), **numeric-range** (a slot / first-number-in-answer within `[min,max]`), **tool-called** (composes the T427 trace — `tool` + optional `status`/`minCalls`, exact not inferred) and **latency-budget** (aggregates the trace's per-call `durationMs` — `maxMs` + `max`/`sum`/`avg`, optional `tool` filter). To feed config to graders the SPI evolved to `appliesTo(ctx, config)` / `grade(ctx, config)` with a new immutable `TurEvalGraderConfigView` (typed `configString`/`configDouble`/`configBoolean`/`has` accessors); `resolveStack` now returns `TurEvalResolvedGrader` (grader + config) pairs and the grading context gained `conversationId` (so the trace graders can read their case's trace). The four dimension built-ins take the new signature and ignore config (byte-identical); these eight are opt-in and never in the default stack. `TurEvalGraderTest` extended to 16 cases (all CODE graders + resolution + trace composition via a real `TurToolCallTraceService`).
- **T587** — **Grader-stack config persistence + inline export.** New `TurEvalGraderConfig` entity (`kind`, `graderId`, `name`, `configJson`, `weight`, `threshold`, `blocking`, `enabled`, `sortOrder`, per-case `caseId` override) as an **EAGER cascade child** of `TurAgentEvalSet` (same aggregate lifecycle as `cases`, so it's available to the non-transactional runner and travels in export/import) — table `tur_eval_grader_config` via Liquibase v2026.3.1.87 (FK `fk_eval_grader_config_set` ON DELETE CASCADE, idempotent). `TurEvalGraderRegistry` gained `resolveStack(set, case)`: set-level rows (null/blank `caseId`) in `sortOrder`, then per-case override rows (matching the case) replace/append by `graderId`; unknown grader ids skipped; **no enabled rows ⇒ the legacy default stack** (byte-identical to T586). The runner threads the set through `runCase`/`scoreCase`. `TurAIAgentImportService.replaceEvalSets` relinks the grader-config parents so the stack imports with the agent (`weight`/`threshold`/`blocking` persisted for the T600 weighted policy; `configJson` for the T588 CODE library). `TurEvalGraderTest` extended (default fallback, sort-order + disabled/unknown skip, per-case override scoping); Liquibase agnostic-types guard green.
- **T586** — **Grader SPI + registry (zero-behaviour-change refactor).** Turned the four hard-wired scoring dimensions of `TurAgentEvalRunnerService.scoreCase` into a seam. New `com.viglet.turing.genai.eval.grader` package: `TurEvalGrader` interface (`graderId`/`kind`/`appliesTo`/`grade`), `TurEvalGraderKind {MODEL, CODE, HUMAN}`, `TurEvalGradingContext` (case + replayed user turns + per-turn assistant replies + captured slots + final node + outcome + judge model), `TurEvalGraderResult` (score/passed/verdict/rationale/`deferred` + structured `Detail` breakdown), and `TurEvalGraderRegistry` (discovers grader beans, resolves the stack). The four dimensions became built-in graders under `grader/builtin/`: `TurSlotMatchGrader`/`TurOutcomeGrader`/`TurNodeGrader` (CODE) + `TurRubricJudgeGrader` (MODEL, wraps the bilingual `TurEvalJudgePrompt` judge). `scoreCase` is now a loop over `registry.resolveGraders(...)`; with no grader config the registry returns the legacy default stack (`slot-match` → `outcome` → `node` → `rubric`), so the per-case score, pass flag, green-baseline/regression logic, the gate, and the `-Pagent-eval` IT are byte-identical. Well-known built-ins still project into the legacy DTO fields (slot diffs, rubric verdict/rationale). `TurEvalGraderTest` (9) covers the CODE built-ins + default-stack resolution; existing eval tests green.

## Block AI — Persona as a first-class actionable surface (§XXXII)

- **T578** — **Persona chat backend** — `POST /api/v2/persona/{personaId}/chat` (SSE, JSON + multipart) lets you talk directly to a `TurPersona` to test its voice. New `TurPersonaChatAPI` drives the shared `TurAgentChatExecutor` with a **transient, never-persisted** `TurAIAgent` shell: `systemPrompt=null` (generic `DEFAULT_SYSTEM_PROMPT` fallback), `nativeTools` = CSV of **all** discovered tool names (`TurNativeToolService.getAllTools()`), no MCP/custom-tool/skills/RAG/flow (fresh entity's empty `HashSet`s), and the loaded persona fused via `setDefaultPersona`. The turn is **stateless** (`conversationId=null`, `flowId=null`) so no flow-state/analytics/chat-memory rows are written. Enforces `persona.isUsableAsSpeaker()` (AUDIENCE-only → 400) and validates the LLM instance via `TurInfraTenantScope.isVisibleToTenant` (this path bypasses the agent allow-list). `@TenantId` auto-scopes the persona load. Backend `mvn compile` green.
- **T579** — **Persona chat SDK client + hook subject** — `postPersonaChat(personaId, llmInstanceId, messages, options)` mirrors `postAgentChat` (CSRF, timezone header, SSE consume, multipart) in both the React SDK (`react-sdk/core/api.ts`) and the vanilla `@viglet/turing-sdk` (`api.ts` + `createTuringApi` bound helper); only the URL + the leaner stateless payload differ. `useTuringChat` grows a `persona: { id, llmInstanceId }` subject as a peer of `agent` (persona wins if both set; stateless — flow/skill/variant ignored), and `createChatController` grows the same `persona` option. The transport is picked by a shared `dispatchSubjectTurn` (persona → agent → site). Extended the T406 SDK parity guard with the new endpoint. Both SDK typechecks + the app typecheck green.
- **T580** — **Deterministic, URL-addressable chat** — new bento routes `/bento/chat/agent/:agentId` and `/bento/chat/persona/:personaId` (kept `/bento/chat` = default agent). `ChatPage` now takes an `initialAgentId` prop resolved from the route instead of reading the **retired `CHAT_INITIAL_AGENT_KEY`** sessionStorage hand-off; the agent launch bar + home `ChatStarter` navigate to the deterministic URL (`ChatStarter` gained a `chatRoute` prop, defaulting to `/bento/chat`). The one-shot auto-send prompt hand-off (`CHAT_INITIAL_PROMPT_KEY`) stays. Back/forward + shareable links work; a `ChatSubject` type was added to `chat.types.ts`.
- **T581** — **Persona chat tab** — new `PersonaChatTab` + `PersonaChatPage` (persona-mode peer of `AgentChatTab`/`ChatPage`) reuse the presentational stack (`ChatInput`/`ChatMessageList`/`ChatEmptyState`/`ContextBar` + session sidebar + model-picker header) via the T579 `useTuringChat({ persona })` subject, dropping the agent-coupled surfaces (no flow/skill selectors, no per-agent pre-flight cost gate, no feedback/rephrase). `ChatEmptyState` gained a `persona` variant and `ChatInput` a `fuchsia` accent; any enabled LLM instance is offered (personas have no allow-list). Reached from `/bento/chat/persona/:personaId` under the frosted `BentoHero`. en/pt i18n added.
- **T582** — **Persona detail → bento dashboard** — the bento persona detail (`/bento/persona/instance/:id`) now reads like the AI-agent dashboard instead of reusing the pill-tab **form** as its landing (superseding T556 for the landing only). `bento.persona.page` became a thin **loader** that fetches the persona once and threads it (`BentoPersonaContext`) to child routes; the **index** renders a new `bento.persona.dashboard` — a `BentoHero` with **inline-edit** name/description + immediate-persist status toggle + `⋮` delete (mirrors the agent dashboard), over a frosted `BentoTile` **mosaic** (2×2 system-instruction hero tile → style, general, guidelines, and — for AUDIENCE/BOTH personas — audience, notebook, fit-report), each tile a doorway into its section. The section sub-pages stay reachable, moved under a pathless `bento.persona.editor` route that renders the unchanged shared-form `PersonaInstanceFormLayout` (single `useForm` + pill tab-bar + save bar); its bento branch now wraps the section `<Outlet>` in `SectionCardChromeProvider chrome="bento"` so every section's `SectionCard` renders as a frosted **`BentoFormSection`** (fixes the T556 half-migration — flat console cards, no hover-lift), and the hero eyebrow links back to the dashboard. New personas redirect to the general editor (nothing to dashboard yet). en/pt i18n added; `tsc -b --noEmit` + bento vitest green.
- **T583** — **Persona Launchpad** — new `PersonaLaunchBar` on the persona dashboard, mirroring `AiAgentLaunchBar` but built as an **extensible** launch bar: **Open chat** is live (navigates to the deterministic `/bento/chat/persona/:id`, T580) while future launch actions — content-fit validation (T584) and persona↔persona dialogue (T585) — render as disabled "soon" affordances (tonal gradient chip + "Soon" badge) so the surface communicates the roadmap without shipping half-features. New actions dock by appending to one `actions` array. en/pt i18n added.
- **T584** — **Content-fit validation from the launchpad** — the **Validate content** launch action is now live for AUDIENCE/BOTH personas (disabled with an "audience personas only" tooltip for SPEAKER, mirroring the dashboard's fit tiles). It opens a new first-class, shareable launch surface — `/bento/persona/validate/:personaId` (`BentoPersonaValidatePage`), a peer of the persona chat page rather than a buried editor tab — that composes Block AA's stack verbatim into an "add sources → score" flow: `PersonaSourcesSection` (T465 notebook: add SN doc / URL / uploaded asset) above `PersonaFitReportSection` (T467 grounded evaluator → T468 red/amber/green report), both self-contained on the persona id and rendered as frosted bento cards via `SectionCardChromeProvider chrome="bento"`. New `usePersona(id)` React-Query hook feeds the `BentoHero`. No backend change (the evaluator/report/notebook APIs already exist). Because this surface is now the single home for the notebook + fit report, the bento persona dashboard tiles and bento editor pills for "Evaluation notebook" / "Audience-fit report" were removed (the legacy console editor keeps them inline). en/pt i18n added; `tsc -b --noEmit` green.
- **T606** — **Style→model calibration on the native provider paths** (follow-up to T605). T605 applied the persona-derived `temperature`/`maxTokens` only on the Spring-AI CALL path (`TurAgentChatExecutor`); native-capable agents (OpenAI Responses / Anthropic Messages / Gemini GenAI) bypassed it. Now `TurNativeChatExecutor` computes `TurPersonaModelCalibration.forPersona(activePersona)` once (right after resolving the persona) and threads the `Params` into all three native services: **Anthropic** `buildParamsFromConversation` (temperature override respects the extended-thinking `temp=1` lock — skipped under thinking — while maxTokens is always honored), **OpenAI Responses** `buildParamsFromItems` (adds `maxOutputTokens`, which the chat path never set before, + temperature override), **Gemini** `buildConfig` (calibration takes precedence over `providerOptionsJson` then the instance default). `Params.NONE` (persona not opted in) leaves every native request byte-for-byte unchanged; Cohere native is out of scope. Per-service builder tests added (calibration overrides + NONE keeps instance values + Anthropic thinking-lock). All native + executor + mapper tests green.
- **T605** — **Opt-in style→model calibration** (follow-up). A persona's Style Guidelines (tone / verbosity) previously reached the LLM only as *text* in the system prompt; the model's `temperature`/`maxTokens` came solely from the `TurLLMInstance`. New per-persona flag `calibrateModelParams` (Liquibase v2026.3.1.85, default 0 = unchanged): when on, a pure mapper `TurPersonaModelCalibration` derives per-turn sampling overrides — **verbosity → maxTokens** (1→256 … 5→4096 via `128<<v`) and **tone → temperature** (TECHNICAL 0.2 / FORMAL 0.3 / EXECUTIVE 0.4 / CASUAL 0.8; null tone leaves temperature untouched). Applied in `TurAgentChatExecutor.providerToolOptions` by layering `.temperature()`/`.maxTokens()` onto the provider-seeded `ToolCallingChatOptions` builder, so it overrides only when opted in and otherwise keeps the instance's values (CALL path — covers agent chat, persona chat, and the dialogue). Admin toggle "Calibrate model from style" on the persona **Style** section; en/pt i18n. Mapper unit-tested (15 cases); persona prompt-cache tests unchanged. Backend + `tsc` green.
- **T604** — **Persona dialogue: N personas + live streaming** (follow-up to T585). The dialogue now supports **two or more** speakers (an unbounded roster with add/remove, min 2) driven **round-robin** — each persona replies to the previous speaker's utterance, cycling through the roster — instead of exactly two, and it **streams live**: `POST /api/v2/persona-dialogue` became an SSE endpoint returning `Flux<TurDialogueEvent>` (`TURN` per completed utterance, then `DONE` / `ERROR`), so the transcript builds turn by turn in the UI rather than arriving all at once. The blocking `TurDialogueTranscript`/`TurDialogueTurn` records were replaced by `TurDialogueEvent`; the service orchestrates on a bounded-elastic worker (the per-turn executor call still blocks-and-collects the CALL-mode token). Frontend: the page grew a dynamic speaker list (each slot a persona picker, "Add persona" button) and consumes the SSE via a `fetch` reader that primes CSRF (`X-XSRF-TOKEN`), appending colour-coded bubbles as turns arrive with a "next persona is thinking…" indicator; turn cap raised 20→40. 8-case Mockito test updated for round-robin + streamed events. Backend `mvn compile` + test green; `tsc -b --noEmit` green.
- **T585** — **Automatic persona↔persona conversation** — a **global** dialogue surface (two peer personas + a topic, so deliberately NOT scoped to one persona instance). Backend `TurPersonaDialogueService` + `POST /api/v2/persona-dialogue` (`TurPersonaDialogueAPI`) orchestrates a bounded, alternating dialogue by driving the same shared `TurAgentChatExecutor` the persona-chat MVP (T578) uses — one transient, never-persisted `TurAIAgent` per turn carrying the active speaker via `setDefaultPersona`, stateless (`conversationId=null`), no native tools (pure voice). The topic seeds persona A's opening turn; each persona then replies to the other's last utterance. Two flow rules: a shared **base system prompt** requires every reply to end with a question so the conversation self-sustains (the persona voice is fused on top by `TurChatPromptAssembler`, so it augments — not replaces — the persona), and the **turn budget** is configurable (default 10, clamped [2, 20]). The executor runs in its default `CALL` mode, so each utterance is collected from a single `"token"` event and joined; a mid-dialogue failure returns the partial transcript flagged with `llmError`. Frontend: global route `/bento/persona/dialogue` (`BentoPersonaDialoguePage`) — topic + two speaker pickers (AUDIENCE personas filtered out) + turns + model, rendering a two-sided transcript; reached from a new `headerAction` button on the persona **list** page (`BentoListPage` gained the optional slot) — a dialogue is between peer personas so it is deliberately a global surface, NOT on an individual persona's launchpad. 7-case Mockito test covers alternation/threading/turn-budget/errors/guards. en/pt i18n added; backend `mvn compile` + `tsc -b --noEmit` green.

## Block AM — HuggingFace embedding provider + provider-aware model discovery (§XXXVI)

- **T623** — **HuggingFace embedding provider (backend).** New `providerType` sentinel `HUGGINGFACE` + `TurHuggingFaceEmbeddingModelFactory` paralleling `TurLocalEmbeddingModelFactory`: reads an HF **repo id** from the existing `modelReference` column (**no new entity columns**) and delegates to the same in-process ONNX runtime. Extracted the build/cache logic out of the local factory into a shared `TurTransformersEmbeddingModelBuilder` (cache keyed by resolved `.onnx`+`tokenizer.json` URLs) so both providers reach one `TransformersEmbeddingModel` — the HF door is the *same* runtime as `TRANSFORMERS_LOCAL`, only the model-selection UX differs. Repo-id→URL resolution lives in a reusable `TurHuggingFaceRepoResolver` that probes the repo tree (`/api/models/{repoId}/tree/main`) to pick the real ONNX path (prefers `onnx/model.onnx`, falls back to root `model.onnx`, avoids quantized when a plain artifact exists) + `tokenizer.json`, with a fail-open fallback to the conventional layout when the tree is unreachable (offline/private/rate-limited); it also exposes `hasOnnxAndTokenizer(...)` for T624's ONNX-availability probe. Wired into `TurRagContextBuilder.resolveEmbeddingModel` alongside the local factory; fail-open. `TurHuggingFaceRepoResolverTest` (5) + `TurHuggingFaceEmbeddingModelFactoryTest` (2) + updated `TurLocalEmbeddingModelFactoryTest`/`TurRagContextBuilderTest`; backend targeted tests green. No Liquibase.
- **T624** — **HuggingFace model discovery service.** `TurHuggingFaceModelDiscoveryService` queries the public `huggingface.co/api/models` endpoint (filtered to `sentence-transformers`, sorted by downloads, optional `search` query), then **ONNX-verifies** each candidate through `TurHuggingFaceRepoResolver.hasOnnxAndTokenizer(...)` — so the picker only offers repos that actually expose a loadable `.onnx` + `tokenizer.json` in-process (bounded: fetches ≤`candidate-limit` candidates, stops at `result-limit` verified, both `@Value`-configurable). Falls back to the bundled, curated `TurHuggingFaceModelCatalog` (`resources/llm/huggingface-embeddings-catalog.json` — 15 known-good ONNX sentence-transformers/Xenova/BAAI/e5 repos with declared dimensions) filtered by the same query, surfacing the source as `Source {LIVE, CATALOG, NONE}` exactly like T577's `TurLlmModelDiscoveryService`. New `TurHuggingFaceModelOption` record (repoId/downloads/likes/dimensions/onnxVerified). Exposed at `GET /api/embedding-model/hf-models?query=` (role-gated `EMBEDDING_VIEW`/`CREATE`/`EDIT`). `TurHuggingFaceModelDiscoveryServiceTest` (3, forced offline → catalog path) green. No Liquibase.
- **T625** — **LLM embedding-model discovery.** Extended the T577 `TurLlmModelDiscoveryService` with `listEmbeddingModels(...)` so the embedding form's LLM-instance path gets the same model picker the chat LLM form got (retiring free-text `modelReference`). Refactored `listModels` + the new method onto a shared `listFiltered(..., Predicate<TurLlmModelOption>)` that filters both the live vendor listing **and** the bundled catalog: when the live `/v1/models` list yields no embedding models (a vendor that omits them, or an all-chat list) it falls back to the catalog filtered to embeddings, keeping the `LIVE`/`CATALOG`/`NONE` source badge honest. The `isEmbeddingModel` heuristic recognizes any id/label containing `embed` plus non-rerank Voyage ids, and excludes rerank + chat models (the catalog's `(embedding)` label markers also match). Exposed at `POST /api/embedding-model/llm-models` (role-gated `EMBEDDING_VIEW`/`CREATE`/`EDIT`), same request shape as T577's `/api/llm/models`. `TurLlmModelDiscoveryServiceTest` (3: filter heuristic + live-filter + catalog-fallback) green; `listModels` behavior byte-identical (filter = always-true). No Liquibase.
- **T626** — **Provider-aware model picker (frontend), completing Block AM's committed scope.** `bento.embedding.form.tsx` reworked from a single free-text `modelReference` input into three provider modes selected via the provider dropdown (LLM instances + `HuggingFace.co` + `Transformers (Local)` sentinels): **(1) LLM instance** renders the T577 `ModelCombobox` fed by a new `useLlmEmbeddingModels` hook (`POST /api/embedding-model/llm-models` → T625, filtered to embeddings), so the remote-embedding path gets the same picker the chat LLM form has; **(2) HuggingFace.co** renders a new `HuggingFaceModelCombobox` (`components/ui/`) — a searchable picker with debounced server-side search (`useHuggingFaceModels` → `GET /api/embedding-model/hf-models?query=` → T624), rendering per-repo metadata (ONNX-verified check, dimensions, formatted downloads) + a `LIVE`/`CATALOG` source badge, and staying editable so an unlisted repo id can still be typed (air-gapped/private); **(3) Local transformers (manual)** keeps the existing `.onnx`/`tokenizer.json` path inputs for fine-tuned/offline models. Provider selection clears `turLLMInstance` for both ONNX sentinels; `onSubmit` sends the instance only for the LLM path and the paths only for the manual path. New models (`huggingface-model.model.ts`), service (`embedding-model-discovery.service.ts`), query hooks + keys; en/pt i18n. Frontend `tsc -b --noEmit` green. No backend change (T623–T625 endpoints already shipped). No Liquibase.
- **T629** — **Default-RAG startup provisioning migrated to `HUGGINGFACE`, closing Block AM.** `TurDefaultRagInfraOnStartup` (T622) now provisions the auto-seeded default embedding model as a `HUGGINGFACE` `TurEmbeddingModel` storing just the repo id `sentence-transformers/all-MiniLM-L6-v2` in `modelReference` (new override `turing.startup.default-rag.model-repo-id`) instead of the hardcoded `resolve/main/…onnx` + `tokenizer.json` URLs — so a fresh install's seeded default is byte-shape identical to a model a user picks through the T626 HuggingFace picker (same in-process ONNX runtime, resolved through `TurHuggingFaceRepoResolver`). **Fallback preserved:** blanking `model-repo-id` provisions the previous `TRANSFORMERS_LOCAL` model from the raw `model-path`/`tokenizer-path` URLs (offline mirror / custom artifact). The background warm-up resolves through whichever factory matches the provisioned provider type. Idempotency/gating unchanged (fresh install + default agent set). Backend compile green; the startup runner stays disabled in tests by design, and the HUGGINGFACE resolution path is covered by the T623 factory tests. No Liquibase (data seeding).
- **T627** — **Dimension detection + reindex-awareness on model select (HuggingFace path).** Embedding dimension is load-bearing — change it and every indexed vector is invalid until a reindex, and the vector store's field must match — so the picker surfaces the mismatch *before* save. `TurHuggingFaceRepoResolver.probeDimensions(repoId)` reads the model's dimension from its `config.json` (`hidden_size`, with `hidden_dim`/`d_model`/`dim`/`n_embd` aliases) — free, no key, no download; fail-open to `null` (unknown). `TurEmbeddingDimensionService.checkHuggingFace(repoId)` returns a `DimensionCheck(dimensions, defaultDimensions, differs)` comparing the picked model against the current global default embedding model's dimension (probed the same way when that default is itself a `HUGGINGFACE` model; else `null`/unknown → never flags). Exposed at `GET /api/embedding-model/hf-dimension-check?repoId=`. Frontend: on picking a HuggingFace model the form shows the detected embedding dimension and, when it differs from the default, an amber warning that switching needs a matching vector store + a full re-index (`useHuggingFaceDimensionCheck` hook; en/pt i18n). Remote-LLM dimension detection (a billed one-shot embed) is deliberately out of scope. `TurEmbeddingDimensionServiceTest` + `TurHuggingFaceRepoResolverTest` probe case; backend + frontend `tsc` green. No Liquibase.
- **T628** — **ONNX variant / quantization picker, closing Block AM.** HuggingFace repos routinely ship several ONNX artifacts (`onnx/model.onnx`, `model_quantized.onnx`, `model_fp16.onnx`, …) trading size/latency against accuracy. `TurHuggingFaceRepoResolver.listOnnxVariants(repoId)` returns them from the repo tree, ordered so the auto-picked default is first (canonical `onnx/model.onnx` → root `model.onnx` → non-quantized → quantized). A chosen variant is stored appended to the repo id in the existing `modelReference` column via a `::` `VARIANT_SEPARATOR` (e.g. `sentence-transformers/all-MiniLM-L6-v2::onnx/model_quantized.onnx`) — **no new entity columns**; `resolve` parses it and uses the explicit artifact (tokenizer still auto-resolved), and `cleanRepoId` strips the suffix so the T624/T627 probes stay repo-id-based. Exposed at `GET /api/embedding-model/hf-variants?repoId=`. Frontend: the HuggingFace picker shows a variant `Select` (default + each artifact) only when a repo ships more than one; selecting a model resets the variant. `useHuggingFaceVariants` hook; en/pt i18n. `TurHuggingFaceRepoResolverTest` variant case; backend + frontend `tsc` green. Ties into T514 (quantized/Matryoshka embeddings on the Lucene store). No Liquibase. **Block AM (T623–T629) complete.**
- **T656** — **Resilient ONNX embedding model acquisition (product-wide).** The in-process ONNX runtime shared by `TRANSFORMERS_LOCAL` (T622) and `HUGGINGFACE` (T623/T629) handed its `https:` model/tokenizer URLs straight to Spring AI's `TransformersEmbeddingModel`, whose `ResourceCacheService` downloads into an **ephemeral tmpdir with no retry, integrity check, or actionable error**. Fragile for the ~90 MB `resolve/main/onnx/model.onnx` — a HuggingFace **Git-LFS** file that 302-redirects to a separate CDN host (`us.aws.cdn.hf.co` / `cdn-lfs.huggingface.co`): on a cold cache a network blip, an egress policy that reaches `huggingface.co` but not the CDN host, or a wiped tmpfs on every boot left RAG silently without its embedding model, while the small same-host `tokenizer.json` kept working (making the failure look mysterious). **Empirically verified** the stock path *does* follow the LFS 302 and downloads fine on an unrestricted network (`ResourceCacheService` fetched the full 90 MB in ~4.5 s) — so redirect-following was **not** the gap; the missing piece is operational hardening. New `TurOnnxModelDownloader` now **owns** remote acquisition via `java.net.http.HttpClient`: bounded retry + exponential backoff, a **configurable persistent cache dir** (`turing.genai.embedding.local.cache-dir`, so a wiped/ephemeral env reuses the warm cache instead of re-downloading; blank → `${java.io.tmpdir}/turing-onnx-cache`), atomic `.part`→final move, integrity gates (non-empty, `Content-Length` match, rejects an undersized `.onnx` or a stray Git-LFS pointer stub), and an actionable failure diagnostic (URL, attempts, cache dir, egress/pre-seed hint — so an egress block finally surfaces as a clear error instead of a silent empty store). `TurTransformersEmbeddingModelBuilder` resolves both artifacts to local `file:` URIs *before* they reach the ONNX runtime; `classpath:`/`file:` paths pass through unchanged. New knobs `turing.genai.embedding.download.{max-attempts,timeout-seconds,initial-backoff-millis}`. `TurOnnxModelDownloaderTest` (7, loopback `HttpServer` — redirect-follow, LFS-stub/undersized-onnx rejection, persistent-cache hit, pass-through) + updated builder-constructor tests; targeted backend tests green. No Liquibase. **Caveat:** if the server failure is a hard egress block to the CDN host, this makes it diagnosable but the actual unblock is infra (allowlist the CDN host **or** pre-seed the cache volume — the "optional belt" is then the real fix). Every ONNX-using deployment benefits from the warm-cache-survives-reset + retry + diagnostics; the demo's grounded-chat-after-reset is the first beneficiary.

## Block AN — Security hardening (post-audit, §XXXVII)

- **T653** — **Frontend XSS surface (CLOSES Block AN).** No client-side HTML sanitiser existed, so every `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` trusted the backend. Added a dependency-free `src/lib/sanitize-html.ts`: `sanitizeHighlight` escapes the whole string then re-allows ONLY a fixed allowlist of attribute-less inline tags (`mark`/`em`/`strong`/`b`/`i`/`u`) — applied to the search `result-card` title + description, so a poisoned indexed document (Solr/ES don't HTML-escape field content by default) can no longer inject script into the authenticated admin console, while `<mark>` highlighting still renders; and `sanitizeFragment` strips `<script>`/`<iframe>`/`<object>`/`<embed>`/`<link>`/`<meta>`, inline `on*=` handlers and `javascript:` URLs — applied to the Google-grounding "search suggestions" chips fragment (`chat-message-list`) while preserving the chip markup + inline styles Google requires. `sanitize-html.test.ts` (8, vitest). The LLM ```html iframe sandbox, react-markdown (no `rehype-raw`), and the D2 SVG (LLM-driven, low) were left as-is. (§XXXVII.15)
- **T652** — **Config & logging hygiene.** (1) Externalised the committed MinIO credentials (`admin` / `minha_senha_forte`) to `${TURING_STORAGE_MINIO_*}` env placeholders with obvious dev defaults — only used when `storage.type=MINIO` (default is FILESYSTEM); the old value must still be rotated + purged from git history as an ops step. (2) H2 console: flipped `web-allow-others` to `false` and **dropped the `web.ignoring("/h2/**")` full security-chain bypass** in `TurSecurityConfigProduction` (kept the URL-encoded-slash firewall) — the console is off by default and, if enabled, is now loopback-only and goes through the security chain instead of being an unauthenticated hole. (3) Redacted secret material from logs: the URL-signing-secret rotation no longer logs the first 8 chars, and the human-approval log correlates on the conversation id instead of the capability token. (4) Constrained self-registration usernames to a safe charset + length (`^[A-Za-z0-9._@-]{3,100}$`) and enforced an 8-char minimum password (self-registered users still get only the `User` group, never admin — T642). `TurUserAPITest` +1 (username validator). (§XXXVII.14)
- **T654** — **Triage & patch dependency CVEs + CI audit gate.** Triaged the open Dependabot advisories (all in the frontend npm tree). Remediated the transitive fixables via `pnpm-workspace.yaml` `overrides`: **undici** `>=7.28.0` (SOCKS5 TLS-bypass / cross-origin routing / WebSocket DoS — several highs), **@babel/core** `>=7.29.6` (arbitrary file read via sourceMappingURL), **js-yaml@3** `>=3.15.0` (quadratic-complexity DoS). **xlsx** (SheetJS, 2 highs — ReDoS + prototype pollution) has **no npm fix** (npm is frozen at 0.18.5; fixes ship only on the vendor CDN, which pnpm's `blockExoticSubdeps` policy disallows as a subdependency) and is **export-only** in Turing (`exportToXlsx`; the app never parses untrusted spreadsheets, so both parse-path vulns are unreachable) — accepted as risk and pinned out via `auditConfig.ignoreGhsas` with a documented rationale. Regenerated `pnpm-lock.yaml`; `pnpm audit --audit-level high` now passes (2 highs, both the ignored xlsx). New CI workflow `.github/workflows/dependency-audit.yml` runs that gate on frontend PRs + weekly, so a NEW high fails the build; the Maven side stays covered by the existing weekly Dependabot `maven` block. (§XXXVII.16)
- **T651** — **Fix IDOR in `TurUserMemoryAPI`.** `delete(@PathVariable id)` removed any memory row by id with no ownership/tenant/agent check, so a caller could delete another visitor's remembered facts by guessing the id. `delete` now requires the `userId` and resolves the site's agent, and `TurUserMemoryService.delete(id, userId, agentId)` only removes the row when it belongs to that `(userId, agentId)` — returning `false` otherwise (`@TenantId` still scopes the read to the current tenant on top). list/remember/clear already key on `userId + agentId` (the anonymous SDK's stable visitor id is the identity). `TurUserMemoryServiceTest` +4 (owned delete succeeds; wrong userId / wrong agent / absent row refused). (§XXXVII.13)
- **T650** — **MCP hardening (agent-as-client URL + stdio command guards).** The agent-as-MCP-*client* path (`TurMcpToolCallbackService.createTransport`) used the admin-configured `server.url()` verbatim (SSRF) and `server.command()` + args for stdio (arbitrary local process = RCE-by-config) with no allowlist. Added `turing.mcp-client.*` (`TurMcpClientProperty`): an optional **stdio command allowlist** (`allowedStdioCommands` — when set, a stdio MCP server whose command base-name isn't listed is refused, blocking arbitrary-process execution) and an optional **private-URL block** (`blockPrivateUrls` — routes the client URL through the T643 `TurSsrfGuard`). Both default to legacy/permissive so an existing internal MCP setup is unaffected until the operator opts in. `TurMcpToolCallbackServiceTest` +2 (empty allowlist permits any; non-empty matches base-name case-insensitively, rejects `bash`/`/bin/sh`). **Notes:** inbound `/mcp` already restricts to the loopback interface by the raw socket address (`TurMcpLoopbackFilter`, not a spoofable forwarded header) and is OFF by default — a deployment behind a same-host proxy must pair `loopbackOnly` with `require-auth` (documented on the property); extending `TurMcpToolScopePolicy` from the inbound server surface to discovered client tools remains a defense-in-depth follow-up. (§XXXVII.12)
- **T649** — **Actuator hardening.** `management.endpoints.web.exposure.include` was `"*"`, so `/actuator/env` + `/heapdump` (leaking `turing.ai.crypto.key`, datasource creds and provider keys) were reachable by any authenticated principal (= admin by default before T642). Restricted the exposure to `health,info,prometheus` (all already `permitAll` for health checks / Prometheus scraping); env/heapdump/beans/mappings/etc. are no longer published over HTTP at all — an operator adds one back explicitly (falling under `authenticated()`) only when needed. `TurActuatorExposureTest` guards against a regression to `"*"`. (§XXXVII.11)
- **T648** — **Input / DoS caps on anonymous endpoints.** `spring.servlet.multipart.max-*-size` was `1024MB`, letting an anonymous `/slot-upload` / `/slot-extract` feed ~1 GB into Tika + a vision call; conversation message count/length was unbounded; and page/skill ZIP import buffered entries via `readAllBytes()` with no cap (zip-bomb OOM). Added a `turing.abuse.chat.*` cap set (all default-on): reduced the global multipart limit to `64MB` and added a pre-parse per-file gate (`maxUploadBytes`, 10 MB) on all three anonymous slot endpoints (rejected on `file.getSize()` before Tika/vision); default message caps (`maxMessagesPerTurn` 100, `maxMessageChars` 24000) enforced at the anonymous `/conversation` entry; and ZIP guards (`maxZipEntries` 5000, `maxZipEntryBytes` 50 MB via a **bounded** `readEntryBounded` that aborts mid-stream instead of `readAllBytes`, `maxZipTotalBytes` 250 MB) in both `TurPageAPI` and `TurSkillFileService`. `TurPageAPITest` (5 — traversal + bounded-read under/over/unlimited) + skill suite green. (§XXXVII.10)
- **T647** — **RAG / tool-call trust boundary for anonymous chat.** Two structural gaps on the anonymous public SN chat path: (1) `TurSNGenAi.buildSystemPrompt` appended retrieved document text into the *system* prompt verbatim, so any content that reaches the index (crawled page, uploaded asset, UGC) carrying "ignore previous instructions / call tool X" executed with system authority on the next query — retrieved content is now wrapped in a distinctive `<untrusted_website_content>` fence preceded by a standing instruction that everything inside is data, never instructions/role-changes/tool requests (the CPF/CNPJ "anti-injection" elsewhere is unrelated slot parsing); (2) there was no authorization boundary between an anonymous visitor and the agent's LLM-invoked native / MCP-client / custom / skill tools — added a tool trust boundary: `TurAgentChatRequest` carries an `untrustedCaller` flag (the public SN path sets it; authenticated agent/persona chat defaults false via a backward-compatible constructor), and the executor strips ALL tool callbacks for an untrusted caller when `turing.abuse.chat.anonymous-tools-enabled=false`. Default `true` preserves legacy behavior (and the Block Y showcase's anonymous-agent demo) — operators opt into the gate. `TurSNGenAiTest` +2 (untrusted framing present, no block without context) + `TurAgentChatRequestTest` (trust default). (§XXXVII.9)
- **T646** — **Stop leaking credential material over the API.** Three fixes: (1) `TurUser.password` had no serialization guard, so the admin user-list returned every user's BCrypt hash (`TurUserDto extends TurUser` inherited the getter) — added `@JsonProperty(access = WRITE_ONLY)` so an incoming password still binds on create/update but the stored hash is never serialized out; (2) `TurDevTokenAPI` had **zero** authorization, minted tokens for anyone, and returned cleartext token values on list/get — added class-level `@Secured("ROLE_ADMIN")`, scrubbed the token on all read paths (list/get/update) leaving the create response as the only one-time reveal, and added soft **revocation** (`enabled`) + optional **expiry** (`expiresAt`) columns (Liquibase `v2026.3.1.99`, defaults enabled/never-expire) with a `TurDevToken.isUsable()` check now enforced at all three validation sites (`TurAuthTokenHeaderFilter`, `TurSNSiteApiKeyGateFilter`, `TurMcpApiKeyAuthenticationFilter`); (3) **dropped `?apiKey=` query-param token acceptance** in the SN and MCP filters (a secret in the URL lands in access logs / Referer / history) — the key must be sent in the `Key` header. Filter + dev-token + user-API tests updated (query-param now rejected, disabled/expired token rejected, password write-only verified); 50+ tests green. (§XXXVII.8)
