- **T645** — **Storage path-traversal / Zip-Slip containment.** Three gaps: (1) `TurTenantScopedStorageService.scopeKey` only rejected `..` when a tenant prefix was active, so the most common deployment (tenancy off / DEFAULT → empty prefix) accepted a traversal key verbatim — containment is now **unconditional** (rejects `..` and `\`, strips leading `/`) regardless of tenancy; (2) `TurAssetAPI` read/deleted a caller-supplied `?objectName=` with **no** authorization (unlike the sibling `TurStoreInstanceAPI`) — added class-level `@Secured("ROLE_ADMIN")`; (3) the page (`TurPageAPI`) and skill (`TurSkillFileService`) ZIP importers concatenated entry names into the storage key without `..` stripping — both now **fail closed** on any entry with a `..` segment (`hasTraversalSegment`), defense-in-depth over the central `scopeKey` guard. `TurTenantScopedStorageServiceTest` +4 (traversal blocked with tenancy off / DEFAULT / backslash, leading-slash stripped) + skill Zip-Slip helper tests; storage + skill + asset suites green. (§XXXVII.7)
- **T644** — **Kill the SpEL RCE sink in chat-flow conditions.** `ChatFlowOps.trySpel` evaluated a node's `conditionExpression` with a `StandardEvaluationContext`, which permits type references (`T(java.lang.Runtime).getRuntime().exec(...)`) and arbitrary method invocation. The expression is not end-user chat input but IS attacker-influenceable: chat-flow definitions arrive via ZIP import (`TurAIAgentImportService`) and are produced by the LLM authoring skill, so any flow author / bundle import / prompt-steered authoring yielded RCE. Swapped to `SimpleEvaluationContext.forPropertyAccessors(new MapAccessor()).build()` + `getValue(ctx, root)` — property access on the variables map only, no `T(...)` and no method calls. Legitimate conditions (`var == 'value'`, numeric/boolean combinations) still evaluate deterministically; a blocked expression fails closed to empty and routes to the existing LLM-judge fallback rather than executing. `trySpel` made package-visible; `ChatFlowOpsSpelSandboxTest` (7 — property access still works, `T(...)` / method-invocation vectors blocked); 42 flow-strategy tests green. (§XXXVII.6)
- **T643** — **Central egress guard for outbound fetches (SSRF).** Turing had one good SSRF guard buried in `TurFileUtils.isSafe` (used only by the persona URL import); every other outbound feature bypassed it, and the guard itself missed IPv6 ULA (`fc00::/7`, since `isSiteLocalAddress()` only matches the deprecated `fec0::/10`). Extracted the single hardened validator `TurSsrfGuard` — protocol allowlist, resolves the host and requires **every** resolved address to be safe (loopback / link-local incl. `169.254.169.254` / any-local / multicast / RFC1918 / CGNAT / **IPv6 ULA** / IPv4-mapped-IPv6), with `isAllowedUrl` / `assertAllowedUrl` / `assertResolvedSafe`. Routed the reachable outbound sinks through it: `TurFileUtils.isSafe` now **delegates** to the guard (closing the IPv6 gap on the persona-import path); the connector **test-connection oracle** (`TurConnectorSelfInstallService`, the CONFIRMED response-observable SSRF that forwards a bearer token) validates the endpoint before probing; the integration **federation proxy** resolves+validates the target (defeating a DNS-rebinding / internal-only endpoint that passed the host-string check) and **disables redirect-following** per-request (a trusted endpoint 302-ing to an internal address is no longer fetched server-side); and the **chat webhook** dispatch (attacker-influenceable via flow-bundle import / LLM authoring) validates its target URL before sending the encrypted auth header. `TurSsrfGuardTest` (29 — IPv4/IPv6 private+special blocked, public allowed, URL protocol/malformed rejection, assert throws) + connector SSRF-block test + hermetic-guard refactor of the connector test. **Residual (documented):** LLM / rerank provider *base* URLs are admin-configured (POTENTIAL, and have no single per-instance validation chokepoint today) — the guard is now available to validate them when those config surfaces are next touched. (§XXXVII.5)
- **T642** — **Fix the `permissions=false` blanket-admin default.** With the shipped default `turing.permissions: false`, `TurCustomUserDetailsService.loadUserByUsername` and `TurAuthTokenHeaderFilter.resolveAuthorities` granted `ROLE_ADMIN` + every DB privilege to any principal that authenticated by any means, making all ~240 `@PreAuthorize`/`@Secured` checks dead and turning the dev-token leak / self-registration into privilege escalation. Flipped the shipped `application.yaml` default to `permissions: true` (the code default was already `true`), so real per-user authorities are enforced — the seeded `admin` keeps full rights via the Administrator group → `ROLE_ADMIN`, while a plain authenticated principal is no longer super-admin. The permissive "everyone is admin" mode is now an explicit opt-in for trusted single-user deploys, and `TurConfigProperties` logs a startup `SECURITY` warning whenever it is active. The full-context ITs pin `turing.permissions=false` in the test profile so their existing admin-as-default behavior is unchanged (zero IT re-auth wiring). `TurConfigPropertiesTest` +2 (secure default + warn-hook safe). (§XXXVII.4)
- **T641** — **App-layer abuse controls on the anonymous chat path.** The public SN endpoints are `permitAll` by design and the shipped `containers/nginx/conf.d/app.conf` had no `limit_req`/`limit_conn`, so the roadmap's assumption that rate-limiting lived in the proxy was false even in the shipped config; a loop of `POST /api/sn/{site}/chat/conversation` (each turn = embedding + retrieval + completion) could drive unbounded LLM spend, and the only budget mechanism (`TurChatCostBudgetGate`) is per-agent, monthly, opt-in and *soft*. Added three layers: (1) **in-process per-IP + per-session rate limiting** — a dependency-free `TurFixedWindowRateLimiter` (bounded, clock-injectable, fail-open at the key cap) behind `TurAnonymousAbuseRateLimitFilter`, scoped to the **LLM-cost** surface only (RAG `/chat` + persona `/content-fit`; cheap per-keystroke `/ac`/`/search` are deliberately left to the proxy so autocomplete UX isn't throttled), over the limit → HTTP 429 + `Retry-After`; (2) a **hard cost kill-switch** — `TurChatCostBudgetGate.isAnonymousChatHardCapExceeded()` (new global `TurLLMTokenUsageRepository.sumCostSince`) refuses a new anonymous conversation with a friendly message *before* any upstream call once `turing.abuse.chat.hard-monthly-cap-usd` is reached (opt-in, unlike the soft gate); (3) real **nginx `limit_req`/`limit_conn`** zones (tight chat zone + looser search zone, 429 status, SSE-friendly chat location). New `turing.abuse.*` config (`TurAbuseControlProperty`), rate limiter default-on (disabled in the test profile so ITs aren't throttled). `TurFixedWindowRateLimiterTest` (6) + `TurChatCostBudgetGateTest` +4. (§XXXVII.3)
- **T640** — **Authenticate `/api/system/chat-analytics/**`.** The read API was `permitAll` in both auth branches of `TurSecurityConfigProduction`, so an unauthenticated caller could enumerate chat sessions (`GET .../sessions`) and pull the full chat-memory transcript of any conversation (`.../sessions/{id}/transcript`), plus router decisions and tool calls — end-user-typed content / PII, guarded only by a code comment saying "restrict at the network layer". Removed both `permitAll` matchers so the paths fall under `anyRequest().authenticated()`, and added class-level `@Secured({ "ROLE_ADMIN", "AI_AGENT_VIEW" })` on `TurChatAnalyticsAPI` (method security already `securedEnabled`). Updated the shipped Grafana Infinity datasource provisioning (`containers/grafana/provisioning/datasources/prometheus.yml`) from `auth_method: none` to HTTP Basic (placeholder admin creds + a note to swap them). `TurChatAnalyticsAPISecurityTest` reflection-guards the annotation; no anonymous test exercised the endpoint. (§XXXVII.2)
- **T639** — **Externalise & reject the sample AI crypto master key in production.** `turing.ai.crypto.key: sample-key-for-crypto` shipped committed in `application.yaml` while `spring.profiles.active: production` was set, and `TurCryptoKeyMaterial` only failed fast on a *blank* key — so the committed sample value silently AES/GCM-encrypted every stored provider API key and store credential (anyone with repo access + a DB copy could decrypt them). The property is now `ai.crypto.key: ${TURING_AI_CRYPTO_KEY:}` (blank default → the existing production fail-fast fires unless the env var is set), and `TurCryptoKeyMaterial` additionally **rejects a set of known sample/dev sentinels** (`sample-key-for-crypto`, the dev fallback, `changeme`, `secret`, …; case-insensitive, trimmed) under the production profile with an actionable message pointing at the rotation doc. Outside production the warned-once insecure dev fallback is unchanged, so local dev needs no key. Added `docs/operations/crypto-key-rotation.md` (rotate-at-source procedure + the T373 cross-container same-key caveat + a bulk re-encryption stopgap). Tests boot under the production profile, so `src/test/resources/application.properties` supplies a valid non-sentinel test key. `TurSecretCryptoServiceTest` +11 (sentinel rejection incl. case/whitespace variants, strong key accepted in prod, sample allowed outside prod); 42 crypto tests green. (§XXXVII.1)
- **T893** — The public faceted search page is anonymous under Keycloak too, by one matcher added to that branch and nothing else widened. The section asked for the defect to be confirmed rather than assumed, and both maskings it named are disproven in code: `webSecurityCustomizer` keeps no `web.ignoring()` bypass at all since T652 pulled the `/h2/**` one, and `/sn/**` is served by `TurSNSearchController`, a real `@Controller` with `@RequestMapping("/sn")`, so every request for it goes through the chain and `anyRequest().authenticated()` was catching it. The site's own SPA assets are served by that same controller under `/sn/{site}/assets/**`, which the `/assets/**` entry never reached either, so the page was not merely gated but unusable. Measured while confirming: the Keycloak list is a strict subset of the local one, 35 entries against 47, and the twelve it lacks are not uniformly accidental — `/admin/**`, `/bento/**`, `/console/**` and `/setup/**` belong behind SSO, which is why the fix is one line and why the guard added here pins that asymmetry rather than erasing it. `TurSecurityConfigProduction` is `@Profile("production")` so no MockMvc call in this suite can prove what it permits; the new `TurPublicSearchPageAuthClassTest` follows the T889 precedent and reads the source, counting both branches. It was verified in all three directions by injecting each first: deleting the new matcher (the pre-fix state), sweeping the console into the SSO branch, and adding an entry only Keycloak permits each turned exactly the intended assertion red. 7147 tests green. What stays unverified here is the reverse proxy the section also mentions, which lives outside this repository.
- **T894** — The anonymous surface is declared once, and where the branches differ they say so. Three immutable `List.of(...)` fields replace the two hand-maintained copies: `PUBLIC_MATCHERS` is what both permit, `LOCAL_AUTH_ONLY_MATCHERS` and `KEYCLOAK_ONLY_MATCHERS` each carry the reason they are not shared, and a `matchers(shared, branchOnly)` helper is what each branch calls, so a new public endpoint is added in one place and cannot be half-added. `List.of` rather than `String[]` because an allowlist a caller can edit in place is not one. Two corrections to what this section claimed. First, the earlier count came from a regex that saw only string literals and missed every constant-named matcher: the real figures are 42 shared with 43 under Keycloak and 54 under local auth, not 35 and 47. Second, the Keycloak branch is therefore NOT a strict subset — `BACK_CHANNEL_LOGOUT_PATH` is Keycloak-only and correctly so, a provider callback meaning nothing without a provider, which is why it is now a declared list of its own rather than an exception nobody wrote down; T893's guard asserted the subset and passed only because the same blind regex could not see it. The refactor is proved behaviour-preserving by resolving both branches' effective permitAll sets before and after and diffing them: identical, 43 and 54, nothing added or removed. Both guards now read the declaration instead of counting occurrences, as this section asked. Five faults were injected: sweeping the console into the shared list, re-adding a path a past audit removed, going back to a mutable list, sharing the provider callback, and a branch abandoning the shared list. The last one PASSED, which exposed a real gap — the guards read the lists but not their use — so a source assertion now pins both branches to `matchers(PUBLIC_MATCHERS, …)`, and with it all five turn red. 7152 tests green.
- **T951** — The decoded path is checked before it becomes an object name, and the firewall's blind spot is pinned as a test rather than remembered. `TurSNSearchController.serveFromStorage` now runs the decoded segment through a containment rule and answers a traversal with 400 — deliberately not through the SPA fallback beside it, because that fallback exists for client-side routing and answering an escape attempt with a page is not the same thing. The rule is not a new one. `TurSkillFileService.safeRelative` already rejected `..`, absolute paths and back-slashes for the skill editor, so it moved to `TurStorageKeys` and both callers share it: a traversal guard written twice is exactly what T894 removed from the security config, and writing a second copy here would have been authoring the next one. The tests carry the measurement the section was built on. `StrictHttpFirewall` configured as `allowUrlEncodedSlaturHttpFirewall()` does it is asserted to reject `../..`, `%2e%2e` and `%252e%252e` — and to **pass** `..%2f..%2fsecret`, because the encoded-slash allowance admits `%2f` and the firewall only rejects `.` and `..` as whole segments. That second assertion is the one worth having: if it ever fails, the allowance changed, and the controller's own check is still not redundant. Four faults each turn the intended tests red: removing the check (the pre-fix state), letting the rule accept `..`, dropping the back-slash normalisation so the Windows spelling escapes, and un-sharing the rule so the skill editor drifts back to its own copy. Ordinary assets and an encoded space still resolve, and a genuine 404 still reaches the SPA index. 7193 tests green.
- **T966** — The answer stream and the copilot now reach both filters, and a declared cost classification the coverage test enforces means a new public SN path cannot skip them silently.

## Block AO — Migration tooling (Algolia / Elasticsearch → Turing, §XXXVIII)

- **T661** — **Shadow / dual-run comparison, closing Block AO.** De-risks a cutover by measuring relevance parity before flipping traffic: `POST /api/sn/migrate/compare` runs a query set against both the source engine and the migrated Turing SN site and diffs the top-N result sets. Source ids come from new read-only `search(...)` methods on `TurElasticsearchClient` (`_search` `query_string`) and `TurAlgoliaClient` (`/query`); Turing ids come from the same internal path an end user hits (`TurSNSearchProcess.search`, guarded by `existsByTurSNSiteAndLanguage`), reading `id` off each `TurSNSiteSearchDocumentBean`. The pure `TurMigrationCompareMetrics` scores each query — set overlap (Jaccard |∩|/|∪|), **source recall** (fraction of the source's top-N Turing reproduced), and **top-1 match** — plus aggregate averages and a zero-overlap count. CLI `turing migrate compare --engine … --queries-file <path>` (one query per line, `#` comments ignored) prints the parity report as percentages. `TurMigrationCompareMetricsTest` (6) + CLI compare cases; backend 24 + CLI 43 tests green. No Liquibase. **Block AO (T657–T661) complete** — the deferred ideas T660/T661 were built on request.
- **T660** — **Field-mapping override layer.** A source schema is rarely 1:1 with the desired Turing schema, so an optional `overrides` list on both migration requests reshapes it on the way in — no record editing. Each `TurMigrationOverride` targets one source field (dotted) and can **rename** it (moves both the manifest field and every document attribute), **retype** it (force the manifest `type`), **drop** it (excluded from manifest + documents), or set a **default** value injected only where the record leaves the field absent (a `type` with no matching source field materialises a constant field). The pure `TurMigrationOverrides` applies the same overrides to `applyToFields` (the derived manifest) and `applyToAttributes` (each document) so schema and data stay consistent; both ES (T657) and Algolia (T658) services run it before provisioning and per document, and the reported `fieldsDetected` reflects the post-override manifest. CLI: `turing migrate … --overrides-file <path>` reads a JSON array (`cli.ts` validates it's an array; `buildMigrateBody` omits an empty list). `TurMigrationOverridesTest` (4) + `migrate.test.ts` override case; backend 18 + CLI 40 tests green. No Liquibase.
- **T659** — **CLI `turing migrate elasticsearch|algolia`.** The real "step 2" of a switch, in the zero-dependency `@viglet/turing-cli`: `turing migrate elasticsearch --source-url … --index … --site …` and `turing migrate algolia --app-id … --api-key … --index … --site …` wrap the T657/T658 backend seams, reusing the existing `TuringClient` (auth + CSRF) to POST `/api/sn/migrate/{engine}` and print a readable summary. `--dry-run` prints the derived manifest (field name/type/facet/multi) without provisioning or importing; `--se-instance`, `--locale`, `--batch-size`, `--max-documents`, and (Algolia) `--use-llm` tune the run. Source credentials stay on the source flags (`--source-user`/`--source-password`/`--source-api-key` for ES; `--app-id`/`--api-key` for Algolia), distinct from the Turing connection flags. New `commands/migrate.ts` (`buildMigrateBody` + `formatMigrationResult`, both pure/tested), wired into `cli.ts` dispatch + help; README documents the command. `migrate.test.ts` (6, `node:test`) green; full CLI suite 39 green; `tsc` clean. A switching team runs one command instead of writing an ETL script.
- **T658** — **Algolia index importer (backend seam).** `POST /api/sn/migrate/algolia` (role-gated, `TurSNMigrationAPI`) migrates an Algolia index from just an app id + API key + index name + target site — REST over `java.net.http.HttpClient` (`TurAlgoliaClient`: `/settings`, cursor-paged `/browse`, `/synonyms/search`), no Algolia SDK. Algolia has **no strict field-type mapping**, so the schema is inferred from a sample of records with the shared T387 kernel derivation — deterministic `VigletManifestSampleAnalyzer` + `VigletManifestHeuristics`, or an LLM draft (`VigletManifestDeriver`) when `useLlm` is set and one is configured — then `TurAlgoliaManifestBuilder` overlays the index *settings*: `attributesForFaceting` → `facet:true`, `searchableAttributes` → ensured-present `TEXT`, `customRanking` attrs surfaced, all with Algolia modifier-unwrapping (`unordered(x)`/`filterOnly(x)`/`searchable(x)`/`asc(x)`) + comma-group splitting. `objectID` becomes the SN document id; records reshape through the shared `TurMigrationDocumentMapper` (extracted from T657's ES mapper, now delegating) and push through the shared `TurMigrationImportService` (manifest provision + batched CREATE + COMMIT). **Synonyms are surfaced, not silently dropped:** the importer fetches them and reports the count with guidance to apply them in the target engine's schema (Turing has no managed synonym API yet — see Block AP), noting Lucene's lack of synonym support. `dryRun` returns the derived manifest without provisioning/importing. Pure-transform unit tests `TurAlgoliaManifestBuilderTest` (4) green; ES tests still green after the mapper extraction. No Liquibase.
- **T657** — **Elasticsearch index importer (backend seam).** Turns the manual "step 2" of the switch-from-Elasticsearch guide into one API call. `POST /api/sn/migrate/elasticsearch` (role-gated `ROLE_ADMIN`/`SN_CREATE`, `TurSNMigrationAPI`) takes only a URL + credentials (basic auth or an ES API key) + source index name + target SN site — **no vendor SDK**, just REST over `java.net.http.HttpClient` (`TurElasticsearchClient`: `_mapping`, then the Scroll API `_search?scroll` → `_search/scroll` → `DELETE _search/scroll`), so it works identically against Elastic Cloud and a self-managed cluster. The deterministic core `TurElasticsearchManifestBuilder` maps an ES `_mapping` onto a neutral `VigletFieldManifest` (`text`→`TEXT`, `keyword`→`STRING`, numeric/date/`boolean` → the matching `TurSEFieldType`; `keyword`-family + `boolean` → `facet:true`; `object`/`nested` recurse into dotted `parent.child` names; unsupported types — geo/vector/completion — **skipped with a warning**, never silently dropped), inferring `multiValued` from a sample of documents since a mapping never declares cardinality. Documents scroll a page at a time, each reshaped by `TurEsDocumentMapper` (dotted-name flattening, list-of-nested → per-leaf multi-valued lists, the T381 grounding contract — absent/blank/empty values dropped) and pushed as batched CREATE job items through the existing `TurSNImportAPI.send` (RAG-aware) via the shared `TurMigrationImportService`, which reuses `TurSNSiteManifestService.provision` so an importer-created field is byte-identical to a manifested one (T382/T386); a trailing COMMIT flushes the engine. `dryRun=true` returns the derived manifest + detected-field count without provisioning or importing (review before commit); `maxDocuments`/`batchSize`/scroll keep-alive/sample size tune via `turing.migration.*` (`TurMigrationProperty`). Pure-transform unit tests `TurElasticsearchManifestBuilderTest` (5) + `TurEsDocumentMapperTest` (5) green. No Liquibase.

## Block AP — Native synonyms & query expansion (parity+ with Algolia)

- **T667** — **AI-assisted synonym mining (better-than-Algolia), closing Block AP.** The differentiator Algolia can't match natively: Turing already computes embeddings, so it can *suggest* synonyms. Pure `TurSynonymClusterer` groups terms by embedding proximity with a greedy nearest-centroid cosine pass (running-mean centroids, configurable threshold + max cluster size), emitting only groups of ≥2 as candidate sets. `TurSynonymMiningService` reads a site's most-searched query-log terms (`TurSNSiteMetricAccessRepository.topTerms`), embeds each via the default embedding model (`TurRagContextBuilder.buildFromGlobalSettings().embeddingModel().embed`), clusters them, and returns **disabled** `REGULAR` proposals (id `null`, never persisted, never auto-applied — honouring the grounding invariant); gracefully returns empty when no default embedding model is configured. New `POST /api/sn/{id}/synonym/mine?locale=&limit=&threshold=`. Frontend: a **"Suggest from search logs"** action on the synonyms page mines then stages the proposals as disabled rules (`useMineSynonyms` → mine + batch) for the admin to review and enable — turning synonyms from a chore into a suggested-and-approved workflow. `TurSynonymClustererTest` (4) + `TurSynonymMiningServiceTest` (3, over mocked embedding/query-log seams); 42 Block AP backend tests green; frontend `tsc` clean. The optional semantic query-expansion-at-search-time remains a future follow-up. No Liquibase. **Block AP (T662–T667) complete.**
- **T666** — **Friendly synonyms admin UI + "Import from Algolia".** The bento admin surface for the whole block, plus the endpoint that closes the migration loop. **Backend:** pure `TurAlgoliaSynonymMapper` maps the raw Algolia synonym objects (which T658 could only surface) onto Turing DTOs — Algolia's five types map 1:1 (`synonym`→REGULAR, `oneWaySynonym`→ONE_WAY, `altCorrection1/2`→ALTERNATIVE_CORRECTION_1/2, `placeholder`→PLACEHOLDER), skipping malformed entries; `TurSNSynonymImportService` fetches via the SDK-free `TurAlgoliaClient` and batch-persists via `TurSNSynonymService`; new `POST /api/sn/{id}/synonym/import/algolia`. **Frontend:** a bento page under the SN site (`/bento/sn/instance/:id/synonym`) — a `BentoListPage` mosaic of rules with a per-engine **support banner**, a **Push to engine** action (`POST …/apply`), and an inline **Import from Algolia** panel; a per-type editor page (`BentoHero` + `BentoFormSection` + `BentoSaveBar`) with a type/locale picker, a friendly chip-based terms editor, a directional-`input` field shown only for the directional types, and an enable toggle. React-Query hooks (`sn-synonym.queries.ts` + `queryKeys.synonyms`), a `TurSNSiteSynonymService`, a new `SN_SECTIONS` tile, routes, and en/pt i18n. `TurAlgoliaSynonymMapperTest` (3); frontend `tsc -b` clean. No Liquibase.
- **T665** — **Lucene synonym impl.** Completes the seam across all three engines. Pure `TurLuceneSynonymMapBuilder` compiles the neutral rules into a real Lucene `SynonymMap`: REGULAR → fully bidirectional (every term expands to every other), ONE_WAY → directional, `ALTERNATIVE_CORRECTION_1/2` → degrade to one-way with a warning (Lucene's filter has no per-typo budget), PLACEHOLDER → **surfaced as unsupported** (the honest Lucene-limits caveat), multi-word phrases joined via `SynonymMap.Builder.join`. Because the embedded store is single-JVM, "apply" swaps the map an in-JVM `TurLuceneSynonymRegistry` holds per core — no reindex. The Lucene plugin (`supportsSynonyms()=true`) registers it in `applySynonyms`; `TurLuceneQueryBuilder` consults it at query time through a new `TurLuceneSynonymAnalyzer` (StandardTokenizer + lowercase + `SynonymGraphFilter`) on the main text query, threaded the search locale from `TurLucene`. **Zero overhead when unused** — the registry short-circuits on `isEmpty()` so sites without synonyms keep the plain `StandardAnalyzer` path unchanged. `TurLuceneSynonymMapBuilderTest` (5, verified end-to-end by running the built map through the analyzer and asserting token expansions). No Liquibase.
- **T664** — **Elasticsearch synonym impl.** The second engine behind the T663 seam. Pure `TurElasticsearchSynonymRulesBuilder` maps the neutral rules onto ES Synonyms API rule lines (Solr/WordNet line format): REGULAR → an equivalent line `"tv, television, telly"`, ONE_WAY → an explicit mapping `"tablet => ipad, galaxy tab"`, `ALTERNATIVE_CORRECTION_1/2` → degrade to one-way with a warning, PLACEHOLDER → reported unsupported (same honest-degradation contract as Solr). The ES plugin (`supportsSynonyms()=true`) PUTs the set to `turing_<locale>` via the official `co.elastic.clients` Synonyms API (new `TurElasticsearchUtils.putSynonymSet` → `client.synonyms().putSynonym(id, synonymsSet)`), so a `synonym_graph` filter with `updateable:true` on the search analyzer reloads it **without reindexing**; fail-open with an actionable warning when the analyzer doesn't reference the set. `TurElasticsearchSynonymRulesBuilderTest` (6). No Liquibase.
- **T663** — **Search-engine plugin synonym seam + Solr query-time impl.** Gives the abstraction a way to push the T662 source of truth into an engine at query time. `TurSearchEnginePlugin` gains `supportsSynonyms()` (default `false`) and `applySynonyms(seInstance, indexName, locale, rules)` (default `TurSESynonymApplyResult.unsupported`), consuming the engine-neutral, lazy-free `TurSESynonymRule` projection (the service maps entities inside its transaction). Results are reported via `TurSESynonymApplyResult` — `applied` count + `unsupportedTypes` + `warnings` — so a rule an engine can't represent is **surfaced, never silently dropped**. Pure `TurSolrManagedSynonymPayloadBuilder` maps rules onto Solr's Managed Synonyms map form: REGULAR → symmetric (each term keys to the others), ONE_WAY → one directional key, `ALTERNATIVE_CORRECTION_1/2` → degrade to one-way with a warning (Solr has no per-rule typo budget), PLACEHOLDER → reported unsupported. The Solr plugin (`supportsSynonyms()=true`) PUTs the map to `/schema/analysis/synonyms/turing_<locale>` (new `TurSolrUtils.putManagedSynonyms`) then reloads the core (`reloadCore`) — a **no-reindex** query-time change matching Algolia; fail-open with an actionable warning if the configset lacks the `ManagedSynonymGraphFilterFactory`. `TurSNSynonymApplyService` (loads the site by id in a read tx to walk the lazy `site→instance→vendor` graph) drives per-locale apply; new REST `GET …/synonym/support` (engine capability banner) + `POST …/synonym/apply?locale=`. `TurSolrManagedSynonymPayloadBuilderTest` (6) + `TurSNSiteSynonymAPITests` extended (support/apply over the Lucene site → honest unsupported); 21 synonym tests green. No Liquibase.
- **T662** — **Synonym model + management API (engine-agnostic source of truth).** Turing gains a synonym subsystem — the gap the Algolia importer (T658) could only *surface*. A `TurSNSynonym` row is authored **per SN site + locale**, one of five Algolia-parity `TurSNSynonymType`s (`REGULAR`, `ONE_WAY`, `ALTERNATIVE_CORRECTION_1`/`_2`, `PLACEHOLDER`), carrying an optional `input` (the LHS for the directional types) plus an ordered `terms` token list (`@ElementCollection` → `sn_site_synonym_term`) and an `enabled` flag; scoping flows through the `sn_site` FK (which carries the `@TenantId`), like the sibling spotlight/locale children. Pure `TurSNSynonymNormalizer` trims + case-insensitively de-duplicates tokens and enforces the per-type shape (REGULAR needs ≥2 distinct terms; the directional types need a non-blank `input` + ≥1 term) so a malformed rule never reaches an engine — invalid input raises `IllegalArgumentException` → HTTP 400 via the global handler. `TurSNSynonymService` does CRUD + substring **search** + **batch** upsert (create/update by id, the write path the T666 Algolia import will reuse) and exposes `synonymsForApply(site, locale)` (JOIN-FETCHed enabled rules) for the T663+ engine seam. REST `TurSNSiteSynonymAPI` at `/api/sn/{snSiteId}/synonym` (list/get/create/update/delete/`batch`), secured like the console siblings. Liquibase `v2026.3.1.95`. `TurSNSynonymNormalizerTest` (6) + `TurSNSiteSynonymAPITests` (7, full-context CRUD/batch/validation over a created Lucene site) green.

## Block AQ — Knowledge Base: microthesauri & index-time term expansion (§XL)

- **T675** — **LLM-assisted hierarchy generation** (CLOSES Block AQ). Draft a controlled-vocabulary microthesaurus from a domain+language prompt, review it, then persist — never auto-saved. A pluggable seam `TurThesaurusHierarchyGenerator` (the T387-style "deriver behind an interface so CI needs no real model") with default impl `TurLlmThesaurusHierarchyGenerator` driving the default LLM through the **T426 strict-schema structured-output path** (OpenAI `json_schema` / Anthropic forced tool, schema bound to the draft record) — no free-text-JSON fallback, throws a clear error when the default LLM can't do structured output. `TurThesaurusGenerationService` **sanitises** the draft (drops dangling/self `broader` and `related`, drops label-less terms) and, on accept, serialises it to the T673 **authority-file XML** and imports it through the shipped `TurAuthorityFileImportService` — persistence reuses one tested BT/RT/variation mapping (the label is always emitted as the first variation so the importer detects the tree language + recognises it). Endpoints `POST /api/kb/{kbId}/microthesaurus/generate` (returns the draft, no persistence) + `POST …/from-draft` (materialise). Frontend: a "Generate with AI" bento section on the KB page (domain/language/guidance → generate → read-only indented tree preview with RT chips → Create/Discard), model/service/React-Query mutations, en/pt i18n. `TurThesaurusGenerationServiceTest` (4): dangling/self-ref sanitisation, label-less drop + never-persists, blank-input rejection, and a **draft→XML→import round-trip** asserting the BT spine, the RELATED edge, and variations land in the domain. No Liquibase (reuses T668 schema + T673 importer).
- **T678** — **Query-time related-term suggestions.** A "related concepts" surface built on the microthesaurus `RELATED` (RT) links, the associative sibling of the Solr spell-check "did you mean". New `GET /api/sn/{site}/{locale}/related-terms?q=…` (`TurSNSiteRelatedTermsAPI`) → `TurRelatedTermService.suggest(site, locale, q)`: reuses the T671 recognition dictionary to spot controlled-vocabulary terms in the raw query, then walks each recognised term's `RELATED` edges in **both directions** (a reciprocal RT pair is stored as one bidirectional edge per ADR 0003/T673 — outgoing `findByTurThesaurusTermAndType` + incoming `findByTargetTermId`), resolves the enabled neighbour labels (deduped, self excluded), and returns one `{term, related[]}` per recognised term. Read-only, defensive (never throws — empty on any failure), no query expansion; independent of the T672 index-time enable flag (works whenever a site has selections). Both JS SDKs get the wrapper (T406 parity): vanilla `fetchRelatedTerms` + `TurRelatedTermSuggestion` type, react-sdk `fetchRelatedTerms` + `useTuringRelatedTerms` hook (mirrors `useTuringSpellCheck`: auto-lookup, `relatedLabels` convenience, request-id race guard). `TurRelatedTermServiceTest` (5): both-direction neighbours, disabled-neighbour skip, no-edge, opted-out site, blank query. Parity contract entry added. No Liquibase (reuses T668 schema).
- **T677** — **Hierarchical concept facet.** Opt-in second field `microthesaurus_path` on the per-site index config (`pathFacetEnabled` + `pathFieldName`, both defaulting off/`microthesaurus_path`). When enabled, `TurSNMicrothesaurusIndexer` also emits **level-prefixed cumulative path tokens** for every recognised term (`0/Doença`, `1/Doença/Doença respiratória`, `2/Doença/Doença respiratória/Pneumonia`) — the standard Solr hierarchical-faceting shape — deduped across a document, with a slash in any label escaped so it can't forge a level boundary. The path field is provisioned as a **STRING** multi-valued facet field (docValues, exact tokens) rather than tokenised TEXT, so drill-down values stay intact; `saveConfig` provisions both fields (TEXT terms + STRING path) when the config is enabled. Off by default keeps the MVP flat-terms path byte-identical. Backend `TurSNSiteMicrothesaurusConfig`/DTO/service + indexer, Liquibase `v2026.3.1.98` (idempotent `columnExists` add-column), frontend model + bento index-config toggle/field + en/pt i18n. Tests: indexer path-token emission + slash-escape + off-path, config service provisions-both. `TurSNMicrothesaurusIndexerTest`/`TurSNSiteMicrothesaurusServiceTest`/`TurLiquibaseChangelogAgnosticTest` green.
- **T679** — **Term-tree full editing.** Extends the T676 tree with an inline `ThesaurusTermEditor` (pencil action per row): rename, enable/disable, scope note, **reparent** via a broader-term picker, edit recognition **variations** (surface form + case/accent checkboxes, add/remove), and manage non-hierarchical **relations** (type `RELATED`/`USE`/`USED_FOR`/`CUSTOM` + a target-term picker drawn from the same microthesaurus). Saves via `useUpdateTerm` (`PUT …/term/{id}`), which invalidates the term list so the tree + chips refresh. Blank variations and target-less relations are dropped on save. Frontend `tsc -b` clean; en/pt i18n. Drag-reorder of siblings (visual `termOrder`) is intentionally left out — reparent/order is handled by the parent picker; a drag affordance can be a later polish. **This completes every implementable task in Block AQ** (T668–T674, T676, T679–T681); only the 💭 ideas remain (T675 LLM hierarchy generation, T677 hierarchical concept facet, T678 query-time related-term suggestions).
- **T681** — **Bento per-site Thesaurus selection + config (frontend for T670).** A new SN-instance sub-section at `/bento/sn/instance/:id/microthesaurus` (linked from the SN config-sections panel with `IconSitemap`) where a site opts into thesaurus expansion. Two frosted sections: **Index configuration** — an `enable` switch (off = legacy indexing), `fieldName`, `boost`, and `includeSynonyms` toggle, saved via `PUT /api/sn/{id}/microthesaurus/config`; and **Selected microthesauri** — the site's selections with a per-row enable switch and remove, plus a two-step picker (choose library → choose microthesaurus, already-selected ones filtered out) that adds via `POST /api/sn/{id}/microthesaurus`. New models (`TurSNSiteMicrothesaurus`, `TurSNSiteMicrothesaurusConfig`), service methods + React Query hooks (`useSiteSelections`/`useSiteConfig`/`useSelectForSite`/`useSetSiteSelectionEnabled`/`useDeselectForSite`/`useSaveSiteConfig`) with keyed invalidation. Frontend `tsc -b` clean; en/pt i18n. **Closes the Block AQ frontend** (T676/T680/T681); remaining are the 💭 ideas (T675 LLM generation, T677 hierarchical facet, T678 related-term suggestions) and the T679 advanced tree-editing follow-up.
- **T680** — **Bento seed-library browse + import + XML upload.** On the thesaurus-library (KB) detail page, a new frosted "Seed library & import" section lists the bundled seed microthesauri (`GET /api/kb/seeds`) with a one-click **Import** into the current library (`useImportSeed`), plus an **Upload XML** control that posts a Turing Thesaurus Exchange authority file to the T673 importer (`POST /api/kb/{kbId}/microthesaurus/import`, multipart, via a hidden file input). Both invalidate the microthesauri list so the new tree appears immediately; toasts on success/failure. Also **fixed a base-URL bug** in `TurThesaurusService`: axios is configured with `baseURL: /api`, so the T676 client's `/api/kb…` paths doubled to `/api/api/kb`; all Thesaurus URLs are now `/kb…` (resolving correctly to `/api/kb…`). Frontend `tsc -b` clean; new en/pt i18n keys. Full tree editing is T679; per-site selection is T681.
- **T676** — **Bento Thesaurus surface + friendly term tree.** The admin UI for curating controlled vocabularies, at `/bento/thesaurus` — branded **Thesaurus** (never "Knowledge Base"; that collides with generative-AI/RAG). Three bento pages: a `BentoListPage` mosaic of thesaurus libraries (Knowledge Bases); a KB detail (`BentoEntityShell` identity + a frosted microthesauri list with inline add — name/language/domain — and delete); and a microthesaurus detail (`BentoEntityShell` + language/domain `BentoFormSection` + the tree). The centerpiece is **`ThesaurusTermTree`**: builds a forest from the flat term list via the soft `parentTermId`, renders expandable frosted rows (chevron rotate, indentation) with **variation-count and per-type relation chips** (Related/Use/Used-for tones), disabled terms struck through, and hover actions to add a child, add a root term, or delete (confirmed) — inline label input with Enter-to-add. Wired end to end: `models/kb/thesaurus.model.ts`, `services/kb/thesaurus.service.ts` (over `/api/kb`), `api/queries/thesaurus.queries.ts` (React Query hooks for KB/microthesaurus/term/seed + mutations that invalidate by key), routes in `bento.routes.tsx`, a nav entry (`IconSitemap`, Enterprise Search, `SN_VIEW`) in `bento-nav.config.ts`, and full en/pt i18n (`thesaurus.json`). Frontend `tsc -b` clean; the 50 bento component tests stay green. Full variation/relation editing + drag-reorder is the follow-up T679; seed-import UI is T680; per-site selection UI is T681.
- **T674** — **Pre-populated EDUCATION seed library.** Ships ready-to-import microthesaurus hierarchies for the priority **education** domain in **pt / en / it** as classpath authority-file bundles under `kb/seed/education/` (12 terms each: levels of education → basic/higher, graduate degrees, distance learning, assessment — with broader/narrower spine, related edges, and recognition variations/synonyms). Following the T373/T655 seed-bundle precedent they are **not** loaded at startup — `TurThesaurusSeedService` scans `classpath*:kb/seed/**/*.authority-file.xml`, derives each seed's id/name/domain/language, and imports a chosen one into a Knowledge Base on demand via the T673 `TurAuthorityFileImportService`. Browse-and-import API (`TurThesaurusSeedAPI`): `GET /api/kb/seeds` lists the library, `POST /api/kb/{knowledgeBaseId}/seeds/{seedId}` imports. `TurThesaurusSeedServiceTest` (3): lists the three languages, imports `educacao-pt` (12 terms, `pt`, `EDUCATION`), rejects an unknown seed. **Naming note:** this block's user-facing surface is branded **Thesaurus** (not "Knowledge Base", which collides with generative-AI/RAG concepts); the seed format is the 100% Turing **Turing Thesaurus Exchange** XML (namespace `https://turing.viglet.org/xsd/thesaurus/1.0`) — vendor references removed across the block's code/XML/docs (classic semantic-navigation systems are inspiration only).
- **T673** — **XML importer + reconstructed authority-file contract.** The Turing Thesaurus Exchange *authority file* (microthesaurus export format, namespace `https://turing.viglet.org/xsd/thesaurus/1.0`) is now importable. Because the original DTD/XSD is unrecoverable (shallow clone, ADR 0003), the contract was **reverse-engineered from the verified 253-term real sample** into `turing-app/src/main/resources/kb/turing-thesaurus-1.0.xsd` — the shared contract for both this importer and the T675 LLM emission format (intentionally lenient: open relation-type token, lax `attributes`, so a faithful export never fails over cosmetic drift). `TurAuthorityFileImportService` parses the XML with a **XXE-hardened** namespace-aware DOM reader (doctype/external-entity resolution disabled — it is untrusted upload input) and maps it to the domain: `authorityFile/name` → microthesaurus name; the variation `language` (ISO 639-2/B `por` → JVM `pt` via a `getISO3Language` lookup) → the tree's locale; **`BT` broader → this term's `parentTermId`, `NT` narrower → the target's `parentTermId`** (the reciprocal spine collapses onto one soft parent pointer); **`RT` → a single `RELATED` bidirectional edge per unordered pair** (reciprocal-pair dedupe), `U` → `USE`, `UF` → `USED_FOR` directed edges; `variations` → recognition surface forms (`case ci`→case-insensitive, `accent as`→accent-sensitive). Self-relations and duplicate `(source,target,type)` edges are dropped; term ids are pre-assigned UUIDs so the soft FKs resolve without flush-order dependence, and the source numeric id is kept in `externalId` for round-trip. Endpoint `POST /api/kb/{knowledgeBaseId}/microthesaurus/import` (multipart). Importing evicts the recognition-dictionary cache. `TurAuthorityFileImportServiceTest` (8): BT/NT spine, RT dedupe + self-skip, U/UF, variation flags, language mapping, non-authority-file rejection, **real 253-term sample imports fully**, and **the reconstructed XSD validates the real sample** (contract fidelity). No Liquibase (reuses the T668 schema).
- **T672** — **Index-time expansion enricher.** The payoff of the block: at index time every document is expanded with the root→term hierarchical path of each controlled-vocabulary term it mentions, written to a dedicated multi-valued field — recall + concept-facet browse with zero query-time cost (ADR 0003 §3). New `TurSNMicrothesaurusIndexer` in `com.viglet.turing.sn.kb`, modelled exactly on the T472 `TurSNContentFitIndexer`: wired into `TurSNProcessQueue.index(...)` **after** the content-fit and Gemini-media enrichers, it (1) early-returns unless the site has an *enabled* `TurSNSiteMicrothesaurusConfig` — a site that never opted in indexes byte-for-byte as before; (2) fetches the cached T671 recognition dictionary for (site, document locale), returning early if empty (no selected microthesaurus matches the locale); (3) assembles the title/description/body text (same helper shape as the content-fit indexer); (4) recognises every mentioned term in one Aho-Corasick pass; (5) unions their pre-computed ancestor paths, deduped, preserving order; (6) provisions the multi-valued TEXT field via `TurSNFieldProvisioner` (its auto `_str` copy field is the concept facet) and writes the list to `attributes`. **Defensive by construction** — any failure is swallowed and logged so a thesaurus problem can never block a document from indexing. The field participates in recall through the standard text pipeline and is faceted via `_str`; the config's stored `boost` is retained for future per-field query weighting (no `qf`-assembly hook exists in the SN query path today, and reworking stable search across Solr/ES/Lucene is a documented non-goal). `TurSNMicrothesaurusIndexerTest` (4 — end-to-end through the real recognizer: full path written, opted-out/no-config/no-match sites untouched) + `TurSNProcessQueueTest` updated for the new enricher dependency (green). No Liquibase / no schema change.
- **T671** — **Recognition dictionary + Aho-Corasick matcher (cached).** The engine that turns a site's selected microthesauri into a fast, single-pass term recogniser for index-time expansion. New `com.viglet.turing.sn.kb` package: `TurTextNormalizer` (length-preserving lowercase + accent-fold, so a match offset in folded text is also valid in the original — no position map needed), a generic `TurAhoCorasick<P>` automaton (goto/fail/output, O(text+matches)), `TurRecognizedTerm` (a canonical term + its pre-computed root→term ancestor path), and `TurRecognitionDictionary` (an immutable compiled read-model: a folded-text automaton over every surface form; `recognize(text)` finds all mentions in one pass, enforcing **word boundaries** and re-verifying each variation's `case`/`accent` flags against the *original* slice — case- and accent-insensitive is the recall baseline, flags only tighten). `TurMicrothesaurusDictionaryService` compiles + caches one dictionary per (site, locale): on a miss it loads the site's *enabled* selections whose `language` matches the document locale, walks each tree once to pre-compute paths, and registers every surface form — preferred label (matched permissively for recall) + variations + (when the config's `includeSynonyms` is on) `USE`/`USED_FOR` synonyms resolving to the **preferred** term so a synonym still expands to the canonical path. The compiled dictionary is an immutable read-model in an in-process map (caching-skill rule: cache read-models never repositories, always paired with an eviction); `evictAll()` is called on every KB / term / selection / config write (`TurThesaurusTermService`, `TurMicrothesaurusService`, `TurSNSiteMicrothesaurusService`). Cyclic parent chains are depth-guarded. `TurRecognitionDictionaryTest` (6) + `TurMicrothesaurusDictionaryServiceTest` (5) green; existing KB service tests updated for the new eviction dependency (26 green in the block). No Liquibase / no schema change.
- **T670** — **Per-site microthesaurus selection + index config.** The opt-in seam that lets an SN site subscribe to Knowledge Base microthesauri for index-time expansion (ADR 0003 §4). Two entities under `persistence/model/kb/`: `TurSNSiteMicrothesaurus` (join — a site selected a microthesaurus via a **soft FK** `microthesaurusId` into the `kb_*` library + a per-selection `enabled` flag; a real `@ManyToOne` to `TurSNSite` so selections cascade-delete with the site) and `TurSNSiteMicrothesaurusConfig` (one row per site: `fieldName` / `boost` / `includeSynonyms` + a master `enabled`, defaults `microthesaurus_terms` / `0.8` / synonyms-on per the ADR). `TurSNSiteMicrothesaurusService` (`@Transactional`) does selection CRUD (validates the microthesaurus exists + uniqueness → `IllegalArgumentException` → 400), returns a transient defaults config when none is stored, and — the roadmap's "**wires `TurSNFieldProvisioner`**" — on saving an *enabled* config converges the backing multi-valued TEXT field (whose auto `_str` copy field becomes the concept facet) across every locale core via a `TurSNJobAttributeSpec`; provisioning is idempotent and swallowed-on-failure so it never fails a config save. `TurSNSiteMicrothesaurusAPI` at `/api/sn/{snSiteId}/microthesaurus` (list/select/enable/deselect + `/config` GET/PUT). Deleting a microthesaurus now also drops its per-site selections (soft FK, no DB cascade). Liquibase `v2026.3.1.97` (2 tables, portable lowercase types, idempotent `MARK_RAN`). `TurSNSiteMicrothesaurusServiceTest` (7 — selection validation, config defaults, provisioning fires only when enabled) green. **Opt-in: a site with no selection indexes byte-identically to before.**
- **T669** — **Console CRUD API for the Knowledge Base.** REST authoring for the whole KB → microthesaurus → term tree so customers create new terms and adjust existing ones during a project. Records under `persistence/dto/kb/` (`TurKnowledgeBaseDto`, `TurMicrothesaurusDto`, `TurThesaurusTermDto` carrying its `variations` + `relations` inline, plus variation/relation sub-DTOs). Three `@Service`s under `com.viglet.turing.kb` (`TurKnowledgeBaseService`, `TurMicrothesaurusService`, `TurThesaurusTermService`) doing `@Transactional` CRUD straight through the JPA repositories (bounded domain-layer rule) with entity↔DTO mapping + required-field validation (blank name/label/domain or missing language/relation-target raise `IllegalArgumentException` → HTTP 400 via the global handler); the term service maps the embeddable `variations` (skipping blanks), wires each relation back to its owning term, and guards a self-parent. Three `@RestController`s under `api/kb/` — `/api/kb` (KB), `/api/kb/{knowledgeBaseId}/microthesaurus`, `/api/kb/microthesaurus/{microthesaurusId}/term` (with `?roots=true` and `/{id}/children` for tree navigation) — admin-secured by the config's `anyRequest().authenticated()` rule, `TurNotFoundException` → 404 for missing parents/entities. Mockito unit tests over mocked repositories: `TurKnowledgeBaseServiceTest` (6), `TurThesaurusTermServiceTest` (5), `TurMicrothesaurusServiceTest` (3) — 14 green. No Liquibase (reuses the T668 schema).
- **T668** — **Domain model + persistence for the Knowledge Base.** The foundation of a tenant-scoped library of hierarchical microthesauri (controlled vocabularies, inspired by classic controlled-vocabulary semantic-navigation systems) that SN sites opt into for index-time term expansion. Entities under `persistence/model/kb/`: `TurKnowledgeBase` (aggregate root, carries the Hibernate `@TenantId`) → `TurMicrothesaurus` (one tree per `language`:Locale + `domain`:String) → `TurThesaurusTerm` (a term node whose broader/narrower hierarchy is a **soft `parentTermId`** id-pointer — the `TurChatFlowState.parentStateId` precedent, no self-referencing JPA entity — so the index-time ancestor walk stays a plain loop; `null` = root) → `TurThesaurusTermRelation` (non-hierarchical edges: `TurThesaurusRelationType {BROADER,NARROWER,RELATED,USE,USED_FOR,CUSTOM}` + `TurThesaurusRelationDirectionality {UNIDIRECTIONAL,BIDIRECTIONAL}`, target as a soft id-pointer). `TurThesaurusTerm` also owns an `@ElementCollection` of `TurThesaurusTermVariation` (recognition surface forms — `surfaceForm` + normalized `caseSensitive`/`accentSensitive` booleans mapped from `ci`/`as` + `weight` + `language`); recognition (variations) is deliberately separate from expansion (parent spine). The relation set + variation flags are grounded in the verified 253-term Turing Thesaurus Exchange authority-file sample (`docs/references/`). Four `Tur*Repository extends JpaRepository<_,String>` (KB/microthesaurus/term/relation) with locale/domain/parent finders + a `JOIN FETCH` variations reader for the T671 dictionary compiler. Liquibase `v2026.3.1.96` (5 tables, portable lowercase types, idempotent `MARK_RAN`). `TurThesaurusModelTest` (6 — defensive collection setters, soft-parent root semantics, variation/relation defaults, full relation-type coverage) + 4 repository-contract tests + `TurLiquibaseChangelogAgnosticTest` green (11 total). Architecture: `docs/adr/0003-knowledge-base-microthesaurus.md`.

## Block AR — Live-demo search & chat quality (Lucene parity gaps, §XLI)

- **T682** — **RAG sources deduped by document.** Chat "Sources" listed one chip per retrieved *chunk*: a document is split into many chunks at index time and every chunk carries the same `source_id`/`url`, so the same source rendered repeatedly. New static `TurRagSource.dedupeByDocument(List)` groups by `url` (fallback `sourceId`), keeps the highest-`score()` chunk, and preserves first-seen ranking order (`LinkedHashMap` re-put keeps position); anonymous chunks with neither key stay distinct. Applied at the two emission chokepoints so both retrieval surfaces are covered: the agent tool path (`TurChatStreamingDispatcher.drainRagSources`) and the public SN chat path (`TurSNGenAi.prependSourcesEvent`). `TurRagSourceTest` +4 (unify-by-url keeps best chunk, sourceId fallback, anonymous-distinct, null/singleton no-copy). Fixed in the product, not the demo.
- **T683** — **Lucene autocomplete keeps internal hyphens.** A suggestion for a hyphenated term (`self-hosting`) came back stripped (`selfhosting`) and clicking it returned nothing — `TurLuceneSearchEnginePlugin.collectPrefixMatches` cleaned tokens with `replaceAll("[^\\p{L}\\p{N}]","")`, removing *internal* punctuation, and the mangled token never matched the `StandardAnalyzer`-tokenized index. Extracted `normalizeSuggestionToken` that lower-cases and trims only *edge* punctuation, keeping internal hyphens/apostrophes; the verbatim term now round-trips through the main query analyzer (which splits `self-hosting` → `self`+`hosting`, matching the index) back to real results. `TurLuceneSearchEnginePluginTest` +3 (internal hyphen kept, edge-punctuation trimmed, lower-cased).
- **T684** — **Demo chat typing indicator.** The site chat demo showed an empty frosted chip while waiting for the first token: the SDK pre-commits an empty assistant bubble (so a UI can render a typing animation immediately), but the demo rendered it as empty content and the separate thinking-dots block never triggered (the last message was already the assistant). Fixed in `chat-demo.tsx` by rendering `TuringThinkingDots` inside the assistant bubble while its content is empty and busy. Demo-only.
- **T685** — **Lucene "similar documents" / More Like This.** "Similar" / "Related by AI" returned nothing on a Lucene-backed SN site because `TurLuceneSearchEnginePlugin` overrode neither `getSimilarDocuments` nor `getDocumentsByIds`, inheriting the interface's deliberate "degrade silently" empty-list defaults (both MLT and VECTOR modes of `TurSNSimilarDocumentsService` route through the plugin). Implemented `getDocumentsByIds` (a `TermQuery` fetch on `id` returning the standard fields in request order, dropping misses — this also unlocks the VECTOR neighbour path over the embedded Lucene store) and `getSimilarDocuments` via Lucene `org.apache.lucene.queries.mlt.MoreLikeThis` seeded from the doc's `title`/`abstract`/`text` (`minTermFreq=1`, `minDocFreq=1`, `minWordLen=3`, `maxQueryTerms=40`), excluding the seed and capping at `rows`. Shared seed-text/field helpers mirror the Solr/ES plugins; fail-open (empty on any error). `TurLuceneSearchEnginePluginTest` +4 over a real in-memory index (ids preserve order + drop misses, similar excludes seed and finds the topical neighbour, empty seed/blank ids).
- **T686** — **Lucene spell-check / "did you mean".** Spell-check was never abstracted into `TurSearchEnginePlugin` — `TurSNSiteSpellCheckAPI` + the main-search path injected `TurSolr` directly, so on a Lucene site the endpoint returned `null` and "did you mean" never appeared. Added a `spellCheck(siteName, term, locale)` seam to the plugin interface (default = uncorrected result); Solr delegates to its `/tur_spell` handler (`TurSolr.spellCheckTerm`), Lucene implements it with a `DirectSpellChecker` over the live index terms (token-by-token: a term present in any candidate text field is kept, an unindexed term is replaced by its best suggestion; corrected only when a token changed). Re-pointed `TurSNSiteSpellCheckAPI` at `TurSearchEnginePluginFactory.getPluginForSite(...)`, and the Lucene main-search path now populates `TurSEResults.spellCheck` in the plugin's `retrieveSearchResults` (reusing the open instance), giving Lucene inline did-you-mean parity with Solr. Added `lucene-suggest` dependency. `TurLuceneSearchEnginePluginTest` +3 (suggests for a misspelled term, keeps an indexed term, blank guard); `TurSNSiteSpellCheckAPITest` rewritten to the plugin-factory contract (3). Closes Block AR.
- **T1004 (the mode that streams it)** — the cause was this platform's default, not a missing capability: STREAM has worked since T18 and is validated at 100-300 chunks a turn, but an agent is CALL until an operator says otherwise and flipping the site's agent to STREAM would have traded away the tools on every admin surface that agent also serves; so TurAgentChatMode gains AUTO, which decides per turn instead of per agent and streams only when neither trade-off applies -- no flow to rewrite the bubble for and no tool callback to drop, which is exactly the public site turn because T647 strips every callback for an untrusted caller; STREAM's own semantics are untouched, and a newly provisioned default agent now ships AUTO while an existing one keeps whatever it had

## Block AS — Scalable & pluggable audio transcription (chunking + local backends, §XLII)

- **T687** — **Transcription config namespace + provider factory.** The transcription seam (Block AA) shipped one OpenAI-compatible impl whose endpoint/credential piggybacked the default `TurLLMInstance`, with a hardcoded model and a fixed 25 MiB guard — no way to point transcription elsewhere without a `@Primary` bean override. Introduced a config-selectable backbone: `TurTranscriptionProviderType {OPENAI, OPENAI_COMPATIBLE, LOCAL_ONNX, NONE}` (lenient `fromValue`, fallback `OPENAI`); a `TurTranscriptionProviderFactory` that indexes `TurTranscriptionProvider` beans by `getType()` and routes the active strategy (fallback to `OPENAI` when a selected backend has no bean yet), with `resolveActive()` as the single caller entry point; a `TurTranscriptionConfigResolver` producing the effective `TurTranscriptionConfig` by merging `turing.transcription.*` env props (win when set — pin per container) over the UI-editable DB Global Settings row over defaults. Dedicated transcription `endpoint`/`model`/`apiKey` (write-only, encrypted) / `maxUploadBytes` / `onnxModelSize` on `TurGlobalSettingsBean`+`TurGlobalSettingsService` (config-var keys `GLOBAL_TRANSCRIPTION_*`) and a new **Transcription (Speech-to-Text)** section on the Global Settings bento page (`/bento/settings`, Generative AI tab) mirroring `RagRerankSection` (strategy dropdown → conditional endpoint/model/write-only key/size/limit). `TurOpenAiTranscriptionProvider` now reads the resolved config with a fall-back to the default LLM instance for any unset field (legacy = byte-identical) and uses the configurable per-request limit; a `TurNoneTranscriptionProvider` serves the disabled backend fail-soft. `TurPersonaAudioDeriveService` routes through the factory. `TurTranscriptionConfigResolverTest` (3 — env-over-DB precedence, DB fallback, non-positive limit ignored) + `TurTranscriptionProviderFactoryTest` (3 — route by type, unregistered→OPENAI, `resolveActive` reads resolver) + updated persona/bean tests. The parameterized backbone the rest of Block AS builds on.
- **T688** — **`TurAudioChunker` seam + ffmpeg impl.** OpenAI's transcription endpoint hard-caps uploads at 25 MiB and byte-slicing a compressed container corrupts it, so large recordings were untranscribable. New provider-agnostic `TurAudioChunker` seam (`chunk(audio, mimeType, maxBytes)` → ordered `TurAudioChunk`s, `isChunkingNeeded`, `checkFfmpeg`/`isFfmpegAvailable`) with an ffmpeg-backed default `TurFfmpegAudioChunker`: probes duration with `ffprobe`, plans segments so each re-encoded chunk (16 kHz mono MP3 @ 32 kbps, ~4000 B/s) stays under the active limit, snaps interior cut points to detected silence (`silencedetect`) within an overlap-sized window (fixed-duration + configurable overlap fallback), and re-encodes each segment via `ffmpeg -ss/-t -ac 1 -ar 16000`. **Passthrough (no ffmpeg call) when audio ≤ limit**, and fail-open to passthrough on any ffmpeg error, so small-clip installs need no ffmpeg. The segment planner (`planSegments`) is a pure function; the subprocess wrapper mirrors the T80 Docker-probe discipline (virtual-thread stream reader, per-call timeout, `destroyForcibly`). New `turing.transcription.*` props: `ffmpegPath`/`ffprobePath`/`chunkOverlapSeconds`/`ffmpegTimeoutSeconds`. An "ffmpeg available?" probe endpoint (`GET /api/system/global-settings/transcription/ffmpeg/status`) + a **Check ffmpeg** button in the bento Transcription section (mirrors the Docker probe). `TurFfmpegAudioChunkerTest` (7 — passthrough never touches ffmpeg, empty input, chunking-needed threshold, bogus-binary probe negative, single/multi segment plans each under the byte limit + overlap wiring, silence snapping).
- **T689** — **Chunk-aware transcription orchestration.** New `TurTranscriptionService` sits **above** `TurTranscriptionProvider`: it resolves the active backend + per-request limit, chunks via `TurAudioChunker`, transcribes the chunks **bounded-parallel and in order** (fixed pool sized by `turing.transcription.chunk-concurrency`, default 3), then stitches the transcripts and aggregates the detected language. Stitching (`stitchTranscripts`, pure) dedupes the repeated overlap window by dropping the longest leading word-run of each chunk that already appears as the trailing word-run of the accumulated text (case/punctuation-insensitive compare, capped at 40 words); language aggregation picks the majority non-blank detected language (first-seen tie-break, else the caller's hint). A single passthrough chunk is a thin delegate (byte-identical to the pre-chunking path); any failed chunk fails the whole transcription (chunk-or-fail). Because it wraps the seam, **every** backend inherits large-file support for free, and the provider's own configurable limit guard (T687) is now only hit when chunking couldn't help (e.g. no ffmpeg). `TurPersonaAudioDeriveService` now routes through the orchestrator. `TurTranscriptionServiceTest` (9 — overlap dedupe incl. case/punctuation, no-overlap concat, blank-skip, majority/fallback language, single-chunk delegate, multi-chunk ordered stitch, chunk-or-fail) + persona test re-pointed at the orchestrator.
- **T690** — **Local OpenAI-compatible transcription backend (faster-whisper-server).** The recommended "run it locally" path: a config-selected `OPENAI_COMPATIBLE` strategy pointing the REST provider at a self-hosted server speaking the OpenAI `/audio/transcriptions` contract. Extracted the shared multipart POST into `TurOpenAiTranscriptionClient` (empty/blank-endpoint/oversize fail-soft guards; **omits the `Authorization` header when the API key is blank**, since self-hosted servers usually need none) and refactored `TurOpenAiTranscriptionProvider` (OPENAI, LLM-instance fallback) to delegate to it. New `TurOpenAiCompatibleTranscriptionProvider` (OPENAI_COMPATIBLE) uses a **dedicated** endpoint/model/key from the transcription config — decoupled from the default `TurLLMInstance` — and is `isAvailable()` only when a dedicated endpoint is set, otherwise fails with a clear "configure a dedicated endpoint" message. Same code path as the cloud provider; only the URL changes (scales horizontally, GPU-capable). Sidecar compose recipe + setup (UI and `turing.transcription.*` env) in [`docs/references/transcription-local-backend.md`](references/transcription-local-backend.md). `TurOpenAiCompatibleTranscriptionProviderTest` (4 — type, unavailable/fail-soft without endpoint never calls the client, delegates with the configured connection) + `TurOpenAiTranscriptionClientTest` (3 — empty audio, blank base URL, oversize actionable message).
- **T691** — **In-process `LOCAL_ONNX` Whisper backend — seam/scaffold.** The air-gapped, no-sidecar `LOCAL_ONNX` slot, shipped as a validated seam following the T193 Jlama discipline: the heavy in-process inference runtime (log-mel front-end + encoder/decoder ONNX decode loop, or a DJL Whisper translator) is **deliberately not bundled** — nothing Whisper-specific exists on the classpath today (`spring-ai-transformers` transitively provides DJL 0.32.0 + ONNX Runtime 1.20.0 + HF tokenizers, but no Whisper model/translator/audio-decoder), so committing a fragile dependency now was avoided. `TurWhisperModelSize {TINY, BASE, SMALL}` (lenient parse → `BASE`) resolves each size to a canonical HF repo id (`openai/whisper-*`) the future runtime downloads via `TurOnnxModelDownloader`. `TurLocalOnnxTranscriptionProvider` **owns the `LOCAL_ONNX` slot** (`getType`) precisely so an air-gapped selection never falls back to a cloud backend via the factory — a privacy footgun; `isAvailable()` is `false` and `transcribe()` fails soft with an actionable message ("…is not bundled…use OPENAI_COMPATIBLE with a self-hosted faster-whisper-server…Audio was NOT sent anywhere.") that never leaks audio off-box. The actual inference runtime is tracked as **T694** (💭). `TurLocalOnnxTranscriptionProviderTest` (4 — type, not-available, fail-soft-never-leaks message, lenient size parse).
- **T695** — **Remove the non-functional `LOCAL_ONNX` transcription backend (retires T694).** The in-process Whisper ONNX runtime (T694) was deliberately deferred (T193 Jlama discipline) and, after review, will not be built — so the `LOCAL_ONNX` **option** (a validated seam that only ever failed with "not bundled") is removed rather than left as a dead choice in the UI/API. Dropped the `LOCAL_ONNX` enum constant, `TurLocalOnnxTranscriptionProvider`, `TurWhisperModelSize`, the `onnxModelSize` field across `TurTranscriptionConfig` / `TurTranscriptionProperty` / config resolver / `TurGlobalSettingsBean` + service getter/updater, the `GLOBAL_TRANSCRIPTION_ONNX_MODEL_SIZE` config-var, and the admin bento **Model Size** dropdown + `LOCAL_ONNX` strategy option (+ i18n). The **supported fully-local path is unchanged**: `OPENAI_COMPATIBLE` pointed at a self-hosted server (e.g. faster-whisper-server). Transcription strategy is now `OPENAI` · `OPENAI_COMPATIBLE` · `NONE`; supersedes the T691 seam and is recorded as a non-goal in ROADMAP. Tests updated (factory unregistered-type fallback now exercises `OPENAI_COMPATIBLE`; config/bean/resolver tests drop the ONNX field); `TurLocalOnnxTranscriptionProviderTest` removed.
- **T693** — **AUDIO-slot wiring + per-backend metrics + confidence fallback.** Three closers on the block. (1) **AUDIO slot → transcription seam:** an AUDIO multi-modal slot upload (T64) now transcribes through the pluggable, chunk-aware `TurTranscriptionService` (any config-selected backend, past any per-request size limit) and writes the transcript into the first target text slot; it falls back to the Gemini native understanding path only when no transcription backend is configured. VIDEO still uses Gemini understanding (the visual track the audio-only seam can't cover). Previously only persona-from-audio used the seam and AUDIO slots were Gemini-only. (2) **Per-backend metrics:** `TurTranscriptionMetricsService` keeps a bounded in-process ring of samples per backend and produces one percentile row each (count, error rate, avg/p50/p95/max latency, total bytes) — the T88 "which is the p95 outlier?" pattern applied to transcription backends; every `transcribe` records one sample keyed by the backend that served it. Exposed at `GET /api/genai/transcription/metrics`. (3) **Confidence fallback:** `TurTranscriptionResult` gained an optional 0–1 `confidence`, populated by the OpenAI-compatible client from a `verbose_json` response's segment `avg_logprob` (mean → `exp` → clamped; `null` when the backend gives no signal). When `turing.transcription.confidence-fallback-enabled` is on, a failed or below-`confidence-threshold` result from the active (typically local) backend escalates once to `confidence-fallback-type` (default cloud `OPENAI`, re-chunked under its 25 MiB cap); default off = legacy single-backend behavior. `TurTranscriptionServiceTest` (+8 — confidence aggregation, fallback on failure / low-confidence, kept-primary when disabled / fallback-also-fails, per-backend metric recorded), `TurOpenAiTranscriptionClientTest` (+3 — logprob→confidence, null when absent, unit clamp), `TurChatMultiModalSlotServiceTest` (+1 — AUDIO transcribes through the seam, Gemini not invoked). Closes Block AS except the deferred in-process ONNX runtime (T694).
- **T716** — **Richer persona-from-audio derivation — two-call, whole-recording (§XLII.8).** The old derivation asked one LLM call for everything, capping the `systemInstruction` at "2-5 sentences" and clipping the transcript to 16k chars — so a 30-min recording (~42k chars) was analysed only for its first ~11 min and produced a thin, generic voice. Now `TurPersonaAudioDeriveService.draftFromTranscript` runs **two calls**: call 1 classifies the constrained fields (`name`/`description`/tone/verbosity/languageStyle/personaKind + audience readingLevel/domainExpertise/vocabularyCeiling/primaryLanguage) as strict single-line JSON; call 2 writes the `systemInstruction` as a **rich, multi-section character brief in plain markdown** (Who you are / background / How you speak — verbal tics, fillers, catchphrases, slang, metaphors quoted verbatim / How you reason / How you react / What you know / Rules / Notice), grounded only in the transcript and written in the transcript's own language (headings included). Splitting the long free-text brief out of the JSON avoids the truncation/escaping that breaks weak models on a 5k+ char multi-line string (`system_instruction` is already `longtext`, so it persists). `MAX_TRANSCRIPT_CHARS` raised 16k→60k so the **whole** recording is analysed. The deterministic fallbacks stay (name from the description slug, a traits-based voice contract when the brief call returns blank) so the two required fields are never empty, and both call outputs are logged at INFO. `TurPersonaAudioDeriveServiceTest` updated to stub the two calls independently (classify JSON + markdown brief), incl. the blank-brief→synthesised path.
- **T715** — **Async persona-from-audio with a live progress bar (§XLII.7).** Persona-from-audio (`POST /api/persona/derive-from-audio`) was one blocking multipart request that transcribed + LLM-analysed on the request thread, so a long recording showed only a spinner with no ETA. Added an **async** surface alongside the (kept) blocking one: `POST /api/persona/derive-from-audio/jobs` → QUEUED `jobId` (HTTP 429 when the bounded pool saturates), `GET .../jobs/{jobId}` poll, `GET .../jobs/{jobId}/stream` SSE. `TurPersonaAudioJobService` (bounded pool + retention sweep, reusing `turing.transcription.async-*`, mirroring `TurTranscriptionJobService`) runs the two phases explicitly so each reports progress: **TRANSCRIBING** (per-chunk `completedChunks/totalChunks`) → **ANALYZING** → SUCCEEDED (carries the never-saved draft + transcript) / FAILED, published over `TurPersonaAudioJobEventBus`. To make progress *move* on a long clip, `TurTranscriptionService.transcribe` gained a `TurTranscriptionProgressListener` overload (per-chunk callback from the bounded pool; legacy no-arg path unchanged) and an opt-in **`turing.transcription.max-chunk-seconds`** caps chunk *duration* so a long recording splits into several shorter chunks that parallelise (via `chunk-concurrency`) **and** report progress — instead of re-encoding down to a single chunk (0=legacy byte-limit-only). The bento "Derive from audio" button now submits the job, streams the SSE, and shows a phase-aware gradient progress bar ("Transcribing 3/7…" → "Analysing…") before opening the pre-filled review form. Also **hardened the derivation itself**: the raw LLM draft JSON is logged at INFO (debug aid), the prompt marks `name`/`systemInstruction` required, and `TurPersonaAudioDeriveService` synthesises a deterministic fallback (name from the description slug; systemInstruction from the extracted traits, with a warn) so those two free-text fields are **never handed the review form blank**. `TurPersonaAudioJobServiceTest` (3 — transcribe→analyse→succeed with chunk counts, fail-soft on transcription failure, empty-audio reject), `TurFfmpegAudioChunkerTest` (+1 — duration cap forces >1 chunk), `TurPersonaAudioDeriveServiceTest` (+1 — name/systemInstruction synthesised when the model leaves them blank).
- **T692** — **Async transcription jobs.** Long audio no longer blocks an HTTP request thread. A job surface (`POST /api/genai/transcription/jobs` → QUEUED `jobId`; `GET .../{jobId}` poll; `GET .../{jobId}/stream` SSE that completes on the terminal event) backed by `TurTranscriptionJobService`: a **bounded** worker pool (`turing.transcription.async-workers`, default 2) + bounded queue (`async-queue-capacity`, default 8) with **back-pressure** — a saturated pool rejects submissions with `TurTranscriptionBusyException` (HTTP 429) rather than letting the queue (each job holds its audio bytes until a worker consumes them) grow unbounded. Each job delegates to the T689 `TurTranscriptionService`, so async jobs inherit chunk-aware large-file support and every config-selected backend for free; state transitions (QUEUED → RUNNING → SUCCEEDED/FAILED) publish immutable `TurTranscriptionJobStatus` snapshots over an in-process `TurTranscriptionJobEventBus` (the workspace/slot SSE-bus pattern, `startWith` current snapshot + 25 s heartbeat, single-node). Terminal results are retained in memory for `async-job-retention-seconds` (default 3600) then swept by a `@Scheduled` eviction; the audio bytes themselves are discarded the moment a worker finishes. The scale lever once chunking made multi-hour recordings tractable. `TurTranscriptionJobServiceTest` (7 — succeeded-with-transcript, fail-soft error propagation, empty-audio reject, unknown-job empty, saturated-pool busy rejection, retention keeps un-expired, bus terminal snapshot delivery).

## Block AT — Persona Match: N×N persona↔content fit projects (§XLIII)

- **T696** — **Persona Match domain model + persistence.** New aggregate `TurPersonaMatchProject` (name/description/enabled, `llmInstanceId`, `TurPersonaMatchSchedule` MANUAL/DAILY/WEEKLY, `lastRunAt`, `@TenantId`) owning project-scoped `TurPersonaMatchSource` (URL/SN_DOC/ASSET + cached text/status + `contentHash`, mirrors `TurPersonaSource` but decoupled from a persona), a project↔persona join `TurPersonaMatchPersona` (soft ref to `ai_persona`), and the matrix cell `TurPersonaMatchCell` (unique `project_id,source_id,persona_id` → fitScore/readabilityScore/summary/fits+misfits JSON/llmUsed/contentHash/evaluatedAt). Plain JPA repositories (admin-CRUD aggregate, per Block AF). Liquibase `v2026.3.1.100` (4 tables, FK deleteCascade on source/persona, unique+index on cells). `TurPersonaMatchIT` end-to-end (LLM-free — degrades to readability-only).
- **T697** — **Project-scoped content extraction.** `TurPersonaMatchSourceService` reuses the Block AA extraction paths verbatim (SSRF-guarded URL fetch / `TurPersonaSnDocResolver` indexed-doc / Tika asset parse) against project-owned sources, stamping a `contentHash` on success for the re-run skip. Contents added once per project, not per persona.
- **T698** — **N×N analysis runner + SSE progress.** `TurPersonaMatchAnalysisService` runs the Cartesian product of extracted contents × personas over the unchanged Block AA `TurPersonaContentFitEvaluator`, persisting each cell; a content-hash guard skips unchanged cells so re-runs are cheap. The expensive LLM evaluations run bounded-parallel on a short-lived pool, each worker re-establishing the caller's tenant via `TurTenantContext.runAs` (writes stay on the caller thread); progress streams over a `synchronized`-emit `TurPersonaMatchEventBus` (STARTED / per-cell CELL / DONE / ERROR) for a live heatmap fill. Orphan cells (content/persona removed) are pruned on each run.
- **T699** — **Project CRUD API + matrix/report endpoints.** `TurPersonaMatchService` + `TurPersonaMatchAPI` (`/api/persona-match`): project CRUD, persona-set replace, content add (URL/SN_DOC) / upload (multipart) / re-extract / delete, the persisted matrix, the two bidirectional report lenses (by-content / by-persona, each ranked + averaged), and the run — blocking (`POST .../run`) and as a live SSE stream (`POST .../run/stream`, kicks the analysis on `boundedElastic` under the captured tenant, merges the bus + heartbeat).
- **T700** — **Scheduled periodic re-analysis.** `TurPersonaMatchScheduleJob` re-runs enabled DAILY/WEEKLY projects (opt-in at the data level — MANUAL is the default) via `@Scheduled` + ShedLock cluster-wide-once, re-extracting sources (drifted URLs) then recomputing changed cells and stamping `lastRunAt`. Global kill-switch `turing.persona-match.schedule.enabled` (default true); crons overridable.
- **T701** — **Bento Persona Match studio (list + workspace).** `/bento/persona/match` project mosaic (`useMatchProjects`) + `/bento/persona/match/:projectId` workspace wired to the real API via `TurPersonaMatchService` + React-Query hooks (`persona-match.queries.ts`, `persona-match.model.ts`): create card, editable name/description/schedule/LLM-instance picker (autosave), multi-add contents (URL / indexed via `SnDocumentPicker` / file upload), persona multi-select from the real persona catalog, delete. Retired the `persona-match.mock.ts` prototype.
- **T702** — **Interactive N×N fit matrix + reports (live SSE fill).** Frosted heatmap (contents × personas, green/amber/red by fit) with Matrix / By content / By persona tabs and a per-cell drill-in Sheet (fits / misfits + rewrite suggestions / summary), plus client-side PDF export per lens. "Rodar análise" streams `POST .../run/stream` and fills cells as CELL events land; the persisted matrix (`useMatchMatrix`) seeds the grid on load.
- **T703** — **Retire per-persona validate → shortcut.** `/bento/persona/validate/:personaId` no longer renders the Block AA notebook; it creates (or re-opens by `Fit: <name>` marker) a single-persona Match project, attaches the persona, and redirects to the studio. The "Analyze fit" launch from the persona detail (T583) still points here, so the entry point is preserved while the canonical home becomes the project.
- **T704** — **Fit trend/history + drift + adjustment suggestions (CLOSES Block AT).** A run overwrote the cell in place, so a project on a DAILY/WEEKLY cadence (T700) had no memory of how a `(content, persona)` pair's fit *moved*. New append-only `ai_persona_match_cell_history` (Liquibase `v2026.3.1.118`, one row per cell **per run** — reused cells included, so a run's average covers the whole matrix, trimmed to `turing.persona-match.history.max-points`, default 24) + two nullable drift columns on the cell (`previous_fit_score`/`previous_evaluated_at`, so the heatmap badges movement without a second request). `TurPersonaMatchHistoryService` owns the append + trim + the two read models: `GET .../trend` (per-cell series + run-average series) and `GET .../drift` (movement between the last two runs, sharpest regression first, with `crossedBelowFit` when a pair fell under the 75% good-fit threshold and `contentChanged` from the hash) — both LLM-free, so drift works on a readability-only project. `TurPersonaMatchAdjustmentService` closes the bidirectional loop: `POST .../adjustments?regenerate=` asks the project's LLM (via the T728 `TurLlmSummaryService` instance override) for concrete edits on **both** sides — CONTENT items (edit the text for the persona) and PERSONA items (correct the audience description for the text) — grounded by dropping any suggestion naming a target outside the project, and degrading to a **deterministic** report assembled from the already-grounded misfit rewrites when no LLM is reachable or the verdict won't parse (never empty, never invented, advisory only — nothing is auto-applied).
