Series are pruned alongside cells on content/persona/project removal. Studio: a frosted **Tuning loop** section (recharts average-fit-per-run trend, dropped/improved drift lists, adjustment cards per side), a ±delta caret on each heatmap cell and an SVG sparkline + "since the last run" line in the per-cell drill-in (en/pt `persona.match.*`). `TurPersonaMatchIT` +1 case (`fitHistoryDriftAndAdjustments`: two runs over a changed content → two-point series, carried-forward previous score, drift entry, trend shape, deterministic adjustments for both targets, prune on delete).
- **Reusable `PersonaSelectGrid`** (Block AT follow-up) — extracted the Persona Match frosted multi-select persona tile grid into a neutral, order-preserving `components/persona/persona-select-grid.tsx` (id/name/optional-subtitle options, `ordered` position badges, keyboard-accessible) and adopted it in Persona Match (persona picker), Persona Dialogue (speaker roster — selection order = speaking order) and Persona Suggest (restrict-to-personas). Persona Match, Dialogue and Suggest are also **fully localized** (en/pt `persona.match.*` / `persona.dialogueProject.*` namespaces; no hardcoded Portuguese).

## Block AU — Persona Dialogue projects (§XLIV)

- **T705** — **Persona Dialogue project backend.** The saved, multi-project analogue of a Persona Match project (and of the ephemeral `/api/v2/persona-dialogue` stream). New aggregate `TurPersonaDialogueProject` (name/description, `topic`, `llmInstanceId`, `turns`, `lastRunAt`, `@TenantId`) owning an **ordered** speaker roster `TurPersonaDialogueSpeaker` (`speakerOrder` = round-robin order, soft ref to `ai_persona`) and the persisted last transcript `TurPersonaDialogueTurn` (replaced each run). `TurPersonaDialogueProjectService` + `TurPersonaDialogueProjectAPI` (`/api/persona-dialogue`): CRUD, ordered-speaker replace, transcript, and a project-scoped `POST .../run/stream` that reuses the **unchanged** round-robin engine (`TurPersonaDialogueService`) — loading the saved config, streaming each turn as SSE, and persisting the transcript + `lastRunAt` on completion (`doOnComplete` under the captured tenant via `runAs`). Plain JPA repositories (admin-CRUD aggregate). Liquibase `v2026.3.1.101` (3 tables, FK deleteCascade on speaker). `TurPersonaDialogueProjectIT` end-to-end (LLM-free — exercises CRUD + ordered roster + transcript persistence directly).
- **T706** — **Bento Persona Dialogue studio (list + workspace).** `/bento/persona/dialogue` is now a project mosaic (`useDialogueProjects`) + `/bento/persona/dialogue/:projectId` workspace (+ `/new` create card) wired to the real API via `TurPersonaDialogueProjectService` + React-Query hooks (`persona-dialogue.queries.ts`, model extended): editable name/description/topic/turns/LLM-instance (autosave), an **ordered** speaker roster via the shared `PersonaSelectGrid` (the position badge is the speaking order), a live SSE transcript (colour-coded per speaker) that persists and re-seeds on re-open. Fully localized (en/pt `persona.dialogueProject.*`). Retired the old single-page ephemeral dialogue surface + its frontend service (the `/api/v2/persona-dialogue` endpoint stays for backward compat).

## Block AV — POST-native search & link-free response contract (§XLV)

- **T707** — **Public POST read-only search contract.** New anonymous-capable `POST /api/sn/{siteName}/search/query` taking a JSON `TurSNSearchRequest` (`q`/`fq`/`fqAnd`/`fqOr`/operators/`page`/`rows`/`sort`/`locale`/`group`/`fieldList`/`targetingRules`/`autoCorrectionDisabled`) — a JSON body has no query-string grammar, so a filter value containing `& = + # %` or a space can no longer be mis-read as query structure. Deliberately decoupled from the impersonation-oriented `TurSNSitePostParamsBean`: the request carries no `userId`, no `populateMetrics` and none of the conditional targeting maps, so the anonymous path cannot reach the impersonation surface (the authenticated `POST /search` keeps its bean and its 401-without-principal gate). `TurSNSearchRequestAdapter` maps the body onto the same `TurSNSearchParams` the GET mapping binds — one pipeline, two entry points — and rebuilds the canonical `/api/sn/{site}/search?…` URI (through the already-hardened `TurCommonsUtils.addParameterToQueryString` encoder) so the legacy link-bearing response keeps emitting deep-linkable hrefs. Search caching is keyed on that canonical query, since a POST has no query string and `TurSNUtils.getCacheKey` would otherwise collapse every body onto `<site>_null`. Already covered by the existing `permitAll` + CSRF-exempt matchers (`/api/sn/*/search/**`, `/api/sn/**`), which settles the §XLV.5 open question: the public search POST needs **no** CSRF priming round-trip. GET and its response are untouched — the block is purely additive. 20 unit tests (`TurSNSearchRequestAdapterTest` + 2 new controller tests).

- **T708** — **Structured link-free response + format negotiation.** New `responseFormat` field on `TurSNSearchRequest` (`TurSNResponseFormat.LINKS` default | `STRUCTURED`) — §XLV.5's open question settled in favour of a **request field** over `Accept`, because it works identically on GET and POST, which is the point of A/B-ing the two shapes against one query. `STRUCTURED` returns `TurSNStructuredSearchBean`: facet items decomposed into `field` + `value` + `filterQuery` + `count` + `selected` + a `TurSNFacetItemAction` (`ADD`/`REMOVE`/`REPLACE`) that replaces the item `link`; pagination as `page` + `type` + `current` instead of `href`; `clearFilterQueries` per facet and `selectedFilterQueries` overall instead of `cleanUpLink`/`cleanUpFacets`; locale switch as a locale tag + `selected`; spell-check as texts the client puts on `q`. The response echoes the **effective request** (every default resolved) as the object the client mutates and re-sends — that echo is *why* no href is needed. `TurSNStructuredResponseMapper` is a pure post-processing pass over the already-computed legacy bean (§XLV.3: the state was already there, it just wasn't exposed), so the two formats can never disagree about results. The one thing not recoverable post-hoc is the facet-item action — whether a click adds or replaces depends on the field's per-field facet-item mode, known only inside `TurSNFacetRenderer` — so it is carried on a `@JsonIgnore transient` field of the legacy item: an in-process hand-off that leaves the legacy JSON byte-for-byte unchanged (guarded by a test). `TurSNSearchResponse` marker interface lets the endpoint return either shape. 29 unit tests, including the load-bearing one that serializes the structured response and asserts it contains no `link`/`href`/`cleanUpLink`/`cleanUpFacets` and no `/api/sn/` string at all.

- **T709** — **Vanilla `@viglet/turing-sdk` → POST-native.** `createSearchController` now drives search over `POST /sn/{site}/search/query` with a JSON body and the `STRUCTURED` response (`postNative`, default `true`): facet/pagination/locale/sort/cleanup/spell-check navigation are all changes to `state.request`, never a parsed href. New controller methods `toggleFacet` / `clearFacet` / `clearAllFilters` / `applySpellCheck` / `searchRequest`; `navigate(href)` is kept but `@deprecated`. New API wrappers `postSearch` / `postStructuredSearch` and the pure `applyFacetAction(fq, item)` that applies an item's `ADD`/`REMOVE`/`REPLACE` — the structured replacement for "parse the href, re-serialize it". `resolveDocuments` was widened to a shared structural type so one resolver serves both formats, plus `resolveStructuredGroups` (group pagination lands on `structuredPagination`, since the link-free response carries no hrefs). **The POST is sent with `csrf: false`** — T707 established `/api/sn/**` is CSRF-exempt, so the SDK pays no `/csrf` priming round-trip and POST search costs exactly what the GET did. The controller adopts the server's **echoed request** (every default resolved) after each response, so the next toggle starts from what actually ran. **Behaviour change, opt-out via `{ postNative: false }`:** `state.data` is `null` POST-native (`state.structured` is authoritative); `documents`/`groups`/`status`/`error`/`params` are unchanged. The two consumers that still read `state.data` — the marketing-site `SearchDemo` hero and the `turing-search` EDS example block — are pinned to `postNative: false` until **T714** migrates them. Scope note: `/ac` and `/similar` stay GET because their POST variants are T712's backend work. 24 new tests (`post-native-search.test.ts`); the SDK gzip budget was raised (it was **already** over at baseline — 16.22 kB vs a 15.63 kB budget — and T709 adds 0.72 kB; T713 will claw it back by dropping the query-string helpers). Changeset: `minor`.

- **T710** — **React `@viglet/turing-react-sdk` + react-ui → POST-native.** `useTuringSearch` **and** the URL-synced `TuringProvider` store now fetch over `POST /sn/{site}/search/query` with the `STRUCTURED` response (`postNative`, default `true`), exposing `structured` + `request` state and `toggleFacet` / `clearFacet` / `clearAllFilters` / `goToPage` / `applySpellCheck` / `searchRequest`; `navigate(href)` kept but `@deprecated`. **The key scoping call: URL sync is *not* the bug.** The browser URL stays the source of truth for search state — deep-linkable/bookmarkable/shareable search URLs are exactly what §XLV.5 protects — so what went POST-native is the *API transport*, where the reserved-character round-trip actually lived; facet toggles write filter queries into the URL from structured data instead of parsing a server-rendered href. `useTuringFacets` / `useTuringPagination` keep their **exact public shape** (`facetGroups[].facets[].toggle`, `pages[].select`, `totalCount`, …) and swap only what runs underneath, so the three marketplace apps and the showcase needed **no changes** (verified by type-check). `applyFacetAction` + the new `searchParamsToRequest` / `requestToSearchParams` bridges are **re-exported from `@viglet/turing-sdk`, not duplicated** — one place for the ADD/REMOVE/REPLACE semantics, so the two SDKs cannot drift (this replaced a first pass that had copied them). react-ui `TuringPagination`: `href` optional, `current` honoured, new `onSelectPage(page)` that wins over the now-optional deprecated `onNavigate` — the component no longer needs a URL to paginate. Same `postNative: false` opt-out as T709 (hook option or on `urlSync`). 8 new tests (4 react-ui pagination, 4 vanilla-SDK bridge); react-ui 82/82 green. Changesets: `minor` for both packages.

- **T711** — **Parity guard + demo wiring.** Extended the T406 SDK contract test: `POST /sn/{site}/search/query` joins `PUBLIC_ENDPOINTS`, and a new **POST-native structured contract** block asserts both SDKs expose the five helpers a link-free client actually needs (`postStructuredSearch`, `applyFacetAction`, `searchParamsToRequest`, `requestToSearchParams`, `resolveStructuredGroups`) **and** publish all ten `TurSearchRequest`/`TurStructured*` type names — because an SDK that loses one silently falls back to parsing hrefs, the exact regression §XLV closes. The guard's `reactDefinesFn` now also accepts an `export { … } from "@viglet/turing-sdk"` **re-export**, not only a local `export function`: T710 deliberately re-exports the pure helpers instead of copying them, and a guard that only accepted copies would have pushed the code back toward the duplication it exists to prevent. One test pins `csrf: false` on the search POST so a future change can't silently re-add a `/csrf` round-trip to every public embed. Guard verified to actually fail (injected a bogus helper name → 2 failures) rather than pass vacuously; 102 parity assertions green. **Demo confirmed, no config change needed:** the anonymous demo's nginx `location ^~ /api/sn/` is method-agnostic, so `POST …/search/query` already reaches the backend, and the 2 MB body cap dwarfs a JSON search body. Documented that in `viglet/cloud` (`cloud-frontend/nginx.conf` + `docker/DEMO.md`) with an explicit "do not harden this to GET-only" — restricting it would break faceted search on the demo while looking like a security tightening. (`nginx -t` not run: Docker was unavailable locally; the edit is comment-only.)

- **T712** — **Autocomplete + select-list POST variants.** `POST /api/sn/{siteName}/ac/query` and `POST /api/sn/{siteName}/search/list/query` accept the same anonymous JSON `TurSNSearchRequest` as T707, so the whole public read surface is POST-capable and a suggestion or select-list narrowed by a filter value containing `& = + # %` or a space no longer has to survive the query-string round-trip. Neither projection carries links (`List<String>` / `List<Object>`), so `responseFormat` deliberately does not apply — there is nothing to negotiate. `TurSNSearchRequestAdapter.searchUri` now strips a trailing `/query` from **any** POST variant (`…/ac/query` → `…/ac`, `…/search/list/query` → `…/search/list`) instead of only `…/search/query`, keeping each canonical URI on its GET counterpart. Because a POST has no query string, both new paths key their search cache on that canonical query rather than `request.getQueryString()` (which would collapse every distinct body onto `<site>_null`): the shared `TurSNSiteSearchService.publicPostSearchContext` / `publicPostCacheKey` extracted out of `executePublicPostSearch`, plus a new `TurSNAutoComplete.autoCompleteWithRegularSearch(site, params, uri, cacheKey)` overload that the GET flavour now delegates into. Security: `/api/sn/*/ac/**` added to both `permitAll` lists in `TurSecurityConfigProduction` (the existing matcher was the exact path `/api/sn/*/ac`, so the new POST would have 401'd anonymously); CSRF was already exempt via `/api/sn/**`, and `/search/list/query` was already covered by `/api/sn/*/search/**`. GET `/ac` and GET `list` are untouched. 6 new unit tests (3 autocomplete POST, 2 list POST, 1 canonical-URI strip).

- **T714** — **Docs + showcase + EDS go POST-native.** Retired the last two consumers pinned to `postNative: false` by T709. The **EDS `turing-search` example block** was rewritten against the link-free contract: it now renders a real facet rail and pagination from `state.structured` (`item.selected` drives the chip, `page.current` the disabled state), navigates with `toggleFacet` / `clearAllFilters` / `goToPage`, and reads the hit count off `structured.queryContext` — the speculative `data-turing-href` click delegation is gone, so the reference EDS surface no longer parses a URL anywhere (+ facet/pagination styles in its CSS). The **marketing-site `SearchDemo` hero** followed: structured facets (`group.items`, keyed on `filterQuery`), `toggleFacet` instead of `navigate(item.link)`, `applySpellCheck()` instead of navigating `spellCheck.corrected.link`, and `clearAllFilters()` instead of the `cleanUpFacets` href — which also **removes a real trap**: a zero-result response carries no facet groups and often no cleanup link, so "Clear filters" used to depend on a link the empty response might not hand over; POST-native, clearing is a change to the request the controller already holds. The **Atlas Store showcase needed no changes** — it drives `TuringProvider` + `useTuringFacets`/`useTuringPagination`, which T710 already moved to POST while keeping their public shape (verified by type-check, not assumed). End-user docs (separate `vigletdocs` repo): a new `rest-api.md` **§ POST-native search** documenting `POST /search/query`, the full request-field table, the `responseFormat` negotiation, an annotated `STRUCTURED` payload and a "instead of following *this link*, set *that field*" mapping table, plus the T712 `/ac/query` + `/search/list/query` variants and the three new rows in the public-endpoints table; `javascript-sdk.md` and `react-sdk.md` swapped `navigate(href)` for the structured actions (incl. `TuringPagination`'s `onSelectPage`); `COVERAGE.md` gains the Block AV row. No SDK source change, so no changeset: the examples are repo-only (`files: dist/**`).

- **T713** — **href round-trip deprecated (not removed); Block AV closed.** With T714 having migrated the last consumers, nothing in the repo parses a server-rendered search URL any more — so this task puts that path on notice rather than deleting it (an explicit call: the alternative, going POST-only and dropping `postNative: false`, would have been a hard break on published packages three tasks after shipping the opt-out). Backend: the five link fields (`TurSNSiteSearchFacetItemBean.link`, `TurSNSiteSearchFacetBean.cleanUpLink`, `TurSNSiteSearchWidgetBean.cleanUpFacets`, `TurSNSiteSearchPaginationBean.href`, `TurSNSiteSpellCheckText.link`, `TurSNSiteLocaleBean.link`) carry a **legacy** javadoc note that names the structured replacement and says explicitly they are *not* going away — they are what makes facet pages deep-linkable, SEO-crawlable and no-JS usable (§XLV.5). No annotation, no wire change. SDKs: `parseHrefToParams` logs a **one-time** console notice naming the structured replacements and is `@deprecated`, as are `navigate(href)` on the vanilla controller / `useTuringSearch` / the URL-synced store, and `onNavigate` on react-ui's `TuringPagination`. The notice sits at the single parse site so a caller sees one line per page, not one per facet chip; `navigate` routes through it rather than warning twice, and react-ui emits nothing because it only *forwards* an href (and must stay zero-runtime-dep). **Bug found and fixed on the way through:** `useCoBrowseSearch`'s `toggle_facet` / `clear_facets` client tools resolved their target from `store.data`, which is `null` POST-native — so both had been silently failing in the *default* mode since T710; they now resolve against `store.structured` with the legacy lookup kept only as the `postNative: false` fallback. 5 new tests (`href-deprecation.test.ts`: warns once, names the replacements, still parses correctly, `navigate` doesn't double-warn, POST-native path stays silent). **Size, honestly:** the T709 changeset expected T713 to claw back gzip budget; under deprecate-only it cannot — a notice adds bytes — so both entry budgets went up (`turing-sdk.js` 17500 → 17700, `turing-sdk.umd.cjs` 15500 → 15750) and the reclaim moves to the actual removal. Changesets: `minor` (sdk, react-sdk) + `patch` (react-ui). **Block AV is now fully shipped.**
- **T936** — useTuringFacets returns the site's secondary facet rail as its own secondaryFacetGroups list in both transports, and the search sidebar renders it
- **T937** — TurWidget.similar is TurSimilarDocument[], the same documents the structured arm carries, so a legacy consumer can iterate what the server always sent
- **T938** — the structured response projects the spotlight into a url-bearing bean, so the no-links guard holds over a fixture that carries every link-bearing part of the legacy bean
- **T939** — the boundary guard runs the same rules over the fenced code in every package README and example, and the react-sdk README no longer hands a reader a legacy pagination read
- **T945** — the README says which of the two legacy reads throws and which renders an empty rail silently, and a test pins the null that makes them differ
- **T946** — the EDS reference block renders the site's secondary facet rail as a second list under its own heading, sharing one group renderer and the existing click delegation with the primary
- **T947** — a convention test over both SDKs, the examples and the apps fails a file that reads structured.facets without naming secondaryFacets, and the marketing demo folds both rails into its capped list
- **T948** — one named rule set carries the surfaces each rule applies to, so both scans and both package suites run the whole search-response contract and the react-sdk README no longer teaches a single facet rail

## Block AW — Synthetic User Research: persona studies, interviews & continuous insight (§XLVI)

- **T734** — **Developer surface — `turing research` CLI.** A `@viglet/turing-cli` verb (`src/commands/research.ts`) mirroring `turing eval`, so a study can be defined/run and its insights fetched from code/CI over the same zero-dependency `TuringClient` (dev-token or Basic auth, CSRF-primed). Subcommands: `research list` (studies on the instance), `research run <studyId> [--force]` (blocking cohort run → reports the interview count, exit 1 when none ran), `research insights <studyId>` (prints the T722 executive summary + ranked themes + recommendations, exit 1 when unavailable so CI gates), and `research rollup <studyId...>` (the T733 cross-study program view). Wired into the `cli.ts` dispatch + help; the pure formatters (`formatStudies`/`formatReport`/`formatRollup`) and the `runResearch` dispatcher are unit-tested with `node:test` + a stub client (8 new cases). The customer-facing **embed** SDKs are unchanged beyond the T717 OCEAN metadata — the programmatic surface is deliberately a CLI concern. **Closes Block AW — the Synthetic User Research block (T717–T734) is fully shipped.**
- **T733** — **Multi-study planner + cross-study rollup (PRISMA).** One surface to plan several studies across different audiences and roll their insights up into a program-level view. `TurResearchProgramService.rollup(studyIds)` is a **deterministic** aggregation (the T724 theme-graph discipline — no new inference): for each selected study it reuses the cached T722 report + the LLM-free T723 saturation counts and folds themes across studies by normalized title, so a theme raised in ≥2 studies surfaces as a **cross-study** signal (`studyCount`/`totalPrevalence`/contributing study names). Returns program totals (studies / participants / interviews / distinct themes / shared themes) + a per-study sufficiency row; fail-open per study (an un-run study is included without triggering report generation, an unresolvable id is skipped, an empty/all-missing selection → `available=false`). `POST /api/research-study/program/rollup` (a study-id list). The `/bento/persona/research/program` planner page multi-selects studies, offers "Run all selected" (sequential blocking runs then re-rollup) and renders the program view (stat tiles + per-study cards deep-linking each study + cross-study themes with shared ones highlighted); reachable from the studio list header. Fully localized under `persona.research.program.*` (en + pt). Covered by `TurResearchProgramServiceTest` (4 cases: theme folding + shared count + totals, empty selection, all-unresolvable, un-run study skips report). **Ships the first 💭 of Phase 5.**
- **T732** — **Research Assistant — guided study authoring.** A skippable conversational guide that turns a free-text "what do you want to learn?" into a structured study **proposal** so a researcher doesn't face an empty protocol editor. `TurResearchAssistantService.propose(brief, regenerate)` reuses the T731 / persona-from-audio strict-schema discipline over `TurLlmSummaryService.generate` — one call → a JSON object (defensively sliced `{`…`}` + `FAIL_ON_UNKNOWN_PROPERTIES=false`) mapped to a proposal (name / goal / hypothesis / protocol coerced to the `TurResearchProtocol` enum with a DYNAMIC_SCRIPT fallback + protocol payload: `conceptText` for CONCEPT_TEST / `questions` for CUSTOM_SCRIPT, `maxQuestions` clamped 1–30, a `suggestedCohortBrief` + `suggestedParticipantCount` 1–12 bridging T731, and a friendly `assistantMessage`). Fail-open (`success=false` when no default LLM / blank brief / unparseable) and **never auto-applied**: `POST /api/research-study/assistant/propose` returns the proposal and the studio's create card pre-fills its fields for the human to edit + create. The studio create flow gains a "Research Assistant" section (a brief textarea → Draft/Redraft → the assistant's summary + pre-filled name/goal/hypothesis/protocol/concept/questions), explicitly skippable (the plain fields work without it). Fully localized under `persona.research.assistant.*` (en + pt). Covered by `TurResearchAssistantServiceTest` (5 cases: concept-test parse + clamps, unknown-protocol → DYNAMIC_SCRIPT, blank brief no-LLM-call, no-LLM fail-open, unparseable). **Closes Block AW Phase 5 committed scope (T730–T732); T733 PRISMA planner + T734 `turing research` CLI remain 💭.**
- **T731** — **Audience cohort synthesis.** `TurAudienceCohortService.synthesize(brief, count, regenerate)` turns a one-paragraph audience brief into a **diverse** set of never-saved `TurPersona` **drafts** with **spread OCEAN facets** (T717), so a study samples a real range of temperaments instead of N clones — reusing the AI-authoring strict-schema discipline of the persona-from-audio `TurPersonaAudioDeriveService` over `TurLlmSummaryService.generate` (one call → a JSON array, defensively sliced `[`…`]` + `FAIL_ON_UNKNOWN_PROPERTIES=false`, count clamped 1–12, OCEAN clamped 0–100, `personaKind=BOTH`). Fail-open — `success=false` with an error when there is no default LLM / blank brief / unparseable output; **nothing is persisted** ("derive, never auto-apply"). `POST /api/persona/cohort` (`@Secured` ADMIN/CREATE/EDIT) returns the drafts; the `/bento/persona/cohort` studio page takes the brief + count, renders a review grid (per-persona OCEAN bars + tone/language chips) and — per the discipline — **keeps** a draft by opening the existing new-persona review form pre-filled (router `state.draft`, same mechanism as persona-from-audio), or **discards** it, with regenerate. Reachable from the Research Studio's audience section ("Synthesize cohort"); fully localized under `persona.cohort.*` (en + pt). Covered by `TurAudienceCohortServiceTest` (4 cases: diverse parse + OCEAN clamp, blank brief no-LLM-call, no-LLM fail-open, unparseable output).
- **T730** — **Bento Research Studio (the frontend hub).** The `/bento/persona/research` studio renders the deliberately-headless Phase 2–4 backends as one surface, following the Persona Match / Dialogue studio shape (list mosaic + `:studyId` workspace, no separate `/new` route). The **list** (`BentoListPage`) is a mosaic of studies with protocol / participant / interview / schedule chips, cross-linked to Personas · Match · Dialogue. The **workspace** carries: a protocol editor (name/goal/hypothesis, protocol select with `CONCEPT_TEST` concept box / `CUSTOM_SCRIPT` question list / adaptive `maxQuestions`, the T726 interview-target picker Default-LLM↔agent, the T728 per-stage Big-Shuffle lanes interview/synthesis/default, and the T729 schedule control), the ordered audience picker (shared `PersonaSelectGrid`), and a **live SSE interview feed** (`POST /run/stream`, CSRF-primed `fetch` + `ReadableStream` like the Match/Dialogue clients) whose transcripts stream in and expand to their Q/A turns. The read surfaces ship as `ResearchInsightsSection`: the T722 insights report (executive summary + by-theme / by-persona lens tabs + regenerate + print-to-PDF export), the T723 saturation gauge (adequate-at-N + per-persona novelty bars), the T724 theme-affinity graph (persona→theme chips), the T729 insight-drift chart (recharts line over runs), the T727 concept-fit report (concept-test studies only), and the T725 promote-to-eval-dataset bridge deep-linking `/bento/eval`. New `persona-research` model / service / query hooks (`useResearchStudies` + per-surface hooks gated on `everRun`); every read is fail-open (renders the backend's `available=false` message). Fully localized under `persona.research.*` (en + pt). All read queries are `enabled`-gated so an un-run study makes no synthesis calls. **Opens Block AW Phase 5.**
- **T729** — **Continuous Insight — scheduled re-runs + insight drift.** A study gains a `TurResearchSchedule` cadence (`ai_research_study.schedule` = MANUAL default / DAILY / WEEKLY, Liquibase v2026.3.1.108) and `TurResearchScheduleJob` re-runs the enabled DAILY/WEEKLY studies through the shared runner — cluster-wide-once via ShedLock, opt-in at the data level, global kill-switch `turing.research.schedule.enabled` — mirroring `TurPersonaMatchScheduleJob` (Block AT); the runner's content-hash guard keeps an unchanged re-run cheap. Every run (manual or scheduled) captures a deterministic **insight-drift** snapshot (`ai_research_insight_snapshot`, FK-cascaded): `TurResearchDriftService` reuses the LLM-free T723 saturation counts (theme coverage + "adequate at N") plus the completed-interview count — so drift costs nothing and reproduces in CI (T385/T466 discipline) — and `GET /api/research-study/{id}/drift` returns the ordered series with per-point theme deltas, turning one-shot research into continuous validation. The snapshot is best-effort (a failure never breaks the run); the study DTO gains `schedule` for the studio's schedule control + trend chart (T730). Sentiment-trajectory drift (T87) is a documented future extension. Covered by `TurResearchDriftServiceTest` (5 cases: snapshot persists saturation counts, unavailable→zeros, unknown-study no-op, series theme-deltas, empty series) + the `TurResearchStudyIT` migration/round-trip. **Closes Block AW Phase 4** (T725–T729).
- **T728** — **"Big Shuffle" per-stage model lanes.** A study can now assign a distinct LLM instance to each stage so no single model's bias colors the whole study: `interview_llm_instance_id` + `synthesis_llm_instance_id` on `ai_research_study` (Liquibase v2026.3.1.107). `TurResearchModelLaneResolver` resolves a `TurResearchStage` to an instance with the same fail-open discipline as the T517 global lane seam — **stage lane → study-wide `llmInstanceId` → default LLM** — each tier skipped when blank / missing / disabled, so a misconfigured lane reverts to the coarser behaviour instead of taking a stage offline. The runner rides the INTERVIEW lane (its bulk of calls); `TurResearchInsightsService` rides the SYNTHESIS lane via a new `TurLlmSummaryService.generate(..., llmInstanceIdOverride)` overload (fail-open to default) with the resolved instance folded into the insights cache key so a lane change misses stale cache. Saturation scoring is deterministic / LLM-free, so it deliberately has no lane. The interview lane joins the runner's content-hash (switching it forces a re-run); the study DTO gains `interviewLlmInstanceId` + `synthesisLlmInstanceId` for the studio's per-stage pickers (T730). Both lanes blank reproduces the pre-T728 single-model study exactly; the shared `TurLlmSummaryService` callers (SN/System-Info insights, content-fit) are byte-identical via the delegating 4-arg overload. Covered by `TurResearchModelLaneResolverTest` (6 cases: stage-wins, study-wide fallback, disabled fall-through, default fallback, independent stages, blank default) + the `TurResearchStudyIT` migration/round-trip.
- **T727** — **Concept-test ↔ Persona Match bridge.** `TurResearchConceptFitService` routes a `CONCEPT_TEST` study's concept text through the Block AT `TurPersonaContentFitEvaluator` — scoring the concept against each roster persona *as a reader* — instead of forking a second evaluator, so concept feedback and content-fit scoring share one evaluator, one cache and one verdict shape (fit %, condiz / não condiz, grounded flagged spans). A complementary lens to the T720 interview: `GET /api/research-study/{id}/concept-fit?regenerate=` returns a per-persona report (in roster order) plus the roster's mean fit. Read-only and fail-open like the insights report — `available=false` with a message when the study is not a concept test, carries no concept text, or has no roster (never a throw); a persona-less roster row is skipped. Reuses the Match report surface, so no UI of its own (the studio, T730, renders it). Covered by `TurResearchConceptFitServiceTest` (5 cases: per-persona scoring + mean, non-concept-test / blank-concept / empty-roster / unknown-study guards).
- **T726** — **Interview your own deployed agent.** A study now carries an optional `targetAgentId` (`ai_research_study.target_agent_id`, Liquibase v2026.3.1.106): when set, `TurResearchInterviewEngine` **inverts the roles** — the roster persona becomes the synthetic *user* (a persona-fused simulator whose system prompt bakes in the persona's name/description/behaviour + OCEAN facet and frames the study goal as the user's objective) and the *answerer* is the live deployed `TurAIAgent` resolved from the repository, so the study UX-tests / red-teams the actual shipped assistant with its real system prompt, tools and RAG. The flagship of Block AW: only possible because Turing owns both the research panel and the agent under test. Reuses the T578 executor unchanged; like persona-chat the turn stays stateless (`conversationId = flowId = null`) — the agent answers with its real prompt + tools + RAG, but a multi-step chat-*flow* is not driven (a future task can add a stateful-flow mode). CUSTOM_SCRIPT replays the fixed question list to the deployed agent; DYNAMIC_SCRIPT / CONCEPT_TEST let the persona drive the conversation until it replies DONE or hits the cap. An unknown `targetAgentId` fails open to the legacy bare-LLM persona interview. `targetAgentId` joins the runner's content-hash so switching the target forces a re-run; the study DTO gains `targetAgentId` (write) + resolved `targetAgentName` (read); the target selector (Default LLM ↔ pick an agent) ships in the T730 studio. Null keeps the legacy behaviour byte-identical (persona is the interviewee answered by a bare LLM). Covered by `TurResearchInterviewEngineTest` (+3 cases: role inversion with the deployed agent answering, custom-script replay to the target, unknown-target fallback) and the `TurResearchStudyIT` migration/round-trip.
- **T725** — **Studies → evaluation bridge (agent-QA harness).** `TurResearchEvalBridgeService.promoteToDataset(studyId, name)` turns a completed Synthetic User Research study into a reusable `TurEvalDataset` (Block AJ / T595–T596), so the persona cohort stops being a one-shot report and becomes a repeatable regression test of an agent against a synthetic audience — the first of Block AW's two interconnection tasks (the part a standalone research SaaS can't do because it owns neither an agent runtime nor an eval platform). One completed interview → one candidate eval row, loaded in **roster order** (mirroring `TurResearchInsightsService`): the interviewer's questions become the row's replay `turns` (the prompts an agent-under-test is driven with), the persona's answers are preserved verbatim as the `referenceAnswer` (reference material for a reference-answer/embedding grader, not an assertion), a study-level `rubric` (goal + hypothesis + the T722 synthesized recommendations when a report is available) grounds a MODEL grader in the findings, and `expectedOutcome` stays **ANY** so — exactly like a mined-failure row — the row asserts nothing until a human curates it in the Eval Studio. Persistence + canonical-row mapping delegate to `TurEvalDatasetImportService.importCanonicalRows`, so a promoted dataset is byte-identical in shape to an uploaded or mined one and round-trips through the same export. Fail-open on the findings side: no default LLM (or a failed synthesis) degrades the rubric to the goal/hypothesis rather than fabricating criteria. `POST /api/research-study/{id}/promote-to-dataset` returns the dataset summary; headless — the "promote to dataset / run as eval" action deep-links the id into the existing Eval Studio (`/bento/eval`) in the T730 studio. Covered by `TurResearchEvalBridgeServiceTest` (5 cases: completed → candidate rows, pending skipped, findings fold into rubric, no completed interviews → 400, unknown study → 404).
- **T724** — **Theme / affinity knowledge graph.** `TurResearchThemeGraphBuilder` is a **pure** transform (no IO, no LLM, no Spring dependency beyond `@Component`) that re-projects the T722 insights report into a persona↔theme bipartite affinity graph — *visualization only, no new inference*: it reuses the already-synthesized (cached) report rather than calling the model. A `THEME` node per synthesized theme (weighted by prevalence) and a `PERSONA` node per participant with at least one **resolved** (traceable) quote (weighted by total supporting quotes); an edge joins a persona to a theme weighted by how many of that theme's quotes came from that persona. Fabricated (`resolved=false`) attributions are dropped, consistent with T722's traceability stance. `TurResearchInsightsService.graph(studyId)` fetches the cached report (`report(id, false)` — generating once if absent, then reusing) and delegates; `GET /api/research-study/{id}/graph`. Fail-open: no report yet → `available=false` with a message. Headless — the interactive graph view ships in the T730 studio. Covered by `TurResearchThemeGraphBuilderTest` (no-themes → unavailable; multi-theme projection with per-persona edge weights + fabricated-attribution drop).
- **T723** — **Saturation scoring (deterministic, LLM-free).** `TurResearchSaturationScorer` is a **pure** novelty scorer (no IO, no LLM, no Spring dependency beyond `@Component` — freely `new`-able) in the T385/T466 discipline, so its tests run free and reproducibly in CI. Folding a study's completed interviews in **roster order**, it reduces each participant's answers to distinct *theme tokens* (significant unigrams — a shared small EN/PT/ES stop-word list, diacritics folded via NFD so the three languages behave consistently) and reports, per step, `newThemes` / `cumulativeThemes` / `noveltyRatio` (`newThemes / personaThemeCount`). When the trailing personas go dry (`≥ MIN_DRY_STREAK=2` consecutive with `noveltyRatio ≤ NOVELTY_THRESHOLD=0.15`) it declares the cohort *saturated* and reports the honest "sample adequate at N" (`adequateAtN`, `-1` when the tail never went dry) — the measurable antidote to the strongest criticism of synthetic research. `GET /api/research-study/{id}/saturation`; the gauge/curve UI ships in T730. Covered by `TurResearchSaturationScorerTest` (3 CI cases: below-threshold → unavailable, repeated themes → saturated at N, ever-novel → never saturates).
- **T722** — **Insights report — themes + verbatim quotes + recommendations.** `TurResearchInsightsService.report(studyId, regenerate)` synthesizes a study's completed transcripts through **`TurLlmSummaryService`** (the same cached, regenerable home as SN-site + System-Info insights) into an executive summary, ranked themes each carrying **verbatim participant quotes**, and recommendations. The model returns strict JSON (parsed defensively — fence-strip + outermost-object slice + `FAIL_ON_UNKNOWN_PROPERTIES=false`, falling back to raw content when the parse can't be trusted, the gpt-4o-mini guard from the repo playbook). Quote attributions are **validated against the study roster** (id match, then a name-rescue fallback; unresolved attributions flagged `resolved=false` rather than trusted — traceability, not fabricated confidence). The *by-theme* lens is the themes themselves; the *by-persona* lens is derived deterministically from the same quotes (no second LLM call) so the two are always consistent. Cache key folds a content hash of the corpus so a re-run naturally misses stale cache; `regenerate=true` evicts. Fail-open: no default LLM / no completed interviews → `available=false` with a message, never a throw. `GET /api/research-study/{id}/insights?regenerate=`; the report renderer + lens toggle + regenerate button ship in T730.
- **T721** — **Study runner: N-persona parallel interviews + live SSE.** `TurResearchRunnerService` interviews every persona in a study's roster over the T720 engine, persisting each transcript as a `TurResearchInterview`. Follows the Persona Match runner pattern verbatim: bounded-parallel (`PARALLELISM=4`) on a short-lived pool, each worker `TurTenantContext.runAs` the captured tenant, **all DB writes on the caller thread** (`saveAll`), orphan-pruning for personas that left the roster, and an input-hash content-skip so an unchanged re-run reuses each `COMPLETED` transcript. Fail-open: no LLM configured (neither study override nor default) → each interview recorded `FAILED` with a clear message, no throw. Progress streams over `TurResearchEventBus` (STARTED / per-interview INTERVIEW / DONE / ERROR) with `POST /api/research-study/{id}/run` (blocking) + `/run/stream` (SSE, 25s heartbeat, tenant-safe) — the Persona Match SSE contract. The live feed UI ships in T730.
- **T720** — **Interview protocols + adaptive interviewer engine.** `TurResearchInterviewEngine` runs one interview per persona over the **T578 persona-chat executor unchanged** — a transient never-persisted `TurAIAgent` carries the persona (fused via `setDefaultPersona`) + the generic native tool loadout, and T718 grounding applies automatically in prompt assembly (no new chat engine). `CUSTOM_SCRIPT` asks the author's fixed question list verbatim; `DYNAMIC_SCRIPT` runs a goal-driven **interviewer sub-loop** (a second tool-less transient agent proposes the next question from the goal/hypothesis + transcript until it replies `DONE` or the `maxQuestions` cap, hard-capped at 20); `CONCEPT_TEST` is the same loop seeded with the proposed concept the participant reacts to. Each turn is stateless (no flow-state/analytics/chat-memory rows); the engine blocks on the call-not-stream executor and the runner drives it bounded-parallel.
- **T719** — **Research Study aggregate + persistence + CRUD/API.** `TurResearchStudy` is the new first-class research aggregate (goal, hypothesis, `TurResearchProtocol`, `@TenantId`, `llmInstanceId`, `lastRunAt`) mirroring the Persona Match project — it owns an **ordered audience roster** (`TurResearchStudyPersona`, soft persona ref + `position`, picked with the shared `PersonaSelectGrid`) and one per-persona `TurResearchInterview` transcript (unique on study+persona, content-hash keyed, FK-cascade). `TurResearchStudyService` does study CRUD + roster set/get + interview read (JSON turns parsed into typed DTOs); `TurResearchStudyAPI` exposes `/api/research-study` CRUD + `/personas` + `/interviews` + run. Liquibase `v2026.3.1.105` (three idempotent tables, portable lowercase types). Headless — the studio (T730) renders it. Covered by `TurResearchStudyIT` (full-context, hermetic) + `TurResearchInterviewEngineTest` (mocked executor).
- **T718** — **RAG-grounded persona (knowledge binding).** Opt-in `groundingSource` (NONE|SN_SITE|NOTEBOOK, default NONE) + `groundingSnSite` on `TurPersona` (Liquibase `v2026.3.1.104`), so a persona/interview participant answers from proprietary content instead of the model's priors. New `TurPersonaGroundingService` retrieves per turn: `SN_SITE` builds the bound site's GenAI context via `TurGenAiContextFactory` and runs a similarity search over its vector store; `NOTEBOOK` uses the persona's Block AA `TurPersonaSource` cached text. Retrieved passages are wrapped with a grounding contract (answer only from the fenced knowledge, abstain otherwise — the T385 spirit) inside `<persona_knowledge>` tags, capped at 8000 chars, and **fail-open** (unresolved site / retrieval error / empty corpus → no block, turn unbroken). Injected as a new PER_TURN `TurPromptContributor` (`TurPersonaGroundingPromptContributor`, `ORDER_GROUNDING=150`, `ORIGIN_GROUNDING`) right after the persona head — so persona chat now, and the Phase-2 interview engine later, are grounded automatically; empty/legacy pipeline stays byte-identical. Frontend: a "Knowledge Grounding" card on the persona Style section (source select + SN-site picker from the real site catalog, en/pt). No SDK change (grounding is server-side).
- **T717** — **Big Five / OCEAN personality facet on `TurPersona`.** Five opt-in 0–100 traits (openness/conscientiousness/extraversion/agreeableness/neuroticism), nullable so an existing persona is byte-for-byte unchanged (Liquibase `v2026.3.1.103`, five additive columns). When set they (a) render as a `# Personality` behavioral-guidance block in the cached persona prompt (`TurPersonaStaticPromptCache` — only traits that deviate from the 34–66 neutral band are emitted) and (b) nudge the calibrated sampling temperature via the T605 seam (`TurPersonaModelCalibration`: openness + neuroticism raise, conscientiousness lowers; each set trait contributes up to ±0.15 around its midpoint, summed delta clamped to ±0.3, applied over the tone temperature or a 0.5 baseline, then clamped to [0,1]; extraversion/agreeableness stay behavioral-only). So a synthetic-research cohort answers *differently* instead of collapsing to one voice. Also fixes a latent bug where the persona PUT path dropped `calibrateModelParams` (only create kept it), so the calibration toggle now round-trips. Frontend: a "Personality (Big Five)" card on the persona Style section — a master enable switch + five 0–100 sliders with low/high endpoint hints (en/pt). SDK: optional `personality` OCEAN metadata on `TurPersonaOption` (+ `TurPersonaPersonality`) in both JS SDKs (display-only; traits already shape answers server-side).

## Block AX — Bento header consistency: the `BentoFormHero` morph everywhere (§XLVII) — **block CLOSED (T735–T738)**

- **T738** — **Collapsed the last hand-wired two-piece forms onto `BentoFormHero`; the pattern is now unforkable.** The 12 remaining own-hero forms that reproduced the morph by hand (`BentoHero` with a `bento-fade-out` Save/Cancel `trailing` + a separate `BentoScrollSaveBar`) now render one `BentoFormHero`: the 11 dual-chrome SN forms (behavior, genai, spotlight, search-rule, merge, result-ranking, field, locale, faceted-field, custom-sort, custom-facet) and the imperative-save `sn.site.custom.facet.item` page. **`BentoScrollSaveBar` no longer has a single consumer outside `BentoFormHero`** — there is exactly one own-hero pattern left, so it can't be half-wired again. Two enabling changes: (a) the §XLVII **prereq** — a new `BentoFormHero.actions` escape hatch that replaces the default `type="submit"` Save/Cancel in *both* copies, so a page saving imperatively via `onClick` (the custom-facet item editor) can use the drop-in without a `<form>`; (b) that page swapped its bento-only `header?: ReactNode` prop for a `chrome?: "console" | "bento"` flag (the T736 `bento.se.core.new` precedent), moving the hero into the page so one component owns hero + save bar. The forms' `DialogDelete` moved from the fade-out action group to the hero `trailing` — where destructive actions belong, and the only placement that can't mount the controlled dialog twice. Also closes the arrow gap T737's audit missed: the 11 SN form eyebrows were plain `<Link>`s and now use `backTo`/`backLabel`, so they lead with `←` like everything else. Console branches are byte-for-byte unchanged; Save stays always-enabled via an explicit `saveDisabled={false}` (these forms don't track dirty state). New `bento-form-hero.test.tsx` (6 cases: both morph halves, `type="submit"`, `backTo` arrow, save-disable, the `actions` override, `trailing` outside the fade group); `tsc` clean, 171 frontend tests pass. → ROADMAP Block AX / §XLVII
- **T737** — **Back-arrow breadcrumb across every own-hero bento surface.** Added the `←` affordance (`<IconArrowLeft size={14} aria-hidden />` inside the eyebrow `<Link>`, `inline-flex items-center gap-1`) to every hero whose eyebrow links back to a parent — the ~16 the §XLVII audit flagged plus `bento.persona.suggest`, so the whole app now matches the arrow `BentoEntityShell`/`BentoFormHero` bake in. One edit to the shared **`bento.ai-agent.sub-hero.tsx`** covers every agent sub-page (settings/LLM/tools/MCP/custom-tools/history/intents/chat-flows); one edit to the shared **`bento.sn.section-list.tsx`** covers all five SN section lists (custom-sort/search-rule/result-ranking/merge-providers/spotlight). Individually updated: SN `custom-facet.item`, `field.coverage`, `facet.list`, `field.list`, `insights`, `locale.list`, `synonym.list`, `top-terms`, `thesaurus`; `admin` user/group/role; `se` cores + system-info; `store` collection-import + system-info; `persona.suggest`. (`bento.se.core.new` got its arrow from the T736 `BentoFormHero.backTo`; the synonym editor likewise.) Non-navigational section-label eyebrows were left alone. Purely additive/cosmetic — no behavior change; `tsc` clean + 66 bento tests pass. → ROADMAP Block AX / §XLVII
- **T736** — **Closed the 3 morph gaps** — the bento forms that showed a static (non-fading) Save row now morph like every other bento detail page. (1) **`bento.sn.synonym.page.tsx`**: its hand-rolled `BentoHero` + lone fade-in `BentoSaveBar` (which only appeared when dirty, with no scroll twin) became a single `BentoFormHero` (`backTo`/`backLabel` for the `←` breadcrumb, delete kept in `trailing`, Save/Cancel now in the hero + a fixed scroll bar). (2) **`bento.se.core.new`**: the reused console `se.instance.core.new.page.tsx` swapped its bento-only `header?: ReactNode` prop (which rendered a static `justify-between` header + actions row) for a `chrome?: "console" | "bento"` flag; the bento branch now renders `BentoFormHero` **inside the `<form>`** (so its `type="submit"` Save posts, and Save-enable/`titleMissing`/loading read the local `useForm` state), and the wrapper just passes `chrome="bento"` — the frosted body still comes from the enclosing `SectionCardChromeProvider`. (3) **`ai-agent.system-prompt.form.tsx`**: the `chrome="bento"` branch dropped its static `justify-end` Save/Cancel row for the same two-piece `bento-fade-out BentoSaveBar` + `BentoScrollSaveBar` pattern its sibling `ai-agent.settings.form` uses (the shared `BentoAgentSubHero` supplies the hero for every agent sub-page, so Save lives in the body, not the hero — keeping all sub-pages consistent). Cosmetic/structural only; the console paths are byte-for-byte unchanged. → ROADMAP Block AX / §XLVII
- **T735** — **`BentoFormHero` single-component hero→sticky-save-bar morph + `backTo` breadcrumb.** New `components/bento/bento-form-hero.tsx`: a drop-in for bento **own-hero form pages** (those that can't use `BentoEntityShell`) that wraps `BentoHero` — injecting fade-out Save/Cancel into `trailing` after any caller `trailing` — and renders the `BentoScrollSaveBar` twin, so the two halves of the `--bento-fade` morph can never drift apart or be half-wired (both Save buttons are plain `type="submit"`, so it must render inside the `<form>`). Adds `backTo`/`backLabel` props that render the eyebrow as a `←`-prefixed `<Link>` breadcrumb, matching the affordance `BentoEntityShell` bakes in. **Adopted in the persona editor** (`persona.instance.form.layout.tsx`): the `chrome="bento"` branch drops its console `StickySaveBar` (which had no scroll morph) for `BentoFormHero`, and its eyebrow now leads with the back-arrow; the `StickySaveBar` stays on the `console` branch only. The retired orphan `bento-form-save-bar.tsx` (a never-wired strip variant) was removed in favour of this. `turing-bento-ui` skill + its frontmatter document `BentoFormHero` as the go-to for own-hero form pages. Read-only audit (`scratchpad/bento-header-audit.md`) confirmed **no** `StickySaveBar`-in-bento breakage anywhere; the residual gaps are tracked as T736 (3 non-morphing forms) / T737 (back-arrow sweep) / T738 (optional manual-two-piece migration). No behavior change. → ROADMAP Block AX / §XLVII

## Block AY — Pluggable URL content fetcher with headless-browser fallback (§XLVIII)

- **T739** — **Pluggable URL content fetcher + headless (browserless) fallback.** URL ingestion (`TurFileUtils.urlContentToText`, used by Persona Match and Persona/Block AA) was a plain `HttpURLConnection` GET → Tika parse: it fetches the raw HTML shell, so JS-rendered SPAs (`openai.com/pt-BR`, `fluxocaixa.sebrae.com.br`) extract no body text and the source lands `FAILED` ("No text could be extracted"). New `com.viglet.turing.genai.urlfetch` seam: `TurUrlFetcher` interface → `TurSimpleUrlFetcher` (the legacy path, unchanged) + `TurHeadlessUrlFetcher` (SSRF-guarded POST to a `browserless/chromium` sidecar's `/content` → rendered HTML → new `TurFileUtils.htmlToText` Tika parse), orchestrated by `TurUrlFetchService` over `TurUrlFetchMode` — **SIMPLE** (default, byte-identical to today), **HEADLESS** (always render; falls back to SIMPLE when no sidecar), **AUTO** (run SIMPLE, escalate to HEADLESS only when the extracted text is below `autoMinChars` **and** a sidecar is configured, keeping whichever yields more text). Config precedence mirrors transcription: `turing.url-fetch.*` env (`mode`/`browserless-url`/`browserless-token`/`timeout-seconds`/`auto-min-chars`) wins over the UI-editable Global Settings row (config-vars `GLOBAL_URL_FETCH_*`, token encrypted+write-only) over the SIMPLE/blank defaults — zero behavior change until enabled. Global Settings → Platform gains a **Content Ingestion** section (mode dropdown + browserless URL + write-only token + "Check browserless" probe → `GET /api/system/global-settings/url-fetch/browserless/status`); `docker-compose.yaml` gets a `turing-browserless` sidecar (internal-only) + commented `TURING_URLFETCH_*` env. Both `extractUrl` callers route through the service. `TurUrlFetchServiceTest` (7 cases: mode dispatch + AUTO escalation/keep-best) + `TurUrlFetchConfigResolverTest` (4: env-over-DB precedence + clamps); `tsc` clean. → ROADMAP Block AY / §XLVIII

## Block AZ — Governed LLM Gateway (OpenAI-compatible governed egress) (§XLIX)

- **T740** — **OpenAI-compatible inbound gateway API.** New `com.viglet.turing.api.gateway.TurOpenAiCompatibleGatewayAPI` under `/v1`: `POST /v1/chat/completions` (blocking JSON + SSE streaming when `"stream": true`, emitting OpenAI `chat.completion.chunk` `data:` frames terminated by `data: [DONE]`), `POST /v1/embeddings`, and `GET /v1/models` (advertises every enabled `TurLLMInstance` by id). Pure wire-format translation — the OpenAI request/response records live in `TurOpenAiWire` (snake_case to match the wire contract, `@JsonIgnoreProperties(ignoreUnknown=true)` so a stock OpenAI SDK talks to Turing unchanged) and `TurGatewayLlmService` resolves the requested `model` (by instance id → `modelName` → configured default LLM), builds the Spring AI model through the existing resilient `TurLlmModelFactory` (no new LLM logic), and records spend through the same `TurLLMTokenUsageService` (`stage=gateway.chat`). The whole controller is `@ConditionalOnProperty(turing.gateway.enabled)` (default off) so no existing path or IT is affected; `/v1/**` is CSRF-exempt + permitAll in the security chain (authorization deferred to the T741 virtual-key bearer filter). Errors return OpenAI-shaped `{"error":{...}}` envelopes. `TurGatewayProperty` (`turing.gateway.enabled` / `requireVirtualKey`). Covered by `TurGatewayLlmServiceTest` (6 cases: model listing filters disabled + advertises by id, resolve by id / by modelName / default fallback / unknown-throws / disabled-id-not-returned). **Opens Block AZ.** → ROADMAP Block AZ / §XLIX
- **T741** — **Virtual keys (`sk-turing-...`).** `TurGatewayKey` entity (`tur_gateway_key`, Liquibase v2026.3.1.109) + `TurGatewayKeyService` mint the "give a dev a key scoped to gpt-4o" primitive LiteLLM sells: a key is `sk-turing-` + 32 SecureRandom Base62 chars, shown once, stored as a SHA-256 `keyHash` (O(1), tenant-agnostic auth lookup) plus the raw key encrypted via `TurSecretCryptoService` (`secretEncrypted`, reveal/rotate) with a masked `keyPrefix` for display. Scope = `allowedModels` allow-list + plain-column `tenantId` (deliberately NOT `@TenantId` — the bearer filter authenticates before the tenant is resolved, so lookup must be global, mirroring `TurLLMInstance`/`TurLLMTokenUsage`) + `enabled`/`expiresAt` (`isUsable()`). Authentication is a new `TurGatewayKeyAuthFilter` (`OncePerRequestFilter`, registered before `BasicAuthenticationFilter`) that runs only on `/v1/*` and only when the gateway is enabled: it reads `Authorization: Bearer sk-turing-...`, and when `turing.gateway.require-virtual-key=true` (default) a missing/invalid key short-circuits with an OpenAI-shaped `401`; a valid key binds a `ROLE_GATEWAY` principal + the `TurGatewayKey` into a request-thread `TurGatewayContext` (cleared in `finally`). `TurGatewayLlmService` enforces the key's model allow-list on every chat/embedding call (out-of-scope model → OpenAI error). Covered by `TurGatewayKeyServiceTest` (8 cases: mint stores hash-not-raw, auth by hash, foreign-prefix short-circuits DB, disabled/expired rejected, model-scope blank/case-insensitive/null-under-restriction). → ROADMAP Block AZ / §XLIX
- **T742** — **Per-key spend + budget + rate-limit.** `TurLLMTokenUsage` gains a plain `key_id` column (Liquibase v2026.3.1.110) + `findCostByKey`/`sumCostByKeySince` aggregations mirroring the T290 by-agent queries; `TurLLMTokenUsageService.recordUsage(...)` gains a 6-arg overload threading the gateway `keyId` (5-arg delegates with null — every existing caller unchanged). `TurGatewayKey` gains `monthlyBudgetUsd` (soft) / `budgetDowngradeLlmId` / `hardMonthlyCapUsd` (hard kill) / `rateLimitPerMinute`. `TurChatCostBudgetGate` is generalized per-key: `resolveInstanceForKey(key, current)` (month-to-date soft budget → auto-downgrade LLM, mirroring the per-agent path) + `isKeyHardCapExceeded(key)` (refuse before any upstream call), both fail-open. New `TurGatewayRateLimiter` reuses the dependency-free `TurFixedWindowRateLimiter` with a per-key bucket sized from `rateLimitPerMinute` (rebuilt on limit change). `TurGatewayLlmService` applies the pre-call governance (rate limit + hard cap → OpenAI-shaped 429 via `TurGatewayLimitException`), the soft-budget downgrade, and records spend by `keyId` on every chat/embedding turn. Covered by `TurGatewayRateLimiterTest` (4 cases) + `TurChatCostBudgetGateTest` per-key additions (5 cases: hard-cap off/at-cap, downgrade over soft budget, keep under budget, no-key keep). → ROADMAP Block AZ / §XLIX
- **T743** — **Cross-cutting headers: semantic cache + guardrails.** Two stackable per-request headers on any `model`, parsed into `TurGatewayOptions` (`x-turing-cache: semantic` / `x-turing-guardrails: strict`). Cache: new `TurGatewayResponseCache` (in-memory, TTL + LRU bound via `turing.gateway.cache-ttl-seconds`/`cache-max-entries`) keyed by SHA-256 of model + messages — modelled on `TurLlmCacheService` but deliberately gateway-local so the header is honoured without the site-chat LLM-cache global flag (exact-prompt match ships; embedding-similarity is a documented follow-up). A hit replays instantly on both the blocking path and the streaming path (as a single content chunk + `[DONE]`), recording no spend. Guardrails: reuses `TurModerationService.moderateAlways` (runs regardless of the global enable flag, fail-open) to moderate concatenated user input before the call and model output after it on the blocking path (streamed tokens are already committed, so output moderation is blocking-only — documented); a flag raises `TurGatewayGuardrailException` → OpenAI-shaped 400 `content_filter`. `TurSecondOpinion` dual-model consensus is noted as a future extension. Covered by `TurGatewayResponseCacheTest` (4: put/get, key stability, empty-not-cached, header parsing) + `TurGatewayLlmServiceTest` guardrail-block case. → ROADMAP Block AZ / §XLIX
- **T744** — **`model`-name routing into the Turing stack (the differentiator).** New `TurGatewayModelRouter` turns the OpenAI `model` string into a route through Turing's own stack — the moat a pure proxy can't copy: `turing-agent:<id>` runs the full agent via `TurAgentChatExecutor.execute()` (system prompt + tools + MCP + grounding), `turing-sn:<site>` returns a RAG-grounded answer over a Semantic Navigation site via `TurSNGenAi.assistantConversation` (retrieval + citations + reranker), and `turing-local:*` forces the embedded ($0) provider instance (resolved by vendor plugin `embedded-jlama`; routing seam complete, execution pending the embedded runtime). Agent/SN answers are produced blocking and wrapped into the OpenAI wire format — replayed as a single chunk on `stream:true`, side events (sources/chips) dropped for wire compatibility. Plus `x-turing-rag-site: <site>` = **RAG-as-a-header**: retrieves the site's top passages (via the site's vector store) and prepends them as a grounded system prompt on ANY base model. Wired into `TurGatewayLlmService` (routing precedes the passthrough path; `turing-local` resolves in `resolveChatInstance`; RAG injection in `buildPromptMessages`) and `TurGatewayOptions` gains `ragSite`. Covered by `TurGatewayModelRouterTest` (5: prefix detection, embedded resolve/throws, blank-arg retrieval) + options parsing. The 💭 T745 (generic router) + 💭 T746 (eval/distillation bridge) remain deliberately uncommitted. → ROADMAP Block AZ / §XLIX
- **T747** — **Gateway observability + unified native tools.** Observability: `TurGatewayUsageService` rolls the T742 per-key metering (`llm_token_usage.key_id`) into dashboard rows via the existing `findCostByKey`/`sumCostByKeySince` aggregations, exposed on a new authenticated `TurGatewayAdminAPI` (`/api/gateway`, `ROLE_ADMIN`): `GET /usage` (per-key cost/tokens/requests over a window) + `GET /budget-status` (month-to-date vs each key's soft/hard cap) — inbound gateway spend now feeds the Block L view sliced by virtual key. Unified native tools: `x-turing-tools: web_search,web_fetch,code_execution` enables Turing's F.15 provider-native capabilities on ANY base model — a unification LiteLLM doesn't offer. New `TurGatewayNativeToolsService` normalises the header's abstract function ids to `TurCapabilityFunctions`, intersects them with the capabilities an admin enabled on the resolved instance (`TurNativeCapabilityService.enabledFor` — the level-1 matrix gate), and dispatches to the vendor-native chat services (`TurOpenAiResponsesService`/`TurAnthropicMessagesService`/`TurGoogleGenAiNativeService`) via `TurNativeProviderClient` — no `TurAIAgent` required. Stacks with `x-turing-rag-site` (retrieved context becomes the native turn's system prompt). Silently falls back to the plain model call when nothing is enabled/supported; native-tool turns aren't token-metered (vendor services surface no usage — documented). `TurGatewayOptions` gains `nativeTools`. Covered by `TurGatewayNativeToolsServiceTest` (normalisation + no-op guard) + options-parsing cases. → ROADMAP Block AZ / §XLIX
- **T748** — **Bento gateway admin (`/bento/gateway`).** Virtual-key CRUD added to `TurGatewayAdminAPI` (`ROLE_ADMIN`): `GET/POST/PUT/DELETE /api/gateway/keys` + `POST /keys/{id}/rotate`, over a `KeyView` projection that never carries the secret or hash — the raw `sk-turing-...` key is returned exactly once inside `CreatedKey` on create/rotate. Frontend `/bento/gateway` page (functional surface: `BentoHero` + frosted `BentoFormSection` cards): a create form (name / allowed-models CSV / monthly budget / hard cap / rate limit), a keys table (prefix + scope + budget + rotate/revoke), a per-key spend dashboard (fed by T747 `GET /usage`), and an OpenAI-compatible playground (base-URL + copy-paste `curl` snippet). New `gateway.model.ts` / `TurGatewayService` / `gateway.queries.ts` (React Query) + `gateway` locale namespace (en+pt) + `BENTO_GATEWAY` route + nav item (generativeAi, `LLM_VIEW`). Covered by `TurGatewayAdminAPITest` (4: create returns raw key once + persists budget, view hides secret, rotate returns fresh key, update applies scope/budget); tsc clean; 52 bento component tests green. **Closes Block AZ committed scope (T740–T744, T747, T748); T745 (router) + T746 (eval/distillation bridge) remain 💭.** → ROADMAP Block AZ / §XLIX
- **T745** — **Router — least-latency / round-robin / failover.** New first-class `turing-router:<strategy>/<id1,id2,...>` model (`TurGatewayRouterService`) that load-balances a request across N enabled LLM-instance deployments with a fallback chain, generalizing the T742 budget-downgrade into a per-request routing policy. Strategies: `rr` (round-robin, in-memory per-router counter, default), `latency` (least observed latency via an in-memory EWMA fed back after each call), `failover` (declared order). `orderedCandidates(model)` returns the pick + fallback chain; `TurGatewayLlmService.routerAnswer` walks it — first success wins, records the winner's latency + spend, throws only when every candidate fails (applied on both blocking and streamed endpoints, the latter replayed as one chunk). All state is in-memory (best-effort, resets on restart) — no new entity/Liquibase. Covered by `TurGatewayRouterServiceTest` (6: detect, parse strategy+targets, failover order, round-robin rotation across calls, least-latency pick, no-target-throws). → ROADMAP Block AZ / §XLIX
- **T746** — **Gateway traffic → eval/distillation bridge ("the gateway that learns").** New `TurGatewayTrafficCaptureService` captures inbound base-model + router turns as rows of a reusable `TurEvalDataset` (opt-in `turing.gateway.capture-traffic`, default off), reusing `TurEvalDatasetImportService.importCanonicalRows`/`appendCanonicalRows` so captured rows are shape-identical to uploaded / mined-failure / research rows and run through the same Block AJ graders + export. Each turn → one canonical row: user turns as replay `turns`, the assistant answer kept as `referenceAnswer` **material** (not an assertion — `expectedOutcome=ANY`, like a mined transcript), tagged `gateway-traffic` with model/instance/key provenance in `metadata`; fail-open (capture never breaks a turn), rows accumulate into one growing dataset. `TurGatewayAdminAPI` gains `GET /api/gateway/traffic` (capture status + dataset id) and `POST /api/gateway/traffic/distill?agentId=` which delegates to the **propose-only** `TurOpenAiDistillationService.distillForReview` — the distilled/cheaper-model recommendation is **never auto-applied** (a human curates the resulting suggestion; opt-in behind `turing.distillation.enabled`). The captured dataset feeds the existing `/api/eval/run` for per-model quality on real traffic; fully-automatic quality-vs-cost auto-routing is a documented forward extension. Covered by `TurGatewayTrafficCaptureServiceTest` (4: no-op when disabled, first-capture creates dataset with canonical row, second-capture appends, skip on no-user-turns/blank-answer). **Closes Block AZ entirely — T740–T748 all shipped.** → ROADMAP Block AZ / §XLIX
- **Block AZ follow-up** — **Visual model picker for a key's allowed models.** Replaced the `/bento/gateway` create form's free-text CSV field with `GatewayModelPicker` — an "Any model" / "Choose models" segmented toggle, live search, frosted vendor-gradient model cards (click-to-toggle with a check badge + hover-lift) grouped by provider, and a removable selected-pills tray. Reads the enabled LLM instances (`useLlmInstances`), stores the same comma-separated model-name value (blank = any) so the backend scope check is unchanged; i18n en+pt. `GatewayModelPickerTest` (5 RTL cases: any-mode hint, reveal grid, select emits CSV, pre-select from value, search filter); tsc clean.

## Block BA — Multi-model LLM instance (default-model selection)

- **T749** — **Select one or more models per LLM instance with a single default.** An `TurLLMInstance` now records a comma-separated `modelNames` superset (entity column + Liquibase `v2026.3.1.111`, `longtext`, null on legacy rows) while `modelName` stays the single **default** model the rest of the (still single-model) platform consumes — additive, so every existing consumer is unchanged. The `/bento/llm/instance/:id` form gains a dedicated **Models** card (`IconStack2`) driven by a new reusable `LlmModelMultiSelect` (`components/ui/llm-model-multi-select.tsx`) — an add-model popover (search + free-text ids, sourced from `useLlmModels`) plus per-model rows with a star toggle for the default (badged), remove, and first-remaining promotion when the default is removed; it always keeps a non-empty selection's default valid. The form seeds the selection from the legacy `modelName`, clears `modelNames` on vendor-preset changes (never mixes vendors), writes both `modelName` (default) + `modelNames` (CSV) back, and gates save on ≥1 model. Backend round-trip via `TurLLMInstanceAPI` update copy + `TurLLMInstanceDto extends TurLLMInstance` (MapStruct maps the inherited column); i18n en+pt. `LlmModelMultiSelect` RTL test (5 cases: empty state, per-model rows + default badge, star moves default, remove drops, remove-default promotes first); backend `mvn compile` + `tsc` clean. **Opens Block BA.** → ROADMAP Block BA

## Block BB — Unified model entity & visual model-type selection (§L)

- **T774** — **Retire the standalone `/bento/embedding` surface.** With embedding management fully absorbed by the unified LLM instance (cloud default T757, in-process ONNX creation T773) and the unified list making instances selectable everywhere (T757), the standalone embedding admin is retired: `/bento/embedding`, `/bento/embedding/instance` and `/bento/embedding/instance/:id` now `Navigate`-redirect to `/bento/llm/instance`; the `embeddingModel` bento-nav entry (and its now-unused `IconCube` import) is removed; and the three dead page components (`bento.embedding.{form,list.page,page}.tsx`, referenced only by the routes) are deleted. The `embedding-model → embedding` console-redirect alias is kept (it now double-hops to the unified surface). The `/api/embedding-model` endpoints (unified in T757/T758) and the `embedding-model.{queries,service}` used by the RAG-config pickers stay. **Closes Block BB entirely** (Phase 1 T750–T751 + Phase 2 T752–T758 + retirement T772–T774). *(Note: a full `tsc` couldn't run in-session — the merged dependabot bumps left the pnpm lockfile failing the `minimumReleaseAge` supply-chain policy, blocking install; the change only removes imports/routes/files and adds no new dependency, verified by inspection + IDE diagnostics.)* → ROADMAP Block BB / §L.3
- **T773** — **Instance-form ONNX embedding creation UI.** When the selected vendor is an in-process ONNX vendor (`TRANSFORMERS_LOCAL` / `HUGGINGFACE`, seeded T754), the unified LLM-instance form now shows an **ONNX embedding** section — HF: a repo-id field (→ `embeddingModelName`); local: `embeddingModelPath` (.onnx) + `embeddingTokenizerPath` fields; plus an optional `embeddingBatchSize` — and **hides the chat-model + cloud-embedding sections**. The "≥1 chat model" save gate is relaxed for these embedding-only instances (they instead require a repo id, or both ONNX paths). Everything is gated behind `isOnnxVendor`, so the chat-instance path renders and validates exactly as before. TS `TurLLMInstance` gains `embeddingModelPath`/`embeddingTokenizerPath`/`embeddingBatchSize`; i18n en/pt. tsc clean. This makes the unified form the home for in-process ONNX embedding creation — the prerequisite for retiring `/bento/embedding` (T774). → ROADMAP Block BB / §L.3
- **T772** — **Round-trip the ONNX embedding-serving fields on the unified instance API.** `TurLLMInstanceAPI`'s update copy now carries `embeddingModelPath` / `embeddingTokenizerPath` / `embeddingBatchSize` (alongside the T757 `embeddingModelName` / `rerankModelName`), so a local/HF in-process ONNX embedding can be fully persisted from the unified instance — the backend prerequisite for form-based ONNX embedding creation (T773) and the eventual `/bento/embedding` retirement (T774). Backend compile clean. (The `/bento/embedding` retirement was split from one oversized task into T772→T774.) → ROADMAP Block BB / §L.3
- **T758** — **Exchange + API/SDK backward-compat.** Closes the unification's compatibility surface. **Endpoint alias:** `GET /api/embedding-model/{id}` is now unified — a real `embedding_model` row still wins (so the standalone editor edits the actual row), falling back to an embedding-capable `llm_instance` mapped to the embedding shape, so an instance-backed embedding id is fetchable by external callers (the list was already unified in T757). **Exchange:** old embedding exports remain importable unchanged — `TurAIAgentImportService.upsertEmbedding` still upserts `embedding_model` rows (the table is intact by the T755 non-destructive design; the T755 reconcile then unifies them), and agents still reference `embedding_model` so agent export/import is byte-compatible. **SDK/shared-types:** verified a no-op — `@viglet/turing-shared-types` and both JS SDKs expose no embedding-model or LLM-instance types, so nothing external breaks. `TurEmbeddingModelListUnificationTest` grows to 6 (get-by-id: legacy wins, instance fallback, empty). **Closes Block BB Phase 2 committed scope (T752–T758);** only the 💭 T772 `/bento/embedding` retirement remains. → ROADMAP Block BB / §L.3
- **T757** — **Unified model admin — embedding-default picker + unified embedding list.** Two parts. **(1) Picker:** the LLM-instance form (`bento.llm.instance.form.tsx`) grew an **Embedding default** section — a `ModelCombobox` filtered to `kind=EMBEDDING` (reusing the T751 visual pickers, sourced from the same per-vendor model list so no extra fetch) writing the new `embeddingModelName`, cleared on a vendor-preset change so kinds never mix; backend round-trips `embeddingModelName` + `rerankModelName` through `TurLLMInstanceAPI`; TS `TurLLMInstance` model + i18n en/pt. **(2) Unified list:** `GET /api/embedding-model` now returns the union of **embedding-capable `llm_instance`s** (the T755-migrated ones + any instance given an embedding default, mapped to the embedding-model shape, id-preserved) and legacy `embedding_model` rows not shadowed by a same-id instance — so every embedding picker (SN-site GenAI / store / agent / default-embedding settings) reads unified options with no per-picker change; a dual chat+embedding instance is now selectable for RAG without a separate embedding row. `TurEmbeddingModelListUnificationTest` (3: merge, same-id shadow/dedup, chat-only excluded); backend compile + tsc clean. **Follow-up carved to T772:** retiring the standalone `/bento/embedding` surface waits until ONNX (local/HF) embedding *creation* is absorbed into the unified form (today that surface is still the only way to create in-process ONNX embeddings). → ROADMAP Block BB / §L.3 **Repoint embedding resolution onto the unified entity (unified-first, legacy fallback).** New bridge `TurRagContextBuilder.resolveEmbeddingEntity(id)` / `resolveEmbeddingModelById(id)`: because T755 preserved ids, an embedding-capable `llm_instance` is resolved first and **adapted into the `TurEmbeddingModel` shape the existing resolver already consumes** (`toEmbeddingModel` — cloud: the instance itself is the connection → set as the adapter's parent; local/HF: ONNX paths, no parent), so *zero* downstream logic changed and the proven resolution path is reused verbatim; falls back to the intact `embedding_model` row when no instance matches. Repointed the read-path consumers: the central `build(embeddingModelId,…)` chokepoint (→ SN-site GenAI + Lucene/pgvector/etc. vector stores), batch re-embedding (`TurReEmbeddingBatchHandler`), and the store API import + re-embed paths (`TurStoreInstanceAPI`) — dropping their now-unused `TurEmbeddingModelRepository` injections. Non-destructive: `embedding_model` stays present. **Scope note:** the agent's embedding *reference* (`TurAIAgent.turEmbeddingModelInstance`) and the default-RAG-startup provisioning that creates an `embedding_model` remain on the legacy entity (a reference/write concern) — they still resolve correctly because the bridge is id-based unified-first; migrating the reference itself rides with T757/T758. `TurRagContextBuilderEmbeddingResolutionTest` (5: cloud adapt, local ONNX adapt, non-embedding instance → legacy fallback, no-instance → legacy, blank → null) + `TurRagContextBuilderTest` updated; full-context smoke IT boots and resolves RAG against the migrated model. → ROADMAP Block BB / §L.3 **Embedding-model → LLM-instance data migration (id-preserving, non-destructive).** New `TurEmbeddingModelUnificationService.reconcile()` creates, for every legacy `TurEmbeddingModel`, an `llm_instance` row **with the same id** (reference stability — stored `embeddingModelId`s keep resolving) carrying the embedding default on the T753 `embeddingModelName` column: **cloud** rows copy vendor/url/encrypted-key from their parent instance; **local/HF** rows take the matching in-process vendor (id = `providerType`, T754) + the ONNX `embeddingModelPath`/`embeddingTokenizerPath`/`embeddingBatchSize`. Runs at startup and is **idempotent** (skips a model whose id already exists) — and, crucially, wired into `TurOnStartup` in a new **always-run** section (outside the `FIRST_TIME` guard) so it — and the T754 vendor self-heal — reach **existing** installs, not just fresh databases. Non-destructive: `embedding_model` is untouched and stays authoritative until T756 repoints consumers. Implemented as a testable/DB-portable reconciliation service rather than raw migration SQL (honours ADR 0004's intent). `TurEmbeddingModelUnificationServiceTest` (4: cloud copies connection, local sets ONNX vendor+paths, already-migrated skip, unresolvable-vendor skip) + `TurOnStartupTest` updated; the full-context smoke IT boots and actually migrates a seeded embedding model ("Unified 1 embedding model(s)"). → ROADMAP Block BB / §L.3 **Local/HF ONNX embedding modes as first-class vendors (per ADR 0004).** `TurLLMVendorOnStartup` now seeds two new `TurLLMVendor`s — `TRANSFORMERS_LOCAL` (Local ONNX / Transformers) and `HUGGINGFACE` (Hugging Face local ONNX) — embedding-only, no cloud/chat; their **id equals the embedding factory `PROVIDER_TYPE`** (`TurLocalEmbeddingModelFactory` / `TurHuggingFaceEmbeddingModelFactory`, referenced by constant for compile-time sync) so the in-process factory resolves by vendor instead of the loose `providerType` sentinel. Also **fixed a latent seeding bug**: the seeder was all-or-nothing (`if table empty`), so any vendor added after first boot never reached existing installs — refactored to idempotent per-vendor `ensureVendor(...)` (creates only when missing, never overwrites a user-edited row), so the vendor set self-heals on upgrade. `TurLLMVendorOnStartupTest` updated (13 vendors seeded when none present; existing vendors skipped while the rest — incl. the two ONNX — are still added). → ROADMAP Block BB / §L.3 **Unified schema + entity (additive, per ADR 0004).** Added six nullable columns to `llm_instance` (Liquibase `v2026.3.1.112`, per-column idempotent `columnExists` guards) so one instance can carry a **default model per kind** plus the embedding-serving fields folded from `TurEmbeddingModel`: `embeddingModelName` (embedding default) + `rerankModelName` (rerank default), and the in-process ONNX fields `embeddingModelPath` / `embeddingTokenizerPath` / `embeddingBatchSize` / `embeddingDimensions` (the last preserved on migration for the T627 dimension guard). `modelName` stays the CHAT default. Matching `@Column` fields on `TurLLMInstance` (camelCase literal mapping; paths `length=500`). Purely additive — all nullable, chat-only rows unchanged, **nothing reads them yet** (consumers repoint in T756); the `embedding_model` table is untouched until the T755 migration.
