Full-context boot applies the changelog cleanly (`TestSupportSmokeIT`); `TurLiquibaseChangelogAgnosticTest` + LLM-instance persistence tests green. → ROADMAP Block BB / §L.3 **Model-kind taxonomy + heuristic classifier (metadata-first).** New `TurLlmModelKind` enum (`CHAT · EMBEDDING · RERANK · IMAGE · TRANSCRIPTION · SPEECH · VIDEO · MODERATION · UNKNOWN`) + a pure `classify(id, label)` heuristic over per-vendor name patterns (order: `rerank` → `embed`/`voyage-` → `moderation` → image → transcription → speech → video → default `CHAT`; blank/null id → `UNKNOWN`). `kind` now rides on the backend `TurLlmModelOption` record (a backward-compatible `(id,label)` constructor auto-classifies, so every live-listing + catalog call site gains a kind for free) and the TS `TurLlmModelOption` model. `TurLlmModelDiscoveryService.isEmbeddingModel` is rewritten as `kind == EMBEDDING`; `model-catalog.json` entries may carry an optional `kind` override (via `TurLlmModelKind.parseOrClassify`). **Beyond the name heuristic**, the classifier prefers each vendor's *authoritative* type signal when the API exposes one: Gemini's `supportedGenerationMethods` (`TurLlmModelKind.fromGeminiGenerationMethods`) and a vendor `endpoints` array (Cohere: chat/embed/rerank, `fromVendorEndpoints`) are honoured in `TurLlmModelListingSupport`, falling back to the heuristic only when absent (OpenAI/Anthropic list ids only). Zero behavior change to consumers. `TurLlmModelKindTest` (26: classify matrix, blank→UNKNOWN, both authoritative mappers, override) + `TurLlmModelDiscoveryServiceTest` green. → ROADMAP Block BB / §L.1
- **T751** — **Visual model picker — kind badges + type filter.** Both pickers are now type-aware: a shared `ModelKindBadge` (`components/ui/model-kind-badge.tsx` — per-kind brand-palette pill + `MODEL_KIND_ORDER`/`resolveModelKind`/`modelKindLabelKey` helpers) renders a colour-coded kind badge on every option row, every selected row (`LlmModelMultiSelect`), and the combobox trigger (`ModelCombobox`); a shared segmented `ModelKindFilter` (`components/ui/model-kind-filter.tsx`) filters the popover by kind, rendering only the kinds actually present (hides itself below 2) and defaulting to a new `expectedKind` prop (`CHAT` on the chat instance form, `EMBEDDING` on the embedding form) — with an `effectiveFilter` guard that falls back to *All* when the chosen kind isn't present so the list never silently empties. i18n en+pt (`modelsFilterAll`/`modelsFilterLabel` + `modelKind*` labels). `LlmModelMultiSelect` RTL test grows to 8 (badge on selected row, filter appears only with ≥2 kinds + filters by kind, `expectedKind` default); tsc clean. → ROADMAP Block BB / §L.2
- **T752** — **ADR 0004 — one entity for every model kind.** Design deliverable (`docs/adr/0004-unified-model-entity.md`, no code) locking the `TurEmbeddingModel`→`TurLLMInstance` unification: keep the `TurLLMInstance` name (no rename — pure churn), add a nullable **default model name per kind** (`modelName` chat unchanged, new `embeddingModelName` + `rerankModelName`), fold the embedding-serving fields (`embeddingModelReference`→`embeddingModelName`, ONNX `embeddingModelPath`/`embeddingTokenizerPath`/`embeddingBatchSize`/`embeddingDimensions`) as nullable columns, model `TRANSFORMERS_LOCAL`/`HUGGINGFACE` as first-class in-process vendors, and an **id-preserving, non-destructive** migration (`embedding_model` kept as a read-only compatibility shadow one release) so every stored `embeddingModelId` reference keeps resolving and the T627 dimension guard sees identical dimensions. Unblocks T753–T758. → ROADMAP Block BB / §L.3

## Block BC — Public curated model catalog API (§LI)

> **Moved 2026-07-21 to [`openviglet/model-catalog`](https://github.com/openviglet/model-catalog)**
> (together with Block BD). Turing now consumes the published endpoint remotely
> (`turing.model-catalog.url`, remote-only). Entries below are the original Turing
> shipping history.

- **T762** — **Turing consumes the public catalog (offline-safe).** `TurLlmModelCatalog` now serves the picker fallback from the public endpoint first: a best-effort `@Scheduled` refresh (`turing.model-catalog.url`, default `https://turing.viglet.org/models/catalog.json`) fetches + parses the catalog on a TTL (default 6 h, ~8 s initial delay off the boot path, 3s/5s connect/read timeouts) and swaps it in **only on a non-empty success** — so a running instance picks up new models without a redeploy, while the bundled `model-catalog.json` (loaded at startup) is the always-present offline fallback and `catalog` never goes empty on a failed fetch. Parse logic extracted to a shared `parseCatalog(JsonNode)` (envelope + legacy-flat tolerant; ignores the published `vendor`/`contextWindow`/… extras). New config `turing.model-catalog.{url,remote-enabled,refresh-initial-delay-ms,refresh-interval-ms}` (env-overridable, documented in `application.yaml`); ITs pin `remote-enabled=false` to stay hermetic/offline. `TurLlmModelCatalogTest` grows to 7 (remote published-shape parse, legacy-flat parse, remote-disabled no-op keeps bundled). **Closes Block BC (T759–T763 all shipped).** → ROADMAP Block BC / §LI.4
- **T761** — **Public browsable `/models` reference page.** New `turing-site` page (`src/pages/models.tsx` + `src/lib/model-catalog.ts`) rendering every curated model grouped by vendor with colour-coded **kind badges** (mirroring the Block BB admin picker palette) + context-window / embedding-dimension / capability chips, a client-side search (id/label/vendor) and a kind type-filter, and a "Public API" callout linking the live `catalog.json` / pinned `catalog-v1.json` / schema with a `curl` example. Imports the emitted `public/models/catalog.json` at **build time** so the page is fully server-rendered/prerendered (crawlable, no runtime fetch, always in lockstep with the endpoint); includes a `Dataset` JSON-LD block for GEO. Wired into the SSG router (`App.tsx` `/models`) + prerender routes (`entry-server.tsx`). **Live at `https://turing.viglet.org/models`** (endpoint verified serving v1); tsc clean. → ROADMAP Block BC / §LI.3
- **T760** — **Public static JSON endpoint on `turing.viglet.org`.** Wired the T759 `emit-model-catalog.mjs` into the `turing-site` build (`build` + `build:client`, before `vite build`, plus an `emit:models` convenience script) so `public/models/catalog.json` + `catalog-v1.json` + `catalog.schema.json` are regenerated deterministically from the canonical source and copied into `dist/` → `_site/` on every deploy. The site is hosted on **GitHub Pages** (custom domain `turing.viglet.org`), which serves all assets with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` — so the endpoint is **CORS-open by default** (GitHub Pages allows no custom headers; cache is host-managed). Contract doc (`docs/references/model-catalog-api.md`) updated to BUILT & WIRED with the GitHub Pages CORS/cache reality; goes live at `turing.viglet.org/models/*` on the next manual **Publish GitHub Pages** run. Generated artifacts verified byte-identical on re-emit (no drift). → ROADMAP Block BC / §LI.2
- **T759** — **Canonical enriched catalog + JSON Schema + emit step.** Promoted `turing-app/.../llm/model-catalog.json` from a flat `{vendor:[{id,label}]}` fallback into the **single source of truth**: a versioned envelope (`version`/`lastUpdated`/`schema` + a `vendors` map) whose entries are enriched with the T750 `kind` (every entry) plus optional `contextWindow`/`embeddingDimensions`/`capabilities`/`deprecated` (curated conservatively — omit rather than guess, per STRATEGY §X.5; labels cleaned since the kind badge now conveys type). New Draft-2020-12 `model-catalog.schema.json` documents envelope + `ModelEntry`. `TurLlmModelCatalog.load()` now reads the envelope's `vendors` map (tolerant of the legacy flat shape). New zero-dep `frontend/apps/site/scripts/emit-model-catalog.mjs` validates the source structurally and emits the **public artifact** (`public/models/catalog.json` + pinned `catalog-v1.json` + `catalog.schema.json`, each entry flattened with its `vendor`) — 11 vendors, 63 models. `TurLlmModelCatalogTest` (4: envelope parse, kind on every entry, explicit per-entry kind, unknown-vendor empty) + existing catalog/kind tests green. → ROADMAP Block BC / §LI.1
- **T763** — **Positioning + public schema/usage docs (designed contract; endpoint still planned).** Ahead of the endpoint (T760), documented the public model-catalog API as a reviewable contract: STRATEGY §X.5 frames a vendor-neutral, kind-aware catalog at `turing.viglet.org` as a discoverability/authority asset (the LiteLLM-registry role, identity+kind-first not pricing; free/unauthenticated; ties into the Block O GEO thesis), and `docs/references/model-catalog-api.md` specifies the planned endpoints (rolling `/models/catalog.json` + pinned `/models/catalog-v1.json` + `catalog.schema.json`), the versioned envelope, the `ModelEntry` schema (`id`/`label`/`kind`=`TurLlmModelKind`/`vendor`/`contextWindow`/`embeddingDimensions`/`capabilities`/`deprecated`), CORS/versioning, curl/fetch usage, and how `TurLlmModelCatalog` will consume it (T762) with the bundled `model-catalog.json` as offline fallback. Both are marked **PLANNED — not yet live** (endpoint 404s until Block BC ships); indexed in `docs/references/README.md`. Docs-only. → ROADMAP Block BC / §LI
- **T775** — **Consume the catalog via the official client library** (post-close follow-up). `TurLlmModelCatalog` now fetches the public `openviglet/model-catalog` endpoint through the published **`io.github.openviglet:model-catalog-client` 1.0.2** (zero runtime deps — JDK `HttpClient` + a tiny built-in JSON reader) instead of a hand-rolled `RestClient` + Jackson `parseCatalog(JsonNode)`: the scheduled TTL refresh calls `client.refresh().entries()` and maps the typed `ModelEntry`/`Kind` into the vendor→`TurLlmModelOption` map (per-entry `kind` honoured when known, else the existing id/label heuristic via `parseOrClassify`), preserving the same remote-only, last-good-on-failure/empty semantics + `staticModels` surface. `turing.model-catalog.url` still selects the source (a trailing `*.json` filename is stripped to the base the client wants); the client is lazily built + injectable for tests. The shared client now owns the envelope contract, so the legacy-flat-shape parse tolerance is **dropped** (the endpoint always serves the envelope). Added the dependency to `turing-app`; `TurLlmModelCatalogTest` reworked to drive the client via an injected fake fetcher (6 units, fully offline). → ROADMAP Block BC / §LI

## Block BD — Multi-source model-catalog regeneration pipeline (§LII)

> **Moved 2026-07-21 to [`openviglet/model-catalog`](https://github.com/openviglet/model-catalog)** —
> the code (pipeline, canonical catalog, emit, public page) and its roadmap now live
> in that dedicated repo. Turing consumes the published endpoint remotely. Entries
> below are the original Turing shipping history.

- **T764** — **Schema enrichment + provenance (additive, still `version: 1`).** Extended `model-catalog.schema.json` with backward-compatible **optional** fields the pipeline populates — `maxOutputTokens`, `modalities` (`{input,output}` enum arrays via a new `$defs/modalities`), `knowledgeCutoff`, `releaseDate`, `aliases`, `status` (`PREVIEW`/`GA`/`DEPRECATED`/`RETIRED`), and per-entry provenance `sources`/`lastVerified`; envelope stays v1 (additive → consumers ignore unknowns). Updated the emit validator (`emit-model-catalog.mjs` — type-checks the new fields when present) + `docs/references/model-catalog-api.md` (new ModelEntry rows + additive note). → ROADMAP Block BD / §LII.1
- **T765** — **Pipeline scaffold + offline source-cache layer.** New zero-dep `frontend/apps/site/scripts/catalog/` (Node built-ins + global `fetch`, mirroring `emit-model-catalog.mjs`): `lib/util.mjs` (paths to the canonical source, arg parsing, snapshot cache, `fetchOrReplay`, kind heuristic, `compact`) + a `SourceAdapter` contract (`{id,vendor,envKey,fetch,normalize}`) with an `adapters/index.mjs` registry. Every live fetch is cached to `sources/<id>.json` with fetch timestamp + provenance (replayable via `--offline`); a missing API key **skips** that source with a warning, never hard-failing. → ROADMAP Block BD / §LII.2
- **T766** — **Per-vendor live-API fetchers.** Adapters normalizing each vendor's own listing endpoint into `ModelEntry` drafts: OpenAI `/v1/models` (ids → heuristic kind, ft: filtered), Anthropic `/v1/models` (display_name + created_at, CHAT), Gemini `models.list` (rich — `supportedGenerationMethods` → kind, `inputTokenLimit`/`outputTokenLimit`), Cohere `/v1/models` (`endpoints[]` → kind, `context_length`), Mistral `/v1/models` (`capabilities{}` → tools/vision, `max_context_length`); id-echoing labels dropped (OpenAI/Cohere/Mistral) so curated labels survive. Env-keyed, skipped when absent. → ROADMAP Block BD / §LII.3
- **T767** — **LiteLLM registry cross-source (non-pricing).** `adapters/litellm.mjs` ingests `model_prices_and_context_window.json` as a vendor-agnostic enrichment layer (`contextWindow`/`maxOutputTokens`/`embeddingDimensions`/`modalities`/capability hints), mapping `litellm_provider` → catalog vendor + `mode` → `TurLlmModelKind`, stripping `provider/` id prefixes and skipping `sample_spec`/wildcard entries. **Strictly non-pricing** — every `*cost*`/`*price*` key dropped (STRATEGY §X.5). → ROADMAP Block BD / §LII.3
- **T768** — **Merge + reconciliation engine.** `lib/merge.mjs` folds all drafts per `(vendor,id)` with precedence *pinned override (100) > live vendor API (50) > override (30) > committed catalog (15) > LiteLLM (10)*; per-field provenance + conflict detection + confidence, union of `capabilities`/`aliases`/`modalities`, deterministic ordering + canonical field order. Two safety rules: **carry-forward** (the committed catalog is itself a low-priority source, so a partial-key run never drops un-fetched vendors) and **positive-evidence removal** (an id is dropped only when its vendor's live API ran and omitted it, unless override-pinned). **Anchoring rule** — LiteLLM can enrich but never introduce a brand-new id. Top-precedence `overrides.json` (`__pin` forces above everything). Produces a **proposed** envelope only. → ROADMAP Block BD / §LII.4
- **T769** — **Validation + diff review gate.** `lib/validate.mjs` — `validateEnvelope` (schema-constraint check: kind/status enums, numeric ≥1, duplicate ids) + `diffReport` (per-vendor add/remove/change with per-field source attribution + `⚠ conflict` lines + skipped/removed summaries). The pipeline writes the proposed envelope + report to `scripts/catalog/out/` and prints the report; the canonical file is written (bumping `lastUpdated`) **only** on explicit `--apply`. → ROADMAP Block BD / §LII.4
- **T770** — **Orchestrator command + pipeline docs + tests.** `regen.mjs` chains fetch → merge → validate → report with `--apply`/`--offline`/`--only=`/`--date=` flags (propose-and-review by default, never auto-overwrite); `pnpm --filter turing-site catalog:regen`. Seed `overrides.json` (verified OpenAI embedding-dimension pins), `sources/`+`out/` gitignored. `docs/references/model-catalog-pipeline.md` documents the trusted source per vendor, env keys, precedence/anchoring rules, and the review workflow. 10 zero-dep `node:test` units (`catalog:test`) cover normalize/merge/validate/diff. → ROADMAP Block BD / §LII.5

## Block BE — Catalog-driven model intelligence (§LIII)

- **T776** — **Carry rich `ModelEntry` metadata through the catalog boundary.** `TurLlmModelCatalog.toCatalogMap` no longer collapses each catalog entry to `id/label/kind` (its Javadoc used to admit "extras ignored"): a new `TurLlmModelMetadata` carrier record (with nested `TurLlmModelPricing`/`TurLlmModelBenchmarks`/`TurLlmModelPerformance`/`TurLlmModelModalities`) preserves the `model-catalog-client`'s full `ModelEntry` — pricing, context/output limits, embedding dimensions, capabilities, modalities, benchmarks, performance, knowledge cutoff, release date, `deprecated`/`status` — plus the client `Classifier`'s tier/tags (`Classifier.classify`, best-effort). Added as an optional 4th component of `TurLlmModelOption` (`null` for live-vendor-listing models; backward-compatible 2-/3-arg constructors kept) and mirrored in the frontend `llm-model-option.model.ts`. Every field is nullable — absence means "unknown", never a default (`fromCatalog` returns a shell only when the entry carries something beyond `id/label/kind`; a bare entry keeps just the classifier tags). Catalog `kind` is trusted directly (heuristic only for a `null`/`UNKNOWN` kind). Foundational — every other Block BE task reads from this. `TurLlmModelCatalogTest` +3 (rich-metadata carry, classifier-tags-for-bare-entry, live-vendor null metadata). → ROADMAP Block BE / §LIII.1
- **T777** — **Auto-seed & refresh `TurLLMPrice` from catalog pricing.** A scheduled `TurLLMPriceCatalogReconciler` (`@Scheduled`, off the boot path on a TTL, gated by `turing.model-catalog.price-sync.enabled`, default on) walks the T776 catalog metadata and populates the manual `tur_llm_price` table from the catalog's indicative `pricing{inputPer1M,outputPer1M,currency,indicative,source,lastVerified}`. New `TurLLMPriceSource` discriminator (`CATALOG`|`MANUAL`) + `indicative`/`price_provenance`/`last_verified` columns on the entity (Liquibase `v2026.3.1.113`, additive, defaults `MANUAL` so every pre-existing row is preserved). `TurLLMPriceService.reconcileCatalogPrices(...)` creates a missing row and refreshes its own `CATALOG` rows but **never overwrites a `MANUAL`** (operator-entered / negotiated) rate; operator saves via the admin API are now stamped `MANUAL`. Closes the "$0 cost for unpriced models" gap so `computeCost` / `TurLLMTokenUsage.costUsd` populate automatically for well-known models. `TurLlmModelCatalog.allModels()` exposes the effective catalog; config keys documented in `application.yaml`. Tests: `TurLLMPriceServiceTest` +5 (MANUAL-on-save, create/skip-manual/refresh-catalog/blank-skip), new `TurLLMPriceCatalogReconcilerTest` (4). → ROADMAP Block BE / §LIII.1
- **T778** — **Price provenance & staleness in the cost UI.** The cost-governance price table now surfaces each row's provenance from T777: a **Source** column (Catalog/Manual badge + an `Indicative` badge, with explanatory tooltips) and a **Verified** column (catalog `lastVerified` date + a `Stale` badge when older than 180 days), plus a "verify at vendor" external link per row. Verify links come from a new `GET /api/v2/llm/price/provider-links` (backed by `TurLlmModelCatalog.providerPricingUrls()`, which reads the catalog client's `providers()` registry — `providers.json` `catalogVendor→apiPricingUrl` — refreshed best-effort on the same schedule, last-good-on-failure). Two warning callouts cross-check the period's billed models against the price table: **used-but-unpriced** (billed at $0, no price row) and **used-on-indicative-catalog-price** (published, unverified rate). Frontend `TurLLMPrice` model + `TurCostGovernanceService.getProviderLinks` extended; i18n (en/pt) added; tsc clean. Editing a `CATALOG` row via the admin API flips it to `MANUAL` (operator override) automatically. `TurLlmModelCatalogTest` +2 (provider-link parse, garbage-shape tolerance). *(The enriched model-picker price surfacing rides along with the T779 picker rebuild rather than being built here then rebuilt.)* → ROADMAP Block BE / §LIII.1
- **T779** — **Enriched model picker.** A shared `components/ui/model-meta.tsx` turns the T776 catalog metadata into UI: `ModelMetaLine` renders a compact, wrap-friendly line of facts per model row — price (in/out per 1M), context window, max output, intelligence index, TTFT, knowledge cutoff, and capability icons (vision/tools/reasoning), each with a full `title` tooltip — and `ModelTierBadge` shows the Frontier/High/Mid/Light tier. Both `model-combobox.tsx` and `llm-model-multi-select.tsx` render the meta line + tier on every dropdown option (and on the trigger / selected rows), and gain a `ModelSortSelect` sort control (`sortModelOptions`: relevance / cheapest / largest-context / most-intelligent; options missing a metric sink to the end, ties stable). All facts come from catalog metadata only — a live-vendor model shows nothing (never a zeroed placeholder), and the sort control hides itself when no option carries metadata. i18n (en/pt) for sort labels + meta tooltips + capability names. `llm-model-multi-select.test.tsx` +2 (metadata line/tier/sort-visibility, cheapest-first sort); 10 green; tsc clean. → ROADMAP Block BE / §LIII.2
- **T780** — **Auto-fill instance metadata from the catalog.** In the bento LLM-instance form, selecting a model now pre-fills the instance's **context window** and **max output tokens** from the T776 catalog metadata (fill-when-empty — an operator's explicit value is never clobbered; they can still edit/clear), and picking an embedding model pre-fills **embedding dimensions**. A new **Context window** field is rendered (the entity field existed but wasn't editable), and a shared `CatalogLimitHint` shows the catalog value under the context-window + max-tokens inputs and warns (amber) when a hand-entered value exceeds the catalog maximum for that model (a likely mis-entry that would fail at runtime). Backend: the LLM-instance **update** endpoint now persists `contextWindow` (previously silently dropped) and `embeddingDimensions` — the latter guarded so a payload omitting it preserves the runtime-detected value (T627). Frontend `TurLLMInstance` model gains `embeddingDimensions`; i18n (en/pt) added. `TurLLMInstanceAPITest` +2 (persist context/dims, preserve-detected-dims-when-omitted; 10 green); tsc clean. → ROADMAP Block BE / §LIII.2
- **T781** — **Capability/modality-aware feature gating.** The bento LLM-instance form now validates the tool-calling and file-upload toggles against the selected model's T776 catalog metadata: a `CatalogCapabilityWarning` shows an amber note under `toolsEnabled` when the model isn't listed with the `tools` capability, and under `fileUploadEnabled` when the model advertises neither a `vision` capability nor a file-like input modality (image/file/pdf/document/audio/video). The check is conservative — silent when the catalog is unknown or the capability is present, so it never second-guesses a model the catalog doesn't describe (only warns on positive evidence the model lacks it). Two pure helpers `catalogSupportsTools` / `catalogSupportsFileInput` live in the shared `model-meta.tsx`. i18n (en/pt); new `model-meta.test.tsx` (10: sort + capability helpers); tsc clean. **Scope note:** seeding the F.15 native-capability matrix (`TurLLMInstanceCapability`) from the catalog was intentionally *not* folded in — that matrix models provider-native tools (web_search / computer_use / bash…), a distinct concept from the catalog's generic capability flags, so mapping one onto the other would be a lossy guess. → ROADMAP Block BE / §LIII.2
- **T782** — **Model lifecycle / deprecation warnings.** A new `TurLLMModelLifecycleService` scans configured LLM instances and flags any whose default model the T776 catalog marks `deprecated` or `status = DEPRECATED/RETIRED` (agents inherit their model from the instance, so instance scanning covers them). For each it computes a **suggested GA replacement** — same vendor + kind, not itself end-of-life, covering the deprecated model's capabilities, at a similar-or-lower indicative input price, ranked by intelligence index then price. Exposed via `GET /api/v2/llm/model-lifecycle/deprecations` (`TurLLMModelLifecycleAPI`) and a daily `@Scheduled` warn-log scan (off the boot path, best-effort) so the signal reaches logs too. Frontend: an amber **deprecation banner** on the bento LLM-instance list (via `headerAction`, links each flagged instance + shows the suggested replacement) driven by a new `useLlmDeprecations` query, and a `ModelDeprecatedBadge` on every deprecated model row in both pickers (`isDeprecatedModel` in `model-meta.tsx`). i18n (en/pt); `TurLLMModelLifecycleServiceTest` (4: flag+suggest, ignore-current, retired-without-flag, not-in-catalog) + `model-meta.test.tsx` +2 (12); tsc + backend compile clean. Closes Block BE Phase 2. → ROADMAP Block BE / §LIII.2
- **T783** — **Pre-flight batch cost estimate.** New `TurLLMBatchCostEstimator` projects the USD cost of a *batch* run (eval dataset, research study, batch re-embed) before it spends: it prices `itemCount` items at a per-item input/output token budget against the catalog-backed rate table (T777) and returns `{perItemCostUsd, totalCostUsd, priced, budgetUsd, overBudget}` — a pure projection (never runs or persists), clamping negatives to 0 and reporting `priced=false` (total $0) for an unknown/local model rather than failing. Exposed via `POST /api/v2/llm/cost/preflight-batch` (`TurLLMPreflightBatchCostAPI`) so a UI can gate an expensive run behind a "this will cost ~$X — proceed?" confirm. Complements the per-turn chat quote (T162) and the runtime soft caps (`TurChatCostBudgetGate`), covering the offline-batch path they don't. `TurLLMBatchCostEstimatorTest` (4: total projection, over-budget, unpriced/local, negative-clamp). *(Backend primitive + endpoint; wiring the confirm UI into each specific batch runner is follow-on.)* → ROADMAP Block BE / §LIII.3
- **T784** — **Cost/quality/latency-aware gateway routing.** Extended the Block AZ gateway router (`TurGatewayRouterService`, T745) with three catalog-driven ordering strategies on top of the existing `rr`/`latency`/`failover`: `cost` (cheapest by catalog `pricing.inputPer1M`), `quality` (highest `benchmarks.intelligenceIndex`) and `ttft` (lowest `performance.latencyTtftSec`) — e.g. `turing-router:cost/gpt-4o-mini-inst,claude-haiku-inst` returns the cheapest-first candidate order (the rest stay the fallback chain). Each strategy resolves the catalog metadata for every declared deployment's `(vendor, modelName)` via `TurLlmModelCatalog`; a deployment whose model has no catalog value for the metric always sorts **last** (a missing figure never wins a cheapest/fastest pick) and ties keep declared order. Plugs into the existing `orderedCandidates` seam, so the gateway consumes it with no further change. `TurGatewayRouterServiceTest` +5 (parse cost/quality/ttft, cheapest/highest-intelligence/lowest-ttft ordering, missing-metric-last). Backend-only; no library change. → ROADMAP Block BE / §LIII.3
- **T785** — **Model advisor ("find me a model").** New `TurLLMModelAdvisorService` — a deterministic, constraint-driven recommender over the T776 catalog metadata (no LLM call). `recommend(AdvisorQuery)` filters catalog models by required `capabilities` (superset), required input `modalities`, minimum `contextWindow`, a `maxInputPricePer1M` budget ceiling, a `minIntelligenceIndex`, a `minTier` (Frontier>High>Mid>Light), and `kind` (default CHAT), then ranks survivors best-first (intelligence index desc → cheaper input price → larger context) capped at a `limit` (default 10). Models with no metadata are admitted only when the query is fully unconstrained. Exposed via `POST /api/v2/llm/model-advisor/recommend` (`TurLLMModelAdvisorAPI`) so an operator can describe what they need ("vision, ≥128k context, ≤ $5/1M, ≥ High tier") and get a ranked shortlist to one-click into a new instance. `TurLLMModelAdvisorServiceTest` (5: all-constraints+rank, budget ceiling, min-intelligence/tier, kind filter, limit). *(Backend recommender core; the advisor wizard UI + one-click-create — reusing the existing instance-create API — is follow-on.)* → ROADMAP Block BE / §LIII.3
- **T786** — **Right-sizing / savings suggestions.** New `TurLLMRightsizingService` runs the model loop backwards: from each agent's observed usage (a new `findUsageByAgentAndModel` roll-up over the `llm_token_usage` ledger — dominant model, request count, summed input/output tokens) it derives the average turn size and monthly spend, then finds the **cheapest same-vendor catalog model that still fits**: same kind, not deprecated (`TurLLMModelLifecycleService.isEndOfLife`, now public), covering the current model's declared capabilities, with a context window ≥ the observed turn size ×1.5, and strictly cheaper per turn (priced via `TurLLMPriceService`). Reports `{currentMonthlyCostUsd, projectedMonthlyCostUsd, projectedMonthlySavingsUsd, savingsPercent, avgInput/OutputTokens, requestCount}` per agent, exposed at `GET /api/v2/llm/cost/rightsizing?windowDays=30`. **Signal caveat:** Turing meters tokens/cost per turn but not which capabilities a turn exercised, so "capabilities actually exercised" is approximated conservatively by the current model's *declared* catalog capabilities — a suggestion never drops a capability the model advertised. Unpriced/local ($0) agents are skipped. `TurLLMRightsizingServiceTest` (4: suggest+savings math, capability-not-covered skip, context-too-small skip, unpriced skip); lifecycle test still green. Closes Block BE Phase 3. → ROADMAP Block BE / §LIII.3
- **T787** — **Surface knowledge cutoff.** The model picker already renders each model's `knowledgeCutoff` (T779 `ModelMetaLine`); this adds the opt-in disclosure path: a per-agent `discloseKnowledgeCutoff` flag (entity + Liquibase `v2026.3.1.114`, default false, carried through agent export/import + the update API) and a new `TurKnowledgeCutoffPromptContributor` (order 450, right after the capability blocks) that, when the flag is on and the catalog knows the configured model's cutoff, appends "Your knowledge cutoff is <date>. You have no reliable knowledge of events after this date…" to the assembled system prompt so answers self-disclose staleness. Resolves the cutoff from the agent's first-enabled instance's `(vendor, modelName)` via `TurLlmModelCatalog`, best-effort (a lazy-load/lookup failure degrades to no disclosure); a no-op segment for default-off agents (byte-identical). Frontend: a "Knowledge Cutoff Disclosure" toggle in the agent settings form + `TurAIAgent` model field; i18n (en/pt). `TurKnowledgeCutoffPromptContributorTest` (4: off→empty, on+cutoff→line, on+no-cutoff→empty, order); pipeline + Liquibase tests green; tsc clean. → ROADMAP Block BE / §LIII.4
- **T788** — **Catalog change-feed notifications.** `TurLlmModelCatalog` now consumes the client `changes()` registry (the public `changes.json` — verified live and populated) on each scheduled refresh, parsing the untyped map into a typed `TurCatalogChanges` (added/removed/changed entries, tolerant of odd shapes, last-good-on-failure). `TurLLMCatalogChangeService.notificationsForUsedModels()` cross-references the feed against every configured instance's `(vendor, modelName)` and emits a `ChangeNotification` per affected model: **REMOVED** (dropped from the catalog), **SUPERSEDED** (removed while a newer same-vendor model was added — carries the added ids as replacement candidates), or **CHANGED** (e.g. repriced — carries the feed's detail). Exposed at `GET /api/v2/llm/model-lifecycle/change-feed`; the bento LLM-instance list shows a blue info banner (via `useLlmCatalogChanges`) listing the changes to your models. i18n (en/pt). `TurLLMCatalogChangeServiceTest` (5) + `TurLlmModelCatalogTest` +2 (feed parse, garbage-shape tolerance); tsc + compile clean. *(Optional email notification deferred — the notification data + endpoint are the primitive; wiring an email channel is a separate concern.)* → ROADMAP Block BE / §LIII.4
- **T789** — **Consumer-plan & provider-pricing reference.** The provider "verify at vendor" links already shipped in T778 (`providers()` `apiPricingUrl`); this rounds out the reference with the consumer-plan side: `TurLlmModelCatalog` now consumes the client `plans()` registry (the live `plans.json`) on refresh into a typed `TurCatalogPlans` (vendor → indicative plans: name/tier/monthly+annual price/url, tolerant of the untyped shape, last-good-on-failure). Exposed at `GET /api/v2/llm/price/consumer-plans`, and the cost-governance page gains a **"Consumer plan reference"** card listing plans grouped by vendor (Claude Pro / ChatGPT Plus…), each linking to the vendor page — with a "reference only, verify at vendor" disclaimer. i18n (en/pt). `TurLlmModelCatalogTest` +2 (plans parse, garbage-shape tolerance); tsc + compile clean. **Closes Block BE — Catalog-driven model intelligence (T776–T789, all 14 tasks shipped).** → ROADMAP Block BE / §LIII.4
- **T886 (UI follow-on)** — **Model Advisor wizard + one-click create.** Realized the deferred T785 UI: a bento tool page at `/bento/llm/advisor` (`bento.llm.advisor.page.tsx`) where an operator picks required capabilities (tools/vision/reasoning), a minimum context window, a max input price, a minimum intelligence index and a minimum tier, then gets the ranked catalog shortlist from `POST /api/v2/llm/model-advisor/recommend`. Each result shows tier badge + price/context/intelligence/capabilities and a **Create instance** button that one-click-creates the LLM instance (maps the catalog vendor slug → the seeded `TurLLMVendor`, POSTs a draft instance with the chosen model, and navigates to its edit page; falls back to the manual new-instance form when no vendor matches). A "Find a model" launcher was added to the LLM-instance list header. New route (`BENTO_LLM_ADVISOR`) + lazy import; `TurLLMInstanceService.recommendModels` + advisor model types; new `modelAdvisor` i18n namespace (en/pt); tsc clean, 64 bento/model-meta tests green. → ROADMAP Block BE / §LIII.3
- **T887 (UI follow-on)** — **Batch-cost confirm dialog.** Realized the deferred T783 confirm UI: a reusable `components/llm/batch-cost-confirm-dialog.tsx` (`BatchCostConfirmDialog`) that quotes a batch run via `POST /v2/llm/cost/preflight-batch` and shows the projected total (+ per-item breakdown, an unpriced/local note, and an over-budget warning) with **Run anyway / Cancel**. Wired into the **Synthetic Research study runner** (`bento.persona.research.workspace.page`): clicking **Run** now gates behind the dialog, estimating `personas × max-interview-turns` items priced against the interview lane's (or default) model — the vendor id lower-cased to match the catalog price table. The dialog is runner-agnostic, so the eval-dataset and batch-embed runners can adopt it with the same props. Frontend `TurCostGovernanceService.preflightBatch` + `TurBatchCostRequest`/`TurBatchCostEstimate` models; `batchCost` i18n (en/pt); tsc clean. → ROADMAP Block BE / §LIII.3
- **T888 (email follow-on)** — **Change-feed email channel.** Realized the deferred T788 optional email: a new `TurLLMCatalogChangeEmailService` — a daily `@Scheduled` + `@SchedulerLock` (cluster-once) job that, when the catalog change feed has notifications for in-use models, emails the Global-Settings recipient a brand-styled HTML digest (removed / superseded / changed models + suggested replacements) via `TurEmailService` (which skips gracefully when email isn't configured). **Opt-in** via `turing.model-catalog.change-feed.email-enabled` (default off — the in-UI banner is always on regardless), and **deduped** by a signature of the notification set so an unchanged feed is never re-mailed (the signature only advances on a successful send, so a transient failure retries). Config keys documented in `application.yaml`. `TurLLMCatalogChangeEmailServiceTest` (5: disabled no-op, empty no-op, send-then-dedupe, re-send on change, retry-on-failure). → ROADMAP Block BE / §LIII.4

## Block BF — Vectorless (Structured-Data) RAG: name it, feed it, integrate it (§LIV)

- **T790** — **Name & surface "Vectorless (Structured-Data) RAG".** Promoted the T392 catalog-copilot retrieval path (NL→DSL over a declared field schema → structured search, **no embeddings** → cited answer) to a first-class, explicitly-named knowledge-base *mode* — the capability already existed but was invisible to an integrator scanning for "LLM Q&A over structured data without a vector DB". New `TurSNKnowledgeBaseMode` enum (`VECTOR` | `VECTORLESS_STRUCTURED` | `HYBRID`, lenient `fromValue` defaulting to `VECTOR`, `isVectorless()`/`needsVectorSetup()` predicates) on `TurSNSiteGenAi` (`@Column knowledgeBaseMode`, Liquibase `v2026.3.1.115`, default `VECTOR` → every existing site unchanged), persisted through the SN-site update API. The `/chat/enabled` readiness walk (`ChatDisabledReason`) is now mode-aware: a `VECTORLESS_STRUCTURED` site reports ready when a default LLM is configured (new `MISSING_DEFAULT_LLM` reason) instead of the classic `MISSING_EMBEDDING`/`MISSING_STORE` — so the AI-Mode surface advertises correctly for a site that legitimately needs neither. Frontend: a **Knowledge Base Mode** selector (Vector / Vectorless-Structured / Hybrid, with per-mode explanatory hints) on the SN-site GenAI form + `TurSNKnowledgeBaseMode` TS type + i18n (en/pt). End-user docs page `docs/products/vectorless-structured-rag.md` (what it is, when to pick which mode, how to enable, the `/copilot` API, guarantees). No new retrieval code — pure discoverability + config surface over the engine that already ships. `TurSNKnowledgeBaseModeTest` (4) + `TurSNSiteGenAiAPITest` +3 (vectorless-ready, vectorless-no-LLM → `MISSING_DEFAULT_LLM`, vector-mode still reports `MISSING_EMBEDDING`); 15 green; backend compile clean. → ROADMAP Block BF / §LIV.1
- **T791** — **Scheduled JSON-feed source (pull ingester).** New `TurStructuredFeedIngestService` (`com.viglet.turing.genai.feed`) — the "pull" side of ingestion for a Vectorless (Structured-Data) RAG knowledge base, closing the one genuine gap (content previously had to be *pushed* by an external connector). A cluster-once `@Scheduled` + `@SchedulerLock` sweep (off the boot path, gated by `turing.genai.structured-feed.enabled`, default off; a no-op with no sources) walks each configured `Source`: GETs the feed's **raw** body via a new `TurUrlFetchService.fetchRaw(String)` — which reuses the exact T739 SSRF/allowlist guard + resolved timeout but **skips the Tika text-extraction** that `fetch()` runs (Tika would mangle JSON) — accepting a top-level JSON array, a JSON-object envelope (records under a configured `recordsField`, flattening a map-of-arrays such as the model catalog's `vendors`), or newline-delimited JSON (`catalog.ndjson`); optionally converges the SN-site field schema from a field manifest (`{schemaVersion, fields:[{name,type,facet,multiValued,mandatory,description}]}`) idempotently + additively via `TurSNFieldProvisioner.ensureField` (never destructive); maps each record's scalar/array fields to `TurSNJobItem` attributes under the T381 grounding contract (blank/null/empty omitted, nested objects skipped, id under `TurSNFieldName.ID`); imports via the existing `TurSNImportAPI.send(TurSNJobItems)` seam (no self-HTTP); and de-indexes ids that vanished since the previous run (in-memory per-node `lastSeenIds` diff — a restart re-syncs, documented best-effort). Fail-open per source (missing site / unreachable feed / unparseable body → a failed `IngestResult`, never throws). New `TurStructuredFeedProperty` (`@ConfigurationProperties("turing.genai.structured-feed")`, opt-in `Source` list) + config documented in `application.yaml`. `TurStructuredFeedIngestServiceTest` (8: array/NDJSON/envelope-flatten parsing, attribute mapping under grounding, site-not-found fail-open, create-import, cross-run de-index, manifest schema declaration); backend compile clean. → ROADMAP Block BF / §LIV.2
- **T792** — **Copilot React widget + react-sdk hook.** Closed the frontend gap for Vectorless (Structured-Data) RAG: a `useTuringCopilot` hook + a thin, embeddable `TuringCopilot` widget in `@viglet/turing-react-sdk`, bound to the non-streaming `POST /api/sn/{siteName}/copilot`. New `postCopilot(site, messages, locale)` + `fetchCopilotAvailable(site)` in `core/api.ts` (plain `axios.post`/`get`, inheriting the host's baseURL + CSRF — no SSE plumbing since the copilot answers in one JSON response) plus mirrored `TurCatalogCitation` / `TurCatalogCopilotResult` wire types. The hook resolves the site from `<TuringProvider>` (or an explicit `siteName`), keeps a bounded multi-turn conversation (posts the full history, latest user turn drives retrieval like `TurCatalogCopilotResult`), probes `/copilot/available` on mount, and fails soft (unavailable copilot / degraded answer surfaces via `error`/`available` without throwing; a degraded result still exposes citations). Every assistant turn carries its `TurCatalogCitation[]`, and the `TuringCopilot` widget renders them as linked `[n]` sources — the copilot analogue of the vector-RAG `ai-mode-chat`, design-agnostic (no bundled CSS, fully skinnable via a `classNames` map + `citationComponent`/`emptyComponent` slots) so an external static site can drop it in. Exported from the SDK index (hook + widget + types); react-sdk `tsc --noEmit` clean. → ROADMAP Block BF / §LIV.3
- **T793** — **Stuff-all mode for small datasets.** The extreme-vectorless path: when a catalog is small enough to fit in one prompt, `TurCatalogCopilotService` skips the NL→DSL parse entirely and grounds the LLM on **every** row. `answer()` now tries a new `stuffAllRetrieve(site, locale)` first (a `match_all` query capped at `stuffAllMaxDocs`, projecting *all* hits — not the `MAX_RESULTS=8` cap — into citations + context blocks via an extracted shared `project(hits, max)` helper), and only when it applies falls back to the filtered `retrieve()`. Self-gating by size: it returns empty (→ filtered fallback) when disabled, when the index is empty, when the fetched page is a *partial* catalog (`totalHits > hits.size()` — never stuff a partial set), or when the assembled context exceeds `stuffAllTokenBudget` (estimated `chars/4`). Enabled by default because it only activates when the corpus genuinely fits, and stays cited + fail-open like the filtered path (applies no filters, so the grounded-query summary is blank). New `TurCatalogCopilotProperty` (`turing.genai.copilot.stuff-all-{enabled,token-budget,max-docs}`, defaults on/40000/1000) documented in `application.yaml` + the capability doc. `TurCatalogCopilotServiceTest` +2 (within-budget grounds on all rows with blank summary; tiny-budget falls back to filtered NL→DSL); 6 green, compile clean. → ROADMAP Block BF / §LIV.4
- **T797** — **Structured-feed nested-object flattening.** Generic, opt-in per-source flag (`flatten-nested`, default false) on the T791 ingester so a feed whose decision fields live under nested objects becomes searchable. `TurStructuredFeedIngestService.toAttributes` now takes a `flatten` param and, via a new recursive `addField(key, value, attrs, flatten)`, expands a nested object into `parent_child` flat keys (`{pricing:{inputPer1M:5}}` → `pricing_inputPer1M`); arrays of scalars stay multi-valued and the T381 grounding contract (omit blank/empty) is preserved. Legacy behaviour unchanged when off (nested objects are still dropped). Closes the gap where a real-world catalog feed's pricing/benchmarks/limits (nested) never indexed. `TurStructuredFeedProperty.Source.flattenNested` + `application.yaml` example; `TurStructuredFeedIngestServiceTest` +1 (flatten expands pricing_*/modalities_input, drops the raw object key) = 9 green, compile clean. → ROADMAP Block BF / §LIV.7
- **T796** — **Startup auto-provisioning of a config-driven vectorless KB.** Generic opt-in that turns a pure-env-var deployment into a working Vectorless (Structured-Data) RAG knowledge base with no manual admin step. A structured-feed `Source` gains `provision: true` (+ optional `seInstanceId`, `description`); a new `@Order(5)` `TurStructuredFeedProvisioningOnStartup` `ApplicationRunner` (after `TurDefaultRagInfraOnStartup`) — for each provisioning source whose SN site is absent — creates the bare site via `TurSNSiteManifestService.provision` (a minimal `VigletFieldManifest`; the declared field schema is then converged by the ingest, keeping field parsing in one place), sets the site's GenAI binding to `knowledgeBaseMode = VECTORLESS_STRUCTURED` (creating an agent-less GenAI record if none — the copilot needs only a default LLM), and runs the first ingest immediately (ongoing refresh stays with the scheduled ingester). **Zero-config SE**: when no `seInstanceId` is given it resolves a default embedded **Lucene** SE instance (reused via new `TurSEInstanceRepository.findByTurSEVendor_Id("LUCENE")`, or created at `./store/lucene-se`), so a deployment needs no search-engine setup. Fully fail-open (any per-source error is logged, never blocks boot) and idempotent (empty-site guard; existing sites only re-assert the mode). Product-generic — the concrete site/feed/manifest live only in env config. `TurStructuredFeedProperty.Source.{provision,seInstanceId,description}` + `application.yaml` example; `TurStructuredFeedProvisioningOnStartupTest` (5: reuse-lucene, create-lucene, explicit-se, existing-site no-op, provision-off skip) = 14 green with the ingester suite, compile clean. → ROADMAP Block BF / §LIV.8
- **T794** — **Dogfood: model-catalog as the first vectorless KB on `turing-demo.viglet.org`.** The reference integration proving Vectorless (Structured-Data) RAG end-to-end across three repos — no new Turing product code (it composes T790–T793 + T796–T797), only wiring + docs. **model-catalog repo** (Block M / T59–T60) emits `query-manifest.json` (the field schema) + `catalog.ndjson` (the feed) + `context.txt` (the stuff-all bundle). **Viglet Cloud** wires the `turing-demo` service purely by env: `turing.genai.structured-feed` with one provisioning source pointing at the published catalog (`provision: true`, no `se-instance-id` → embedded Lucene, `flatten-nested: true`), so on boot T796 auto-creates the `model-catalog` SN site, converges its schema, sets Vectorless mode (T790), and the ingester (T791) pulls ~250 rows the copilot answers whole via stuff-all (T793) — reachable at `POST /api/sn/model-catalog/copilot` (allowlisted by the demo's nginx `/api/sn/` rule). **Turing** side is this ROADMAP/CHANGELOG entry + the `docs/products/vectorless-structured-rag.md` live-example section; the deploy config lives in `cloud` (`docker/docker-compose.yml` + `DEMO.md`) and the emit artifacts in `model-catalog` (each committed in its own repo, per that repo's conventions). The flagship query *"cheapest embedding model with ≥ 1M context?"* now answers with cited model links. → ROADMAP Block BF / §LIV.5
- **T795** — **Vectorless RAG over the OpenAI-compatible gateway.** Extended the Block AZ gateway (§XLIX) so any OpenAI-style client gets grounded, cited structured answers with zero Turing-SDK code: `TurGatewayModelRouter` gains a `turing-copilot:<site>` route (+ `isCopilot`/`copilotAnswer`, folded into `isNative`) that invokes `TurCatalogCopilotService.answer(site, …)` and appends the `TurCatalogCitation[]` as a plain-text **Sources** footer (id/title + link) so citations survive the OpenAI wire format (which has no side channel). A header form `x-turing-copilot-site: <site>` routes any `model` to the copilot too — added to `TurGatewayOptions` (new `copilotSite` field, a backward-compatible 4-arg constructor + `from(...)` overload keep every pre-T795 caller/test compiling) and read in `TurOpenAiCompatibleGatewayAPI`; `TurGatewayLlmService.routedAnswer` now branches on the prefix or the header (both blocking + streamed-as-one-chunk paths). A natural extension of the T744 `turing-sn:<site>` / RAG-as-a-header routing — no embedding model / vector store needed. `TurGatewayModelRouterTest` +3 (detect prefix, cited-sources footer, unavailable→throw, blank-site→throw); 19 gateway tests green, compile clean. **Closes Block BF — all of T790–T797 shipped.** → ROADMAP Block BF / §LIV.6
- **T798** — **Ground returned citations to what the answer actually cites (fix stuff-all citation flooding).** Bug fix: in stuff-all mode (T793) `TurCatalogCopilotService` grounds the LLM on **every** catalog row via `project(hits, hits.size())`, so `answer()` returned the whole catalog (~246 rows) as "Cited models" instead of the handful the answer names (the filtered `retrieve()` path caps at `MAX_RESULTS` and never flooded). `answer()` now separates the *grounding set* from the *returned citations*: after the LLM responds, a new `groundCitations(answer, all)` parses the distinct `[n]` markers the answer emitted (`CITATION_MARKER` regex → `parseCitedRanks`) and keeps only those citations, mapped rank→citation in answer order, for **both** paths (stuff-all still grounds on all rows; only the projected `citations` shrink). Fail-open: when no `[n]` is parseable — or none match a citation — it degrades to the filtered top-k slice (`min(MAX_RESULTS, size)`), never back to all rows; the LLM-failure degraded path bounds the list the same way (no answer text → top-k). A `MAX_RETURNED_CITATIONS = 24` backstop caps the list regardless. Transport-agnostic — the `/copilot` endpoint, the T795 gateway route and the `catalog_search` tool all inherit the fix — keeping citations meaningful, bounded and deep-linkable for the model-catalog "Ask the catalog" widget. `TurCatalogCopilotServiceTest` +2 (stuff-all returns only cited `[3][7]`; no-markers fails open to bounded top-k of 8), existing filtered test updated to cite `[1][2]`; 8 green, compile clean. → ROADMAP Block BF / §LIV.9
- **T801** — **`VECTORLESS_STRUCTURED` truly suppresses embeddings everywhere (fix) + explicit UI.** The T790 mode was only *advisory* — the indexing/ranking paths ignored it, so a vectorless site whose (own or T622 default) agent had RAG on still generated embeddings on import/reindex, and its public search still ran the embedding-based hybrid ranking (the origin of the model-catalog `LazyInitializationException` on `TurSNSiteGenAi.getSnRankingMode`, which only bit sites that *have* a GenAI binding). Now the mode is the single gate on every embedding path: **(1)** `TurGenAiContextFactory.build` returns a disabled context for a vectorless site — the one chokepoint both the batch `reindexVectorStore` and the per-document real-time `TurSNGenAi.addDocument` share — so neither ever embeds; **(2)** `TurDefaultAgentResolver.isRagReady` folds in `needsVectorSetup()`, so the ANN search API/controller and the `/api/features` live-search chip report not-ready; **(3)** `reindexVectorStore` early-returns with a clear skip log (no worker-pool spin-up); **(4)** `TurSNHybridRankingService.isEnabled` short-circuits vectorless → the public search stays on the pure lexical path (`fuse`/`indexDocument`/`deIndex`/dedup/similar all no-op) — a simpler path with no vector machinery to fail. Decoupled from the default-agent fallback: a `VECTORLESS_STRUCTURED` site never embeds even when the Default AI Agent has RAG on. Frontend makes the implicit explicit: an amber "embedding indexing is OFF for this site" callout on the SN GenAI Knowledge-Base-Mode selector (en/pt), and `ragEnabled`/`annEnabled` now fold the mode in as a single source of truth (the RAG reindex button and the ANN-Search launch tile disappear in vectorless mode). `TurGenAiContextFactoryTest` +2, `TurDefaultAgentResolverTest` +3, `TurSNHybridRankingServiceTest` +2; 53 green; backend compile + frontend `tsc` clean. → ROADMAP Block BF / §LIV.11
- **T799** — **Rank by the named metric, not the biggest number (copilot answer quality).** Bug fix: asked *"which model is the most intelligent?"* the model-catalog copilot answered **GPT-OSS-120B** citing an "intelligence index" of **93.4** — but 93.4 is that model's **math** sub-score; its real `benchmarks_intelligenceIndex` is **23.8** (below Claude Fable 5's **59.9**). Root cause: `TurCatalogCopilotService.contextBlock` printed every flattened attribute as a raw `key: value` (`benchmarks_scores_math_value: 93.4` sitting beside `benchmarks_intelligenceIndex: 23.8`), so the LLM grabbed the largest number. Fix, both sides of the mis-read: **(a)** each grounded value is now labelled by its *declared field description* (`TurSNSiteFieldExt.description`, surfaced through the `TurNLFacetField` schema) with the flat key kept as a parenthetical anchor — e.g. `Overall intelligence index — the headline metric (benchmarks_intelligenceIndex): 23.8` — via a new `fieldDescriptions(schema)` map threaded into the shared `project(...)`/`contextBlock(...)` for **both** the filtered `retrieve()` and the T793 stuff-all path (stuff-all now resolves the site schema too); **(b)** a new system-prompt HARD RULE: a "best/most/highest/cheapest X" question ranks by the field whose *label* names X as the overall metric and never treats a per-domain sub-score as the headline index just because its number is larger. Transport-agnostic (endpoint + T795 gateway + `catalog_search` tool). Sibling of the shipped T798. `TurCatalogCopilotServiceTest` +1 (context labels values by description + prompt carries the named-metric rule); 9 green, compile clean. → ROADMAP Block BF / §LIV
- **T802** — **Structured-query planner matches facet values case-insensitively + honours numeric sort intent (copilot answer quality).** Bug fix: the model-catalog copilot returned the empty *"no matching results"* fallback (HTTP 200, `totalHits:0`) for most natural questions because the NL→facet planner emits facet values **lowercased** (`kind=embedding`) while SN facet matching was **case-sensitive** (the value is indexed as `EMBEDDING`) — so *"embedding models"* → 0 hits, while `kind=EMBEDDING` → 28. `vendor=openai`/`capabilities=vision` worked only because that data is already lowercase; uppercase enums (`CHAT`/`EMBEDDING`/`RERANK`) silently retrieved nothing. Fix, both halves: **(a)** the embedded Lucene executor now matches a `term`/`terms` filter on a **STRING** field case-insensitively — a double-quoted Lucene `RegExp` literal (no metachar interpretation) matched with `RegExp.CASE_INSENSITIVE`, field-type-aware via the already-resolved `fieldTypes` map, applied to `Term` and each `Terms` value; the `id` field and every non-STRING type keep their exact `TermQuery` so unique-id and numeric semantics are unchanged. **(b)** `TurLlmNLFacetParser` prompt hardening: the rendered schema now carries each field's **declared description** (so the model can map *"cheapest"* to the right numeric field and read what each facet means), and a new HARD RULE turns superlative/comparative phrasing (*"cheapest"*, *"lowest price"*, *"most affordable"*, *"highest/best <metric>"*) into a `sort` (asc for a minimum, desc for a maximum) — never a bogus term filter on the adjective, which was the second 0-hit path. Transport-agnostic (endpoint + T795 gateway + `catalog_search` tool). Sibling of T799 — the retrieval/query-planning side. `TurDslLuceneExecutorTest` +2 (STRING term → case-insensitive `RegexpQuery` while `id` stays `TermQuery`; live-index proof that `embedding` matches indexed `EMBEDDING`, `OPENAI` matches `openai`, and `terms` is per-value case-insensitive); 71 dsl/copilot/eval tests green, compile clean. → ROADMAP Block BF / §LIV

## Block BG — Batch / bulk structured-feed indexing (§LV)

- **T803** — **Batch the indexing-queue consumer through the existing `indexDocuments(...)` bulk API.** `TurSNProcessQueue.processIndexingQueue` now groups consecutive non-spotlight `CREATE` job items by `(siteName, locale)` and routes each group through the already-present-but-unused `TurSearchEnginePlugin.indexDocuments(site, locale, List<attributes>)` (one round-trip for Solr/ES) plus a single trailing `commit(...)`, instead of a per-item `index()` → `indexDocument()` + implicit per-doc flush. `DELETE`/`COMMIT`/spotlight items flush the open batches first (so ordering relative to the CREATEs is preserved) and then process per item exactly as before; the T335 `ensureCoreExists` dedup is untouched. A **single-document group stays on the proven per-item path** (already one round-trip) so only genuine multi-doc messages pay the bulk machinery — every existing single-CREATE behaviour is byte-for-byte unchanged. The per-document enrichment chain (`TurSNMergeProvidersProcess.mergeDocuments`, content-fit/Gemini-media/microthesaurus enrichers, `removeDuplicateTerms`, T381 grounding) is preserved by pre-building each document's attribute map through a shared `buildIndexAttributes(...)` extracted from `index()`; the T383 hybrid-vector side-write still runs per doc (no-op for vectorless/legacy sites). Robust fail-open: a bulk failure — or a partial result (`indexed < size`) — degrades the whole group to per-item indexing (which carries the engine's own retries). `TurSNProcessQueueTest` +3 (bulk path uses `indexDocuments`+`commit` once and never `indexDocument`; failure falls back to per-item); 18 green, compile clean. → ROADMAP Block BG / §LV.1
- **T805** — **Hoist field-schema convergence out of the per-document loop.** The bulk path (T803) converges the **union** of the batch's `TurSNJobAttributeSpec`s once per site — deduplicated by field name via a new `convergeFields(...)` — before the bulk index, instead of calling `turSNFieldProvisioner.ensureField(...)` inside every item's `index()`. The provisioner is already idempotent (T382), so this purely stops paying the `fieldExists`/DB check hundreds of times: schema-check cost drops from O(docs × fields) to O(distinct-fields). Folds naturally into the T803 batching as its schema-prep step (the single-doc path keeps calling `createMissingFields` as before). `TurSNProcessQueueTest` covers the union converging each field exactly once. → ROADMAP Block BG / §LV.3
- **T804** — **True bulk `indexDocuments` override for the embedded Lucene plugin.** Solr and Elasticsearch already override the batch API (coalesced update request), but the embedded Lucene SE plugin — the **default zero-config engine** the vectorless dogfood runs on (T796) — inherited the per-doc default loop, which commits **per document** (`TurLuceneDocumentHandler.addDocument` did `updateDocument` + `commit()` + storage-sync each time). New `TurLuceneSearchEnginePlugin.indexDocuments(...)` delegates to a real batched write: a shared `buildDocument(...)` (extracted from `addDocument`, so a batched doc is built identically to a single one) + `updateDocument` for every doc in **one** `IndexWriter` session with **no** per-doc commit, then a single `commit()` and one storage sync at the end — an 800-document catalog pays one flush instead of 800. Upsert-by-id semantics are preserved (re-ingesting never duplicates). On any I/O error the whole batch throws so `TurSNProcessQueue` (T803) degrades to the per-document path, which still carries the schema-conflict / stale-writer self-healing retries. `TurLuceneSearchEnginePluginTest` +3 (bulk delegates to `indexingBatch`; empty → 0; no instance → 0); 39 green, compile clean. → ROADMAP Block BG / §LV.2
- **T806** — **Chunk the structured-feed ingester into bounded bulk imports.** `TurStructuredFeedIngestService.ingestSource` previously accumulated **all** mapped records into one `TurSNJobItems` and sent a single JMS message consumed in one long `@Transactional` (a huge message + a huge transaction for an 800+-row feed). It now splits the mapped records — CREATEs plus the full-run de-index DELETEs — into bounded chunks via a new `turing.genai.structured-feed.batch-size` (default 200; `0`/negative = one unbounded chunk, legacy behaviour) and sends each chunk as its **own** bulk import ending in a terminal `COMMIT`, with per-chunk progress logging and **per-chunk fail-open** (one failing chunk is logged and skipped, never aborting the rest). The de-index diff (`lastSeenIds`) is still computed over the **full** run, so chunking never changes which vanished ids are reconciled (`appendDeletions` became `buildDeletions` returning the DELETE items into the same chunked stream). Keeps JMS message + consumer-transaction size bounded and gives visible progress instead of one silent long pass. `TurStructuredFeedProperty.batchSize` + a static `partition(...)` helper; `TurStructuredFeedIngestServiceTest` +3 (splits into 3 chunks each ending in one COMMIT; one bad chunk never aborts the rest; partition edge cases) = 12 green, compile clean. → ROADMAP Block BG / §LV.4
- **T807** — **Opt-in direct bulk-index fast path bypassing JMS for vectorless reindexes.** The JMS `indexing.queue` hop exists to offload async embedding; a strictly **vectorless** / RAG-disabled site (T801) has none, so for a very large reindex the queue + its per-message transaction buy nothing. New `TurSNBulkImportService` + a `POST /api/sn/import/bulk` endpoint on `TurSNImportAPI`: when the fast path is enabled (`turing.sn.import.bulk-direct.enabled`, default **off**) **and** every targeted site is RAG-disabled, the documents are written straight to the search engine by calling the consumer logic (`TurSNProcessQueue.processIndexingQueue`) synchronously in bounded chunks (`turing.sn.import.bulk-direct.batch-size`, default 500) — reusing the exact T803 batching, T805 convergence and per-document enrichment the queued path uses, but skipping the queue and one transaction-per-message (each chunk keeps its own `@Transactional` boundary). Per-chunk fail-open. **Vectorless-only**: if any targeted site has RAG enabled the request is declared ineligible and transparently **falls back to the ordered queue** (`send(...)`) — never rejected, never starving a site of its async embedding — so the queue stays the default, authoritative path. `TurSNBulkImportServiceTest` (7: eligibility gating on the flag + vectorless-only, bounded-chunk dispatch, per-chunk fail-open, single unbounded chunk); 7 green, compile clean. **Closes Block BG — T803–T807 all shipped.** → ROADMAP Block BG / §LV.5

## Block BO — Answer-first search: the steerable, auditable answer engine (§LXII)

- **T845** — **The answer surface feeds the self-tuning loop — and closes Block BO.** An answer engine on a real faceted index emits a relevance signal Perplexity structurally can't: a user narrowing a facet *after* reading an answer is explicit feedback that retrieval returned the wrong subset. This captures that (plus thumbs, citation-click, follow-up, re-answer) and folds it into the shipped `selftuning` miner. Because the answer endpoint is stateless SN-site streaming (no chat-analytics session to attach to), the signals live in a dedicated append-only table — `TurAnswerSignalEvent` (`tur_answer_signal`, Liquibase `v2026.3.1.122`, indexed on `agentId`), mirroring the T170 `chat_turn_feedback` DPO table rather than widening the chat-analytics session document across both store backends. `TurAnswerSignal` (6 values, each flagged `relevanceMiss` — only `FACET_REFINED_AFTER_ANSWER` and `THUMBS_DOWN`) drives a persisted flag so `TurAnswerSignalService.record(...)` can stamp each signal with the site's **effective agent** (the same `TurDefaultAgentResolver` the answer runs through — the miner's key) via a fail-open `POST /api/sn/{site}/answer/signal`. `TurSelfTuningMiner` now folds an agent's recent relevance-misses into its failure digest alongside the ABANDONED/ERROR/tool-error sessions — so the drafter learns from answer feedback, not just chat. Across the SDKs `recordAnswerSignal` + a `signal(kind)` method on the `useTuringAnswer` hook and the vanilla controller (each minting a per-answer `conversationId` to correlate), and `TuringAnswer` emits organically — citation-click, follow-up-taken, re-answer, facet-refined-after-answer — plus an opt-in thumbs row (`showFeedback`). Additive and fail-open throughout (a dropped signal never surfaces; a site with no agent is a no-op). `TurAnswerSignalServiceTest` (4) + `TurSelfTuningMinerTest` (3, incl. fail-open on a repo error) = green, `TurSelfTuningServiceTest` unbroken at 6; `answer.test.ts` +2 (emit after answer, no-op before) = 14 vanilla-sdk green; react-sdk `tsc` clean. → ROADMAP Block BO / §LXII.14
- **T844** — **Every good answer becomes a linkable, unfurlable artifact.** An answer engine that can't be linked to leaves its best output trapped in one session; this mints a stable permalink for an answer and gives it an Open-Graph card, reusing the shipped `TurChatShareOgService`. Backend: a `TurAnswerShare` entity + repository (Liquibase `v2026.3.1.121` — `tur_answer_share`, `longtext` JSON columns, idempotent `preConditions`) persists the immutable snapshot (query + filter state + source/citation JSON + answer text + `answeredAt`); `TurAnswerShareService.create/find/renderOgCard` mints it and renders the card (question as `title`, a 200-char answer excerpt as `description`, redirecting humans to the read-only SPA view); three endpoints on `TurSNAnswerAPI` — `POST /answer/share` (mint), `GET /answer/share/{id}` (read the snapshot back), `GET /answer/share/{id}/og` (the crawler card). Across the SDKs `createAnswerShare`/`fetchAnswerShare` call them, both `useTuringAnswer` and the vanilla `createAnswerController` gained a `share()` that snapshots the current successful answer (query/filters/answer/sources/citations/answeredAt) and returns the share id (rejecting before an answer completes), and `TuringAnswer` gained an opt-in `showShare` button with `onShared`/`onShareError` callbacks. Additive and fail-open — `fetchAnswerShare` returns `null` on a miss. `TurAnswerShareServiceTest` (3: create persists + returns id, `renderOgCard` maps title/dest/description, empty for unknown id) = green; `answer.test.ts` +2 (mints a permalink snapshotting the answer, refuses before completion) = 12 vanilla-sdk green; react-sdk `tsc` clean. → ROADMAP Block BO / §LXII.13
- **T843** — **The answer shows its work as it works.** Pure presentation over shipped plumbing — the cheapest way to make a multi-second answer feel deliberate rather than slow. The runtime already emits `tool_call` start/end SSE events (T436); `TurAnswerStreamFramer` **dropped** them, and now forwards each as a `trace_step` event (kept distinct from T839's terminal `trace` summary). Across the SDKs the named-event parser collects `trace_step` frames into a `traceSteps` list (reusing the existing `TurChatToolCall` shape — `{callId, name, phase, argsSummary?, status?, durationMs?}`) on the answer result/state (hook + vanilla controller), and `TuringAnswer` renders a collapsible research-trace timeline from the completed steps ("search_knowledge_base · 42ms · ok"), opt-out via `showTrace={false}`. Additive and fail-open: a turn that invokes no tools shows no trace. `TurAnswerStreamFramerTest` +1 (tool_call → trace_step, in order) = 11 backend green; `answer.test.ts` +1 (trace steps collected in order) = 10 vanilla-sdk green; react-sdk `tsc` clean. → ROADMAP Block BO / §LXII.12
- **T842** — **Multi-query fan-out: one question, several retrievals.** Perplexity's recall advantage on multi-hop / comparative questions is mostly fan-out, not model quality — one turn issued one retrieval. Now a comparative question ("Anthropic vs OpenAI models", "price and context window of Claude") is decomposed into bounded sub-queries whose recall is unioned before rerank. Two pure, reusable primitives: `TurRagRrf.fuseKeyed(rankedLists, keyOf, topK)` generalizes Reciprocal Rank Fusion to **N ranked lists over any keyed item** (Documents by id, DSL hits by id), and a deterministic, fail-open `TurQueryFanOut.decompose(question, maxQueries)` splits on explicit comparison/conjunction markers (`vs`/`versus`/`compared to`/`and`/`or`/commas), always keeping the whole question for holistic recall (no LLM, CI-testable; a question with no marker stays single-pass). Wired into `TurCatalogCopilotService.retrieve` behind `turing.genai.copilot.planning.fan-out` (**off by default**), bounded by `planning.max-passes`, and **never for DETERMINISTIC sites** — each sub-query is planned + searched, the hit lists RRF-unioned by id, then the fused page goes through the existing hybrid rerank + projection. Fail-open at every step (a failed sub-query or fan-out falls back to the single-pass path), so the change is purely additive — the existing catalog tests (which use the DETERMINISTIC planner) are untouched. `TurQueryFanOutTest` (5) + `TurRagRrfTest` +2 (N-way union, top-k/null tolerance) + `TurCatalogCopilotServiceTest` unchanged at 12 = green. → ROADMAP Block BO / §LXII.11
- **T841** — **The answer can be an artifact, not just prose.** For comparative / enumerable questions over a structured catalog, the answer renders as a comparison table / spec grid instead of paragraphs. Key insight: the answer endpoint already runs through the agent executor, which can invoke the shipped **answer-as-app** generative-UI client tools — so the answer-shape decision (prose vs. table vs. list) is the LLM's existing tool-calling decision; no new planner needed. The only gap was transport: `TurAnswerStreamFramer` **dropped** `client_tool_call` events. It now forwards them as a new `artifact` SSE event carrying the render spec (`{callId, name, args}`). Across the SDKs the named-event parser collects `artifact` frames into an `artifacts` list on the answer result/state (hook + vanilla controller), and `TuringAnswer` renders them through a `renderArtifact` slot the host wires to the answer-as-app components (`TuringGenerativeContent` / `ANSWER_AS_APP_COMPONENTS`), with the prose becoming a lead-in above the artifact. New `TurAnswerArtifact` type. Additive and fail-open: a site whose agent has no answer-as-app tools simply streams prose as before. `TurAnswerStreamFramerTest` +1 (client_tool_call → artifact, in order) = 10 backend green; `answer.test.ts` +1 (artifacts collected into state) = 9 vanilla-sdk green; react-sdk `tsc` clean. → ROADMAP Block BO / §LXII.10
- **T840** — **An answer that knows when it has decayed.** No answer engine on the market models an answer as something that goes stale; Turing already computes citation drift (T155) and only reported it internally. This wires that signal to the answer surface. A new `TurCitationDriftService.checkFreshness(question, refs, answerProducedAt, topK)` re-resolves an answer's cited sources against the live index and returns per-source verdicts, **reusing the exact `evaluate` policy the daily drift scan runs** (STALE = re-indexed after the answer, or the cited passage is gone; UNVERIFIABLE = no longer retrievable). A `POST /api/sn/{site}/answer/freshness` endpoint (safe, JSON) exposes it. Across the SDKs `postAnswerFreshness`/`fetchAnswerFreshness` call it; the `useTuringAnswer` hook and the vanilla controller now stamp `answeredAt` when the stream completes, expose a `checkFreshness()` that populates `staleSources`, and `TuringAnswer` renders a drift **badge + one-click re-answer** when a cited source has changed (opt-in auto-check via `checkFreshnessOnAnswer`). Fail-open throughout — gated by the existing `turing.genai.citation-drift.enabled` (default off), so no badge rather than a false one. `TurCitationDriftServiceTest` +2 (re-indexed/vanished flagged, fresh stays fresh, disabled → empty) = 14 backend green; `answer.test.ts` +1 (only STALE sources flagged) = 8 vanilla-sdk green; react-sdk `tsc` clean. → ROADMAP Block BO / §LXII.9
- **T839** — **"Why this answer" — the answer engine shows its work.** The audit trail a regulated buyer asks for first, positioned directly against black-box answer engines. The answer stream's `trace` event now carries the retrieval provenance the service knows up front: the site's **knowledge-base mode** and the **rerank strategy** (with an on/off flag) — computed in `TurSNAnswerService` from the site's `TurSNSiteGenAi` and `TurGlobalSettingsService` and folded into the terminal `trace` by `TurAnswerStreamFramer` (a new 3-arg `frame(...)` taking a `TurAnswerProvenance`; the 2-arg overload stays). The per-source rank breakdown — the T389 `rankingExplanation` (lexical / semantic / fused rank, RRF score) — already rides each `TurAnswerCitation` on the `citations` event. A new headless `TuringAnswerProvenance` panel (`@viglet/turing-react-ui`, zero-dep, structural-mirror types) renders it all as a native `<details>` disclosure — KB mode, rerank strategy, source count, then per-cited-source the `rankingExplanation` entries — every slot class-nameable with default token styles; `TuringAnswer` mounts it under the answer (opt-out via `showProvenance={false}`). It renders nothing when there's nothing to audit. `TurAnswerStreamFramerTest` +1 (provenance rides the trace) = 9 backend green in the class, `TurSNAnswerServiceTest` still 4 green; `TuringAnswerProvenance.test.tsx` (3) → **78 react-ui tests green in 11 files**; react-sdk `tsc` clean. → ROADMAP Block BO / §LXII.8
- **T838** — **The answer is synthesized within the filters you can see and turn — the block's flagship claim.** A Perplexity answer has no filter state to turn; Turing's sits on a real faceted index, so the answer can be *steered*. The active facet filter set now reaches the answer engine: `GET /sn/{site}/answer` accepts repeated `fq=field:value` params, `TurSNAnswerAPI` parses them (split on the first colon so a value may contain colons) into the `field → values` map, and `TurSNAnswerService` threads them into the streaming request — so retrieval narrows and the answer is grounded **within** that set (empty = whole KB). Across the SDKs `postAnswer`/`fetchAnswer` and both `ask` methods gained a `filters` option serialized back to `fq` params; the `TuringAnswer` component renders a removable **constraint chip** row ("Answering within: Vendor = Anthropic · Context ≥ 200k") and re-runs the current question whenever the filter set changes (turning a facet re-synthesizes; removing a chip widens), inverting the classic answer-engine failure — a confident answer over the wrong subset — into a visible, editable control. Mechanically small (the filter map already reached retrieval), deliberately additive. `TurSNAnswerServiceTest` +1 (filters reach the streaming request via an `ArgumentCaptor`) = 4 backend green; `answer.test.ts` +1 (filters serialize to repeated `fq=` params) = 7 vanilla-sdk green; react-sdk `tsc` clean. → ROADMAP Block BO / §LXII.7
- **T837** — **One answer surface, three consumers instead of three forks.** Closes Phase 2: a headless `useTuringAnswer` hook + a design-agnostic `TuringAnswer` component in `@viglet/turing-react-sdk`, plus an observable `createAnswerController` in the vanilla `@viglet/turing-sdk`, so the admin search page, the showcase and the public-site hero consume one implementation of the fused answer surface. All three drive the T833 streaming `GET /sn/{site}/answer` endpoint, which required a **new named-event SSE parser** (`consumeAnswerStream` / `fetchAnswer` in each package): the existing chat parser is data-only (event name in the JSON `type`), but the answer stream emits real `event: <name>\ndata: …` frames — so the parser tracks the `event:` line, accumulates multi-line `data:` per frame (a token's internal newlines survive), and dispatches `sources`/`token`/`citations`/`followups`/`trace`/`error`. The hook returns `{ answer, sources, citations, followups, trace, status, error, isStreaming, ask, stop, reset }`, falls back to the `<TuringProvider>` site, and guards superseded/aborted asks; the component sits on the `TuringCopilot` skinning contract (every part class-nameable, zero bundled styles), renders the answer through `TuringMarkdown` with the T836 `[n]` anchors wired to source cards it stamps with `id="turing-source-${rank}"`, and renders follow-up chips that re-ask. Fail-open throughout (a mid-stream `error` event lands in state, never a thrown stream). New answer types (`TurAnswerCitation`, `TurAnswerTrace`, `rank` on `TurChatSource`). `answer.test.ts` (6: sources→tokens→citations→followups→trace into observable state, subscriber notification, mid-stream error fail-open, blank/wildcard no-op, reset, and a multi-line-token parse) → **6 vanilla-sdk tests green**; react-sdk `tsc` clean; a Storybook story documents the surface. → ROADMAP Block BO / §LXII.6
- **T836** — **`[n]` in the answer becomes a click that jumps to the source.** The main RAG answer shipped inert `[n]` prose (gap #2's visible half); this makes each marker a live anchor. New `rehypeCitationAnchors` plugin for `TuringMarkdown` (`@viglet/turing-react-ui`) rewrites every `[n]` text marker into a `<sup data-turing-citation="n">` element — walking the hast tree by hand to keep the package zero-runtime-dep, and skipping `code`/`pre` so `arr[0]` is never mistaken for a citation; an optional `isValidCitation` gate leaves unbacked numbers as plain text. Its companion `citationMarkdownComponents` factory returns the `components` map that renders those marked sups as `TuringCitationAnchor`s (and passes genuine superscripts / GFM footnote refs straight through): clicking one scroll-links to the matching source card — the element with id `${idPrefix}${n}` (default `turing-source-n`) — and flags it with `data-turing-citation-target` for a brief pulse, while hovering shows the cited passage. The native-provider path's exact-span underline was already shipped (`TuringCitedAnswer`, T154), so this closes the RAG-path half. Zero-dep, headless (skin via `classNames` or the token stylesheet, which gains the anchor + pulse styles); depends on T832 so the `[n]` and the chip share a footnote. `TuringCitationAnchor.test.tsx` (7: the rehype rewrite / code-skip / `isValidCitation`, the scroll+highlight+onActivate behavior and custom idPrefix, the plain-sup passthrough, and an end-to-end render through `TuringMarkdown`) → **75 react-ui tests green in 10 files**, tsc clean. → ROADMAP Block BO / §LXII.5
- **T835** — **Turning AI on no longer costs the user their results.** Gap #1, the single highest-leverage change in the block: `search-content.tsx` rendered `showAiMode ? <AiModeChat/> : <SearchResultsPanel/>`, so entering AI mode *replaced* the results, facets and pagination with a chat — an answer engine never makes that trade. Replaced the exclusive toggle with composition: the answer panel (`AiModeChat`) now renders **above** a permanently-mounted results area (`SearchResultsPanel` / `SearchEmpty`), so the streaming answer, source cards and results share one page. `_aimode` becomes an **expand/collapse preference** — expanded shows the full answer panel, collapsed falls back to the compact `AiChatCard` — never a mode switch that hides results. Submitting the search bar in AI mode now fires **both** in parallel (per the T833 contract): `store.submitSearch()` refreshes results/facets/pagination *and* the answer panel re-asks, with the query left in the bar since the results are on-screen (previously it cleared the input and ran only the chat). New `search-content.test.tsx` (3: answer panel renders above still-mounted results when AI mode is on; collapses to the compact card keeping results when off; submit fires search AND answer) → **165 frontend tests green in 33 files**, the changed file type-clean. → ROADMAP Block BO / §LXII.4
- **T834** — **The answer suggests where to go next, for free on a repeat.** Fills the `followups` slot T833 wired: a new `TurFollowUpQuestionService` makes one cheap LLM pass over the finished answer + its citations and returns 3–5 next questions a reader of *this* answer would naturally ask. Cached on `(site, query, source set)` through `TurLlmSummaryService`'s discipline (source ids sorted into the key, so the same answer over the same evidence always hits), so a repeated query costs nothing. Honours the ROADMAP Non-goal on structured output — the model returns one question per line (not JSON), parsed into a bounded, de-duplicated list that strips stray bullets/numbering and drops any echo of the original question. Wired into the answer stream through a `TurFollowUpGenerator` seam so `TurAnswerStreamFramer` stays a pure, LLM-free transform (production passes the service; tests pass a stub); the framer emits the `followups` event lazily *after* citations so the (instant) chips never wait on the follow-up LLM call, and the SN path's old co-occurrence `options` follow-ups are now dropped in favour of this answer-aware pass.
