Fail-open at every step — no default LLM, an LLM error, an unsuccessful result or a thrown generator all degrade to no chips, never a broken stream. Distinct from `use-turing-related-terms` (search-term co-occurrence) and Gemini's `searchSuggestions` (web-only). New `TurFollowUpQuestionService` + `TurFollowUpGenerator`; `TurFollowUpQuestionServiceTest` (6: parsing/dedupe/echo-drop/cap, and the no-LLM / blank-answer / success / unsuccessful fail-open paths) + `TurAnswerStreamFramerTest` +2 (generated follow-ups between citations and trace; generator failure → no chips) + `TurSNAnswerServiceTest` updated = 17 green across the answer package, compile clean. → ROADMAP Block BO / §LXII.3
- **T833** — **A dedicated streaming answer endpoint, sources before the first token.** Gap #3 of the block: there was no single streaming answer endpoint — `/copilot` is POST→JSON and the SN chat SSE is a *conversation*, not an answer, with no guarantee sources arrive before prose. New `GET /api/sn/{siteName}/answer` (SSE) streams the answer as named `ServerSentEvent`s in the contract order: `sources` (before a single token, so the client paints source cards first), then `token`s, then `citations`, `followups` and `trace`. Rather than re-implement retrieval, it **reuses the proven public SN retrieve → rerank → ground → stream pipeline** (`TurSNGenAi.assistantConversationStreaming`, which already applies the rerank seam, respects the site's `TurSNKnowledgeBaseMode` and emits sources-before-tokens) and re-frames that internal `Flux<ChatResponse>` (event name in the record `type`) into the answer contract via a pure, unit-tested `TurAnswerStreamFramer`. The framer numbers the incoming sources positionally through the T832 `TurAnswerCitationNumberer` (so each chip carries a footnote), accumulates the answer text, and on completion emits a `citations` event (the `TurAnswerCitation` projection, re-aligned to any `[n]` markers the answer actually emitted — fail-open to positional when the prose has none, never truncating the chip list) plus a minimal, honest `trace` (source count + answer length; T843 enriches). Conversation side-channels (`grounding`/`secondOpinion`/`form`/`tool_call`) are dropped so the answer stream stays clean; the SN path's `options` follow-ups map to the `followups` event (T834 generates real ones). Deliberately **not** an `answer` field on `TurSNSiteSearchBean` — the hot search path never pays LLM latency; the client fires search and answer in parallel. Every failure mode (blank query, unknown site, no LLM, mid-stream provider error) degrades to a single terminal `error` event via `Flux.defer` + `onErrorResume`. New `TurSNAnswerService` + `TurSNAnswerAPI` (`com.viglet.turing.genai.answer` / `api.sn.genai`), `TurAnswerTraceEvent`, and `TurAnswerCitation.withRank`. `TurAnswerStreamFramerTest` (6: ordering, positional numbering, marker re-alignment, no-marker no-truncation, unparseable-sources pass-through, empty-token drop) + `TurSNAnswerServiceTest` (3: blank-query and unknown-site error events, happy-path composition) = 9 green, compile clean. → ROADMAP Block BO / §LXII.2
- **T832** — **One footnote-numbering contract for every answer path, and a chip that carries its number.** The T800 renumbering that makes an answer's `[n]` markers agree with its citation chips lived *inside* `TurCatalogCopilotService.groundCitations`, so only the catalog copilot got numbered citations — the main SN/RAG answer shipped un-numbered chips and inert `[n]` prose. Lifted the logic verbatim into a shared, generic `TurAnswerCitationNumberer` (`genai.citation`, the package neutral to both `genai.catalog` and `genai.rag`): `renumberByMarkers(answer, all, rankOf, renumber, maxReturned, failOpenTopK)` parses the `[n]` markers actually present, remaps raw ranks to compact answer-order footnotes `1..k`, rewrites the prose, and returns the citations in footnote order — with the same fail-open (marker-less prose degrades to a bounded top-k renumbered `1..k`, text untouched) and `MAX_RETURNED_CITATIONS` backstop as before. The catalog path now supplies only how to read/rewrite a `TurCatalogCitation`'s rank and delegates; its 12 existing tests pass unchanged, proving the extraction is behaviour-preserving. The SN/RAG path has no `[n]` markers in its streamed text, so it takes the numberer's positional `numberInOrder(...)` in `TurChatStreamingDispatcher.drainRagSources` — right after `dedupeByDocument` — assigning each surviving source a 1-based footnote. That required a new `rank` on `TurRagSource` (7th component with a back-compat 6-arg constructor so every existing call site and `fromDocument` compile unchanged, plus `withRank`). Finally, one `TurAnswerCitation` projection the catalog, SN/RAG and native-citation paths all normalize onto (`fromCatalogCitation` / `fromRagSource`, with the per-sentence `TurChatCitation` answer-span folded in via `withAnswerSpan` as an *enrichment*, not a fourth shape) — the shared shape the streaming `/answer` endpoint (T833) and `useTuringAnswer` (T837) will emit. New `TurAnswerCitationNumbererTest` (9) + `TurAnswerCitationTest` (5) + `TurRagSourceTest` +2 = 37 green across the four affected classes, compile clean. → ROADMAP Block BO / §LXII.1

## Block BP — Next-generation transcription models (`gpt-transcribe` / `gpt-live-transcribe`) (§LXIII)

- **T851** — **Who said what, or nothing at all — never a confident guess.** Promoted from 💭 by making the decision it was blocked on. **The shape decision**: a new `TurTranscriptionSegment` (speaker, text, start/end seconds) and an *additive* `segments` list on `TurTranscriptionResult`. Segments **annotate** the flat `text`, never replace it — so persona-from-audio, AUDIO slots and the T693 confidence fallback all keep reading exactly the field they read today, and "diarization unavailable" is indistinguishable from "this backend never had it": both are an empty list. Diarization is opt-in (`turing.transcription.diarization-enabled` / a Global Settings switch / per request), sends `response_format=diarized_json`, and is silently ignored by whisper — whose `verbose_json` "segments" are timing boundaries with no speaker, so honouring it there would attribute turns to nobody. **The chunk-boundary problem, answered honestly**: speaker labels are scoped to a *single request*, and the vendor exposes no voice identity to reconcile them, so chunk 2's "A" is not chunk 1's "A". Rather than concatenate turns into confidently-wrong attribution, a chunked recording keeps its flat stitched transcript with **no** segments, logs why, and its chunks are asked *without* diarization at all (never pay for a format we would discard) while keeping their prompt/keywords, which stay valid per chunk. This is not as limiting as it reads: with the 25 MiB cap and the default byte-only chunking, a ~50-minute interview is one chunk — the realistic meeting/interview case works, and only genuinely long recordings degrade. **Diarization also wins over T849 streaming** when both are asked for: `diarized_json` is not a stream format, and losing live text is cosmetic where losing the turns would be silent data loss. Segments ride `TurTranscriptionJobStatus`, so `GET /api/genai/transcription/jobs/{id}` returns them. `TurTranscriptionDiarizationTest` (16: when it is requested at all, the response-format upgrade, segment mapping incl. missing speaker/timings and junk input, the flat transcript staying authoritative, the per-chunk strip, merge OR-ing the flag) + `TurTranscriptionServiceTest` +2 (single-chunk keeps the request and its segments; multi-chunk drops diarization but keeps the vocabulary) = **6891 backend green**, `tsc -b` clean, 162 frontend green. **Closes Block BP — T846–T851 all shipped.** → ROADMAP Block BP / §LXIII.6
- **T849** — **A single-chunk recording stops showing a bar that says nothing, and shows the words instead.** Promoted from 💭 with its own stated condition met: the design said "stays 💭 until a UI renders partial text", so this ships the renderer, not just the plumbing. `gpt-transcribe` accepts `stream=true` on `/audio/transcriptions` and emits `transcript.text.delta` / `transcript.text.done` events while it works; `TurOpenAiTranscriptionClient` now sends that flag and folds the events through a testable `StreamAccumulator` (deltas accumulate, a `done` event's `text`/`language` wins, an empty stream yields `null`). **Fail-open by construction**: streaming is attempted only when the family supports it *and* a real listener is attached, a non-2xx or any exception returns `null`, and `null` means "retry the blocking call" — which produces the same transcript. Streaming can therefore only ever cost a retry, never a result. New `TurTranscriptionPartialListener` seam (a 5-arg `default` overload on `TurTranscriptionProvider`, so a third-party `@Primary` provider is untouched) carries the text *accumulated so far* rather than the delta, so no consumer keeps its own buffer. **Partials are single-chunk only**: chunks are transcribed bounded-parallel (T689), so relaying concurrent deltas would interleave text out of order — a chunked recording keeps its honest chunk-index progress, and single-chunk is exactly the case that has no sub-progress today. `TurPartialTextThrottle` bounds the SSE cost with a two-sided policy (publish on ~400ms elapsed **or** ~200 new chars, never rewinding on a shorter buffer, and always publishing the first partial so a spinner becomes text immediately) shared by both job services; `partialTranscript` rides the `TurTranscriptionJobStatus` / `TurPersonaAudioJobStatus` snapshots as a preview — the authoritative transcript still rides the terminal one. The UI half: the persona-from-audio create card renders a frosted `aria-live` panel with the transcript building, auto-scrolled, append-only against out-of-order snapshots (en + pt). Kill switch `turing.transcription.stream-partial-text` (default **on** — the blast radius is only surfaces that render live text, and the fallback is total). `TurTranscriptionStreamTest` (11: SSE frame extraction, the delta→done fold, when streaming is even attempted) + `TurPartialTextThrottleTest` (6) + `TurTranscriptionServiceTest` +3 (single-chunk forwards the listener, multi-chunk drops it, the kill switch drops it) = **6873 backend green**, `tsc -b` clean, 162 frontend green. → ROADMAP Block BP / §LXIII.4
- **T846** — **The transcription request stops being whisper-shaped by construction.** `TurOpenAiTranscriptionClient` hardcoded `DEFAULT_MODEL = "whisper-1"` for *both* strategies, always asked for `response_format=verbose_json`, and sent the singular `language` — so an operator who typed `gpt-transcribe` into the (already free-text) model field got a *worse* result than whisper: the language hint was dropped (the new models read **`languages`**), and `verbose_json` is either rejected or yields no `segments`, which silently killed T693's confidence-based local→cloud fallback. The default was also both pricier and less accurate than the vendor's current recommendation. It stays **one** client — the endpoint contract is genuinely shared — but the dialect is now resolved from the effective model: a new `TurTranscriptionModelFamily` maps a model id to its `response_format` (`verbose_json` vs `json`), its language field (`language` vs `languages`) and two capability flags (`supportsSegmentConfidence`, `supportsKeywords`). **Three** families, not two: `WHISPER`, the superseded `GPT_4O_TRANSCRIBE` (json but still *singular* `language` — lumping it with either neighbour would break a hint), and the current `GPT_TRANSCRIBE`. Unknown/blank ids fall back to `WHISPER`, the most permissive dialect, so a custom self-hosted model name can never be made worse by the dialect choice. The default is now **per backend** (`TurTranscriptionProviderType.defaultModel()`): `OPENAI` → **`gpt-transcribe`**, `OPENAI_COMPATIBLE` → **`whisper-1`** (a faster-whisper-server serves whisper, not an OpenAI-hosted id) — and it comes from the *calling provider*, not `config.type()`, because the T693 fallback builds a cloud request while the resolved config still describes the local strategy. A new `TurTranscriptionTarget` record carries endpoint/key/model/default/limit into the client so the signature stayed under the parameter cap. Confidence became an explicit per-family capability: `confidenceFor(family, segments)` returns `null` for the `gpt-*transcribe` families, which T693 already reads as "no signal" — the fallback degrades instead of inventing a score. `TurTranscriptionModelFamilyTest` (16) + `TurTranscriptionTargetTest` (4) + `TurOpenAiTranscriptionClientTest` (9) new/extended. → ROADMAP Block BP / §LXIII.1
- **T847** — **Turing hands the transcriber the vocabulary it already curates.** The differentiating half of the block: speech models guess at product names, model ids and jargon, and Turing owns exactly the vocabulary that stops the guessing — the Thesaurus (Block AQ). New `TurTranscriptionHints` record (free-form `prompt` + a bounded `keywords` list) widens `TurTranscriptionConfig` and the `TurTranscriptionProvider` seam; the record itself enforces the bounds rather than trusting callers — blanks dropped, keywords de-duplicated case-insensitively (first spelling wins), capped at `MAX_KEYWORDS` (100), prompt truncated at 2000 chars, because a keyword list is a hint, not a dictionary dump. Hints resolve **globally** (`turing.transcription.prompt` / `.keywords` / `.keywords-from-thesaurus` + three new Global Settings config vars, env winning as usual) and **per request** via `merge(...)`, where an override prompt replaces the prompt and a non-empty override keyword list *replaces* rather than appends — a per-request vocabulary is a decision, not an addition. `TurTranscriptionKeywordSource` is the opt-in Thesaurus source (default **off**): a new `findEnabledLabelsBroadestFirst` label projection returns enabled preferred terms with root descriptors first, bounded by a `Pageable`, and fail-open to an empty list — a missing hint costs accuracy, never the transcript. The operator's own keywords always come first, so the automatic source can only fill what's left under the cap. **Nothing is forced on a backend that can't use it**: the client drops `keywords` unless the family accepts them, and the seam's new 4-arg `transcribe` is a `default` method delegating to the original 3-arg one, so a third-party `@Primary` provider written against the old seam keeps working — `TurTranscriptionService` even keeps routing hint-less asks through the 3-arg call so such a provider is never bypassed. Chunk orchestration now carries one `Ask` record (audio + mime + language + hints) so every chunk and the confidence fallback see the same context and a stitched transcript stays internally consistent. Global Settings gets a context-prompt textarea, a keywords textarea and a Thesaurus switch (en + pt). `TurTranscriptionHintsTest` (9) + `TurTranscriptionConfigResolverTest` +5 + `TurOpenAiCompatibleTranscriptionProviderTest` +2. → ROADMAP Block BP / §LXIII.2
- **T850** — **The catalog and the copy now name the models an operator should actually pick.** In `openviglet/models-catalog`: `gpt-transcribe` and `gpt-live-transcribe` added as committed `TRANSCRIPTION` entries with `status: GA`, plus curated `overrides.json` pins (the anchoring source, so a regen can't lose them), and `gpt-4o-transcribe` / `gpt-realtime-whisper` flagged `status: DEPRECATED` + `deprecated: true` — from there the shipped pipeline does the rest with no Turing-side code (T777 seeds `TurLLMPrice`, T782 warns operators still pinning a retired id, the T750 classifier already resolves `*transcribe*` → `TRANSCRIPTION`). Pricing was deliberately **omitted** rather than invented: both models are billed per *minute* of audio and the catalog's `pricing` shape is per-1M-*tokens*, so shoehorning it in would have published a wrong number — `whisper-1` and `gpt-realtime-whisper` already carry no price for the same reason. Turing-side, the `whisper-1` placeholders are gone: the Global Settings model field now shows the *backend's* default as its placeholder (`gpt-transcribe` on the cloud path, `whisper-1` on the self-hosted one), the en/pt descriptions explain when each model is the right pick (whisper-1 still wins for timestamps/translation/subtitles), and the `TurLlmModelKind.TRANSCRIPTION` javadoc example was refreshed. `docs-turing/transcription.md` gains a "Choosing a model" table and a "Context hints" section, with the config reference extended for T847's three keys; `COVERAGE.md` carries the Block BP row. `TurConfigVarOnStartupTest` +1 (the three new config vars bootstrap unset/opt-out) and its saved-row count moved 50 → 53. **Closes Block BP's planned tasks** — T846–T848 + T850 shipped; 💭 T849 (streamed file transcription) and 💭 T851 (diarization) remain unpromoted. → ROADMAP Block BP / §LXIII.5
- **T848** — **The browser stops pinning an ASR model the vendor has superseded.** `use-turing-realtime-voice` hardcoded `input_audio_transcription: { model: "whisper-1" }` in its `session.update`, so the voice kiosk could not move to OpenAI's low-latency live model without an SDK release — vendor policy living in the client, the exact thing the T147 ephemeral-token design pushed server-side. The session now carries it: `TurRealtimeVoiceSession.inputTranscriptionModel` (a 7th record component) is resolved server-side by `TurRealtimeVoiceSessionService` from the per-capability `configJson` (`inputTranscriptionModel`) and then the provider's own `defaultInputTranscriptionModel()` — a new `default`-method on `TurRealtimeVoiceProvider` returning `null` ("no opinion"), overridden by `TurOpenAiRealtimeVoiceProvider` to **`gpt-live-transcribe`**. The provider also declares it on the minted session itself (`session.audio.input.transcription.model` in the `client_secrets` body), so the browser only echoes a decision the server already made. **Fail-open at both ends**: `TurRealtimeVoiceSessionRequest` keeps a 4-arg delegating constructor, the SDK's `TurVoiceSession.inputTranscriptionModel` is optional and the hook falls back to `"whisper-1"` when absent, so an older server leaves the previous behaviour untouched. `TurOpenAiRealtimeVoiceProviderTest` +1 and `TurRealtimeVoiceSessionServiceTest` +1 (the resolved ASR model reaches the request; a capability config pins a different one) = 10 + 10 green. → ROADMAP Block BP / §LXIII.3

## Block BS — The JS SDK release gate is not a gate (§LXVI)

- **T861** — **The vanilla SDK owns its test runner, and CI actually runs it.** `@viglet/turing-sdk` declared `"test": "vitest run"` with `typescript` / `vite` / `vite-plugin-dts` as its only devDependencies, so `pnpm --filter @viglet/turing-sdk test` failed with *"Command 'vitest' not found"* — and the binary was not hoisted into `frontend/node_modules/.bin` either, so the suite was reachable only by borrowing `apps/turing-app/node_modules/.bin/vitest` by hand. `vitest@^4.1.10` (the version the other two suites already pin) is now a declared devDependency, and a dedicated `vitest.config.ts` keeps the run off `vite.config.ts` — that file is the *library build* (lib mode, ESM + UMD, `emptyOutDir`), and inheriting it means every test run carries build-only options. The config is deliberately minimal: no plugin, since the package is zero-dependency and framework-agnostic; `include: src/**/*.test.ts`; `clearMocks`/`restoreMocks` matching react-ui. `tsconfig.json` already scopes emit to `src/**/*`, so the new root config cannot leak into `dist`. **The confirmation was the point, and it came back negative**: `validate-js.yml` built each workspace and weighed its bundle but never ran a single test — so the missing dependency could not have been caught there. A `Test` step now runs `pnpm --filter <workspace> run --if-present test`, `--if-present` so the three marketplace example apps (which genuinely have no suite) stay green. The suite now *runs*: **214 tests in 16 files, 207 passing and 7 failing** — the pre-existing analytics failures §LXVI.2 describes, left for T862 rather than folded in here. → ROADMAP Block BS / §LXVI.1

- **T862** — **The suite is green at rest, and the reason it was red is now itself covered.** All 7 failures had one cause, and it was neither fake-timer nor jsdom-lifecycle drift as suspected: the suite had **no jsdom at all**. Node's `globalThis` is not an `EventTarget` and there is no `document`, so `createAbandonmentWatcher` took its documented SSR branch and returned `NOOP_WATCHER` — which is why 6 of the 7 failed on `globalThis.dispatchEvent is not a function` and the 7th (the idle-timeout test) silently never armed its timer. The tests were right about the product; the environment was wrong. The fix is scoped rather than global: `jsdom@^29.1.1` is declared, and the two lifecycle files (`analytics-abandonment.test.ts`, `analytics-conversion.test.ts`) opt in with a `@vitest-environment jsdom` docblock **instead of** flipping the suite default. Turning the whole package jsdom would have been one line and would have quietly destroyed the only place the SSR claim is checkable — `@viglet/turing-sdk` is framework-agnostic and explicitly SSR-safe, so "no DOM" is a supported runtime, not an accident of the test setup. New `analytics-lifecycle-ssr.test.ts` stays on `node` and locks that down: it asserts there really is no `document` / `addEventListener`, that `ping()`/`stop()` are callable and idempotent regardless, that the idle timer is never armed (so an SSR render cannot emit an abandonment event), and that a chat controller runs a full turn with no DOM present. **217 tests in 17 files, all green** (was 207/214 in 16). → ROADMAP Block BS / §LXVI.2

- **T863** — **The size budget guards the delta, runs on PRs, and the rot turned out not to be one package's problem.** `check-size.mjs` enforced absolute gzip ceilings that have to be re-tuned by hand on every legitimate growth — each re-tune indistinguishable from silencing the guard — and it rotted in the *other* direction too: a bundle could sit over a stale ceiling for months, discoverable only by whoever next tried to publish. The shared script now takes a committed `sizeBudget.baseline` (`{measured, total, entries}`) plus a `delta` allowance (default 512 B per entry / 1024 B total) and fails on **growth over baseline**, so legitimate growth updates the baseline via the new `--update-baseline` / `pnpm run size:update` in the same PR that causes it, where a reviewer sees the cost next to the feature. A stale baseline is now self-reporting rather than silent: the report prints when it was measured and a `Δ base` column per entry, `strict` accepts "budget **or** baseline", and a baseline naming a file that no longer exists is a violation like a stale budget already was. Per-entry baselines are written only for `strict` packages — react-sdk emits ~100 `tsc` files and would otherwise turn its package.json into an unreviewable size manifest. **Adopting it across the three publishable packages found that §LXVI.3 understated the problem**: `@viglet/turing-react-ui` (35.50 kB against a 33.20 kB ceiling, plus three individual entries over) and `@viglet/turing-react-sdk` (97.83 kB against 90.00 kB) were *both* already over — so `validate-react-ui.yml` was failing on every PR touching that package and neither could be published. All three therefore drop their absolute ceilings entirely rather than being re-tuned to a new number that will rot the same way; the delta guard is the only guard, and the mechanism was verified to actually fail (a 0.1 kB allowance against a deliberately low baseline exits 1 with both the per-entry and total violation named). **On PRs**: `validate-js.yml` was `workflow_dispatch`-only, so nothing in the matrix ran automatically — it now triggers on `pull_request` for `frontend/**`, putting build + size + test (T861) in front of the change's author. **Closes Block BS.** → ROADMAP Block BS / §LXVI.3

## Block BT — The POST-native default left its consumers behind (§LXVII)

- **T865** — **`/sn/{siteName}` stops rendering its error state on every successful search.** The block's headline bug and the one that gated a whole page: `SearchContent` did `if ((store.status === "error" || !store.data) …) return <SearchError …>`, and T710 made `postNative: true` the provider default — POST-native the provider calls `setData(null)` and fills `structured` instead. So the third branch (`if (!store.data) return null`) was unreachable, the second one fired on *every* search, and everything past it was legacy-shaped too (`data.widget.locales`, `data.widget.icon` — the link-free response has no `widget` at all). The fix is **not** three `structured ?? data` expressions in three components, because that is the mistake the block is about: the whole page now reads one projection, `search/lib/search-view.ts`. `toSearchView(store)` resolves `ready` / `queryContext` / `locales` / `siteIcon` / `spellCheck` / `appliedFilters` / `multiSelectFacetItems` from whichever shape arrived — structured first, `data` as the legacy fallback, exactly the `structuredGroups ?? legacyGroups` precedent `useTuringFacets` set — and resolves the *actions* with them, so a legacy spell-check correction navigates an href and a structured one re-runs the request with new text while `SpellCheckBanner` knows neither. The render gate is now `view.ready` ("a response of either shape arrived"), `SearchResultsPanel` takes the view instead of `TurSearchResponse`, and `SearchHeader`'s `locales` drops the legacy `TurLocaleItem.link` it never used. Adding a consumer can no longer repeat the mistake: there is nothing to reach into. → ROADMAP Block BT / §LXVII.1
- **T866** — **The applied-filters row and the facet-item mode come back.** `FacetSidebar` read two more legacy-only values, and both are null POST-native: `store.data?.widget?.facetToRemove` wrapped the entire "Applied filters" chip row *and* the **Clear all** button, so both silently disappeared; `store.data?.queryContext?.facetItemType` decided whether to draw checkboxes, so every facet on every site rendered as if it were single-select. Both now come off the T865 projection. The chips are built from `structured.selectedFilterQueries` and **labelled** by looking the filter query up in the response's own facet items (falling back to the raw value after the field prefix, because a facet renders only up to its item limit and an applied filter can legitimately be absent from the rail), and each carries its own `remove()` — `toggleFacet({…, action: "REMOVE"})` structured, `navigate(link)` legacy. The row's gate tightened from "the object exists" to `appliedFilters.length > 0`, which also stops the legacy path from ever drawing an empty row with a disabled *Clear all*. `multiSelectFacetItems` reads `queryContext.facetItemType` — present and identically populated in **both** responses, since `TurSNQueryContextBuilder` fills it from the site and the T708 mapper carries the bean straight across — with the echoed `request.fqItemOperator` as backstop. → ROADMAP Block BT / §LXVII.2
- **T867** — **A result's facet chip filters the search again.** T864 gave each chip a `filterQuery` and made it render, but left it a dead badge: only the legacy `href` shape was clickable, so clicking a result's tag — which filtered the search before T710 — did nothing. The chip is now a real `<button>` wrapping the badge rather than a `BadgeColorful` handed a filter query as its `href`: that component only fires `onClick` when `href` is truthy, and passing a non-URL as an href is precisely the "looks like a link, isn't one" confusion T860 removed from `EnrichedFacetItem.link`. Both shapes are extracted into one `DocumentFacetChip` that narrows on `"href" in meta`, so the legacy chip keeps navigating and the structured one calls `store.toggleFacet` — the same call the facet rail makes for the same `field:value`. `aria-pressed` reflects the chip's `selected` state and the button is keyboard-focusable, neither of which the old badge was. → ROADMAP Block BT / §LXVII.3
- **T868** — **This class of regression is catchable, because the test now runs against the shape that ships.** The three bugs above are one mistake repeated and the frontend suite was green through all of them, for the same reason T708's link-free assertion ran over a response with no documents (§LXV.6): `search-content.test.tsx` mocks `useSearchStore` and hands the component a `data` object that **production has not been able to produce since T710**. A fixture that cannot occur proves nothing about the code path that does. New `search/__tests__/search-page.post-native.test.tsx` applies T859's backend remedy to the frontend — assert at the real boundary: it mounts the actual route (`/sn/:siteName` → `SearchPage` → the real `TuringProvider`, no `postNative` override, the real store) and stubs the **network** instead. The boundary is `axios`, not `fetch` — the React SDK deliberately stays on the host's axios instance so its interceptors keep applying — and the payload is a `STRUCTURED` response shaped as the T708 mapper actually emits it: no `widget`, no href anywhere, `selectedFilterQueries` top-level, `selected`/`action` on the chips. **9 tests**: the POST carries `responseFormat: "STRUCTURED"` and results render instead of `SearchError`; the applied-filters row appears, labelled from the facet item, and removing a chip drops exactly that filter query from the next request's `fq`; checkboxes appear for `facetItemType: OR` and not for `AND`; a result chip's click adds its filter and an already-applied one removes it; a genuine network failure *still* reaches the error state; and the legacy `postNative: false` transport is exercised end-to-end so the fallback T865 kept is not a claim. The old file keeps its cheap T835 composition assertions with a docblock saying why it is a *legacy* fixture and that response-shape coverage does not belong there. **Admin suite: 180 tests in 35 files, all green** (was 171/34). → ROADMAP Block BT / §LXVII.4
- **T869** — **A document's chip says whether it is applied and what clicking does.** Promoted from 💭 because T867 needed it: a chip that can only ever *add* renders identically whether or not its filter is already on, and clicking it a second time is a no-op. `TurSNStructuredMetadataBean` gained `selected` + `action`, so "identical in shape to a facet item" is now literal. Neither is computed twice: the mapper builds a `ChipState` from the widget's `selectedFilterQueries` plus every facet item's resolved action (primary **and** secondary facets), keyed by filter query, and a chip inherits the rail's verdict for the same `field:value` — including `REPLACE`, the one value that cannot be derived from the chip alone because it depends on the field's facet-item mode. The rail and the chip therefore cannot disagree. A chip outside the rendered rail (facet item limits) falls back to its selection state, and no widget at all yields `ADD`. `TurSearchRequest`-side, the new `TurFacetToggleTarget` (`Pick<TurStructuredFacetItem, "action" | "field" | "filterQuery">`) is what `applyFacetAction`, the React store's `toggleFacet` and the vanilla `SearchController.toggleFacet` now take, which is what lets one call serve both surfaces. Legacy JSON is untouched — the legacy chip's `field`/`value`/`filterQuery` stay `@JsonIgnore` in-process hand-offs. **Closes Block BT.** → ROADMAP Block BT / §LXVII.5

## Block BR — Block AV follow-through: the gaps the POST-native migration left (§LXV)

- **T856** — **`responseFormat` stops being a POST-only flag.** §XLV.5 settled the negotiation mechanism on a *request field* rather than an `Accept` header with an explicit justification — *"it works over both GET and POST, which is what A/B-ing the two shapes against one query requires"* — and then only `TurSNSiteSearchAPI.negotiate` (the POST body path) read it, so the structured shape was unreachable over GET and the documented A/B story did not exist. Closed rather than amended, because a server-rendered surface that wants facet *data* without parsing its own links is a real consumer and the cost is one parameter. `GET /api/sn/{siteName}/search` now binds `responseFormat` (new `TurSNParamType.RESPONSE_FORMAT`) and routes through the **same** `negotiate(...)`, which was generalized from "(request bean, params)" to "(format, `Supplier<TurSNSearchRequest>`)" — so the overwhelmingly common `LINKS` path builds no echo object at all, and the two transports cannot drift into two negotiations. The echo itself comes from a new `TurSNSearchRequestAdapter.effectiveRequest(params, locale, format)` that rebuilds the request from the bound `TurSNSearchParams` alone (targeting rules stay empty — they are a POST-body concept; `autoCorrectionDisabled` is read back off `nfpr`), with the existing POST overload delegating into it so both echoes are produced by one piece of code. The flag is parsed **case-insensitively** via a new `TurSNResponseFormat.of(String)` because a GET flag is typed into a URL by hand, and an *unknown* value throws `IllegalArgumentException` → 400 rather than silently degrading to the legacy shape — parsed before the search runs, so a typo'd A/B URL costs nothing. The GET return type widened to the `TurSNSearchResponse` marker interface; with the flag absent the body is the same `TurSNSiteSearchBean` instance as before, so the default GET response stays byte-identical (asserted). V2 (`/api/v2/sn/**`) deliberately untouched. `TurSNSiteSearchAPITest` +3 (absent flag hands back the legacy body unchanged; `structured` returns `TurSNStructuredSearchBean` with the effective request echoed; an unknown value throws without touching the search process) = 17 green. → ROADMAP Block BR / §LXV.1

- **T857** — **A POST-native sort control now costs one round-trip, not two.** The structured response deliberately does not carry the sort option list — the list already has an endpoint and duplicating it on every response would be waste on the common path where the control is already rendered. That default stays; what changes is that a surface painting its **first** frame can ask for it inline: opt-in `includeSortOptions` on `TurSNSearchRequest` (and on the T856 GET transport, same terms) inlines `sortOptions` in the `STRUCTURED` body. The option list moved out of the controller method into one private `sortOptions(siteName)` returning `Optional<List<…>>`, used by **both** `GET /search/sort-options` and the inlined copy, so the two surfaces cannot disagree; the nested `TurSNSiteSearchAPI.TurSNSiteSortOptionBean` record was promoted to `com.viglet.turing.commons.sn.bean.TurSNSortOptionBean` (same `{value,label}` JSON) so the structured bean in `turing-commons` can carry it. Three deliberate no-ops: the flag does nothing on a `LINKS` response (frozen, byte-identical), an unknown site omits the list rather than failing a search over a decoration, and the flag is **not echoed** on the effective request — the list is a first-frame need, so re-sending the echo asks for results, not for the option list again (the "off by default, and the client asks for what it needs" shape of the rest of Block AV). Both SDK type surfaces updated (`TurSearchRequest.includeSortOptions`, `TurStructuredSearchResponse.sortOptions` in `@viglet/turing-sdk`, re-exported by the React SDK, so the two cannot drift); no hook rewiring — `useTuringSortOptions` keeps its own fetch, and opting in stays the consumer's call. `TurSNSiteSearchAPITest` +5 (omitted by default; inlined when asked with the echo's flag asserted false; never inlined into `LINKS`; missing snapshot degrades to no list; reachable over GET) = 22 green, 385 in the `api.sn` package, SDK `tsc` clean. → ROADMAP Block BR / §LXV.2

- **T858** — **The search cache key can no longer collide by construction.** `TurSNUtils.getCacheKey(siteName, request)` was `siteName + "_" + request.getQueryString()` — correct exactly as long as the query string *is* the request, which is true for GET and false for every POST endpoint, where it collapses to `<site>_null` and one cached result is served for **every distinct body**. That trap was hit twice independently (T707 for `/search/query`, T712 for `/ac/query` + `/search/list/query`), which is evidence about the primitive rather than about the two authors: the signature made the wrong thing the easy thing, and the failure is *silent* — wrong results, no error. New `TurSNSearchCacheKey.of(TurSNSiteSearchContext)` in `turing-commons` keys on the **effective parameters** every transport already builds, so the key cannot depend on how the request arrived and a new POST endpoint cannot forget to supply one; the `HttpServletRequest` overload and the two hand-rolled workarounds (`TurSNSiteSearchService.publicPostCacheKey`, the `"%s_%s".formatted(siteName, canonicalUri.getRawQuery())` inline in the autocomplete POST) are **deleted**, and `executeSearchWithCache` / `TurSNAutoComplete.autoCompleteWithRegularSearch` both lost their now-impossible-to-misuse parameters (the AC key is now derived *after* the `title:q*` rewrite, so it describes the search that actually runs). The key is a readable escaped encoding rather than a hash, so it stays greppable in the cache log; injectivity comes from escaping the three separators (`\`, `|`, `,`) in every value, which is exactly what the query-string key could not do. Two coverage gains fall out: **targeting rules are now part of the key** (they change which documents match, and on the authenticated POST they travel in the body while the old key was read off the URL — a live collision), and facet sets are sorted before encoding since a `Set` has no iteration order. Deliberately excluded: `userId` and `populateMetrics`, metrics/impersonation concerns that would fragment the cache per user for no gain. New `TurSNSearchCacheKeyTest` (12: determinism, two bodies with no query string, transport-independence, each fq group, separator forging, targeting rules, userId non-fragmentation, highlighting, facet-set order, pagination/sort/rows, allowed facets, locale) + `TurSNSiteSearchServiceTest` updated to assert the derived key → 1097 green across the `api.sn` + `sn` packages. → ROADMAP Block BR / §LXV.3

- **T859** — **The Block AV backend finally has end-to-end coverage, and it immediately found something.** Everything in T707–T714 was verified by unit tests over mocks, which is exactly what let the T858 cache-key collision through: a mocked client cannot observe that two different bodies returned one cached response. `TurSdkContractIT` (real HTTP port, real Lucene marketplace site) gains **8 tests** over the read surface: `POST /search/query` in `LINKS` asserted **JSON-equal to the GET response** for the canonical equivalent query (the T707 promise that switching transport changes nothing); `STRUCTURED` asserted to contain no `link`/`href`/`cleanUpLink`/`cleanUpFacets`/`/api/sn/`; the original production bug end to end — a facet value containing `&` selected by putting its `filterQuery` on the request, asserted to return exactly the documents the facet counted and to be the right document; the **T858 regression guard** (the context now runs with `turing.search.cache.enabled=true` on purpose — the collision is invisible with the cache off — and two distinct bodies must not share a response); the T712 `/ac/query` and `/search/list/query` variants; plus T856 structured-over-GET and T857 `includeSortOptions` asserted equal to what the dedicated endpoint serves. The fixture needed a reserved character and the committed sample has none, so one synthetic creature with `creature_type: "Ash & Ember"` is appended **in memory** at zip time — the same treatment the Lucene `endpointUrl` already gets — rather than diverging the file from its marketplace source. **The finding**: run over a response that actually has documents (the unit test used an empty bean), the structured body is *not* link-free — `results.document[].metadata[]` still carries `{href, text}` per-document facet links, passed through because §XLV.3 assumed `results` was already link-free. The no-href scan is therefore scoped to the navigation surface with the carve-out named and pointed at the new **T864**, whose acceptance criterion is deleting that carve-out. 14 tests in the IT (1 skipped: the chat SSE handshake, which needs a configured agent). → ROADMAP Block BR / §LXV.4

- **T860** — **`EnrichedFacetItem.link` stops lying POST-native.** `useTuringFacets` built each item with `link: ""` in the link-free mode, because the structured response carries no href while the type declared `link: string` — so `<a href={facet.link}>` compiled, rendered, and linked to the *current page*: a silent, plausible-looking bug that only shows up as a user click going nowhere. The enriched item now declares `link?: string` (`Omit<TurFacetItem, "link"> & FacetAction & { link?: string }`, leaving the raw API type alone — the LINKS response really does carry one) and the structured projection **omits** the field rather than emptying it, because an empty string still satisfies an `href` while `undefined` forces the consumer to decide. Legacy (`postNative: false`) mode is unchanged. The type change immediately found its own consumers: four apps keyed their facet list on `` key={facet.label + facet.link} `` — a `string + (string | undefined)` that no longer compiles — now keyed on `facet.label`, which is unique within a group anyway (mythical-creatures, space-missions, vinyl-records, showcase storefront, plus the admin `facet-sidebar`). Minor changeset on `@viglet/turing-react-sdk` spelling out the one-line migration. `tsc -b` clean across react-sdk, turing-app, showcase and all three marketplace apps; **171 frontend tests green in 34 files**. **Closes Block BR's original scope** — T856–T860 all shipped; T864 (the per-document facet links T859 uncovered) remains open. → ROADMAP Block BR / §LXV.5

- **T864** — **The last link in the "link-free" response is gone.** §XLV.3 carried `queryContext` and `results` over from the legacy bean on the stated grounds that they "were already link-free"; T859's end-to-end run — the first time the structured shape was exercised over a response that actually *has* documents, rather than the empty bean the unit test used — showed each document's `metadata[]` was a list of `{href, text}` pairs pointing at `/api/sn/{site}/search?q=…&fq[]=habitat:Mountains`. So `STRUCTURED` was link-free everywhere except the one place a result renderer looks. The fix mirrors what T708 did one level up: the field name and raw value are captured **where the href is built** (`TurSNUtils.addFilterQueryByType`, the only place that still knows them) onto three `@JsonIgnore` fields — the same in-process hand-off the T708 facet-item action uses, so the legacy JSON stays byte-for-byte unchanged and nothing has to parse an href back. `TurSNStructuredResponseMapper` then projects `results` into new `TurSNStructuredResultsBean` / `TurSNStructuredDocumentBean` / `TurSNStructuredMetadataBean` types: every property keeps its legacy name and shape (`source`, `elevate`, `fields`, `rankingExplanation`, `duplicateCluster`), and only `metadata` changes — from `{href, text}` to `{field, value, filterQuery, text}`, the *same* shape as a structured facet item, because it is the same action. `TurSNStructuredGroupBean.results` follows. Frontend: `TurStructuredDocument`/`TurStructuredResults`/`TurStructuredDocumentMetadata` added and exported from both SDKs; `ResolvedDocument.raw` widened to the union (the resolver only ever reads `fields`, so `resolveDocuments` serves both shapes as before); react-ui's locally-redeclared `TuringRawDocument.metadata` became a discriminated union on `"href" in chip` — it stays zero-runtime-dependency. The admin result card renders the structured chip as a plain badge instead of dropping it, which is what it had been doing since T710 (no href → `return null`). **The acceptance criterion was mechanical and is met**: `TurSdkContractIT`'s no-href scan lost its `navigationOnly(...)` carve-out and now covers the whole body, plus asserts each chip carries a non-blank `filterQuery`. `TurSNStructuredResponseMapperTest` fixture gained a document with metadata (closing the empty-bean blind spot that let this through) +3 = 17 green; 1099 backend green in the `api.sn`+`sn` packages, 155 in turing-commons, 14 in the IT; `tsc -b` clean across both SDKs, react-ui, turing-app, showcase and the three marketplace apps; 171 frontend tests green. **Closes Block BR.** → ROADMAP Block BR / §LXV.6

## Block BN — Content-fit tuning loop: model-aware, enforceable, and it tells you (§LXI)

- **T830** — **Movement per content and per persona, in the studio and in the exported PDF.** T704 charted the project average per run and gave one sparkline per *pair* in the drill-in; the question an editor actually asks sits between them — *is this content getting worse for its audiences?*, *is this persona drifting away from the portfolio?* — and answering it meant opening cells one at a time. The data was already on the wire (`/trend` returns every cell's series), so this is an aggregation, **not a new endpoint**: a pure `buildTargetTrends(trend, "source" | "persona")` averages each target's cells per run instant (llmUsed if *any* cell used the LLM, reused only if *all* were, points ordered oldest-first) and `seriesDelta` reports the movement between the last two runs. Each `RankPanel` in the By content / By persona lenses now carries a small sparkline + a signed delta chip in its header, tinted by the existing `driftTone` and silent until a target has two runs on record. The PDF exports had the matching hole — both lenses printed a pure snapshot, so the artifact an operator circulates could not say "this dropped 11 points since last week": every exported row now shows `previous% → current% (±d)` from the T704 `previousFitScore` already on each cell, and each group heading carries the target's own run-over-run movement. Movement is rendered numerically (`78% → 71% (-7)`), so the export needed no new i18n. `persona-match.model.test.ts` (new, 11: per-run averaging for both axes, input-order independence, the llmUsed/reused folding, empty payload, delta rounding + null on a first run, plus the T826 band boundaries at the default and at a lowered project bar) → **162 frontend tests green in 32 files**, `tsc -b` clean. **Closes Block BN** — T825–T830 all shipped; only the 💭 T831 apply action remains unpromoted. → ROADMAP Block BN / §LXI.6
- **T829** — **Both persona verdicts are now bound to a provider-enforced schema, and their degradations are counted instead of silent.** `TurPersonaContentFitEvaluator` and `TurPersonaMatchAdjustmentService` each described their JSON contract *in the prompt* and then scraped the reply between the first `{` and the last `}` — precisely the recipe the ROADMAP Non-goals rule out and that agent memory records weaker models truncating. Both now go through the T819 surface: a new `TurStructuredCompletionPort` (implemented by `TurProviderStructuredCompletion`: OpenAI `json_schema` with `strict=false`, then Anthropic's forced tool) plus a `TurLlmSummaryService.generateStructured(...)` overload that keeps the cache, the T728 instance override and the token accounting while routing the completion through it. Two new schema types (`TurPersonaFitVerdict`, `TurPersonaMatchAdjustmentVerdict`) carry `@JsonPropertyDescription` guidance that travels *with* the schema; `BeanOutputConverter` derives the schema and its format instruction is appended **only** on the plain-chat fallback, where nothing else constrains the shape. The brace-scan parse survives as the last fallback rather than the only path, so a provider without a structured surface behaves exactly as before. **The degradation is now legible**: a new `TurVerdictSource` (`PROVIDER_SCHEMA` / `PROMPT_JSON` / `CACHE` / `UNAVAILABLE` / `UNREADABLE`) rides on `TurContentFitResult` — via an added record component with a *legacy 11-arg constructor* so no existing caller or fixture changed — and `TurPersonaVerdictHealth` tallies each outcome behind `GET /api/persona-match/verdict-health` (including an `unreadableRate`, the number to watch after changing model or prompt). "Heuristic only" can finally distinguish *nothing is configured* from *the model answered and we could not read it*: the studio's fit detail view now says so explicitly (en + pt). Grounding is untouched — a schema constrains shape, never truthfulness, so the T385 verbatim-span hard-fail and the unknown-target drop still run. `TurPersonaContentFitEvaluatorTest` +6 (the schema is really sent and names the verdict's properties; provider-enforced, prompt-JSON-with-fences, unreadable, cache and textless paths each counted correctly) = 15 in the class, **full backend suite 6811 green**, `tsc -b` clean. → ROADMAP Block BN / §LXI.5
- **T828** — **`turing match` turns content fit into a CI gate.** Turing already gates *agent* quality in CI (T599/T602's `turing eval`); content-fit had the same shape — a score over versioned artifacts — and none of the enforcement, so a docs repo that merged a page rewritten for the wrong reading level found out at the next scheduled run, in a UI, days later. New `match` command in `@viglet/turing-cli` beside `eval`/`research`: `match list` (projects + the fit bar each uses), `match run <projectIdOrName> [--min-fit N] [--fail-on-drop] [--force]` and `match drift <projectIdOrName>`. `run` streams the real SSE runner (progress reported at ~10% steps so a large matrix doesn't flood a CI log, **falling back to the blocking endpoint** when the stream can't be opened — old instance, SSE-buffering proxy), then **exits non-zero** on either of two independent rules, printing the offending `(content × persona)` pairs with resolved names: a cell **below the fit bar** (`--min-fit`, defaulting to the project's own T826 `effectiveFitThreshold`, so CI and the studio agree unless told otherwise) or a pair that **regressed** versus the previous run (`--fail-on-drop` only, read off the same T704 drift report the studio and the T827 webhook use, sharpest drop first). `<project>` accepts an id or a case-insensitive name. An empty matrix fails rather than passing vacuously. **No new backend surface** — it reuses the existing run/matrix/drift endpoints over the shared `TuringClient`; the gate evaluator and every formatter are pure functions. Documented caveat: the fit score is partly LLM-judged and therefore not bit-reproducible, which is why the *threshold* is the gate rather than an exact score match (the same reasoning that keeps T385's NL→facet scorer LLM-free) — set `--min-fit` with headroom. `test/match.test.ts` +16 (bar/override/regression rules, sort order, id fallback, all three formatters, dispatch incl. name resolution, empty matrix, unknown project, bad `--min-fit`) → 67 green in the package. → ROADMAP Block BN / §LXI.4
- **T827** — **A scheduled re-analysis that regresses a project can now say so.** T700 re-runs a DAILY project at 03:30 and T704 records exactly what moved — into a table nobody reads until a human opens `/bento/persona/match/:id`, which defeats the point of scheduling continuous tuning. New `TurPersonaMatchDriftAlertService` POSTs the T704 drift report to an **opt-in per-project webhook** after a *scheduled* run (manual runs are deliberately never alerted — the person who clicked is already looking at the result) when either at least one pair **crossed below** the T826 threshold, or the project average dropped by more than the project's configured `driftWebhookAvgDrop` margin (the broad shallow slide no per-pair flag catches; `null` = crossings only). Three nullable columns on the project (Liquibase `v2026.3.1.120`): `driftWebhookUrl` (blank = alert off, the default), `driftWebhookAuthHeader` (**encrypted at rest** via `TurSecretCryptoService`, write-only — never returned by the API, blank on update keeps the stored cipher, and clearing the URL clears the secret with it) and `driftWebhookAvgDrop`. Delivery follows the T62 `TurChatWebhookDispatcher` pattern rather than reinventing it: the shared `VigletWebhookDispatcher` with the same bounded policy (3 attempts, 10s, 2s back-off), the `Authorization` value decrypted at send time, and the target URL gated through `TurSsrfGuard` (T643). **Fail-soft throughout** — a blocked egress target, a dead endpoint, a rotated crypto key (sends unauthenticated) or any other failure is logged and swallowed, because an alert that could fail the nightly analysis would be worse than the silence it fixes. The payload carries the drift DTO verbatim (same shape as `GET /{id}/drift`, so no second serialization) plus the reasons that fired, the bar used and the average delta. Webhook-only on purpose: `turing-app` has no mail dependency and this block does not add one. Studio: a new frosted "Drift alert" section (URL / write-only secret / margin, the latter two disabled until a URL exists) in the project workspace, en + pt. `TurPersonaMatchDriftAlertServiceTest` (11: crossing fires, no-URL/clean-run/first-run/under-margin stay quiet, margin fires with no crossing, blocked SSRF + failed delivery + undecryptable secret never throw, header decrypted and attached, keep-existing-secret) = 11 green, `tsc -b` clean. → ROADMAP Block BN / §LXI.3
- **T826** — **"Below fit" is now the project's bar, not the platform's literal.** `TurPersonaMatchHistoryService.GOOD_FIT_THRESHOLD = 75.0` decided `crossedBelowFit`/`crossedAboveFit`, and the frontend's `fitTone` bands (75/50) plus the PDF's `fitHex` and the legend text each repeated the same number independently — one literal, four copies, deciding the heatmap colours, the drift alert and (with T827/T828) who gets paged and which builds fail. A portfolio's bar is genuinely portfolio-specific: expert engineering notes are fine at 60, a first-contact onboarding page is not fine at 74. The project now owns it — a **nullable** `fitThreshold` column (Liquibase `v2026.3.1.119`, `null` = inherit) resolved through one new `TurPersonaMatchThresholdService`: the project's own value when sane, else the deployment default `turing.persona-match.fit-threshold` (**75**, so an unconfigured deployment behaves exactly as before), with out-of-range values (≤0, >100) and a bogus property both failing back to 75 rather than colouring a matrix red. `drift(...)` resolves the bar per project and **echoes it** on `TurPersonaMatchDriftDto.fitThreshold` so the studio, the future alert and the future gate all cite the same number; the project DTO carries both the raw `fitThreshold` (what the form edits) and the derived `effectiveFitThreshold` (what is in force). Frontend: `fitTone(score, threshold?)`/`fitHex(score, threshold)` take the bar (defaulting to the exported `GOOD_FIT_THRESHOLD` so no caller broke), the amber floor is proportional via the new `mediumFitFloor` (two thirds — 75→50 unchanged, 60→40), the legend now *states* the bands it is colouring by, and the heatmap/rank panels/drill-in/badge/PDF exports all read the project's number. New numeric field in the project settings card (empty = inherit, out-of-range snaps back). Also fixed in passing: `saveProject` used `patch.x ?? project.x`, so picking "Default LLM (Global)" — a deliberate `null` — silently kept the previous instance; both nullable fields now use `!== undefined`. `TurPersonaMatchThresholdServiceTest` (7) + `TurPersonaMatchHistoryServiceTest` (5: the same 80→70 slide alerts at 75, stays quiet at 60, is never "crossed" at 90; recovery flags `crossedAbove`; a single run still carries the bar) = 12 green, `tsc -b` clean. → ROADMAP Block BN / §LXI.2
- **T825** — **The Persona Match project's Model (LLM) picker now drives the fit score, not just the suggestions.** T704's `TurPersonaMatchAdjustmentService` honoured the project's `llmInstanceId` through the T728 override seam, but `TurPersonaContentFitEvaluator` called the four-argument `TurLlmSummaryService.generate(...)` — so every number in the matrix came from the **global default** LLM regardless of what the project selected, and changing the project's model visibly changed the suggestions while leaving the scores they reason about untouched. `evaluate`/`evaluateText` gained an optional `llmInstanceId` (the old signatures stay as delegating overloads passing `null`), threaded into the five-argument overload; `TurPersonaMatchAnalysisService` resolves `project.getLlmInstanceId()` once per run and passes it into every parallel cell evaluation. Blank/missing/disabled still fails open to the platform default via `resolveUsableLlmId`. **The cache key carries the model**: `persona-fit:<personaId>:<sourceId>:<contentHash>` gains a `:<llmInstanceId>` segment, so switching models is a miss instead of serving the previous model's verdict (the rule T728 states for the research synthesis lane) — but the segment is appended **only when an override is set**, so the key stays byte-for-byte identical for the four default-LLM call sites (Block AA notebook, SN content-fit endpoint, persona-suggest, T727 concept-fit) and no existing cached verdict is invalidated by the upgrade. Frontend needed nothing — the picker's label carries no "suggestions only" wording, it simply becomes accurate. `TurPersonaContentFitEvaluatorTest` +4 (the instance reaches the summary service; two instances → two keys; the default caller keeps the 3-colon legacy key and passes no override; a whitespace instance is treated as none) = 9 green, and the four caller test classes are unchanged (9 / 4 / 5) confirming the no-op. → ROADMAP Block BN / §LXI.1
- **T831** — An operator who agrees with a persona suggestion now confirms it field by field instead of retyping it in the persona form, and every confirmed field leaves a row saying which suggestion caused it. A PERSONA item's verdict gained `fieldEdits`, carried on the T829 provider schema rather than a second pass, and the digest now feeds the model each persona's current audience — without it a model can call a reading level wrong but cannot name a different value or avoid proposing the same one back. Four gates decide what an operator ever sees: the item is a PERSONA one, the field is one of the four writable (`accessibilityNotes` and `primaryLanguage` stay out of reach, having no verdict evidence), an enumerated value is a real constant, and the value differs from what the persona already says; a CONTENT item gets none, since Turing does not own the document. New `TurPersonaAudienceField` gives each field its own accessor, so `TurPersonaAudienceApplyService` writes one column at a time — the persona `PUT` replaces the whole embeddable and would clobber precisely the fields the operator left alone. `POST /{id}/adjustments/apply` carries the persona-write roles; a field that fails a gate is reported as skipped rather than thrown, so one bad field cannot discard the other ticks. Provenance is not best-effort: `ai_persona_audience_apply_log` shares the write's transaction, one append-only row per field with the value it replaced, the rationale and whether the judge or the fallback proposed it, `project_id` nullable so a row outlives the project. The studio panel pre-ticks nothing and offers no select-all. Verified by injecting each failure first: a wholesale embeddable write, the dropped CONTENT guard, an `accepts` that waves everything through, unlinking the changelog from the master list, and a `confirmedFieldsOf` that ignores the ticks each turned exactly the intended test red. 7142 backend and 241 frontend tests green; with Docker down here the Testcontainers MySQL path did not run, so a new Docker-free H2 test applies the whole master changelog instead.

## Block BH — SN result & facet rendering robustness for feed/crawl-indexed sites (§LVI)

- **T808** — **Default-field resolution falls back to the canonical field names.** `TurSNQueryContextBuilder.defaultFields` echoed the site's `defaultTitleField`/`defaultDescriptionField`/`defaultTextField`/`defaultURLField`/`defaultImageField`/`defaultDateField` columns verbatim, and every client resolves a result through that name map (`doc.fields[queryContext.defaultFields.title]`, `frontend/packages/sdk/src/resolve.ts`) — so a site whose columns are **blank** handed the client a `null` field name and every result rendered `""`, silently, right next to a correct result count. The sites that hit it are exactly the ones provisioned by a path that never runs `TurSNTemplate.defaultSNUI`: the exchange import/restore (`TurSNSiteImport.createSNSiteFromExchange` copies the columns verbatim — null if the source had them null) and the T796 structured-feed auto-provisioner. Each column now falls back **per column** to its canonical `TurSNFieldName` constant (`title` / `abstract` / `text` / `url` / `image` / `publication_date`) when blank, so a configured value always wins and an unmapped site still names the conventional fields instead of nothing. Safety net only — it does not fix a feed field *name mismatch* (`label` ≠ `title`), which is T810's job. `TurSNQueryContextBuilderTest` +2 (all-null falls back to all six canonical names; a configured column survives while a whitespace-only sibling falls back) = 9 green. → ROADMAP Block BH / §LVI.1
- **T809** — **Facet group headers fall back to a humanized field name.** An auto-provisioned crawl/structured-feed facet field has `facetName = null` **and** zero locale-label rows (`TurSNFieldProvisioner.saveSiteFieldExt`/`saveFacetLocales` — the job attribute spec carried no labels), so a fully populated facet rendered under a **blank** header. The design's suspected culprit (`searchFacetBean`'s `orElse(...label(getFacetName()))`) turned out to be unreachable: `TurSNSearchProcess.facetLocalesFor` synthesizes **one** label row stamped with the request locale and a `null` label, so the locale filter always matched it and the `facetName` fallback never ran. Both ends are now fixed. New `TurSNFieldLabel` in **turing-commons** (next to `TurSNFieldName`) humanizes a raw field name — `_`/`-`/`.` become spaces, camelCase and acronym runs split, letter→digit splits but digit→letter does not, so `pricing_inputPer1M` → *"Pricing Input Per 1M"*, `defaultURLField` → *"Default URL Field"* and `MODEL` stays `MODEL`; it capitalizes without lowercasing the rest and returns `null` when there is nothing to humanize. `facetLocalesFor` now skips label rows whose label is blank and labels its synthetic row `orHumanized(facetName, name)`; the renderer's `searchFacetBean` was refactored to a `groupLabel(...)` resolving **locale-matched non-blank label → `facetName` → humanized name** (previously a blank locale row won outright). Configured labels always win, and facet *value* labels already fell back to the raw attribute value — this closes the remaining label-less case. `TurSNFieldLabelTest` (19) + `TurSNFacetRendererTest` +5 (nothing configured → humanized; `facetName` when no locale row; locale label wins; blank locale label skipped; both blank → humanized) = 13 green. → ROADMAP Block BH / §LVI.2
- **T810** — **Auto-provisioning wires the render mapping from the feed's own field names.** T808/T809 stop a feed-indexed site rendering *blank*; this makes it render *right* with no post-deploy admin step. A structured feed carries its own field names (the model catalog indexes `label`, `vendor`, `kind`, `contextWindow`, … and has **no** `title`/`abstract`), so the canonical fallback still names fields the documents don't have. `TurStructuredFeedProperty.Source` gained `defaultTitleField` / `defaultDescriptionField` / `defaultTextField` / `defaultUrlField` / `defaultImageField` / `defaultDateField` plus a `facetLabels` map (fieldName → group label), and `TurStructuredFeedProvisioningOnStartup` applies them: `applyDefaultFields` **before** the first ingest (so the very first search after boot renders titles) and `applyFacetLabels` **after** it (facet labels need the field rows the ingest creates from the manifest; on an existing site they are already there). Both are **fill-blank-only** — a column/label an operator already set in admin is never overwritten, so re-running is a no-op — and declaring a label never turns a non-faceted field into a facet (that stays the manifest's call). The six columns live in one `defaultFieldWirings(...)` table so a new column is a single line, and every applied value is logged. The ingester side now also honours labels declared **in the remote manifest**: `provisionSchema` reads each field's `facetName` (or the `facetLabel` alias) as either a plain string (→ the reserved `default` key) or a locale map, feeding the `TurSNJobAttributeSpec.facetName` map `TurSNFieldProvisioner` already consumes for `facetName` + locale rows; an unparseable locale key is dropped with a warning rather than allowed to abort field creation. New `TurSNSiteFieldExtRepository.findByTurSNSiteAndName`. The concrete mapping stays in the deployment's config (viglet/cloud) — Turing only gained the ability to honour it. `TurStructuredFeedProvisioningOnStartupTest` +4 (declared columns applied + undeclared left null for the T808 fallback; an admin edit never overwritten; labels applied only where blank; nothing declared → no writes at all) = 9 green, `TurStructuredFeedIngestServiceTest` +1 (string label, locale map, bad locale key dropped, undeclared field → empty) = 13 green. **Closes Block BH — T808–T810 all shipped.** → ROADMAP Block BH / §LVI.3

## Block BM — Headless admin bootstrap (`TURING_ADMIN_PASSWORD`)

- **T824** — **`TURING_ADMIN_PASSWORD` now actually sets the admin password on startup.** Both READMEs documented `docker run -e TURING_ADMIN_PASSWORD=…` as the way to get a usable admin login, but **nothing read that variable** — no property, no `@Value`, no relaxed-binding match: `TurUserOnStartup` seeded `admin` with a **null** password and the only way to set one was the interactive first-access setup screen, so every headless/CI/`docker run` deploy landed on a setup form (and the README's "log in with `admin`/`admin`" was impossible). New `turing.admin.password` (`TurAdminProperty` on `TurConfigProperties`, declared as `${TURING_ADMIN_PASSWORD:}` in `application.yaml` so the env name binds **exactly** — the `turing.allowedOrigins`→`TURING_ALLOWEDORIGINS` trap is why the property is dotted, not hyphenated). The password logic moved out of the controller into a new `TurAdminPasswordService` that the setup endpoint **and** the startup bootstrap share, so both enforce the same 6-character minimum and the same encoder: `TurSetupAPI` is now a thin delegate (invalid password → `IllegalArgumentException` → 400 via the global handler, unchanged contract; still 403 once configured — including when startup set it). `TurOnStartup` calls `bootstrapFromConfiguration()` **outside** the `FIRST_TIME` block on purpose, so an install seeded before the variable was supplied (or one whose setup screen was never completed) still gets a login. Bootstrap is deliberately **set-once and boot-safe**: it only applies while the password is unset (a restart with the variable still present never overwrites what an operator chose in the console), and a blank/too-short value only logs — it never fails startup. Both READMEs corrected (the `admin`/`admin` example violated the 6-char minimum → `turing123`, plus the set-once semantics). `TurAdminPasswordServiceTest` (9) + `TurOnStartupTest` (2, both paths assert the bootstrap runs) + `TurUserOnStartupTest` (2) = 13 green.

## Block BL — Stuff-all at catalog scale: compact, size-adaptive projection (§LX)

- **T822** — **Compact, size-adaptive stuff-all projection.** The T793 stuff-all path (ground the LLM on the whole catalog) used one verbose block per row — title + snippet ≤320 chars + *every* field labelled with its full T799 description — so a catalog that grew past a few hundred rows blew `stuffAllTokenBudget` and silently dropped to brittle filtered retrieval, losing the whole-catalog reasoning that answers advisory questions ("a cheap chat model for my site"). `TurCatalogCopilotService.stuffAllRetrieve` is now **size-adaptive**: it tries the full projection first, and if that is over budget retries a new **compact** projection — one lean line per row (`[rank] title | key: value | …`) over only the declared *key* fields (numeric/sortable + facet, from `TurNLFacetField.numeric()`/`facet()`), no snippet, no descriptions (~10× smaller per row) — falling back to filtered retrieval only when even the compact whole-catalog exceeds budget. `project` was refactored to a shared `projectWith(…, ContextBlockBuilder)` feeding both the verbose (`contextBlock`) and compact (`compactContextBlock`) builders; citations are identical either way. The INFO log distinguishes `STUFF_ALL (full)` / `STUFF_ALL (compact)` / `OVER BUDGET even compact -> FILTERED` (T823). `TurCatalogCopilotServiceTest` +1 (compact rescues an over-budget catalog; parser never consulted) = 12 green. **Closes Block BL.** → ROADMAP Block BL / §LX.1
- **T823** — **Copilot retrieval-mode observability.** The copilot silently chose between stuff-all (ground on the whole catalog) and filtered NL→DSL retrieval, and the filtered path never logged the query it ran — so when the model-catalog grew past the stuff-all token budget and quietly dropped to filtered mode, "no results" answers were undiagnosable. Now every request logs its decision at **INFO** in `TurCatalogCopilotService`: stuff-all emits `STUFF_ALL: grounding on all N rows (~X tokens, budget Y)`, or the exact reason it fell back — `stuff-all OVER BUDGET: ~X tokens > Y (rows=N) -> FILTERED` / `stuff-all SKIPPED: index has N rows > fetched M (stuffAllMaxDocs)`; the filtered path logs `FILTERED mode: rankingIntent=<field order|none>, query=<full DSL JSON>` before executing and `FILTERED returned N hits (filters: …)` after. New `toJson`/`blankToNone` log helpers; `TurCatalogCopilotServiceTest` 11 green. (The T822 compact-projection fix is the scalable remedy; this makes the behaviour it addresses visible.) → ROADMAP Block BL / §LX.2

## Block BK — Configurable copilot query-planning strategy (deterministic / LLM-assisted / hybrid) (§LIX)

- **T818** — **Planning-strategy seam + `TurCopilotPlanningStrategy` enum.** NL→query planning was hardcoded inside `TurCatalogCopilotService.retrieve()` (T811 ranking overlay + one LLM facet parse), so a deployment could not trade cost for quality. New `com.viglet.turing.genai.catalog.planning` package: a `TurCopilotQueryPlanner` seam (`plan(PlanRequest)` + an optional `replan(request, previous, hits)` second chance used by T820, plus the shared `DEFAULT_TOP_K`), the `TurCopilotQueryPlan` record carrying the executed query + strategy + LLM-pass count + a human note, `TurCopilotQueryBodies` (the `match_all` / free-text bodies and the `withSort`/`withBoundedSize` immutable rewrites lifted out of the service, built by copying a canned body instead of calling the 29-component `TurDslQueryRequest` constructor), and `TurDeterministicCopilotQueryPlanner` — today's behaviour verbatim (ranking clause stripped before the parse, sort + top-k injected after, no LLM at all when the question is sort-only), now reporting its true LLM cost. `TurCopilotQueryPlannerFactory` resolves the planner + analysis depth per site: `TurSNSiteGenAi.copilotPlanningStrategy`/`copilotPlanningMaxPasses` (both **nullable** = inherit) → `turing.genai.copilot.planning.*` → `DETERMINISTIC`; the per-site read goes through a new `TurSNSiteRepository.findGenAiByNameIgnoreCase` projection so the non-transactional copilot path never navigates a LAZY `@OneToOne`. Liquibase `v2026.3.1.117`. `TurCopilotPlanningStrategyTest` (2, lenient parse never silently enables LLM calls) + `TurDeterministicCopilotQueryPlannerTest` (8) + `TurCopilotQueryPlannerFactoryTest` (8) + `TurCopilotPlanningStrategyIT` (4 — the columns round-trip, a planner exists for every enum value, precedence holds against a live schema); zero behaviour change at the default. → ROADMAP Block BK / §LIX.1
- **T819** — **LLM multi-pass planner (parse → judge → refine).** `LLM_ASSISTED` decomposes planning into narrow verifiable steps instead of one "produce the whole DSL" call: `TurLlmAssistedCopilotQueryPlanner` parses the **full** question (nothing stripped — this is where i18n comes from, "modelo mais barato" needs no lexicon), then `TurCopilotPlanJudge.judge` audits the query against it (dropped facet? missing/wrong sort? ungrounded field?) and `refine` restates the question as a flat constraint list. Depth is a parameter (`turing.genai.copilot.planning.max-passes`: 0 = parse only, 1 = + judge, 2 = + refine, clamped). Both passes are schema-bound through the provider's own structured-output surface (OpenAI `json_schema`, Anthropic forced tool, then a converter-format chat call for other vendors) per the `gpt-4o-mini` raw-JSON caution. The LLM only *names* constraints: `TurCopilotPlanCompiler` turns them into DSL deterministically, dropping every undeclared field, every range on a non-numeric field and any sort whose field isn't declared-numeric or whose direction isn't `asc`/`desc` — so grounding holds by construction, not by prompt discipline. At depth 1 the sort the judge named is still applied (verified, not trusted) without a second generation call. Fail-open at every step: failed parse → free-text, failed judge → the parse, ungrounded repair → the previous plan. `TurCopilotPlanValidatorTest` (12) + `TurCopilotPlanCompilerTest` (14) + `TurLlmAssistedCopilotQueryPlannerTest` (13). → ROADMAP Block BK / §LIX.2
- **T820** — **HYBRID escalation.** `TurHybridCopilotQueryPlanner` runs the deterministic fast-path, then escalates to the T819 passes through the new `replan` hook **only** when the result failed: zero hits, or a degenerate plan (bare `match_all`, no filter and no sort — "the parse came back empty") on a question specific enough to deserve better. A broad "what do you have?" legitimately plans to `match_all` and is never escalated, so the common case still costs one parse; `TurCopilotPlanValidator.isDegenerate`/`isSpecific` make both gates deterministic and unit-testable, and the escalation is itself fail-open (an escalated plan that is degenerate or spent no LLM pass is discarded and the fast-path result stands). Every executed plan now logs `strategy=… llmPasses=… plan=… query=…` at INFO, extending T823's observability to the strategy dimension. `TurHybridCopilotQueryPlannerTest` (8). → ROADMAP Block BK / §LIX.3
- **T821** — **Admin surface + comparative strategy eval.** Two halves. *Admin*: the GenAI site form gained a *Copilot Query Planning* card — a four-way strategy picker (**Default** = pin nothing and inherit `turing.genai.copilot.planning.strategy`, Deterministic, LLM-assisted, Hybrid) with a per-choice cost/quality/i18n hint, an analysis-depth selector (inherit / 0 / 1 / 2) disabled unless the picked strategy actually spends passes, and a callout stating each judge/refine pass is one extra LLM call per planned question; `copilotPlanningStrategy`/`copilotPlanningMaxPasses` on the `TurSNSiteGenAi` TS model (null = inherit) with en + pt i18n; `sn.site.genai.form.copilot-planning.test.tsx` (6 — the payload contract). *Comparative eval*: a strategy is now **measurable per catalog before switching**. New `TurCopilotPlanningComparisonService` runs one NL→facet eval pack through every planner and reports the three axes side by side per strategy — mean score (quality), total LLM calls (cost) and wall-clock (latency) — plus the per-case breakdown, over `TurCopilotPlanningComparisonRequest` (optional strategy subset + depth override, so an `LLM_ASSISTED` row's up-to-3-calls-per-case can be opted out of). It lives on the planning side, not in `TurNLFacetEvalService`, because `genai.catalog.planning` already depends on `sn.dsl.eval` — the dependency stays one-way, reusing the pack, the T385 scorer and (now public) `resolveSchema`. The run is **plan-only** and says so: `HYBRID`'s escalation only fires on live retrieval, so it necessarily scores identically to `DETERMINISTIC` here, and the report carries that as a first-class `caveats` list + a per-row `note` rather than letting the table imply parity. Exposed as `POST /api/sn/nl-facet-eval/planning-comparison` (+ `/dataset/{id}/planning-comparison`) and surfaced in the Eval Studio's NL→Facet tab as a *Compare planners* action rendering the table with the caveats. `TurCopilotPlanningComparisonServiceTest` (9) + `bento.eval.page.test.tsx` +1 = 15 green. **Closes Block BK.** → ROADMAP Block BK / §LIX.4

## Block BI — Deterministic (LLM-independent) query planning for the copilot (§LVII)

- **T813** — **Model-catalog NL→facet eval sample + local regression harness.** Added `turing-app/src/test/resources/nl-facet-eval/model-catalog.nl-facet-eval.json` — the catalog's declared schema (`kind`, `vendor`, `modality`, `benchmarks_intelligenceIndex`, `benchmarks_scores_math_value`, `pricing_inputPer1M`/`_outputPer1M`, `contextWindow`, `maxOutputTokens`) plus the exact live-failing cases with golden `sort`/`filter` expectations ("chat models sorted by intelligence index" → kind=chat + intelligenceIndex desc; "which embedding has the lowest price" → kind=embedding + pricing asc; "biggest context window"; "most intelligent"; and the two no-sort facet-only baselines). New always-on `TurNLRankingPlannerSampleTest` loads the pack and asserts the deterministic planner resolves every golden sort **and** that stripping the ranking clause leaves the facet token intact (the crux — `kind=CHAT`/`kind=EMBEDDING` never lost), reproducing the reported bug's fix locally with **no LLM and no index** (runs in CI). The pack format is the same one `TurNLFacetEvalIT` provisions against a real embedded Lucene index + real LLM, so it doubles as a `-Pnl-facet-eval` fixture. `TurNLRankingPlannerSampleTest` (1, 8 cases) green. **Closes Block BI — T811–T813 all shipped.** → ROADMAP Block BI / §LVII.3
- **T812** — **Genericize the copilot + NL→facet system prompts.** With ranking now handled procedurally (T811), the domain-flavoured superlative enumerations were removed from both prompts. `TurLlmNLFacetParser.systemPrompt`'s sort rule dropped its example list ("cheapest"/"lowest price"/"most affordable"/"fastest") for a schema-driven abstract rule ("a superlative expresses a SORT on the numeric field whose DESCRIPTION matches that quality — asc for a minimum, desc for a maximum; never put the superlative word in a filter"). `TurCatalogCopilotService.systemPrompt` keeps the T799 rank-by-labelled-metric guardrail but neutralised ("cheapest" and the model-catalog "headline index"/"benchmark" framing removed → "a narrower per-category sub-metric is not the overall metric"), says the results are already ordered for the question, and cites the "cited result" (not "cited model"). No behaviour change — the copilot still ranks via T811; the prompt just stops leaking one domain's vocabulary so it generalises to any catalog. `TurCatalogCopilotServiceTest` prompt assertion updated; 21 green. → ROADMAP Block BI / §LVII.2
- **T811** — **Deterministic ranking-intent planner (LLM-independent numeric sort).** NL→DSL query planning was 100% LLM (`TurLlmNLFacetParser`) and T802(b)'s "superlative→sort" was prompt-only, so on a cheap default model a superlative/interrogative phrasing ("which chat model scores **highest** on the intelligence index?", "which embedding has the **lowest** price?") returned an empty query and dropped even the valid `kind` facet → `totalHits:0`.
