New pure `TurNLRankingPlanner` (no LLM, no DB): a small superlative lexicon fixes the direction (`most/highest/largest/best/top`→desc, `cheapest/lowest/smallest/least/fastest`→asc, plus explicit `sorted by …`→desc), and the target **numeric** field is resolved by scoring the query's tokens against each declared field's own name + description tokens (camelCase-split), bridged by a compact generic adjective→noun synonym seed (`cheapest`→price/pricing, `intelligent`→intelligence, `biggest`→context/window). `TurCatalogCopilotService.retrieve()` now detects the intent, **strips the ranking clause** from the text handed to the LLM (so it keeps `kind=CHAT` instead of choking), then injects the deterministic `sort` (never overriding a `sort` the LLM already produced) and guarantees a top-k `size` — a stripped query of only stopwords degrades to a sort-only `match_all`, so "highest intelligence index" returns a bounded ranked page. Additive + fail-open: no intent → the path is unchanged; a resolution miss → no sort. The engine side needed nothing — `TurDslLuceneExecutor.buildSort` already sorts numeric fields correctly. `TurNLRankingPlannerTest` (10, pure/always-on) + `TurCatalogCopilotServiceTest` +2 (strip-then-facet keeps the filter; sort-only runs match_all) = 21 green, compile clean. → ROADMAP Block BI / §LVII.1

## Block BQ — The answer engine as a first-class search lens (`/answer`) (§LXIV)

- **T852** — `/answer/{siteName}` streams the answer endpoint beside `/sn` and `/ann`, with `q`, locale and `fq` read from the URL — so an answer's input is a link.
- **T853** — An `answer` launcher tile gated by `GET /sn/{site}/chat/enabled`, so a vectorless site gets the AI lens and a closed gate names the condition to fix.
- **T854** — The `/answer` page shows its work: provenance expanded, the research trace, footnote rank + score + chunk per source, and the raw `JsonSheet` one click away.
- **T855** — A lens switcher on `/answer` and `/ann` replays one question as keyword, vector and answer, translating the `fq` and bare-param filter dialects between them.
- **T890** — Share mints a permalink and copies a link to a new read-only snapshot view; freshness re-check flags the drifted source, and one thumbs verdict is recorded per answer.
- **T891** — The switcher reaches the keyword page behind a `_lens=1` opt-in the switcher and the console launcher append, so the round trip closes and a visitor's page is unchanged.
- **T889** — The answer stream, freshness and signal are anonymous like the chat beside them; minting a share permalink and the operator page stay authenticated, guarded by a source test.
- **T976** — A new answer.md documents the endpoint from its contract — parameters, the eight SSE events, the five errors, freshness, signals and why share is not anonymous — and the matrix now accounts for every block.

## Block BU — The search cache is off by a thousand and never evicted on write

- **T870** — Indexing, deindexing and commit now evict the search cache per site, so a reindexed document is visible on the next search instead of after the 24h sweep.
- **T871** — The backstop sweep now reads `turing.search.cache.ttl`, a Duration that states its unit (24h, PT1H, 30m); the old millisecond key stays bound and warns.
- **T872** — The MongoDB log purge moved to its own `TurLoggingPurgeService`, so the cached-search seam's constructor now names only the search process it actually calls.
- **T895** — The three site-name-keyed caches wrap their key in one `scoped(...)` primitive, and a conformance lint fails any new explicitly-keyed cache that neither scopes nor justifies itself.
- **T896** — A JPA callback on the 19 entities a search answer is derived from now clears the cache, and a test fails when a new SN entity declares neither that it affects answers nor why not.
- **T899** — Spotlight and field-ext deletes load their rows and delete them one by one, so removal fires the callbacks that evict the snapshot and search caches; a lint refuses a new bulk delete.
- **T900** — The indexing queue coalesces the whole-cache clears its own spotlight writes provoke into at most one, since it already evicts every written site when the message ends.
- **T901** — Eviction is registered on the transaction and runs after commit, so nothing is discarded while the write is still invisible and a rollback discards nothing at all.
- **T903** — Both snapshot caches clear through the shared after-commit scheduler the search cache now uses, so one seam decides when every SN eviction becomes visible.
- **T904** — The three evictions on transactional paths clear through the after-commit scheduler, and a lint refuses a new @CacheEvict in a transactional class.
- **T906** — The three bulk deletes are gone so the callbacks fire, the two hand compensations that stood in for them are removed, and the lint now derives its scope from the entities that declare a listener.
- **T907** — The lint reads the entity out of each repository declaration instead of guessing it from the file name, and names the repositories the derivation must keep finding.
- **T908** — The annotation half of the lint reads bytecode, where an annotation means one thing however it is spelled; the call half stays on source, where a method name has to appear literally.
- **T909** — The rule is now `@Modifying` itself rather than the word delete, so a bulk update on a listener-bearing entity fails too, with a stated-reason door for the ones that are deliberate.
- **T910** — Both doors are pure functions now, exercised against fixtures: a marked call is exempt, one marker never covers a second, and an allowlisted member is not reported.
- **T911** — An IT indexes and edits a spotlight against a live cache and asserts the entries go and the answer moves, so the stored key and the evictor's prefix are checked against each other.
- **T912** — The evictor names the IT that proves it and the IT declares it owns the cache-enabled context, so the end-to-end proof is reachable from the code it covers.
- **T905** — A daily locked sweep bounds how long a missed eviction can serve stale SN configuration, and says "backstop" in the log so a healthy sweep is not read as a defect.
- 🗑 **T897** — abandoned: The "silently" was fixed by the WARN this task already shipped, and the generation counter would replace removal with abandonment — dead entries accruing until the daily sweep — to port to a non-Map provider this codebase does not wire.

## Block BV — The quality gate stops at the package boundary

- **T875** — publish-js-sdk.yml now filters react-sdk instead of the vanilla SDK, so the package it publishes is the one it builds rather than a prepublishOnly side effect, and the publish is gated on the size budget and on tests when present.
- **T876** — publish-sdk.yml releases the vanilla SDK behind the same build, size and test gates the other packages get, and the react-sdk release now patch-bumps only react-sdk so unpublished workspaces stop drifting from npm.
- **T873** — validate-js.yml now builds and tests the CLI and the flow DSL on every dispatch, so their 115 tests fail in CI instead of at release time, and the size gate is a declared matrix property rather than a guess from the package scope.
- **T874** — the admin app is a validate-js.yml matrix entry, so its 222 vitest tests and its tsc-plus-vite build now run in CI instead of only on a developer's machine.
- **T913** — the undici override keeps its security floor but stops at the 7 line, so jsdom loads again and the 51 test files that could not start a worker run their 304 tests.
- **T877** — the React SDK has a vitest suite, and its first 17 tests cover the provider/store contract T710 changed: which transport runs, which of data and structured is authoritative, that facet and page actions write the URL, that implicit params never reach it, and that a late response cannot overwrite a newer one.
- **T892** — a table-driven test reads the Java enum constants and the TypeScript union literals from source and asserts set equality for four mirrored pairs, so the next constant added on one side fails the SDK suite instead of narrowing to undefined.
- **T878** — the size script takes an opt-in groupBy dir, so react-sdk now baselines each top-level dist directory as well as its total and a regression is reported as the directory that grew rather than as one package-wide number.
- **T914** — every publish workflow calls pnpm publish, so the workspace protocol is rewritten to the versions actually on npm instead of shipped verbatim, and a test over the workflow files fails the SDK suite if bare npm publish comes back.
- **T915** — the React SDK's tsconfig excludes its Storybook tree the way react-ui's always did, so the published package drops from 422 entries to 254, and the size budget's now-dead exclusions are gone with it so what ships is what is weighed.
- **T916** — the showcase and the marketing site are validate-js.yml matrix entries, so all four apps build in CI and the site's build is no longer verified by the act of publishing it.
- **T917** — the tracked manifests carry no timestamp and a post-build --dist step stamps buildDate onto the copy in dist, so two consecutive builds leave the four files byte-identical while the ZIP still ships a real build date.
- **T918** — each package has a tsconfig.test.json that includes what the shipping config excludes and emits nothing, wired into the matrix, and it already caught a zero-arg mock indexed as if it took one and a JSX namespace React 19 removed.
- **T919** — the CLI and the flow DSL publish dist/src rather than all of dist, dropping 40 and 16 compiled test files, and a shared contents check runs in the matrix asserting no tarball carries tests or stories and that every declared entry point is in it.
- **T920** — no package emits or publishes a source map, so nothing ships a reference that resolves to nothing, and the five tarballs drop from 2432 kB unpacked to 1226 kB with every entry point still present.
- **T921** — one unanchored dist rule replaces thirteen per-workspace lines and the flow DSL's 20 compiled files are out of the index, so a rebuild of any workspace leaves the tree clean and a new workspace is covered without an edit.
- **T922** — the three gates that were written to run per branch now do: validate-js, validate and roadkeep trigger on pull requests and on pushes to a 2026 branch, with concurrency cancelling superseded runs and the Sonar analysis held to events that carry a token.
- **T923** — all five publish workflows run the contents check as their last gate before the tarball leaves, so the check written for three publish defects finally runs at the moment it is about to matter.
- **T924** — the audit runs weekly and on a push that changes the dependency tree instead of never, with the pull_request trigger its header asks for held back until T927 clears the ten advisories that would make it red before any change was made.
- **T928** — both were reverted: commit 24a848332e had set the workflows to manual dispatch on purpose, and the stale header comments the two tasks reasoned from predate it, so the automatic triggers they added are gone and the dispatch-only state stands.
- 🗑 **T925** — abandoned: Filed on the assumption that validate-js.yml runs per push, which T928 reversed: under manual dispatch the matrix has no per-entry input, so a single-package workflow is the cheap way to validate react-ui alone rather than a duplicate of something that runs anyway.
- **T926** — the CLI and the flow DSL carry a measured sizeBudget, so all five published packages are weighed by the matrix and by their own publish workflow, the CLI by directory and the flow DSL file by file.
- **T929** — the three headers say on demand only and say why, so no comment in the workflows still describes a trigger the file does not have, and roadkeep's base_ref fallback is documented as deliberate rather than left reading as residue.
- **T927** — the audit exits 0: seven advisories took capped floors and react-router's RSC-mode CSRF is recorded as unreachable with the evidence, the RSC runtime being absent from the tree, rather than paid for with a breaking major across 220 files.
- 🗑 **T931** — abandoned: The premise is false: a forced downgrade via the key 'postcss@8' applied cleanly, so the version-selector spelling works in this pnpm and js-yaml@3 is not inert for the reason claimed. What the js-yaml@5 episode actually showed is refiled as T932.
- **T933** — check-overrides.mjs reads the declared overrides against what the lockfile resolved and fails when one names nothing or is not in force, which is how js-yaml@3 is now visible as selecting no copy in the tree.
- **T934** — the js-yaml floor is documented as deliberately defensive with the evidence that it once bound 3.14.2, and the block now states the rule that separates a security floor kept after its dependency leaves from a compatibility pin that goes.
- **T935** — seventeen tests run the three guards as subprocesses against temp fixtures and assert the exit code, and breaking each guard in turn fails exactly one of them, so the mutation evidence that was run once by hand now runs on every dispatch.
- **T944** — Every prop a README fence passes is now checked against the props its component declares, and the `TuringResultList` example says `itemComponent`. The section proposed compiling the fences with `tsc`, and measuring first said not to. Of the workspace's 41 README fences, 18 are `tsx` and 7 reference a Turing component — and almost none is a compilable program: they are snippets whose `turing`, `pages` and `store` arrive from the paragraph above. Compiling would have meant marking most of them partial, and a guard most of its subjects opt out of is a guard in name. Checking the props needs no fence to be self-contained and catches exactly the class this task names, a prop that outlived its rename. The prop names are **derived**, never listed: `frontend/scripts/readme-prop-rules.mjs` reads each package's own `export interface Turing…Props` by brace-matching, indexing 35 components, and the one violation it reported across all of them was the documented `renderItem`. Zero-dependency by the house rule the sibling rules keep, which also ruled out the TypeScript compiler API — `typescript` is not hoisted to the workspace root. It runs under `node --test` and from the app suite, mirroring how T939's entry point works, and `.github/workflows/validate-js.yml` already runs `test:scripts`. Three faults were injected: restoring the original `renderItem`, and a different typo in a different fence, each turn the check red — so it is not fitted to this one bug — and renaming `TuringResultListProps` turns the index assertions red rather than silently emptying the index, which is how a guard like this usually dies. 243 frontend tests green, `tsc -b` clean.
- **T954** — The search snapshot now pins the `Serializable` contract its clustered cache relies on, the way two smaller caches already did. `TurCacheConfig` returns a Hazelcast-backed `CacheManager` whenever a `HazelcastInstance` is present, and a single-node install falls back to `ConcurrentMapCacheManager`, which never serializes anything — so a component that cannot round-trip passes every suite and every local run and fails only in a cluster. Block AC had just replaced all six of the snapshot's components with records, so every byte in that cache was new and nothing checked it. Three cases: a fully-populated snapshot round-trips, with every component populated because an empty collection would prove nothing about its element type; the rebuilt site is intact afterwards, including the GenAI fields the ranking service reads and the vendor plugin the factory reads; and the value the cache actually stores is the **unwrapped** one. That last case exists because the obvious reading of this code is wrong in an expensive way. `getSnapshot` returns `Optional`, `java.util.Optional` is not `Serializable`, and it looks exactly like a `NotSerializableException` waiting for a clustered deployment — I wrote that assertion, watched it go red, and checked the bytecode before filing anything: `CacheAspectSupport.unwrapReturnValue` runs the value through `ObjectUtils.unwrapOptional` before the put and re-wraps it on the read, so the snapshot is what serializes and an absent site is cached as `null`. The test now pins that instead of a defect that does not exist. Three injected faults each turn it red: dropping `Serializable` from the site view, from a nested view, and losing the GenAI graph across the round-trip. 7203 tests green.
- **T902** — STRATEGY.md has its own [refs] namespace, so the 26 doubled anchors are zero; eight intra-file citations were repointed and the seven blocked by older prose debt are T955.
- **T955** — Four sections were split rather than cut, so every word survives; 28 citations now carry the namespace, section.too-long is zero and the spent ids are gone.
- **T967** — The 24 dangling citations are repointed at the tasks that shipped them, and with RK1217 landed upstream the gate runs absolute: baseline dropped from the workflow.
- **T969** — The workflow pins roadkeep at a SHA verified to lint this corpus clean, and the skill says the pinned copy decides a disagreement while /plugin update aligns the local one.
- **T971** — Surefire prints the causal chain now, so a context failure names the missing bean on the console instead of hiding it in a report file behind five identical walls.
- **T972** — A probe IT went from BUILD SUCCESS exit 0 to BUILD FAILURE exit 1; the suite was green on the day, so the gate came back without excluding anything.
- **T974** — The class comment now says these five tests run, with the skeleton state named as the history it is, so nobody discounts a live gate on the strength of a stale paragraph.
- **T977** — The skill now carries the command that answers it — roadkeep block list against COVERAGE.md — and says a label the matrix spells differently is a question rather than an orphan.
- **T978** — shared-types is the twelfth matrix entry now, so its own tsc runs in CI instead of only sideways through whatever dependent happened to build it.

## Block BW — What the link-free response and its consumers still lack (§LXX)

- **T879** — TurSNStructuredSearchBean now carries the icon and the mapper copies it off the widget, so a site that configured one renders it POST-native instead of the generic Turing logo. search-view reads structured.icon; a pure toSearchView suite plus two mapper tests pin both shapes.
- **T880** — TurStructuredSearchResponse now declares similar: TurSimilarDocument[] and types spotlights as TurSpotlightDocument[], both exported from the vanilla SDK and re-exported by the React one, so a typed client can read what the mapper has written since T709. Asserted by a cast-free read in the post-native suite, type-checked via tsconfig.test.json.
- **T881** — toSearchView, its view models and a structural ProjectableSearchStore now live in @viglet/turing-sdk beside resolveDocuments, exported from both SDKs, so an EDS block resolves a response the same way the admin page does. The app keeps only the useSearchView memo wrapper, and the T835 suite imports the real projection instead of stubbing it.
- **T882** — react-ui ships TuringDocumentChips, which narrows the metadata union once and turns each arm into the action it already described: an aria-pressed button for a filter query, an anchor for a legacy href, plain text when no handler was given. TuringResultList forwards onToggleFacet/onNavigate to its row component, and both SDKs re-export the component.
- **T883** — The README now opens with the two response shapes and a table saying data.widget.facet is null by default; both examples were rewritten off it — the manual one onto structured.facets + toggleFacet/goToPage, the URL-synced one onto useTuringFacets with the urlSync prop it always needed. Both hook surfaces list structured and the link-free actions.
- **T884** — Both co-browse lookups now walk the secondary rail as well — structured.secondaryFacets and, once TurWidget declared it, widget.secondaryFacet — so the agent toggles a secondary facet in either transport instead of answering Facet not found for a rail the user can see. Five cases pinned in a new co-browse suite.
- **T885** — A convention test over frontend/apps/** fails on the three shapes T865-T867 shipped — a widget read off data, a store.data read, a data destructured out of the store — with comments stripped so a file may still explain the rule. The boundary is stated in the file: the SDK reads both shapes on purpose and is out of scope, and two counter-suites prove the rules match the bugs and spare the app's unrelated data/widget vocabulary.

## Block BJ — Configurable, per-language NL ranking lexicon (Lucene analysis)

- **T814** — The ranking vocabulary is a resource bundle a `TurRankingLexicon` is built from, so a deployment tunes it as data instead of editing Java.
- **T815** — Query and declared field go through one Lucene analyzer, so `pricing` meets `price` by stemming instead of by an enumerated inflection.
- **T816** — The lexicon and analyzer follow the request locale, so a `pt`/`es` question resolves its own sort and any other language falls back to `en`.
- **T817** — A site extends the resolved lexicon with its own domain words from the admin form, merged over the shipped bundle rather than replacing it.
- **T940** — A declared field is analysed once per lexicon instead of on every query, so the ranking pass stops scaling with field count.
- **T941** — The override cache evicts least-recently-used instead of refusing new entries, so no site past the cap rebuilds its lexicon per request.
- **T942** — A reload endpoint recompiles the lexicons from the resources and `lexicon-dir` as they are now, so editing a mounted file no longer needs a restart.
- **T943** — A check in the admin form reports what a bundle added and the sort a sample question gets, so an ignored typo is no longer invisible.

## Block J.10 — Viglet AI: the managed LLM onboarding credit (§XIV.10)

- **T956** — A viglet vendor row now resolves TurVigletLlmProvider, which pins the managed baseUrl and the viglet-fast alias for chat and embeddings, and offers that one id as the whole model list.
- **T957** — TurVigletPairingService starts and polls a device-code attempt, and on approval one transaction encrypts the credential, creates the viglet instance and sets DEFAULT_LLM when none was chosen.
- **T958** — The bento home offers Activate Viglet AI and Use my own API key at equal weight while no model exists, chat/agents/skills/SN say so instead of failing late, and the tour leads with it.
- **T959** — Quota refusals now carry resource, used/limit, the reset instant and a signed upgrade target; the bento shell raises one banner offering the paid plan and BYOK at equal weight.
- **T960** — Pairing now mints an Ed25519 keypair and registers the public half; every managed call carries a signed short-lived install assertion, so a lifted credential is not a resellable key.
- **T964** — Streaming mints the assertion per request; the SDK-backed blocking path, which has no interceptor seam, rebuilds its model once the assertion is past half-life.
- **T965** — Rotation is now a pairing variant — mint, register signed with the key in force, then swap — behind a Rotate key action; any failure leaves the old key working.
- **T968** — A MockRestServiceServer bound to the pairing client asserts the requests that actually leave — paths, JSON keys, header names, signed payload and the RFC 8628 error strings.
- **T970** — A throwaway JDK HttpServer stands in for the endpoint on both transports, and the arriving assertion is verified against the public key pairing registered.
- **T973** — The advice now runs in a test that reads the response the console gets, and both ends pin the type URI as a literal so a drift on either side fails a build.
- **T975** — Viglet AI is documented for the operator now — activation, pairing, the fixed alias and key rotation on llm-instances, the real plan limits and the refusal on multi-tenancy, with both COVERAGE rows.
- **T980** — Both pages now say the managed endpoint is not live and that your own key is the working path today, with the description kept for the day it answers.
- **T984** — Both stale notes are gone now that /pair answers, and the one clause that was about signing rather than liveness stayed.
- **T985** — Paired against the live service: the credential landed and a chat turn answered. Rotation was declined with 401 invalid_client and the old key kept working; that refusal is now a fixture.
- **T986** — The reason now goes to stderr as well as the log, and the provider-key-without-crypto-key pair is refused at bean init, before the seed import writes anything.
- **T987** — `turing.startup.default-llm.endpoint` replaces the OpenAI base URL that TurLLMInstanceOnStartup used to hardcode, so a container whose OPENAI_API_KEY is a credential for an OpenAI-compatible gateway is auto-provisioned pointing at that gateway instead of at api.openai.com - the endpoint half of the trio turing.transcription.* has had since T687, applied to the OpenAI path only and logged as ignored on a native-Gemini boot, with three tests in TurLLMInstanceOnStartupAgentProvisionTest pinning the vendor default, the override and the Gemini exclusion.

## Block BX — The bento layer leaves the product it grew in (§XCVI)

- **T988** — turing-app installs 2026.3.3 and takes the bento subpath: the barrel imports the package stylesheet, five representative names resolve to the package, and a parity suite renders them from it and asserts the tile classes, the span passthrough and the --bento-fade property the save-bar morph reads.
- **T989** — The nine leaves resolve to the package: eight local modules are one-line re-exports and BentoEntityTile is re-pointed in the barrel, 657 lines of duplicated implementation are gone, and no page under src/app was edited.
- **T990** — The four compounds resolve to the package: the shell, form hero and save-bar morph are one-line shims, and the list page is an adapter that wires this product's layout hooks to the shared component, because the package takes the layout as a prop rather than fetching it.
- **T991** — No suite in this repository asserts a re-export: the four covering shimmed components are gone, their cases live beside the code in the package, and the ones covering activation, quota, the first-run tour and the not-yet-moved shell all still test local code.
- **T997** — A component re-declared here fails validate-js: the design system's export-manifest lint runs as a turing-app step, names the import that replaces each copy, and the twenty known duplicates carry an allow-duplicate line naming the task that removes them.
- **T998** — copy-ds.cmd is now a thin wrapper over the design system's own pnpm use:local, which discovers its consumers from their package.json and follows each one's install link, so this checkout runs against an unpublished package build with no version directory named anywhere
- **T992** — bento-nav.config.ts is now the array and nothing else: the entry, section and group shapes and bentoNavTarget come from the design system that ships the rail and the palette, and what stays here is this product's map of itself -- its routes, entity ids and the privileges that gate them, carried on TurBentoNavItem, which the package's shape deliberately does not know about; the rail and back-to-top are one-line re-export shims and the shell hands the rail a privilege-filtered group list and its own home route, so a surface is still declared once and appears in both
- **T993** — both are one-line re-export shims now: the palette takes the product array as items -- the same list the rail is handed, so the two cannot disagree about which surfaces exist -- and the shortcuts dialog needed nothing of this product beyond the mac-or-not prop it already had; the palette's product-side test feeds it the real config through a MemoryRouter and asserts the route a reader lands on, which is what the split had to prove
- **T995** — the activation card, quota banner, Viglet activation dialog, no-LLM state and the first-run tour the boundary doc rules product now live in components/turing-bento with their tests and their own barrel, which imports the package stylesheet itself so a page taking only the no-LLM state still gets the frosted rules behind the classes it renders; the names keep their Bento prefix because they are bento tiles and a rename would make a file move read as a behaviour change, and components/bento is left holding shims plus what T999, T1002 and T1003 still own
- **T1002** — bento-layout, bento-inline-edit and bento-hero-icon-picker are one-line re-exports and bento-tones re-exports BentoTone while keeping the Tailwind gradient map two heroes still index, which is T996's to replace; the design's note about the list-page suite was wrong on the facts -- it did not merely build fixtures from resolveBentoLayout, it asserted four of its cases directly, so those left with the code under T991's rule and the package's suite covers the same four plus a displayOrder tie, a missing item and an empty list; the fourteen unexplained names are gone and only T1000's and T1003's markers remain
- **T1007** — the file is deleted, and the two sentences that pointed a reader at it -- CONVENTIONS.md's reduced-motion rule and the bento-ui skill's frosted-look paragraph -- now name the design system's bento.css, whose guard is a superset of the one that was here and covers the dropdown item and the fade pair the local file never did; both also say the part that was missing, which is that a keyframe on a shared class is added in that repository and only a class this product alone renders belongs in this one
- **T1000 (the chrome whose package copy was wider)** — the console routes went in T570, so what was left was chrome, not pages: useSubPageBreadcrumb, SubPageHeader, LanguageSelect and the user provider are now re-exports, checkable because this product's breadcrumb, sidebar and select modules were already package shims, so each takes the same context it wrote to before and picks up the fixes made after the copies diverged -- a breadcrumb level lost behind a repeated parent, a ref that removed nothing on cleanup, an unlabelled combobox, and a user status that let a failed fetch show a skeleton for ever; FloatingFormulasBg turned out to be a deliberate wrapper over the package's, not a pending migration, and its reason says that now
- **T1003** — the menu is a re-export now: T1000 made this product's UserProvider a re-export of the package's, so the package's menu reads the very context the shell mounts and the two-providers-one-user problem is gone rather than worked around; its four entries are routes the shell passes -- account and logout always, organizations and tenant administration only where the feature query says this deployment has tenancy and this reader may administer it -- so the package shows an entry or does not without mirroring a feature model it cannot see
- **T1008 (the two that needed no package change)** — PageHeader is gone rather than swapped: its only use was a fallback header in the two chat surfaces, unreachable since T570 retired the console routes because the bento shell always passes a BentoHero, so emptyStateHeader is required now and the copy went with HeaderUserMenu and the hand-rolled useDiscovery cache it kept alive; DialogDelete is a re-export, its blocked-by link lifted to an href per item, which cost the two call sites in the search-engine cores page that are the only two passing blockedBy at all, keyed by localeId because a site blocks a core once per locale
